# Defect triage

<!-- cspell:disable -- the findings quote code identifiers verbatim -->

The 449 suspected defects listed in the design.md of the OpenSpec baseline changes, each re-read in the code at `c9c196a9b` (2026-10-21) and, where jsdom can show it, reproduced with a throwaway Vitest spec. The repro specs are kept under `docs/review/defect-repros/<component>/`; each one passes while the defect exists. To run one, copy it into `packages/components/<component>/` and run it with `npx vitest run --project angular <path>`.

- Reproduced: 248
- Confirmed in the source only (jsdom cannot show it): 197
- Refuted: 3
- Needs a browser: 1
- Unclear: 0

Severity of the confirmed ones:

- **P0** (release blocker: breaks the build, data, security or the public API): 0
- **P1** (a real bug that hits users in normal use): 11
- **P2** (a real defect under specific conditions or an edge case): 211
- **P3** (hygiene: a11y polish, stale coupling, minor inconsistency): 201
- **P4** (nit): 22

## P1

- **[actions-panel] A global panel aria-hides the application root although the panel is not modal** — reproduced.
  CDK Dialog.open (node\_modules/@angular/cdk/fesm2022/dialog.mjs:480-488) subscribes to the container's \_focusTrapped on the first open dialog, then calls \_hideNonDialogContentFromAssistiveTechnology (dialog.mjs:633-643), which sets aria-hidden="true" on every sibling of the overlay container. KbqActionsPanelContainer.waitForAnimation calls \_trapFocus() when the entrance ends (actions-panel-container.ts:229). That runs afterNextRender and then \_focusTrapped.next() (dialog.mjs:200), which triggers the marks. On the global path, actions-panel.ts:174 uses the root Dialog with the body-level container. The panel is not modal: ariaModal defaults to false (dialog.mjs:56), hasBackdrop is false (actions-panel.ts:182), restoreFocus is false and autoFocus is null (actions-panel.ts:180-181). The repro shows the body child that holds the app is marked aria-hidden="true", the panel shows role=dialog with aria-modal=false and no backdrop, and a button in the hidden app can still be focused. axe reports aria-hidden-focus under `incomplete`, not `violations`, because it takes role=dialog for an open modal. The existing spec line 929 (`markedSiblings() > 0`) also asserts the marks.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/actions-panel/global-aria-hidden.spec.ts`](defect-repros/actions-panel/global-aria-hidden.spec.ts)
  Fix: Keep the global panel from aria-hiding the app: give it a Dialog subclass whose \_hideNonDialogContentFromAssistiveTechnology does nothing, or wrap the container as the scoped path does.
- **[code-block] openLink() calls window.open(url, '\_blank') without noopener, so the opened page gets window.opener; a URL the sanitizer rejects is still opened as unsafe:…** — reproduced.
  code-block.ts:635-645: `const safeURL = this.domSanitizer.sanitize(SecurityContext.URL, file.link); if (safeURL) this.window.open(safeURL.toString(), '\_blank');`. The implicit noopener of target=\_blank applies to \<a>/\<area>/\<form> only, not to window.open, so the new page keeps `window.opener` and can navigate the opener (reverse tabnabbing, CWE-1022). The repro records window.open called with exactly \['https://example.com/', '\_blank'\], with no windowFeatures. Angular's \_sanitizeUrl returns 'unsafe:' + url for a rejected URL, which is truthy. The repro shows a `javascript:alert(1)` link opened as 'unsafe:javascript:alert(1)' after the dev-mode sanitizer warning. That half is harmless, since no script runs; it only opens a dead tab. Rated P1 because of the rubric's security floor; the realistic impact is opener navigation by an untrusted link target, not script execution.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/code-block/open-link-no-noopener.spec.ts`](defect-repros/code-block/open-link-no-noopener.spec.ts)
  Fix: Call window.open(url, '\_blank', 'noopener,noreferrer'), and skip opening when the sanitized value starts with 'unsafe:'.
- **[datepicker] 5. Typing a lone first part after selecting the whole text (January month; year-first month shifted back)** — reproduced.
  getDateFromString for one part (datepicker-input.directive.ts:1133-1142) keeps the month from getDefaultValue, which is the 0-based adapter.getMonth (:1186-1197). createDateTime then passes value.month - 1 (:1649-1651). A day-first format forces `date.month = 1`, which gives January. Repro in yyyy-MM-dd: 2020-06-15 with a typed 2021 becomes 2021-05-15. The consequences go beyond the description. (a) For a January value or today, the month is -1, and luxon createDate throws `Invalid month index "-1"` (node\_modules/@koobiq/luxon-date-adapter/index.esm.js:432-434). The repro shows onInput throwing on an empty field on 15 Jan, so in January an empty field cannot be filled by typing. (b) When the previous month is shorter than today's day (29-31 Mar, 31 May, Jul, Oct and Dec), the date is invalid and nothing is set. The repro on 31 Mar leaves the model null and the text '2021'. The caret does not advance, the separator is rejected (defect 6) and Right does nothing without a value (defect 9), so the user is stuck. yyyy-MM-dd is the en-US dateInput (index.esm.js:20). KBQ\_DATE\_LOCALE defaults to LOCALE\_ID (core/datetime/date-adapter.ts:12-14), which is 'en-US' in a plain Angular app, so this hits the default LuxonDateModule set-up. zh-CN and fa-IR (yyyy/MM/dd) are affected too. Day-first case reproduced: 15.06.2020 with a typed '20' becomes 20.01.2020.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/datepicker/lone-first-part.spec.ts`](defect-repros/datepicker/lone-first-part.spec.ts)
  Fix: Make getDefaultValue return a 1-based month (adapter.getMonth + 1) and drop the `date.month = 1` override (clamp the day to the month instead).
- **[dropdown] A partial KBQ\_DROPDOWN\_DEFAULT\_OPTIONS (e.g. { safeArea: false }) leaves xPosition undefined and opening throws a TypeError** — reproduced.
  dropdown.component.ts:165 initialises xPosition = model(this.defaultOptions.xPosition), so a partial object gives undefined. The validation effect at dropdown.component.ts:360-378 snapshots that default and skips it (posX !== defaultPosX), and its comment says partial options are expected. On open, dropdown-trigger.directive.ts:768-769 destructures positionMap.xPositions\[undefined\], which throws 'undefined is not iterable'. The nested path at 784-786 does the same. The docs at dropdown.en.mdx:55-56 send consumers to KBQ\_DROPDOWN\_DEFAULT\_OPTIONS to set safeArea globally, and useValue is untyped, so { safeArea: false } compiles. After that, every dropdown in the app throws on open. The repro shows xPosition() is undefined, open() throws a TypeError and the trigger stays closed. A control test with { ...KBQ\_DROPDOWN\_DEFAULT\_OPTIONS\_FACTORY(), safeArea: false } opens normally.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dropdown/partial-default-options.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dropdown/partial-default-options.spec.ts)
  Fix: Merge the injected options over KBQ\_DROPDOWN\_DEFAULT\_OPTIONS\_FACTORY() (or fall back per field) before using them as input defaults.
- **[dynamic-translation] The helper sets attributes with Renderer2.setAttribute and accepts any tag, bypassing Angular sanitization** — reproduced.
  dynamic-translation.ts:182 calls `renderer.createElement(tag)` with any tag. :184 appends the slot content (from the translation context) as text, and :186 calls `renderer.setAttribute(key, value)`. No sanitizer is involved, whereas Angular's own `\[attr.href\]` binding prefixes unsafe URLs with 'unsafe:'. Repro: the slot {tag:'a', attributes:\[{href:'javascript:void(0)'}\]} produces `\<a href="javascript:void(0)">`. The slot {tag:'script'} with the marker '\[\[code:globalThis.kbqTriageInjected = true\]\]' inserts a connected `\<script>` whose text comes from the translation. Exploitation needs a consumer to pass untrusted data into attributes/tag, or to use a script or event-handler attribute. The API is @docs-private but exported and shown in the examples tab. The spec passed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dynamic-translation/helper-unsanitized.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dynamic-translation/helper-unsanitized.spec.ts)
  Fix: Sanitize URL attributes via Sanitizer (SecurityContext.URL), reject script/style/iframe tags and on\* attributes, or restrict tag/attributes to an allow-list.
- **[input] Paste parsing ignores the field's locale configuration and, without KBQ\_LOCALE\_SERVICE, tries en-US first** — reproduced.
  onPaste (input-number.ts:485-488) calls checkAndNormalizeLocalizedNumber(text, localeService?.localeId()). That function (core/locales/locale-service.ts:343-378) reads only KBQ\_DEFAULT\_LOCALE\_DATA\_FACTORY(), the shipped data, never this.config, so the provider, \[localeOverrides\] and app-registered locales are ignored. With no locale id it walks `items`, en-US first (:34-35, :370-376), and en-US normalizeNumber turns '1,5' into 15. KBQ\_LOCALE\_SERVICE has no default provider (:95). Repro 1, no locale service: typing '1,5' gives 1.5 and renders '1,5', while pasting '1,5' renders '15' with model 15. Repro 2, en-US with kbqInputLocaleConfigurationProvider({fractionSeparator: ',', groupSeparator: \['.'\]}): typed '1,5' gives 1.5, pasted '1,5' gives 15.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/input/paste-ignores-locale-config.spec.ts`](defect-repros/input/paste-ignores-locale-config.spec.ts)
  Fix: Parse the clipboard text with the directive's resolved this.config (normalizeNumber/numberByParts) first, and fall back to other locales only when that fails.
- **[list] Under virtual scrolling the value reported after a user change holds only the selected options currently rendered; an out-of-view selection drops out of the form value and is cleared when its option comes back** — reproduced.
  reportValueChange (list-selection.component.ts:1237-1246) reports getSelectedOptionValues(), which reads only this.options (1100-1102), the content query of rendered options. That value replaces \_value. When an option renders again, options.changes (877-881) runs setOptionsFromValues with the shortened \_value (1340-1347), so the option stays unselected. This is the shape of the documented list-virtual-scroll example: multiple, ngModel, cdk-virtual-scroll-viewport. Repro test 1: the form value holds \['Item 15'\] off screen, clicking 'Item 1' reports \['Item 1'\]. Repro test 2: select 'Item 1', render 12..18, select 'Item 15': the value is \['Item 15'\]. After rendering back to 0..6, 'Item 1' is unselected. Both tests passed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/list/virtual-scroll-value.spec.ts`](defect-repros/list/virtual-scroll-value.spec.ts)
  Fix: Build the reported value from the previous value plus the delta of the rendered options (keep model values whose option is not rendered), or from the SelectionModel / \_value rather than from the content query.
- **[markdown] Raw HTML in the source reaches the DOM unsanitized, so event-handler attributes (e.g. img onerror) run** — reproduced.
  markdown.component.ts:97-99 passes the result of `parseToHtml` through `sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml` into `\[innerHtml\]` (line 38). markdown.service.ts:20-22 calls `marked.parse` (marked 17, which no longer sanitizes), and `transform` only adds classes. In the repro, `markdownText` set to `Hello \<img src="x" onerror="this.setAttribute('data-pwned','yes')"> world` leaves the `onerror` attribute on the rendered img. Dispatching `error` runs the handler and sets `data-pwned="yes"`. When `markdownText` is bound to user input, this is a stored or reflected XSS path. Only the JSDoc of the `@docs-private` service mentions it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/markdown/unsanitized-event-handler.spec.ts`](defect-repros/markdown/unsanitized-event-handler.spec.ts)
  Fix: Sanitize the HTML before trusting it, either with `DomSanitizer.sanitize(SecurityContext.HTML, ...)` or a DOMPurify-style pass that keeps the alias classes. If raw HTML has to stay possible, put it behind an explicit opt-in and document the risk.
- **[tabs] In a nav bar without tabNavPanel, Enter on a link does nothing** — reproduced.
  tab-nav-bar.html:19 binds (keydown)="handleKeydown($event)" on the strip container, so a keydown on any link bubbles into it. In paginated-tab-header.ts:575-576, ENTER only emits selectFocusedIndex, which the nav bar never listens to (its itemSelected at tab-nav-bar.ts:187 is a no-op). Lines 579-580 then call preventDefault for ENTER whatever the mode. Only KbqTabLink.handleKeydown clicks the link (tab-nav-bar.ts:419-433), and only in panel mode; its comment "Don't prevent enter since it can break link navigation" is cancelled out by the strip. Browsers follow a focused link as the default action of Enter on keydown, so a cancelled keydown means no navigation. The repro dispatches Enter on a focused \<a href kbqTabLink> without a panel: defaultPrevented is true and no click reaches the link. The control test with a panel shows exactly one click. The docs app uses panel mode (component-viewer.template.html:10), so it is not affected. In tabs-actionbar-example.ts:21 the links have no href, so Enter would not activate them even without the preventDefault.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tabs/nav-enter-without-panel.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tabs/nav-enter-without-panel.spec.ts)
  Fix: Stop preventing the default of Enter in handleKeydown, or limit it to the tab group header (KbqTabHeader.itemSelected already prevents it there).
- **[toast] DS-5407 again: container resize re-broadcast through the global ScrollDispatcher closes unrelated close-strategy overlays (tooltips)** — reproduced.
  toast-container.component.ts:281-292 merges SharedResizeObserver.observe(host) into elementScrolled(). CdkScrollable registers the container with ScrollDispatcher, which re-broadcasts every emission through scrolled(). The CDK CloseScrollStrategy (node\_modules/@angular/cdk/fesm2022/\_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:115-129) detaches on any scrollable its overlay does not contain. The kbq tooltip defaults to this strategy (tooltip.component.ts:249-251), and so does the tabs overflow (tab.component.ts:38). In a browser the ResizeObserver fires whenever the container box changes: a toast is added, or a leaving toast collapses its height (kbq-toast-leave animates height, toast.component.scss). The repro stubs SharedResizeObserver the way the existing 'stack reflow' suite does. One reflow notification detached a foreign close-strategy overlay and removed an open .kbq-tooltip, which had survived without it. The three 'global scroll notifications' tests (toast.spec.ts:1333-1379) pass only because the jsdom ResizeObserver never fires. Introduced by 4bd143b70, the DS-5407 fix itself.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/toast/reflow-closes-overlays.spec.ts`](defect-repros/toast/reflow-closes-overlays.spec.ts)
  Fix: Stop merging the resize stream into the container's elementScrolled(); reposition only the overlays anchored inside the stack, for example by filtering on origin containment or calling their updatePosition().
- **[tree-select] In an open single panel any non-navigation key (typeahead letter, Shift alone, a letter typed in the search field) selects the active option; typeahead then moves highlight without selecting; Enter/Home/End/PageUp/PageDown leave value behind highlight** — reproduced.
  panelKeydownHandler's catch-all branch (tree-select.component.ts:1682-1705) calls tree.setSelectedOptionsByKey(activeItem, ...) whenever autoSelect is on, whether or not the key moved anything. In single mode that branch clears and toggles (tree-selection.component.ts:1094-1098). KbqSelectSearch only stops SPACE/HOME/END (core/select/common.ts:151-153), so letters typed in the search bubble to the panel handler. Repro (default autoSelect, nothing selected): Shift alone sets the value to 'Apple' and emits selectionChange. Typing 'b' selects 'Apple' at once, and 200 ms later typeahead highlights 'Banana' while the value stays 'Apple'. Typing 'b' in the search field of a single tree-select sets the value to 'Apple'. End highlights 'Cherry' without selecting, and Enter then closes the panel with the value unchanged (ENTER branch 1671-1679, HOME/END/PAGE branches 1655-1670). The filter-bar single pipe avoids this only because it sets \[autoSelect\]="false".
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree-select/nonnav-key-selects.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree-select/nonnav-key-selects.spec.ts)
  Fix: Call setSelectedOptionsByKey only when the active item actually changed by a navigation key (and from the typeahead change), never for letters/modifiers, and make Enter select the active option.

## P2

- **[accordion] collapsible="false" is checked only on click; Enter/Space and toggle()/close()/closeAll() ignore it** — reproduced.
  accordion-trigger.directive.ts:57 is the only place that reads `accordion.collapsible()`, and it sits inside onClick. The keyboard path is onKeydown (accordion-trigger.directive.ts:81-89), then KbqAccordion.keydownHandler (accordion.ts:315-321). It checks only `!activeItem.disabled`, calls preventDefault (which also suppresses the native click a real button would fire) and then `activeItem.toggle()`. KbqAccordionItem.toggle()/close() (accordion-item.ts:245-260) and closeAll() (accordion.ts:340 into accordion-item.ts:306-313) also check only `disabled`. Repro: with \[collapsible\]="false", a second click leaves the item open (the control), while Enter or Space on the same trigger collapses it (aria-expanded goes to false), and so do toggle(), close() and closeAll(). The keyboard path is the user-facing bug because mouse and keyboard users get different behaviour. The methods ignoring the input is arguable, since the JSDoc says "by the user".
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/accordion/collapsible-keyboard.spec.ts`](defect-repros/accordion/collapsible-keyboard.spec.ts)
  Fix: Move the collapsible guard into a shared user-toggle path, e.g. check `collapsible()` in keydownHandler (or in a trigger method that both click and keydown call) before toggling an expanded item.
- **[accordion] In single mode, switching the expanded item emits valueChange twice, the first with the old value** — reproduced.
  accordion.ts:251-257 re-emits valueChange on every item's expandedChange, using getCurrentValue() (accordion.ts:425-429), which in single mode is `expandedValues()\[0\]` in document order. When B opens, reportExpanded (accordion-item.ts:284-304) emits expandedChange(true) at :290 before notifying the dispatcher at :298, so A is still expanded. The first emission is therefore expandedValues()=\[a,b\] and gives 'a'. A then collapses through the dispatcher listener (accordion-item.ts:218-220 to reportExpanded) and emits a second time, 'b'. Repro: going from a to b emits \['a','b'\], and going from b to a emits \['a','a'\]. The existing test at accordion.spec.ts:517 uses a single item, so it cannot catch this.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/accordion/value-change-twice.spec.ts`](defect-repros/accordion/value-change-twice.spec.ts)
  Fix: Emit valueChange once per user action after the dispatcher has settled, e.g. skip the emission from an item that opens in single mode and let the sibling's collapse report it, or dedupe and compare against the last emitted value.
- **[accordion] orientation and Directionality are read once when the content initializes** — reproduced.
  accordion.ts:275-281 configure the FocusKeyManager orientation only in ngAfterContentInit. No effect tracks `orientation()` and nothing subscribes to `dir.change`. The CDK stores the direction string once (list-key-manager withHorizontalOrientation). The `data-orientation` host binding (accordion.ts:99) does follow the input. Repro 1: an accordion created vertical and switched to horizontal shows data-orientation="horizontal", but Right on the first trigger does not move focus. Repro 2: a horizontal accordion whose Directionality switches from ltr to rtl at runtime still moves Right to the next header (c), not the previous one (a). The existing test at accordion.spec.ts:710 shows rtl is mirrored when it is set from the start. The impact is small because the shipped styles have no horizontal layout, so I rated it P2 at the low end, an edge case.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/accordion/orientation-read-once.spec.ts`](defect-repros/accordion/orientation-read-once.spec.ts)
  Fix: Reconfigure the key manager in an effect on `orientation()` and on `dir.change` (calling withHorizontalOrientation(null) or withVerticalOrientation as appropriate).
- **[accordion] Non-button trigger hosts (\<kbq-accordion-trigger>, \<div kbq-accordion-trigger>) get role="button" but no tabindex** — reproduced.
  The KbqAccordionTrigger selector allows the element form (accordion-trigger.ts:19). The host directive binds role="button" (accordion-trigger.directive.ts:10) but never a tabindex. focus() (accordion-trigger.directive.ts:92-94) calls nativeElement.focus(), which does nothing on a non-focusable element, so Tab, arrow-key navigation and Enter/Space (which need focus, :82) all fail. Repro: both hosts have role=button, no tabindex attribute and tabIndex -1. element.focus() and accordion.setActiveItem() both leave document.activeElement elsewhere. Layout part, which jsdom cannot show: `flex: 1; min-width: 0` is scoped to `button.kbq-accordion-trigger` (accordion-trigger.scss:57-63), so the element form does not fill the header row. No example or e2e scenario in the repo uses the element form.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/accordion/element-trigger-unfocusable.spec.ts`](defect-repros/accordion/element-trigger-unfocusable.spec.ts)
  Fix: Bind `\[attr.tabindex\]` to 0 when the host is not a native button (or drop the element selector), and move the flex rule onto `.kbq-accordion-trigger` itself.
- **[actions-panel] A partial kbqActionsPanelDefaultConfigProvider value drops the class defaults (block scroll strategy, data undefined)** — reproduced.
  The provider stores the value as is (actions-panel-config.ts:16-19), and open() spreads it in place of `new KbqActionsPanelConfig()` (actions-panel.ts:133-136). A partial value therefore has no scrollStrategy, so actions-panel.ts:189 passes `scrollStrategy: undefined`. CDK's \_getOverlayConfig then falls back to `this.\_scrollStrategy()` (dialog.mjs:516), which is DIALOG\_SCROLL\_STRATEGY, the block strategy (dialog.mjs:419). KBQ\_ACTIONS\_PANEL\_DATA is provided as `config.data`, which is undefined instead of the class default null (actions-panel-config.ts:39, actions-panel.ts:210). The repro uses the same `{ disableClose: true }` shape as the existing test at spec line 744. With that value the overlay's scroll strategy is a BlockScrollStrategy (RepositionScrollStrategy without the provider), `cdk-global-scrollblock` is added to \<html> on a page taller than the viewport, and the injected data is undefined (null without the provider). closeOnNavigation is unaffected because CDK's own default is true. direction falls back to the application's direction.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/actions-panel/partial-default-config.spec.ts`](defect-repros/actions-panel/partial-default-config.spec.ts)
  Fix: Merge the provided value over `new KbqActionsPanelConfig()` in the token or in open(), instead of replacing the defaults whole.
- **[actions-panel] Closing that bypasses KbqActionsPanelRef.close() skips the exit animation and never emits beforeClosed** — reproduced.
  beforeClosed is emitted only from KbqActionsPanelRef.close() (actions-panel-ref.ts:78-92). History navigation (disposeOnNavigation from closeOnNavigation), Dialog.closeAll() (dialog.mjs:496-498 -> DialogRef.close, which disposes the overlay) and an external overlayRef.dispose() all reach `dialogRef.close(this.result)` directly through handleOverlayDetachments (actions-panel-ref.ts:113-120) or CDK. None of them touches containerInstance or the hidden state. The repro covers all three paths: afterClosed is emitted, beforeClosed is not, and the container leaves the DOM still carrying `kbq-actions-panel-container\_visible`, so there is no exit transition. A later `ref.close('late')` still finds containerInstance set and emits beforeClosed after afterClosed (the emitted order is \['afterClosed:undefined', 'beforeClosed:late'\]).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/actions-panel/close-bypasses-ref.spec.ts`](defect-repros/actions-panel/close-bypasses-ref.spec.ts)
  Fix: In handleOverlayDetachments, emit and complete beforeClosed and clear containerInstance when it is still set, so that a later close() is a no-op.
- **[actions-panel] The close button and Escape close the KbqActionsPanel resolved through the injector option, not their own panel** — reproduced.
  The container injects KbqActionsPanel (actions-panel-container.ts:139), and both the close button and handleEscape call `this.actionsPanel.close()` (actions-panel-container.ts:161-163, 195-201). The container's injector is parented on `config.injector || this.injector` (actions-panel.ts:201-204, then dialog.mjs \_attachContainer). When `injector` points outside the opening instance's scope, KbqActionsPanel resolves to another instance, for example the root one (providedIn root, actions-panel.ts:90). In the repro, a host provides its own KbqActionsPanel and opens with `injector: TestBed.inject(Injector)`. Clicking the close button or pressing Escape leaves the panel open. When the root instance has a panel of its own, the first panel's close button closes that other panel instead.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/actions-panel/injector-close-target.spec.ts`](defect-repros/actions-panel/injector-close-target.spec.ts)
  Fix: Have the container close through its own KbqActionsPanelRef or DialogRef (provided per panel) instead of the injected service.
- **[actions-panel] direction reaches only the overlay dir, not the content's Directionality; the 'ltr' default ignores an RTL application** — reproduced.
  CDK \_createInjector adds a per-dialog Directionality only when the user injector cannot resolve one (dialog.mjs:601-608). The Kbq service always passes an injector (actions-panel.ts:201-204) that reaches the root Directionality (providedIn root), so the provider is never added. The direction therefore reaches only the overlay host's dir attribute (createOverlayRef in \_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:2083, then \_updateElementDirection) and the RTL class (actions-panel-container.ts:90). The default `direction = 'ltr'` (actions-panel-config.ts:93) is passed explicitly, so createOverlayRef never falls back to `directionality.value`. In the repro, with `direction: 'rtl'` the overlay host has dir=rtl while the content's injected Directionality.value is 'ltr'. With \<html dir="rtl"> and the default config, the app and the content read 'rtl' while the overlay host has dir=ltr.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/actions-panel/direction-not-provided.spec.ts`](defect-repros/actions-panel/direction-not-provided.spec.ts)
  Fix: Provide a Directionality for config.direction in the content injector the panel builds, and default direction to the application's Directionality instead of 'ltr'.
- **[actions-panel] Closing from the close button or with Escape leaves focus on body** — reproduced.
  The panel passes `restoreFocus: false` (actions-panel.ts:180). It also overrides \_contentAttached with only applyContainerClass (actions-panel-container.ts:206-208), so CDK's \_initializeFocusTrap (dialog.mjs:239-246), which records \_elementFocusedBeforeDialogWasOpened, never runs, and \_restoreFocus (dialog.mjs:205-230) has no target. The focused close button or content control is removed with the overlay, so the browser's focus fixup moves focus to body. jsdom does the same. In the repro, focus starts on a trigger in the app and the panel does not take it. After focusing the close button and clicking it, or focusing a control in the panel and pressing Escape, document.activeElement is body instead of the trigger.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/actions-panel/focus-lost-on-close.spec.ts`](defect-repros/actions-panel/focus-lost-on-close.spec.ts)
  Fix: Record the focused element before the panel content is reached, and restore focus to it on close when focus was inside the panel.
- **[alert] A disabled `kbq-icon-button` close control gets `pointer-events: none`, so the click lands on the wrapper and still emits `closed`** — confirmed in the source.
  icon/icon-button.component.ts host binds `\[class.kbq-disabled\]: 'disabled'`, and icon/icon-button.scss:16-18 sets `.kbq-icon-button.kbq-disabled { pointer-events: none; }`. Hit-testing therefore skips the disabled control and picks its parent, the `div.kbq-alert\_\_close-button` wrapper, whose box contains the control. The click event targets the wrapper, and the listener at alert.component.html:22 calls `closed.emit()` with no check of the control's disabled state (alert.component.ts has no such guard). A consumer who disables the close control still gets the alert dismissed by a mouse click. Keyboard is unaffected, because a disabled icon-button has no tabindex and native `disabled`. This rests on CSS hit-testing, which jsdom does not implement.
  Fix: Listen for activation on the control itself (skipping a disabled one) instead of on the wrapper, or ignore wrapper clicks whose target is not inside an enabled control.
- **[app-switcher] A pick from the current site's list or from the search results never writes selectedApp or selectedSite** — reproduced.
  In app-switcher.html, the current-list rows (45-53), the expanded alias rows (64-72) and the search-result rows (165-171) have no (click) binding. Only the flyout rows (128-133, 142-147) call selectAppInSite, which is the only code that writes the models (app-switcher.ts:602-607). KbqAppSwitcherListItem.clickHandler (app-switcher-list-item.ts:93-100) only toggles group headers. This has been the behaviour since the component was added (68c529fa2). Repro, single site, 8 apps without links: clicking 'App 3', or the 'App 5' search result, emits no selectedAppChange. The host's \[(selectedApp)\] stays 'a0', the row gets no kbq-selected and the popup stays open. So in the overview docs example, which has no links and shows 'App: …', a click does nothing.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/app-switcher/current-pick-no-selection.spec.ts`](defect-repros/app-switcher/current-pick-no-selection.spec.ts)
  Fix: Bind a click on the current-list and search-result rows that writes selectedApp (and selectedSite for a multi-site search result) and closes the popup, like selectAppInSite.
- **[app-switcher] offset has no effect because the placement classes (classMap) are never bound; the size modifier class is lost too** — reproduced.
  The host metadata (app-switcher.ts:280-285) sets only the static 'kbq-app-switcher' class, and neither app-switcher.html nor the host binds classMap. classMap is computed by KbqPopUp.updateClassMap (pop-up.ts:178-193), which is called from the trigger (pop-up-trigger.ts:430, app-switcher.ts:430-432, 946-950). applyPopupMargins (app-switcher.ts:399-405, pop-up-styles.ts:3-28) chooses the margin side from the host's kbq-app-switcher\_placement-\* class, so it never writes anything. Repro with \[offset\]="20": popup.offset is 20, and classMap contains a placement key and kbq-app-switcher\_medium, but the host has neither class and margin-top/margin-bottom stay ''. Control: adding the computed placement class to the host makes the same step write 20px. The default looks right only because app-switcher.scss:10-11 hard-codes 4px margins, which equal the default offset.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/app-switcher/offset-no-effect.spec.ts`](defect-repros/app-switcher/offset-no-effect.spec.ts)
  Fix: Bind classMap on the popup host (for example host '\[class\]': 'classMap' next to the static class) so the placement and size classes, and with them the offset margin, apply.
- **[app-switcher] Moving focus to another focusable element outside the popup (Tab from the search field, or a click) closes it and moves focus to the trigger, taking it from the chosen element** — reproduced.
  focusoutHandler (app-switcher.ts:587-598) calls hide(0) and trigger.focus() for any relatedTarget outside the popup, including a real focusable element the user chose. Also, the trigger's visibleChange subscription (app-switcher.ts:919-923) calls this.focus() on every close: outside click, outer scroll, hideIfNotInViewPort. The existing spec treats this as intended ('returns focus to the trigger when focus leaves', app-switcher.spec.ts:1056-1069). Repro: with focus on a menu row, focusing an \<input> outside the switcher (what a click on it does) leaves the popup closed and focus on the trigger button, not the input. In jsdom the final focus comes from the visibleChange path, because jsdom completes the original focus move after the focusout handler. In Chromium, the focus() inside focusout aborts the move instead. The end state is the same either way.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/app-switcher/focusout-steals-focus.spec.ts`](defect-repros/app-switcher/focusout-steals-focus.spec.ts)
  Fix: Restore focus to the trigger only when focus is lost (relatedTarget null) or on Escape/Tab, and leave it alone when it moved to another focusable element or the close came from an outside pointer event.
- **[app-switcher] Flyout rows have role="menuitem" but the dropdown panel holding them has no role="menu" (aria-required-parent)** — reproduced.
  The flyout rows are KbqAppSwitcherDropdownApp with host role 'menuitem' (app-switcher-dropdown-app.ts:49), rendered by kbqDropdownContent templates (app-switcher.html:115-150). Those templates go into the dropdown panel (dropdown.html:2-16: a .kbq-dropdown\_\_panel div and a .kbq-dropdown\_\_content div, neither with a role) in a separate overlay pane, outside the main role="menu" (app-switcher.html:28). Repro: opening Site B's flyout with Enter gives a .kbq-app-switcher-dropdown-app with role=menuitem and no \[role=menu\] ancestor, while the main list's site row does have one. axe with runOnly \['aria-required-parent'\] on the flyout pane reports a violation. The existing axe tests (app-switcher.spec.ts:2078-2110) never open a flyout.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/app-switcher/flyout-menuitem-no-menu.spec.ts`](defect-repros/app-switcher/flyout-menuitem-no-menu.spec.ts)
  Fix: Give the flyout panel role="menu" (for example a panel role input on kbq-dropdown, or a wrapper with role="menu" inside the kbqDropdownContent templates).
- **[app-switcher] Hovering a row focuses it, taking focus out of the search field so the next keystrokes go to type-ahead** — reproduced.
  KbqDropdownItem.handleMouseEnter (dropdown/dropdown-item.component.ts:246-254, bound at :87) calls focus('mouse') unless parentDropdownPanel.inSearchMode() is true. The app-switcher's kbq-dropdown hosts (app-switcher.html:41, 157) have no KbqDropdownSearch and no activeDescendantNavigation, so inSearchMode (dropdown.component.ts:159) is false. Once a row has focus, keydownHandler no longer treats keys as coming from the input (app-switcher.ts:464-471) and passes them to keyManager.onKeydown with withTypeAhead (365-368, 517). Repro, 8 apps so the search field renders: after typing 'App' the input has focus. A mouseenter on the first search result moves document.activeElement to that row while the popup stays open.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/app-switcher/hover-steals-search-focus.spec.ts`](defect-repros/app-switcher/hover-steals-search-focus.spec.ts)
  Fix: While the search field exists (or has focus), make hover only set the active item and keep DOM focus in the field, for example by putting the panels in search/active-descendant mode.
- **[autocomplete] autoActiveFirstOption: choosing a non-first option with Enter selects the first option after the panel closes** — reproduced.
  autocomplete-trigger.directive.ts:558-560: Enter calls activeOption.selectViaInteraction() and then resetActiveItem(). The selection reaches setValueAndClose synchronously (777-794), and closePanel sets overlayAttached to false (457). resetActiveItem (1006-1013) then calls keyManager.setFirstItemActive(). list-key-manager.ts:218-227 emits change because the index changed, and the trigger's change handler (396-409) takes the `!this.panelOpen && activeItem` branch and calls selectViaInteraction() on the first option (option.ts:403-411, isUserInput true). The repro uses default options and has two tests. In the first, Enter on California leaves both California and Alabama `selected`, and optionSelections emits \[California, true\] then \[Alabama, true\]. In the second (text mode), insertOption deselects the inserted option (1122) but Alabama stays selected. The form value is correct; the visible effect is a wrong option marked selected (and aria-selected) when the panel opens again.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/autocomplete/enter-selects-first.spec.ts`](defect-repros/autocomplete/enter-selects-first.spec.ts)
  Fix: Do not select the active option from the change handler after a choice: reset the active item before selecting, or let the closed-panel branch run only for keyboard navigation of a closed panel.
- **[autocomplete] Arrow keys and hover move DOM focus onto the option and back, so the field fires blur/focusin and is marked touched** — reproduced.
  The keyManager.change handler (autocomplete-trigger.directive.ts:402-406) calls scrollActiveOptionIntoView (640-642). That calls option.focus() (option.ts:348-349, then core/utils/dom.ts:22-34 element.focus), and the handler then refocuses the input (405). The host '(blur)': 'onTouched()' (154) marks the control touched. Hover takes the same path: option.ts:436-441 onMouseenter calls keyManager.setActiveItem(this). In the repro (required control, panel opened by focus, autoActiveFirstOption off), one ArrowDown fires blur once and focusin once, sets control.touched to true and aria-invalid to 'true' (default ErrorStateMatcher: invalid && touched), while document.activeElement is still the input. A mouseenter on an option fires a second blur.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/autocomplete/arrow-blurs-field.spec.ts`](defect-repros/autocomplete/arrow-blurs-field.spec.ts)
  Fix: Reveal the active option by scrolling the panel viewport, not by focusing it: aria-activedescendant already announces the option, and focus can stay in the field.
- **[autocomplete] Panel bound after the trigger's first render: no subscription to active-option changes** — reproduced.
  autocomplete-trigger.directive.ts:392-411: ngAfterViewInit reads this.autocomplete() once and subscribes to keyManager.change only if a panel is bound then. Nothing subscribes again when the input changes later, unlike openedChange/optionSelections, which follow autocompleteChanges (195-226). Repro: with the panel inside an @if that turns true later, ArrowDown moves activeOption to the second option but scrollActiveOptionIntoView is never called. The eager control test calls it once. The handler's markForCheck for aria-activedescendant (400) and updateInlineHint (406) are skipped the same way.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/autocomplete/late-panel.spec.ts`](defect-repros/autocomplete/late-panel.spec.ts)
  Fix: Subscribe to keyManager.change through autocompleteChanges with switchMap (as optionSelections does) instead of reading the input once in ngAfterViewInit.
- **[autocomplete] opened/openedChange(true) fire again with no closed in between when options run out and come back** — reproduced.
  In subscribeToClosingActions (autocomplete-trigger.directive.ts:703-726), when the options drop to zero, setVisibility hides the panel (panelOpen becomes false) and no closed is emitted: only the `if (this.panelOpen)` branch emits, and it only emits opened. When the options return, wasOpen is false, so opened is emitted again (723-725). Repro: opened is called 2 times, closed 0 times, and openedChange gives \[true, true\], while panelOpen goes false and back to true. A consumer that tracks (opened)/(closed) stays in the 'open' state while the panel is hidden, which happens whenever a typed query matches nothing.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/autocomplete/opened-twice.spec.ts`](defect-repros/autocomplete/opened-twice.spec.ts)
  Fix: Emit closed when the options run out on an attached panel (wasOpen && !panelOpen), mirroring the opened branch, as Angular Material does.
- **[autocomplete] Any selection event of an option while the panel is open counts as a choice (programmatic select/deselect close the panel, write the value and emit optionSelected)** — reproduced.
  optionSelections (autocomplete-trigger.directive.ts:215-226) merges every option's onSelectionChange. panelClosingActions (483-494) passes each KbqOptionSelectionChange through, and setValueAndClose (777-791) uses event.source without checking isUserInput or source.selected. option.ts select()/deselect() (325-345) emit by default. Repro: with the panel open, options\[1\].select() closes the panel, writes 'California' to the input and the control, and emits optionSelected. After choosing Alabama and setting the control to 'Flo', a programmatic options\[0\].deselect() leaves the option deselected but writes 'Alabama' back over 'Flo' and emits optionSelected for the deselected option.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/autocomplete/programmatic-selection.spec.ts`](defect-repros/autocomplete/programmatic-selection.spec.ts)
  Fix: Treat only user selections of a selected option as a choice: filter closing actions to `isUserInput && source.selected`.
- **[badge] Icon position counts whitespace-only text nodes (preserveWhitespaces: true breaks icon spacing)** — reproduced.
  badge.component.ts:77 uses `getNodesWithoutComments`, which drops only comments (core/utils/utils.ts:31-33). Button uses `getContentNodes` (utils.ts:39-43, button.component.ts:216), which also drops whitespace-only text nodes. In a host with `preserveWhitespaces: true`, the child nodes are \[ws, icon, text\], so the leading icon is at index 1. Neither the `iconIndex === 0` branch (:89) nor the last-index branch (:94) runs, so the icon gets neither class. The same happens to a trailing icon followed by a whitespace node. The repro passes: the control with default whitespace gets `kbq-icon\_left` / `kbq-badge-icon\_left`, and the same markup with preserveWhitespaces gets neither the left nor the right classes.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/badge/whitespace-icon-position.spec.ts`](defect-repros/badge/whitespace-icon-position.spec.ts)
  Fix: Use `getContentNodes(this.nativeElement)` instead of `getNodesWithoutComments(childNodes)`, as button and button-toggle do.
- **[badge] Icon classes recomputed only when the set of projected icons changes (text/caption toggled later leaves stale classes)** — reproduced.
  badge.component.ts:70: the effect's only reactive dependency is `this.icons()` (:78). `this.nativeElement.childNodes` (:77) is not a signal. A signal content query hands back the same array while its results do not change, so adding or removing text through `@if` does not rerun the effect. The repro passes in both directions. With `\<i kbq-icon>` plus `@if (showText) {\<span>}`, text added after the first render leaves the icon without `kbq-icon\_left` and the host without `kbq-badge-icon\_left`. A control rendered with the text from the start does get them. Text removed later leaves both classes on what is now an icon-only badge, which keeps the stale margin from badge.component.scss:30.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/badge/text-toggle-stale-icon-class.spec.ts`](defect-repros/badge/text-toggle-stale-icon-class.spec.ts)
  Fix: Also recompute on content changes, e.g. through a MutationObserver (ContentObserver/cdkObserveContent) on the host, or a signal bumped from ngAfterContentChecked.
- **[breadcrumbs] kbq-button and the roving focus both bind tabindex; an entry re-enabled after being disabled stays at tabindex="0" (extra Tab stop)** — reproduced.
  Two independent host bindings write the same attribute on every entry: KbqButton `'\[attr.tabindex\]': 'tabIndexAttribute'` (packages/components/button/button.component.ts:319, getter at 432-436: -1 while disabled, else \_tabIndex 0 on an anchor) and RdxRovingFocusItemDirective `'\[attr.tabindex\]': 'tabIndex'` (roving-focus-item.directive.ts:23, getter at 84: 0 only for the current tab stop). The roving item is a host directive of KbqBreadcrumbButton (breadcrumbs.ts:88-90), so it runs after the KbqButton component and wins on the first pass. Angular keeps a separate binding slot for each, so each writes only when its own value changes. When `disabled` goes from true to false (trail: breadcrumbs.html:99, item: same line), KbqButton changes from -1 to 0 and writes "0". The roving value stays -1, because unregisterItem (roving-focus-group.directive.ts:174-176) cleared currentTabStopId, and an unchanged binding never writes back. The repro passes. A trail created disabled and then enabled leaves Home and Library at tabindex="0", while RdxRovingFocusItemDirective.tabIndex is -1 for both. The same happens when one item's `disabled` goes from true to false. A trail enabled from the start keeps them at -1 (control test). The expand `\<button>` (breadcrumbs.html:38-39) should hit the same path, with KbqButton writing null instead of 0, so the attribute is removed. This follows from the source; the repro does not assert it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/breadcrumbs/stale-tabindex-after-enable.spec.ts`](defect-repros/breadcrumbs/stale-tabindex-after-enable.spec.ts)
  Fix: Keep a single owner for tabindex on breadcrumb entries: have KbqBreadcrumbButton drive KbqButton.tabIndex from the roving value, or have the roving item re-assert its value whenever the button's disabled state changes.
- **[breadcrumbs] Disabled default entry keeps the href from its routerLink; middle click / open in new tab still follows it (incl. the always-disabled last entry)** — reproduced.
  breadcrumbs.html:97-99 binds `\[routerLink\]="item.routerLink?.urlTree"` together with `\[disabled\]="disabled() || item.disabled() || last"`. RouterLink on an anchor always writes `href`. KbqButton only halts `click` and Enter/Space/arrow `keydown` in the capture phase (button.component.ts:479, 590-603). No disabled style sets pointer-events:none (\_button-base.scss:41-43 sets only cursor:default). The repro passes. An entry with `disabled` keeps `aria-disabled="true"` and `href="/home"`. A primary click is prevented and Router.navigateByUrl is not called. An `auxclick` with button 1 is not prevented, and its browser default is opening the href in a new tab. Every entry of a `\[disabled\]` trail keeps its href. On the last entry this only links to the current page, so the real impact is on entries the consumer disabled.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/breadcrumbs/disabled-entry-keeps-href.spec.ts`](defect-repros/breadcrumbs/disabled-entry-keeps-href.spec.ts)
  Fix: Bind the entry's routerLink to null (or drop href) while the entry is disabled, as the dropdown row already does for its own disabled state.
- **[button] Disabled \<a href kbq-button> keeps its href; middle click / context-menu 'Open link in new tab' still open the link** — confirmed in the source.
  packages/components/button/button.component.ts:479-480 registers capture listeners only for 'click' and 'keydown'; haltDisabledEvents (590-596) and the key filter (598-605: ENTER, SPACE, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) are the only guards. Nothing touches href: the host bindings (315-323) write only disabled, aria-disabled, kbq-disabled, tabindex and class. \_button-base.scss:41-43 gives .kbq-disabled only 'cursor: default', with no pointer-events: none. KbqLink, by contrast, keeps the mouse away with pointer-events (link/link.ts:336-338, core/styles/common/\_link.scss:83). In Chromium and Firefox a middle click fires 'auxclick', not 'click', and its default action opens the href in a new tab. The context-menu 'Open link in new tab' and dragging the link use the href directly. None of these events is halted. jsdom cannot navigate, so there is no repro; the browser behaviour is standard but was not checked in a real browser here.
  Fix: While disabled, also halt 'auxclick' (and 'contextmenu'/'dragstart'), or give disabled anchors pointer-events: none like KbqLink, or drop href while disabled.
- **[button] On \<a kbq-button>, a consumer-set role is replaced by 'button' without href and removed with href, on every check** — reproduced.
  button.component.ts:543-557 updateRole() computes the role only from href (null with href, 'button' without) and writes or removes it whenever the attribute differs. It never considers a role the consumer authored. It runs from ngAfterViewInit (518) and from every ngAfterViewChecked (526), so a re-applied role is overwritten again. Repro: \<a kbq-button role="tab"> ends with role="button", and \<a kbq-button href role="menuitem"> ends with no role. The spec passes. No anchor in the repo currently combines kbq-button with a role (grep), so this hits consumers only.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/button/anchor-role-override.spec.ts`](defect-repros/button/anchor-role-override.spec.ts)
  Fix: Record whether the consumer authored a role at init and leave it alone; only add or remove the role='button' the component wrote itself.
- **[button] A button whose own kbqStyle turns falsy inside a non-filled group becomes filled instead of taking the group style** — reproduced.
  The kbqStyle setter (button.component.ts:367-371) clears kbqStyleSetExplicitly and calls applyKbqStyle(value), which falls back to 'filled' (612). The group re-propagates only from its kbqStyle setter (button-group.ts:37-41) or from the effect at 111-116. That effect tracks only buttons(); \_kbqStyle is a plain field, not a signal. So the released button stays filled until the group style or the set of buttons changes. The existing test (button.component.spec.ts:1384) uses a filled group, where the two coincide. Repro: an outline group with a child \[kbqStyle\] going from transparent to undefined leaves the child at kbq-button\_filled while its sibling is outline. The child follows the group again only after the group style changes. The spec passes.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/button/released-style-ignores-group.spec.ts`](defect-repros/button/released-style-ignores-group.spec.ts)
  Fix: When the own style is released, re-apply the group's current style, e.g. by making the group style a signal the button reads through a narrow injection token.
- **[button-toggle] Changing `multiple` after initialization keeps the selection mode it was created with** — reproduced.
  button-toggle.component.ts:297 creates the SelectionModel once in ngOnInit with `this.multiple()`. Nothing recreates it later. Meanwhile role (226), aria-orientation (235), toggle `type` (601) and `isSingleSelector` (660) follow the live input. The reported value and selection read `selected\[0\]` in single mode (193, 222). Repro, multiple to single: after clicking A and B, then switching, A and B are both checked as radios. Clicking C checks all three (aria-checked true x3). The group value stays 'a' and the group `change` reports 'a' for the click on C. Repro, single to multiple: after the switch, clicking B still deselects A and the value is \['b'\]. KbqSelect throws on a runtime `multiple` change (select.component.ts:1230, getKbqSelectDynamicMultipleError); the toggle group raises no error. The existing test 'should follow the mode when it changes at runtime' only checks roles and `type`.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/button-toggle/multiple-runtime-switch.spec.ts`](defect-repros/button-toggle/multiple-runtime-switch.spec.ts)
  Fix: Recreate the SelectionModel (keeping the compatible part of the selection) when `multiple` changes after init, or throw like KbqSelect.
- **[button-toggle] Changes made in code never reach the form control; removing the selected toggle desyncs the control** — reproduced.
  Only user input calls controlValueAccessorChangeFn: emitChangeEvent (343-349) runs from applyToggleSelection only when isUserInput (389-392), and followToggleValue (415) skips the form on purpose. A destroyed selected toggle calls syncButtonToggle(this, false) on a microtask (793-795). That reaches dropPendingValue (386, 446-451), which clears rawValue, so the group value becomes undefined. When the toggle is rendered again, isPrechecked (426-433) sees rawValue undefined and does not check it. Repro with \[formControl\] = 'red': after removing the 'red' toggle, group.value is undefined while control.value stays 'red'. After rendering it again, the control still holds 'red' but both toggles are unchecked and group.value stays undefined. The existing test 'should update the model if a selected toggle is removed' never looks at the bound model.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/button-toggle/form-desync-on-removal.spec.ts`](defect-repros/button-toggle/form-desync-on-removal.spec.ts)
  Fix: Either keep a value that the form still holds when its toggle is destroyed (as an unmatched assigned value is kept) or report the dropped value to the form; pick one contract and test it against the control.
- **[button-toggle] An out-of-order array through `\[(value)\]` makes the group emit the reordered array during change detection (NG0100)** — reproduced.
  setSelectionByValue (498-503) selects the matched toggles in toggle order. currentValue (203-213) puts the selection first, in that order. sameValue compares arrays by position (66). So assignValue (140) treats the reordered array as a change, and emitValueChange (472-480) emits it whenever it differs from reportedValue. Repro 1: initial values \['two','one'\]. The prechecked toggles' ngOnInit (746-747, then 591, then 396) emits \['one','two'\] during the host's change detection, and the first fixture.detectChanges() throws NG0100. Repro 2: going from \['one'\] to \['two','one'\] after init also throws NG0100. Narrower than described: reordering an already reported selection (\['one','two'\] to \['two','one'\]) is silent, because reportedValue already equals the normalised value (475). In that case the binding just keeps the consumer's order.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/button-toggle/reordered-array-ng0100.spec.ts`](defect-repros/button-toggle/reordered-array-ng0100.spec.ts)
  Fix: Compare assigned and reported arrays as sets (or keep the assigned order in `value`) so a write the group only reorders is not echoed back through `valueChange`.
- **[button-toggle] Arrow, Home and End handler ignores modifier keys** — reproduced.
  onKeydown (852-881) checks only keyCode and never looks at altKey, ctrlKey, shiftKey or metaKey. It calls preventDefault (876), then focusViaKeyboard and onToggleClick on the target (880-881), which changes the value, writes the form and emits `change`. Repro: Alt+ArrowLeft, Ctrl+ArrowRight, Shift+ArrowRight and Meta+ArrowLeft on the first radio are each defaultPrevented and move the selection. As a result, Alt+Left (browser back) is swallowed and changes the form value instead. The CDK key managers ignore modified keys by default.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/button-toggle/arrow-keys-with-modifiers.spec.ts`](defect-repros/button-toggle/arrow-keys-with-modifiers.spec.ts)
  Fix: Return early from onKeydown when any modifier (or at least alt, ctrl, meta) is pressed, as `hasModifierKey` does in the CDK.
- **[button-toggle] Nothing marks the control touched on blur** — reproduced.
  The group's onTouched is called in one place only, toggleByUser (826), and only when a click actually changes the toggle's state. The toggle monitors its host with FocusMonitor (783) but never subscribes to the origin stream, and there is no blur listener. KbqRadioGroup marks touched when focus leaves a radio (radio.component.ts:637-642, radioGroup.touch()). Repro: a required \[formControl\] group. Focus its first inner button, then move focus to another button and dispatch blur and focusout. The control stays invalid and untouched, so an error shown on touched never appears.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/button-toggle/blur-not-touched.spec.ts`](defect-repros/button-toggle/blur-not-touched.spec.ts)
  Fix: Subscribe to the FocusMonitor stream in the toggle and call the group's onTouched when the origin becomes null, as KbqRadio does.
- **[checkbox] A checkbox without label text cannot be given an accessible name (host aria-label / aria-labelledby not forwarded to the native input)** — reproduced.
  checkbox.html:8-23: the native input binds aria-checked, name, value, id and so on, but no aria-label, aria-labelledby or aria-describedby. checkbox.ts declares no aria-label or aria-labelledby input, yet the class JSDoc (checkbox.ts:37-43) promises an \[aria-label\] input. The sibling toggle has both inputs (toggle.component.ts:81-82) and binds them on its input (toggle.component.html:9-10). Repro: with `\<kbq-checkbox aria-label=...>` and `\<kbq-checkbox aria-labelledby=...>`, the attributes stay on the role-less host, neither input gets them, and axe-core 4.12 reports the `label` rule on both inputs. 'Cannot' overstates it a little: projecting visually hidden text, or an external `\<label for="\<id>-input">`, still names the input. The documented aria-label route does not work.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/checkbox/aria-label-not-forwarded.spec.ts`](defect-repros/checkbox/aria-label-not-forwarded.spec.ts)
  Fix: Add `aria-label` and `aria-labelledby` inputs (aliased, as in kbq-toggle) and bind them on the native input with `\[attr.aria-label\]` and `\[attr.aria-labelledby\]`.
- **[checkbox] Checkbox ignores KBQ\_CHECKABLE\_CLICK\_ACTION (toggle reads it first and falls back to the checkbox token)** — reproduced.
  checkbox.ts:99-101 injects only KBQ\_CHECKBOX\_CLICK\_ACTION. toggle.component.ts:165-170 reads KBQ\_CHECKABLE\_CLICK\_ACTION ?? KBQ\_CHECKBOX\_CLICK\_ACTION. The shared token is documented as 'the default click behavior for checkable controls' (checkable.ts:17-26) and as 'How checkboxes and toggles react to clicks' (core.en.mdx:83, core.ru.mdx:83). Repro: with KBQ\_CHECKABLE\_CLICK\_ACTION = 'noop', a click on the toggle's input leaves it unchecked, while a click on the checkbox's input checks it. With KBQ\_CHECKBOX\_CLICK\_ACTION = 'noop', neither changes. That second half is the intended legacy fallback (comment at toggle.component.ts:165-166) and is not a defect.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/checkbox/checkable-token-ignored.spec.ts`](defect-repros/checkbox/checkable-token-ignored.spec.ts)
  Fix: Default the checkbox clickAction input to `inject(KBQ\_CHECKABLE\_CLICK\_ACTION, {optional: true}) ?? inject(KBQ\_CHECKBOX\_CLICK\_ACTION, {optional: true}) ?? undefined`, matching kbq-toggle.
- **[checkbox] Deferred indeterminate clear overwrites a value set by a change handler and re-emits indeterminateChange(false)** — reproduced.
  checkbox.ts:278-283 schedules `Promise.resolve().then(() => { indeterminate.set(false); indeterminateChange.emit(false) })` without comparing the current value. Before that microtask runs, `change` is emitted synchronously (checkbox.ts:293). The guarded path setIndeterminate (checkbox.ts:209-225), which skips the emit for an unchanged value, is bypassed. Repro case 1: a (change) handler sets `indeterminate = true` while it is still true, which is a no-op. The microtask then sets it to false, the host loses `kbq-indeterminate`, and indeterminateChange emits \[false\]. Repro case 2: a handler that sets it to false emits false, and the microtask emits false a second time, giving \[false, false\]. kbq-toggle has the same pattern (toggle.component.ts:298-303).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/checkbox/indeterminate-clear-overwrites.spec.ts`](defect-repros/checkbox/indeterminate-clear-overwrites.spec.ts)
  Fix: Clear indeterminate synchronously through `setIndeterminate(false)` before toggling and emitting `change`, or at least route the deferred clear through `setIndeterminate` so it respects the current value.
- **[clamped-text] The content is measured only when the content box changes size; a change of `rows` (or of the projected text) that keeps the box size is never measured** — reproduced.
  clamped-text.ts:148-161: the only caller of updateToggleVisibilityState() (181-184) is the SharedResizeObserver subscription on the content div. Nothing re-measures when `rows` changes. `rows` only reaches layout through the custom property (54), and that property is consumed only under the collapsed class (clamped-text.scss:7-13). So while the text is unclamped, a change of `rows` changes no size and a real ResizeObserver stays silent. The repro uses a mock observer that fires only on request, as the existing spec does, and passes with two cases. (a) 20-line text, rows 5: expand, then set rows to 30. The toggle stays. Clicking it applies the clamp at 30, which hides nothing, but aria-expanded is false and the toggle still offers Expand. A control measurement removes the toggle. (b) 20-line text, rows 30, so no toggle: set rows to 5. The text stays unclamped with no toggle until the next resize. A control measurement clamps it. The content case in the description holds by the same path: collapsed text that shrinks to rows+1 lines keeps its 5-line box, so it keeps the toggle. Expanding it resizes the box and removes the toggle, so that case is minor.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/clamped-text/rows-change-no-remeasure.spec.ts`](defect-repros/clamped-text/rows-change-no-remeasure.spec.ts)
  Fix: Re-measure from an effect on rows() (and on content mutation, e.g. a MutationObserver), not only on resize.
- **[clamped-text] The trigger toggles on every Enter or Space keydown, auto-repeat included; KbqLink's repeat guard never sees the event** — reproduced.
  clamped-list.ts:83-84 bind keydown.enter and keydown.space to onKeydown. onKeydown (108-111) calls preventDefault() and root.toggle() without checking event.repeat. KbqLink registers its own Enter/Space listener later, in afterNextRender (link.ts:278). That listener returns on event.defaultPrevented (link.ts:322), so its Space repeat guard (link.ts:331) is never reached. The repro passes. One Space keydown followed by three repeats on the kbq-clamped-text toggle emits isCollapsedChange \[false, true, false, true\], and the text ends collapsed. A native button would emit \[false\] once. On a `\<a kbqLink kbqClampedListTrigger pseudo>` list trigger, three keydowns emit \[false, true, false\]. A control test shows a KbqLink pseudo link on its own clicks only once for the same key sequence. The repeat of Enter also toggles each time, but a native button does the same, so only Space differs from a native button.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/clamped-text/key-repeat.spec.ts`](defect-repros/clamped-text/key-repeat.spec.ts)
  Fix: In KbqClampedListTrigger.onKeydown, still call preventDefault() but return without toggling when event.repeat is set for Space.
- **[clamped-text] Lines are counted from the line boxes of an inline wrapper; block-level content counts as far fewer lines and gets no toggle** — confirmed in the source.
  clamped-text.ts:57-59 wraps the projected content in an inline \<span #text>. getRowsCount (186-190) counts the distinct `top` values of span.getClientRects(). When an inline contains block boxes, it is split around them, and its fragments do not include the blocks' line boxes. This was checked in real browsers with a standalone page that copies the component's DOM, CSS and counting code (Playwright headless). Three \<p> that render 36 lines (720px) count as 2 rows in Chromium and 4 in WebKit. One \<div> of 12 lines counts as 2 in both. Inline text of 12 lines counts as 12. With rows=5, the check `count > rows + 1` (182) is false, so hasToggle stays false, the collapsed class is removed (130-134), and long block content is shown in full with no clamp and no toggle. jsdom has no layout, so a Vitest repro cannot show this.
  Fix: Count lines from the rendered height (scrollHeight / line-height of the content, or the content's height with the clamp removed) rather than from the inline wrapper's client rects, or document that only inline content is supported.
- **[code-block] With tabs hidden and without alwaysShowActionbar, the action bar is display:none until hover; keyboard focus never reveals it, so keyboard and screen reader users cannot reach copy, download, soft wrap or the link** — reproduced.
  code-block.scss:140-146 sets `.kbq-code-block\_hide-tabs .kbq-code-block\_\_actionbar { display: none }` and only `.kbq-code-block\_show-actionbar` (scss:185-187) brings back display:flex. `actionbarVisible` (code-block.ts:381-388) is alwaysShowActionbar || IOS || ANDROID || !tabsHidden() || actionbarHovered(), and `actionbarHovered` is written only from `:hover` and mouseenter/mouseleave in trackHoverState (code-block.ts:484-511); there is no focus/focusin handling. `tabsHidden` is true for any single unnamed file (code-block.ts:329-333). The repro focuses the overflowing code area (tabIndex 0) and then the copy button with keyboard origin, advances past the 100 ms debounce, and the host never gets `kbq-code-block\_show-actionbar`. A block whose code does not overflow has no tab stop at all.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/code-block/actionbar-keyboard-hidden.spec.ts`](defect-repros/code-block/actionbar-keyboard-hidden.spec.ts)
  Fix: Also count focus-within (focusin/focusout on the host, or :focus-within in CSS) as revealing the action bar, the same way hover does.
- **[code-block] With maxHeight set and code that fits vertically but overflows horizontally, the code area scrolls but keeps tabIndex -1** — reproduced.
  `canCodeContentBeFocused` is `!this.calculatedMaxHeight() && hasScroll(...)` (code-block.ts:352-354), and `calculatedMaxHeight` is non-null for any positive maxHeight while viewAll is off (code-block.ts:223-227). The afterEveryRender hook (code-block.ts:399-406) therefore writes tabIndex -1. With no 'Show all' button (contentExceedsMaxHeight false, code-block.ts:583-588), the later rule `.kbq-code-block:not(:has(.kbq-code-block\_\_view-all)) .kbq-code-block\_\_main { overflow: auto }` (code-block.scss:189-193, same specificity as the overflow:hidden rule at scss:134-138) applies, so the area scrolls sideways (kbqScrollbarViewport sets no overflow of its own). The repro mocks a horizontal overflow: tabIndex is 0 without maxHeight, and becomes -1 once maxHeight=200 is bound, with no 'Show all' button and the max-height style applied.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/code-block/maxheight-horizontal-tabindex.spec.ts`](defect-repros/code-block/maxheight-horizontal-tabindex.spec.ts)
  Fix: Gate focusability on whether the content actually scrolls (or on the absence of the collapsed 'Show all' state), not on calculatedMaxHeight alone.
- **[code-block] A negative maxHeight sets no limit, but the overflow measure treats any non-zero value as one, so 'Show all' appears over code that is not clipped** — reproduced.
  `calculatedMaxHeight` requires `maxHeight > 0` (code-block.ts:226), so -1 applies no max-height. `measureContentOverflow` sets `contentExceedsMaxHeight` to `!!maxHeight && !!preElement && preElement.offsetHeight > maxHeight` (code-block.ts:587), and the effect guard `!this.maxHeight()` (code-block.ts:558) also lets -1 through. Any height is > -1, so the button renders. Its presence also switches the main area to the overflow:hidden rule (code-block.scss:134-138 instead of 189-193), clipping long lines with no scroll, and adds bottom padding (scss:44-50). The repro binds maxHeight=-1 with a 60px pre: main.style.maxHeight is '' and the 'Show all' button is rendered. Only matters for invalid (or computed negative) input, hence an edge case.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/code-block/negative-maxheight-show-all.spec.ts`](defect-repros/code-block/negative-maxheight-show-all.spec.ts)
  Fix: Use the same `maxHeight > 0` guard (or calculatedMaxHeight) in measureContentOverflow and in the resize-observer effect.
- **[content-panel] Content margin computed from the unclamped width** — reproduced.
  content-panel.ts:359: `widthState = linkedSignal(() => this.width())` follows the `width` input with no clamp. content-panel.ts:370-372: `contentMargin` = widthState + parseInt(SizeL) (16). content-panel.ts:242 binds that value to `margin-right`. The panel itself gets min-width, width and max-width as three separate inline styles (content-panel.ts:255-257), so CSS clamps the panel but not the margin. Only restoreState (445) and drag (530) call clampWidth. The double-click reset (519) sets `this.width()` unclamped and also persists it (523). Repro: width 900 with maxWidth 800 gives margin-right 916px while max-width is 800px, a 100px gap. Width 300 with minWidth 480 gives margin-right 316px while min-width is 480px, so the content runs 164px under the panel. After a restore clamped to 700 the margin is 716px; a double click on the resizer then sets width 900 and margin 916px and writes {opened:true,width:900} to the store.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/content-panel/content-margin.spec.ts`](defect-repros/content-panel/content-margin.spec.ts)
  Fix: Derive one clamped width (a computed over widthState, minWidth and maxWidth) and use it for both the panel's width style and contentMargin; also clamp in handleResizerDBLClick.
- **[content-panel] Container not attached to the document at init never restores or writes state, even with an explicit stateSavingKey** — reproduced.
  content-panel.ts:536-540: `persists` requires `stateSaving.host?.isConnected`, even when an explicit `stateSavingKey` is set. restoreState (content-panel.ts:430-431) is called only from ngOnInit (415-417) and keyChanges (400), and returns before `stateSaving.read()`. As a result: readKey stays null (state-saving.ts:116, set only at 165); the dev-mode warning that suggests a key (state-saving.ts:150-160) never runs; every later write is refused silently because key !== readKey while readKey === null (state-saving.ts:202-212); and keyChanges never fires (state-saving.ts:130). Repro: the host is created with createComponent() and its first detectChanges (ngOnInit) runs while detached; it is then appended to body with stateSavingKey set. Result: the seeded {opened:true,width:420} is not restored, open() renders 640px, and the store is never written by open or close. hasSavedState stays false and no console.warn is emitted.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/content-panel/state-saving.spec.ts`](defect-repros/content-panel/state-saving.spec.ts)
  Fix: Skip the isConnected guard when stateSavingKey is set, or defer the first restore until the host is connected (e.g. afterNextRender), so the directive reads and its warning can surface.
- **[content-panel] Turning useStateSaving on after initialization never starts persisting** — reproduced.
  With useStateSaving false at ngOnInit, restoreState returns at content-panel.ts:431, so read() never runs and readKey stays null. Nothing calls restoreState when useStateSaving changes: it is called only at 416 (ngOnInit) and 400 (keyChanges). The directive refuses writes while readKey is null (state-saving.ts:202-212), and its key-change effect returns early while readKey is null (state-saving.ts:130), so changing the key does not trigger a read either. Core test state-saving.spec.ts:586-597 pins the directive side ('keeps writes suppressed after a read taken while disabled'). Repro: start with useStateSaving false, set it to true, open(), change stateSavingKey, close(): the store stays empty and hasSavedState stays false. The clearSavedState JSDoc (content-panel.ts:465, 'Unset useStateSaving to stop it') implies the flag can be toggled.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/content-panel/state-saving.spec.ts`](defect-repros/content-panel/state-saving.spec.ts)
  Fix: Restore (read) when useStateSaving turns on after init, e.g. an effect on stateSaving.useStateSaving() in the container, or have the directive emit keyChanges or an enabled event the owner restores on.
- **[content-panel] Panel exposes nothing to assistive technology; no focus management on open/close** — reproduced.
  Neither the panel wrapper (content-panel.ts:249-258) nor the kbq-content-panel host (content-panel.ts:192-194) sets a role or ARIA attributes. The file contains no focus() call at all. The close button calls close() (content-panel.ts:89), the `@if (openedState())` block removes the panel (248), and the focused button is removed with it. Repro in jsdom: after opening, the panel and kbq-content-panel have no role, aria-label or aria-labelledby, and focus stays on the trigger. After clicking the focused close button, the button is disconnected and document.activeElement is body. In a browser removal waits for the 125ms leave animation, then focus also goes to body. P2 rests on the focus loss, which every keyboard user hits on close (WCAG 2.4.3; the repo requires WCAG AA focus management). The missing role and name alone would be P3. Whether open and closed states are announced was not checked with real assistive technology.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/content-panel/a11y-focus.spec.ts`](defect-repros/content-panel/a11y-focus.spec.ts)
  Fix: Remember document.activeElement on open and restore it on close (or when the panel's focused descendant is removed), and give the panel a landmark role (complementary or region) with an accessible name, e.g. aria-labelledby on the header title.
- **[content-panel] openedChange not emitted when a restored state opens the panel** — reproduced.
  restoreState sets openedState directly (content-panel.ts:441-443) inside stateSaving.applying(); setOpened (507-511) is the only place that emits openedChange. useStateSaving defaults to true (KBQ\_STATE\_SAVING\_ENABLED factory, state-saving.ts:25-28), so any app that leaves `opened` unbound and tracks `(openedChange)` gets out of sync after a reload with the panel left open. Repro: with {opened:true,width:420} seeded and `opened` unbound, isOpened() is true and the panel renders, but the openedChange event list is empty. The JSDoc (content-panel.ts:306-309) promises an emit whenever the opened state changes.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/content-panel/state-saving.spec.ts`](defect-repros/content-panel/state-saving.spec.ts)
  Fix: Emit openedChange when a restore changes the opened state (after init, so the parent's binding is not changed during its own check), or document that restored state is reported only through isOpened().
- **[datepicker] 1. The calendar cannot be used from the keyboard** — reproduced.
  Opening with Alt+Down leaves focus on the input: openCalendar/openAsPopup (datepicker.component.ts:435-452, 489-513) never move focus into the panel. Day cells are `\[tabindex\]="-1"` (calendar-body.html:19) and nothing in the body handles keys; the skipped Enter test is at datepicker.spec.ts:404. Tab in the input calls close(false) (datepicker-input.directive.ts:800-801). The toggle icon has `\[tabindex\]="-1"` (datepicker-toggle.component.ts:36), and the toggle host has no role or tabindex (:47-53). The month and year kbq-select hosts render tabindex=-1 (calendar-header.html:6,33). The repro shows all of this: focus stays on the input, every cell has tabindex=-1, Enter on a day does nothing, and Tab closes the panel. One sub-claim is wrong: the previous/today/next header buttons are NOT tabindex=-1. The template binds lowercase `\[tabindex\]` (calendar-header.html:66,78,90), which sets the native property and does not reach kbq-button's `tabIndex` input (button.component.ts:448). kbq-button's host `\[attr.tabindex\]` (button.component.ts:319, 432-436) is null for an enabled native button, so it removes the attribute. The repro measures tabindex=null on all three, so they are as tabbable as the select triggers. The panel still cannot be reached, because focus never enters it and Tab closes it. Typing in the input stays available as a keyboard alternative.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/datepicker/keyboard-a11y.spec.ts`](defect-repros/datepicker/keyboard-a11y.spec.ts)
  Fix: Make the panel keyboard-operable (move focus in on open, roving-tabindex grid with arrow/Enter handling, a focusable toggle button, no close on Tab into the panel), and bind kbq-button's `\[tabIndex\]` input if the header buttons really should be -1.
- **[datepicker] 3. A read-only input still opens from the toggle icon, and a picked day writes it** — reproduced.
  toggleCalendar (datepicker-toggle.component.ts:119-140) focuses the input and calls datepicker.open() without checking read-only. The toggle's `disabled` getter (:57-59) also ignores it. Only toggle() checks isReadOnly (datepicker.component.ts:422-425). openCalendar (:435-438) checks only opened and disabled. The selectedChanged subscription (datepicker-input.directive.ts:400-407) then calls setValue and cvaOnChange. Repro: datepicker.toggle() keeps a `readonly` input closed, but a click on the toggle icon opens it. Clicking day 5 changes the FormControl from 2020-01-01 to 2020-01-05 and the text to 05.01.2020, while input.readOnly is still true.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/datepicker/readonly-toggle.spec.ts`](defect-repros/datepicker/readonly-toggle.spec.ts)
  Fix: Refuse to open (or to write a pick) when datepickerInput.isReadOnly, e.g. in openCalendar() or by having the toggle treat a read-only input as disabled.
- **[datepicker] 4. monthSelected and yearSelected carry the previously shown date** — reproduced.
  calendar.html:5-6 handles the header outputs with `monthSelected.emit(activeDate)` and `yearSelected.emit(activeDate)`. Both ignore $event and read the calendar's own activeDate. The header emits monthSelected or yearSelected before activeDateChange (calendar-header.component.ts:177-178, 192-193), so the two-way `\[(activeDate)\]` has not updated the calendar yet. Repro: from 2017-01-31, picking June moves the calendar to 2017-06-30 but emits monthSelected=2017-01-31. Picking 2019 next moves it to 2019-06-30 but emits yearSelected=2017-06-30. KbqDatepicker re-emits these values (datepicker-content.html via selectMonth/selectYear).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/datepicker/month-selected-stale.spec.ts`](defect-repros/datepicker/month-selected-stale.spec.ts)
  Fix: Emit `$event` in calendar.html, or emit after activeDateChange has been applied.
- **[datepicker] 6. Separators: the format's own separator is rejected; onPaste discards its replace()** — reproduced.
  A one-character non-digit key goes through getNewValue plus replaceSymbols (datepicker-input.directive.ts:814-826). replaceSymbols (:1115-1120) splits on the format's separator, so '5.' becomes \['5',''\], comes back unchanged and is rejected with incorrectInput. '5/' matches /^(\[0-9\]+)\W$/ and becomes '05', which completes the day. Repro in dd.MM.yyyy: '.' after '5' is prevented, emits incorrectInput and leaves '5'. '/' then gives 05.01.\<year> with day 5 in the model. So the natural 5.6.2020 cannot be typed. onPaste line 944 calls `rawValue.replace(/\[^A-Za-z0-9\]+/g, this.separator)` and drops the result. The match at :950 allows only a single \W between groups, so '15. 06. 2020' is shown raw and the model stays null (repro), while '15.06.2020' parses.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/datepicker/separator.spec.ts`](defect-repros/datepicker/separator.spec.ts)
  Fix: Treat the format separator like other punctuation in onKeyDown/replaceSymbols (pad the part and move on), and assign `rawValue = rawValue.replace(...)` in onPaste.
- **[datepicker] 8. valueChanges stores the raw control value as value(): ISO string stays a string, invalid date kept** — reproduced.
