  setControl (datepicker-input.directive.ts:1586) runs `valueChanges.subscribe((value) => this.valueState.set(value))` with no deserialize or validity check. writeValue (:759-761) deserializes, but valueChanges fires right after it in FormControl.setValue and overwrites the state. Repro: ngModel bound to an ISO string renders 01.06.2017, yet value() is a string. Typing the complete '15.07.2017' then does not reach the model: getDefaultValue (:1186-1197) reads year and hours from the string as undefined, and createDateTime (:1645) returns null on NaN. A FormControl set to DateTime.invalid() leaves value() as that invalid instance instead of null. The skipped test datepicker.spec.ts:1955 documents the string part.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/datepicker/iso-ngmodel.spec.ts`](defect-repros/datepicker/iso-ngmodel.spec.ts)
  Fix: In the valueChanges subscription store `getValidDateOrNull(adapter.deserialize(value))`, or drop the subscription and rely on writeValue.
- **[datepicker] 9. No input event is handled: Backspace/Delete reach the model only later; arrows dead without a value** — reproduced.
  The host listeners (datepicker-input.directive.ts:319-323) include no `(input)`. Backspace and Delete hit isKeyForByPass (:802-803, :1067) and return without scheduling onInput. Repro: select all, Backspace, empty text and an `input` event leave the FormControl at 2020-01-15 until blur. Only blur's onInput (:932, :840-844) sets it to null. So Enter-submit or a valueChanges/required check right after clearing sees the stale date. Left, Right, Home, End, PageUp and PageDown are prevented (:810-813), and changeCaretPosition returns when value() is null (:1439-1441). Repro: Left on the partly typed '1' with no model is defaultPrevented and the caret stays at 1.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/datepicker/no-input-event.spec.ts`](defect-repros/datepicker/no-input-event.spec.ts)
  Fix: Handle the `input` event (or schedule onInput for Backspace/Delete/cut), and do not preventDefault caret keys when there is no value to step through.
- **[divider] A vertical divider without length in a container with no definite height collapses to 0 px; a horizontal one in a flex row collapses to 0 px wide** — confirmed in the source.
  divider.component.ts:22 has an empty template (`template: ''`), so the host has no content height or width. divider.scss:4 sets `display: block`, and divider.scss:18-20 gives the vertical divider `width: 1px; height: 100%`. Under CSS 2.1 §10.5, a percentage height against a containing block with an indefinite height behaves as `auto`, so the empty element is 0 px tall. In a flex row the computed height is `100%`, not `auto`, so `align-self: stretch` does not apply either, and the line disappears. The horizontal rule (divider.scss:7-9) sets no width. As a flex item in a row its flex-basis is `auto`, its content width is 0 and `flex-grow` is 0, so it is 0 px wide. Supporting evidence: every in-repo vertical divider placed in a flex row sets its own height to avoid this, in notification-center.scss:67-68 (`height: var(--kbq-size-m)`), filters.scss:34-37 (`height: var(--kbq-size-m) !important`) and actions-panel-container.scss:38-40. The e2e scenario only shows the default inside a `td` with `height: 50px` (e2e.ts:28-30), where the percentage resolves. The JSDoc (divider.component.ts:64-67) and divider.en.mdx 'Length' both say the default takes its length from the layout. jsdom has no layout engine, so this cannot be shown in a spec.
  Fix: Size the default vertical divider with `align-self: stretch` / `height: auto` (and give a horizontal one `align-self: stretch` or `flex: 1 1 auto` in flex contexts), or document that it needs a definite container height or `length`.
- **[dl] kbq-dl, kbq-dt and kbq-dd carry no role and are not native dl/dt/dd, so AT gets flat text with no term-description relation (WCAG 1.3.1)** — confirmed in the source.
  packages/components/dl/dl.component.ts:50-59 (kbq-dt host has only class 'kbq-dt'), :61-70 (kbq-dd host only class 'kbq-dd'), :111-123 (kbq-dl host bindings: classes and CSS variables, no role). All three selectors are custom elements, which HTML-AAM maps to generic. A grep of packages/components/dl finds no role other than role="separator" on the resize handle (:90). The pairing exists only visually, through the CSS grid columns in dl.scss:37-43, so screen readers get a linear run of text. Not reproduced: jsdom has no accessibility tree, and the missing role is plain from the source.
  Fix: Give kbq-dt role="term" and kbq-dd role="definition" through their host bindings (and consider a grouping role on kbq-dl), or render native dl/dt/dd semantics.
- **[dl] A dtWidth bound after the first measurement is not clamped when it arrives: the grid column takes the raw value while the separator is held at the bound and aria-valuenow reports the separator** — reproduced.
  dtWidth is a linkedSignal of the input (dl.component.ts:155). The clamp effect (:285-301) tracks only dtMinWidth() and ddMinWidth() (:287-288) and reads dtWidth inside untracked, so a new bound value does not trigger it. The only other clamp is updateLayout (:362-367), which runs only from the resize-observer subscription (:277-280). Until a clamp runs, the raw value goes to --kbq-description-list-dt-width (:121) and so to grid-template-columns (dl.scss:53-56). The track keeps its min-width/max-width bindings (:83-84), which hold the separator at the bound, and aria-valuenow reads the track's clientWidth (:98, :233-237). Repro, on a measured 600 px list with bounds 200..500: binding dtWidth 50 leaves dtWidth()=50, the CSS variable at 50px, the track at width 50px with min-width 200px, and nothing emitted; only a later resize-observer emission clamps it to 200 and emits 200. Binding 1000 leaves the CSS variable at 1000px while the track has max-width 500px.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dl/unclamped-bound-dtwidth.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dl/unclamped-bound-dtwidth.spec.ts)
  Fix: Also track dtWidth (or dtWidthInput) in the post-measurement clamp effect, so a consumer's value is clamped as soon as it arrives once the bounds are known.
- **[dl] A drag writes the raw pointer width onto the separator track; after a drag that ended past a bound, a lower minimum or a wider list leaves the separator away from the column border** — reproduced.
  The dl template uses kbqResizer without disableSizeUpdate (dl.component.ts:99-103), so KbqResizer.updateSize writes the unclamped width onto the track (resizer.ts:308-319). dl clamps the value in setDtWidth (:377-385) and writes dtWidth only when the clamped value changes. Angular's \[style.width.px\]="dtWidth()" (:85) writes to the DOM only when the bound value changes, so once the column sits at the bound, every further overshoot leaves the raw width on the track, hidden only by the track's min-width/max-width (:83-84). Repro 1: bounds 150..500, drag to raw 120 then 110 gives dtWidth 150 and track style.width 110px; lowering dtMinWidth to 50 sets min-width to 50px, so the separator rests at 110 px while the column variable is 150px. Repro 2: bounds 100..500 at 600 px, drag to raw 550 then 580 gives dtWidth 500 and track width 580px; widening the list to 900 px raises max-width to 800px while the column stays at 500px.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dl/drag-raw-track-width.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dl/drag-raw-track-width.spec.ts)
  Fix: Set disableSizeUpdate on the kbqResizer in the dl template, so that only the \[style.width.px\] binding (the clamped dtWidth) sizes the track.
- **[dl] Default minimums are measured once after the first render from the first kbq-dt: hidden lists or late terms keep 0; a list first rendered vertical keeps a full-row minimum when it turns horizontal** — reproduced.
  measureDtWidth (dl.component.ts:306-310) is called only from afterNextRender (:271-272) and reads terms()\[0\] once. Nothing re-measures when terms() changes, when the layout leaves vertical, or when a hidden list gets a box. A display:none list measures 0, the same path as a missing term. resolvedDtMinWidth and resolvedDdMinWidth (:216, :219) both fall back to that one value. Repro, with layout modelled by a getBoundingClientRect stub (a term is 120 px in the horizontal layout and the full 600 px row in the vertical one): a term present at the first render gives aria-valuemin 120 (control); terms added after the first render leave aria-valuemin 0 and --kbq-description-list-dt-min-width 0px; a list rendered with vertical=true and then switched to false shows --kbq-description-list-dt-min-width 600px and aria-valuemin = aria-valuemax = 600. The resting grid minmax(600px, 1fr) (dl.scss:47-51) then gives the term column the whole row, and there is no room to resize.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dl/default-min-measured-once.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dl/default-min-measured-once.spec.ts)
  Fix: Re-measure the first term when terms() changes, when the layout becomes horizontal, or when the list first gets a non-zero width (for example in updateLayout while the measurement is still 0 or stale).
- **[dl] A narrow list in automatic mode renders horizontally for its first 100 ms and then jumps to vertical; on the server it is always horizontal, so a hydrated narrow list shifts layout** — reproduced.
  isVertical (dl.component.ts:207) falls back to false while hostWidth is null (:193, :200-204). hostWidth is set only in updateLayout (:365), whose first call comes through startWith(null) + debounceTime(100) (:279) inside afterNextRender (:271), which never runs on the server. So both the SSR output and the first ~100 ms in the browser use the horizontal layout, with the separator rendered when resizable. Repro: a resizable 320 px list (default breakpoint 400) has no kbq-dl\_vertical and shows the separator at render and at 99 ms, then switches to kbq-dl\_vertical and removes the separator at 100 ms. The baseline spec states this behaviour as shipped (spec.md 'first measure its width 100 ms after its first render'), so a fix also changes the spec.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dl/auto-layout-flash.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dl/auto-layout-flash.spec.ts)
  Fix: Measure once without the debounce in afterNextRender, or better, drive the breakpoint with a CSS container query so the server render and the first paint already use the right layout.
- **[dropdown] Explicit panelWidth is ignored on nested panels and on overlapTriggerY && !overlapTriggerX panels, and their CSS min-width floor drops to 0px** — reproduced.
  shouldMatchTriggerWidth (dropdown-trigger.directive.ts:719-723) is false for a nested panel and for one with overlapTriggerY set and overlapTriggerX unset. The pane size from getOverlaySize() is applied only behind that flag: updateSize at 426-428 and the config spread at 739. So an explicit width never reaches the pane. panelMinWidthToken (dropdown.component.ts:260-268) still returns '0px' for an explicit panelWidth, which removes the CSS floor. This contradicts the panelWidth JSDoc ('Any other value is used as an exact width', dropdown.component.ts:222-227). The repro uses \[panelWidth\]="300". A top-level control pane gets style.width '300px'. The nested pane and the vertical pane both get style.width '' and --kbq-dropdown-size-container-width-min '0px'.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dropdown/nested-panel-width.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dropdown/nested-panel-width.spec.ts)
  Fix: Apply an explicit panelWidth to the pane regardless of shouldMatchTriggerWidth, and keep only the trigger-width floor conditional.
- **[dropdown] On an item in progress the item overlay swallows pointer clicks (panel stays open), while Enter/Space on a native button host still activates it** — reproduced.
  dropdown-item.html:15-19 always renders .kbq-dropdown-item-overlay with (click)=haltDisabledEvents. It is position:absolute with inset -1px (dropdown-item.scss:41-50) inside a position:relative item (list.kbq-list-item-base), and the text is static, so a real pointer click lands on the overlay. haltDisabledEvents (dropdown-item.component.ts:335-341) calls preventDefault and stops propagation when progress() is set, so neither the consumer (click) on the host nor the content (click)=close() (dropdown.html:13) runs. handleKeydown (dropdown-item.component.ts:257-258) returns early on progress without preventDefault, so on a \<button> host the native Enter/Space click goes to the host and runs normally. In search mode, panel Enter (dropdown.component.ts:600-610) also replays host.click() regardless of progress. jsdom does no hit-testing and does not turn Enter into a click, so the repro dispatches each event where a browser delivers it. A click on the overlay is defaultPrevented, the handler does not run and the panel stays open. Enter on the host is not prevented, and the host click runs the handler and closes the panel. The docs (dropdown.en.mdx:11) also imply that progress alone keeps the item interactive.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dropdown/progress-item-activation.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dropdown/progress-item-activation.spec.ts)
  Fix: Choose one contract for progress: either block the keyboard path too (preventDefault in handleKeydown, skip the search-mode replay) or stop the overlay from halting clicks; then align the docs.
- **[dynamic-translation] \[\[name:\]\] lets the context run on to a later \]\] and swallow the text and markers in between** — reproduced.
  dynamic-translation.ts:104: in `(?::(.+?))?`, `.+?` needs at least one character. For `\[\[a:\]\]` the lazy match therefore expands until the next `\]\]`. Repro: the text '\[\[a:\]\] x \[\[b:y\]\]' renders one `a` slot (strong) with the context '\]\] x \[\[b:y' and no `b` slot (em), and the host text is '\]\] x \[\[b:y'. The spec passed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dynamic-translation/empty-context.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dynamic-translation/empty-context.spec.ts)
  Fix: Forbid '\]' in the context (e.g. `(\[^\\]\]\*?)` or `((?:(?!\\]\\]).)\*)`) or accept an empty context with `.\*?`.
- **[dynamic-translation] () gives a list template the string () instead of an empty array** — reproduced.
  dynamic-translation.ts:110 matches '()' with listString ''. At :120, `listMatch && listString` is falsy for '', so `$implicit` is the raw string '()'. Angular's @for iterates any iterable (node\_modules/@angular/core/fesm2022/\_debug\_node-chunk.mjs:13768-13770 `newCollection\[Symbol.iterator\]()`). Repro: '\[\[list:()\]\]' gives the template a non-array (data-is-array='false'), and `@for (item of list)` renders the two items '(' and ')'. An empty list interpolated into a translation produces this. The spec passed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dynamic-translation/empty-list.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dynamic-translation/empty-list.spec.ts)
  Fix: Check `listMatch !== undefined` and map '' to \[\] (e.g. `listString ? listString.split(',') : \[\]`).
- **[dynamic-translation] The helper replaces its placeholders once; later text changes leave stale, empty or orphaned elements** — reproduced.
  dynamic-translation.ts:172-174 calls replaceSlots from a single afterNextRender. When only the context changes, NgTemplateOutlet keeps its view and forwards the new context through a proxy (@angular/common \_common\_module-chunk.mjs NgTemplateOutlet.\_shouldRecreateView/\_createContextForwardProxy). The `\[attr.data-slot-content\]` binding (:149) therefore updates the span that `replaceWith` (:188) already detached. Repro: after the text changes to '\[\[bold:second\]\] and \[\[italic:new\]\]', the \<b> still says 'first'. The new marker stays as `\<span data-slot-name="italic" data-slot-content="new">` with no text, and no \<i> is created. After the text changes to 'plain text', the \<b>first\</b> is still in the DOM, because Angular removes only the nodes it rendered. A locale switch changes `text` in normal use. All 3 tests passed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dynamic-translation/helper-text-change.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dynamic-translation/helper-text-change.spec.ts)
  Fix: Replace placeholders after every render (afterEveryRender, or an effect on the viewChildren query) and track/remove created elements, or render through templates instead of DOM replacement.
- **[dynamic-translation] A helper slot without content, used in a marker without a context, renders the text "null"** — reproduced.
  dynamic-translation.ts:149 `\[attr.data-slot-content\]="slot.content || content"` evaluates to undefined, so Angular removes the attribute. :180 getAttribute then returns null, and :184 `createText(null)` creates the text node 'null'. Repro: slot {name:'icon', tag:'i'} with the text 'Status \[\[icon\]\] ok' renders `\<i class="test-icon">null\</i>`, and the host text is 'Status null ok'. The spec passed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dynamic-translation/helper-null-content.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dynamic-translation/helper-null-content.spec.ts)
  Fix: Fall back to '' (`slotContent ?? ''`) and skip createText when there is no content.
- **[ellipsis-center] A change of the text's rendered width that leaves the host's width unchanged (web font swapping in) keeps the stale split and tooltip state** — reproduced.
  ellipsis-center.directive.ts:141-148 observes only the host's size, and the callback re-splits only when `clientWidth !== lastMeasuredWidth`. The text is measured once, in the zero-delay timer at :193-233 (`dataTextStart.scrollWidth` at :196). Nothing listens to `document.fonts` or to the text itself. When a wider face swaps in, the host keeps its size, so the observer stays silent. Even if it fires (for example on a height change), the width guard drops it. The repro measures a 300px host: the text is 280px in the fallback face, so it fits, stays unsplit, and the tooltip is disabled. The text then becomes 360px. After 1s, and after a resize notification, the state is unchanged: no split and `disabled === true`. A manual `refresh()` with the same widths does split and enables the tooltip, which shows the earlier state is stale. packages/e2e/utils/fonts.ts:7-10 states the same race, and file-upload/e2e.playwright-spec.ts:76 works around it with `e2eWaitForFonts`. The realistic result is a text that overflows only in the web font: it is cut at the end and has no tooltip. A clipped tail needs glyphs about twice as wide, because the tail is sized to about half the host (:207), so that part is unlikely.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/ellipsis-center/font-swap-stale-split.spec.ts`](defect-repros/ellipsis-center/font-swap-stale-split.spec.ts)
  Fix: Also re-measure when `document.fonts` reports loading done (or observe the start span's size) and drop the host-width guard for that path.
- **[file-upload] Reading a dropped folder calls readEntries() once per directory, so entries past the first batch (~100 in Chromium) are lost** — reproduced.
  primitives/file-drop.ts:146-151 (unwrapDirectory) creates a reader and pushes one `new Promise(resolve => directoryReader.readEntries(resolve, reject))` per directory. It never calls readEntries again until it returns an empty array, which the File and Directory Entries API requires and which Chromium needs because it returns at most 100 entries per call. The repro drops a folder whose reader mimics Chromium: it returns 100 of 150 entries per call. readEntries is called once and `filesDropped` emits exactly 100 files. The other 50 are lost without a warning or a `rejected` emission. Nested sub-directories are affected the same way.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/file-upload/read-entries-batch.spec.ts`](defect-repros/file-upload/read-entries-batch.spec.ts)
  Fix: Loop readEntries() on the same reader until it resolves an empty batch, concatenating the batches.
- **[file-upload] kbqLocalDropzone opens its overlay on drag-enter while disabled; the overlay then never closes on leave and does not take the drop** — reproduced.
  dropzone.ts:268-274 subscribes to the host's dragenter with no `filter(() => !this.disabled())` and calls open(). open() attaches the overlay and calls init() (:296), whose dragover/dragleave/drop handlers on the pane are all filtered by disabled (:329, :339, :346). The full-screen service (:124) and KbqFileDropDirective (file-drop.ts:106) filter dragenter as well. Repro with `\[disabled\]="true"`: dragenter on the host attaches the `.kbq-local-dropzone` pane. A dragleave on the pane leaves it attached, and dragover and drop on it are not defaultPrevented. A control test shows the same overlay closes on leave once enabled. The overlay then stays until destroy or re-enable (open() early-returns while attached, :281). In a browser the uncancelled dragover means a drop falls through to the browser default (opening the file). The pane also covers the host and runs CdkTrapFocus with autoCapture defaulting to true (:484), so it captures focus.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/file-upload/local-dropzone-disabled.spec.ts`](defect-repros/file-upload/local-dropzone-disabled.spec.ts)
  Fix: Add `filter(() => !this.disabled())` to the host dragenter stream (and close an open overlay when disabled turns on).
- **[filter-bar] The bar's reset stream is a BehaviorSubject that stays true after the first reset, so a pipe with openOnReset rendered later opens on its own** — reproduced.
  filter-bar.ts:174 creates `onResetFilter = new BehaviorSubject\<boolean>(false)`. filter-reset.ts:32 calls `next(true)` and nothing ever resets it. base-pipe.ts:203-206 subscribes in ngAfterViewInit with `filter(Boolean)` and no `skip(1)` (unlike openPipe at :177-187), so every pipe created after any reset gets the replayed `true` and calls open(). `openOnReset` is also never deleted from the pipe object (openOnAdd is deleted at :168-171), and KbqFilters.selectFilter (filters.ts:186) copies pipes with the flag kept. The latch is load-bearing: the reset-built filter's pipes render after `next(true)` and only open through the replay. The repro passes: a control pipe rendered before any reset stays closed; after one reset (the intended open), close, then re-selecting a saved copy of that filter opens the new pipe's panel while the reset count is still 1 and `onResetFilter.value` is still `true`. Only the comments in the filter-bar-overview and filter-bar-required examples (around lines 380 and 132) warn about it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/filter-bar/reset-latch.spec.ts`](defect-repros/filter-bar/reset-latch.spec.ts)
  Fix: Treat the reset as a one-shot command: either reset the subject to false after the rendered pipes have handled it, or deliver it to pipes created in the same reset cycle another way, and drop openOnReset from the pipe once it has been applied.
- **[filter-bar] Multi-select pipes emit onChangePipe with a copy of the pipe ({ ...data, value: \[\] }) under "all selected equals nothing selected"** — reproduced.
  multi-select-pipe-state.ts:94-100 (line 96) emits `{ ...this.host.data, value: \[\] }` when all options are selected, although the callers have already set `data.value = \[\]` (pipe-multi-select.ts:195-205 and 248-256; pipe-multi-tree-select.ts:202-208 and 259-267). filter-bar.ts:279 `removePipe` checks `current.pipes.includes(pipe)` and silently does nothing for the copy. The repro passes: after toggling 'select all', the emitted pipe has `value` \[\], is not the object in `filter().pipes` (which is the pipe's own `data`), `pipes.includes(emitted)` is false, and `removePipe(emitted)` leaves the pipe in the filter. In the control test, a deselect-all toggle emits the very object held in the filter. The bar's own `changed` flag is unaffected because its subscriber ignores the payload.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/filter-bar/multiselect-change-copy.spec.ts`](defect-repros/filter-bar/multiselect-change-copy.spec.ts)
  Fix: Emit `this.host.data` in both branches of emitChangePipeEvent, since data.value is already \[\] at that point.
- **[form-field] A cleaner next to a control without a form directive appears once text is typed, but activating it only focuses the input** — reproduced.
  cleaner.ts:99-107 `canShow` needs only an enabled, non-empty control. KbqInput reads emptiness from the native element (input.ts:327-331, 375-377), so it works without forms. cleaner.ts:157-163: when the form-field context has no `clear()` (form-field.ts:114-126 never sets one), the cleaner calls `this.context.control.ngControl?.reset()`. That is a no-op when `ngControl` is null (input.ts:71), and then it only calls `focus()`. Repro `\<kbq-form-field>\<input kbqInput />\<kbq-cleaner />\</kbq-form-field>`: typing shows the cleaner; clicking it focuses the input but leaves `input.value === 'koobiq'` and the cleaner visible. Select, tag-list, tree-select and timezone are not affected because they provide their own `clear()` context (select.component.ts:231, tag-list.component.ts:81, tree-select.component.ts:206, timezone-select.component.ts:81). kbqInput, kbqTextarea, datepicker and timepicker are affected.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/form-field/cleaner-without-form-directive.spec.ts`](defect-repros/form-field/cleaner-without-form-directive.spec.ts)
  Fix: In the form-field cleaner context, fall back to clearing the control's value (for example `control.value.set('')` or writing the element and dispatching `input`) when `ngControl` is null. Or hide the cleaner when there is nothing it can reset.
- **[form-field] The cleaner's Escape handler on the form-field host ignores defaultPrevented and stops propagation (autocomplete panel stays open, modal does not close)** — reproduced.
  The keydown listener sits on the form-field host (form-field.ts:121-124, cleaner.ts:135-141). It clears and calls `stopPropagation()`/`preventDefault()` whenever the field is focused and filled (cleaner.ts:154-155, 170-174) and never checks `defaultPrevented`. The autocomplete trigger only calls `preventDefault()` on Escape and relies on the key reaching `body` (autocomplete-trigger.directive.ts:529-533). There the CDK OverlayKeyboardDispatcher listens (cdk \_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:416) and feeds `keydownEvents` (autocomplete-trigger.directive.ts:822-828), which close the panel. Repro mirrors the autocomplete-overview docs example (input + kbq-autocomplete + kbq-cleaner). Escape with the panel open sets the value to null, the event never reaches body, and `panelOpen` stays true. A control fixture without the cleaner shows the event reaching body, the panel closing and the value kept. The modal half is intended rather than a defect: KbqModal skips a defaultPrevented Escape (modal.component.ts:675-678), and Escape being stopped in a filled field is the documented baseline (first Escape clears, second closes). So only the autocomplete (popup-over-cleaner) case is a defect.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/form-field/escape-autocomplete-cleaner.spec.ts`](defect-repros/form-field/escape-autocomplete-cleaner.spec.ts)
  Fix: Skip clearing in `onKeyDown` when `event.defaultPrevented` is already set, so a control that consumed Escape to close its popup wins.
- **[form-field] The kbq-form-field-type-\<type> class and the ng-\* refresh subscription are set up once, for the first control** — reproduced.
  form-field.ts:510-521 `initializeControl()` runs only from `ngAfterContentInit` (376-380). It adds `kbq-form-field-type-${controlType}` once and subscribes to the first control's `valueChanges` only. Replacing the control is a supported scenario (form-field.spec.ts:732 'should reflect the state of a control that replaces the previous one'). Repro: swapping `\<input kbqInput>` for `\<input kbqInputPassword>` under `@if` keeps `kbq-form-field-type-input` and never adds `kbq-form-field-type-input-password`. Styles key on this class: select suffix margin (form-field.scss:51), 136px width for datepicker/timepicker (datepicker-input.scss:1, timepicker.scss:8), tag-list (tag-list.scss:71). So swapping between these types leaves wrong styling. The second half of the claim does not hold: in the same spec, ng-valid/ng-touched still follow programmatic `setValue`/`markAsTouched` on the swapped-in control. NgControlStatus reads the forms signals, and the embedded view reports them to the component view that runs KbqFormField's host bindings.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/form-field/swapped-control.spec.ts`](defect-repros/form-field/swapped-control.spec.ts)
  Fix: Derive the type class from `control().controlType` reactively (a host class binding or an effect that swaps the class) instead of adding it once in ngAfterContentInit.
- **[icon] iconName assigned after initialization changes only the font class (no SVG resolution, stale max-height, old SVG stays)** — reproduced.
  `iconName` is a public plain field (icon.component.ts:129, also in tools/public\_api\_guard/components/icon.api.md:49). Only setIconName (:192-196, protected) and ngAfterContentInit (:226-231) call resolveSvgIcon (:234), and max-height is only rewritten from ngAfterContentInit/applyIconSize (:199-213, :290-300). The host binding `\[class\]: svgIcon() ? null : iconName` (:42) is the only thing that reads the new value. Repro: after init, assigning `iconName` plus markForCheck gives a font icon the new class but no SVG, even though the name is registered, and max-height stays 16px for a `\_64` name. An SVG icon keeps the old SVG (path, data-kbq-icon-name, width 16) and gets no class for the new name. Without markForCheck, an OnPush parent never re-reads the field and the exhaustive check throws NG0100, because it is not a signal.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/icon/icon-name-after-init.spec.ts`](defect-repros/icon/icon-name-after-init.spec.ts)
  Fix: Make `iconName` a setter (or a signal) that goes through setIconName after content init, or make it read-only/@docs-private and point consumers to the input.
- **[icon] A disabled icon button on \<a href> stays in the tab order and Enter follows the link** — reproduced.
  The host bindings (icon-button.component.ts:51-53) only drop the `tabindex` attribute (getter :71-73) and write `disabled`/`aria-disabled`. `disabled` means nothing on `\<a>`, and the component has no keydown/click handler. icon-button.scss:17-19 `pointer-events: none` blocks only the pointer. Repro with `\<a kbq-icon-button href disabled>`: no tabindex attribute but `tabIndex === 0`, `focus()` makes it activeElement, an Enter keydown is not defaultPrevented, and a click (which Enter fires on a focused link) reaches the document not defaultPrevented, so navigation would proceed. KbqDropdownItemAction (dropdown/dropdown-item-action.ts) compensates with tabindex=-1 and preventDefault only inside a disabled dropdown item, whose comment notes that `\<a>` has no native disabled. Actual navigation on Enter is browser behaviour; the repro shows that nothing cancels it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/icon/disabled-anchor-focusable.spec.ts`](defect-repros/icon/disabled-anchor-focusable.spec.ts)
  Fix: When disabled on a non-native host (especially \<a href>), set tabindex=-1 and preventDefault/stopImmediatePropagation on click and Enter keydown, as KbqDropdownItemAction does.
- **[icon] iconSize turning undefined on an unsized name leaves the old width/height on the inserted SVG while max-height is removed** — reproduced.
  ngOnChanges (icon.component.ts:221) calls applyIconSize (:290-300), which writes width/height only when `size !== undefined` and never removes them. updateMaxHeight (:207-212) removes the host max-height. Repro: `\<i kbq-icon="logo" \[iconSize\]="24 -> undefined">` with a registered SVG keeps the same SVG with width=24/height=24 while host max-height becomes ''. This contradicts the iconSize JSDoc (:83-86), which says an unsized name renders at whatever size the surrounding styles give.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/icon/icon-size-unset-keeps-svg-size.spec.ts`](defect-repros/icon/icon-size-unset-keeps-svg-size.spec.ts)
  Fix: In applyIconSize, remove the width/height attributes from the injected SVG when the resolved size is undefined.
- **[inline-edit] The view's aria-label ("Edit" by default) replaces its content as the accessible name, so assistive technology announces neither the value nor the field label (focus anchor named the same way)** — reproduced.
  inline-edit.html:14-17 gives the view content role=button and aria-label=accessibleName(), and inline-edit.ts:408 sets accessibleName = ariaLabel() || a11y.edit, so the label is always present. The focus anchor at inline-edit.html:25-31 gets the same name. Under accname, aria-label wins over the name from content, and button children are presentational. The repro computes the name with axe-core's accessibleText on a view that contains 'John Smith' under \<kbq-label>Assignee\</kbq-label>. The name comes out as exactly ru-RU a11y.edit ('Редактировать'). It contains neither the value nor the label, and the element has no aria-labelledby or aria-describedby. In focus mode every field therefore reads as 'Edit, button, collapsed'. The visible text (the value) is not part of the name, which breaks WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name. This is tested on purpose: spec:1266-1274 asserts the aria-label equals a11y.edit. The JSDoc of ariaLabel (inline-edit.ts:259-262) assumes the projected value is still conveyed, and it is not. What a screen reader reads in browse mode could vary and was not checked with real AT.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/inline-edit/aria-label-hides-value.spec.ts`](defect-repros/inline-edit/aria-label-hides-value.spec.ts)
  Fix: Name the view button from the label and the value (aria-labelledby on the kbq-label and the view content), and keep 'Edit' as the description or role hint instead of the whole name.
- **[inline-edit] With getValueHandler bound and no setValueHandler, cancel and rollback() restore nothing, unless the getter returns an array, whose items are then written into the form fields by index** — reproduced.
  initialValue = getValue() (inline-edit.ts:581), which returns getValueHandler() when one is bound (1150-1153). Without setValueHandler, setValue (1166-1192) returns unless the value is an array (1177). If it is an array, it writes value\[index\] into formFieldRefList\[index\] (1179-1191). cancel() (830-842) and rollback() (513-525) both go through this path. Repro, scalar getter: Escape emits canceled and switches to view, but the bound FormControl keeps 'Edited'. The control test without handlers restores 'Initial'. So a cancelled edit stays in the consumer's model. Repro, array getter on a multi-select (getValueHandler = () => control.value): cancel() writes the string 'A' into the multi-select's FormControl and throws 'Value must be an array in multiple-selection mode.' (select.component.ts:2313-2315). The editor stays open with a corrupted value. Not run, from source: an array longer than the number of fields reaches coerceControl(undefined) and throws a TypeError (1180, 1194-1199). The docs examples always bind both handlers (inline-edit-custom-handler-example.ts:25-26), but neither JSDoc requires that.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/inline-edit/getter-without-setter.spec.ts`](defect-repros/inline-edit/getter-without-setter.spec.ts)
  Fix: When setValueHandler is missing, restore from a per-field snapshot taken independently of getValueHandler, or require both handlers with a dev-mode error.
- **[inline-edit] In the select-style editor without commit() bound to selectionChange, picking an option closes only the options panel; the editor stays open with its field hidden and focus probably on the body** — reproduced.
  A single select makes isSingleSelect() true (inline-edit.ts:331-335). The panel then gets kbq-inline-edit\_\_panel\_select (inline-edit.html:65), and inline-edit.scss:202-204 sets display:none on its control container. Picking an option closes the select and calls focus() on the select host (select.component.ts:2473-2476, 1772-1774), which is now hidden. While open, the select subscribes to the outside pointer events of its own overlay (select.component.ts:1736, 2053-2060). The CDK OverlayOutsideClickDispatcher walks overlays from the top and stops at the first one that contains the target (@angular/cdk \_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:510-518), so onOverlayOutsideClick (inline-edit.ts:903-910) never runs. Repro: a pointer click on an option updates the value and sets select.panelOpen to false. inlineEdit.mode() stays 'edit', saved is not emitted, and the select-style edit panel stays attached. With (selectionChange)='inlineEdit.commit()' bound, the same click returns to view mode. Not shown in jsdom, because it focuses display:none elements: focus falling to the body. The user can still recover with a click outside (save) or Escape (cancel). The docs never say that binding commit is required (design finding 6).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/inline-edit/select-without-commit.spec.ts`](defect-repros/inline-edit/select-without-commit.spec.ts)
  Fix: In select-style mode, subscribe to the select's user-driven selectionChange and commit, or at least document the required binding.
- **[input] Negative values are never grouped under a startFormattingFrom threshold** — reproduced.
  input-number.ts:656-660 sets useGrouping = intPart >= Math.pow(10, startFormattingFrom) on the signed integer part, so every negative number fails the check. ru-RU ships startFormattingFrom: 4 (core/locales/formatters.ts:141). Repro (ru-RU, locale service): FormControl(12000) renders '12\<thin space>000' but FormControl(-12000) renders '-12000', and typing '-12345' stays '-12345' with model -12345. Without a threshold (en-US) grouping is unaffected.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/input/negative-not-grouped.spec.ts`](defect-repros/input/negative-not-grouped.spec.ts)
  Fix: Compare Math.abs(intPart) against the threshold in createLocalizedNumberFromParts.
- **[input] Keyboard filter blocks Shift+digit, most Ctrl/Cmd shortcuts, and Page Up/Down** — reproduced.
  input-number.ts:418-429 allows only Home/End/Left/Right/Delete/Backspace/Tab/Esc/Enter, minuses, Ctrl/Cmd+A/C/V/X/Z, F1-F12 and separators. Everything else reaches :431-435, where `event.shiftKey || isLetter` calls preventDefault: Shift with a digit keyCode (49) is blocked, and so are Ctrl+Y, Ctrl+F, Cmd+R (keyCode is not a digit, so isLetter) and PAGE\_UP/PAGE\_DOWN (33/34). Repro: keydown ONE with shiftKey -> defaultPrevented true; Ctrl+Y, Ctrl+F, Meta+R, PageUp, PageDown -> true; plain '1' and Ctrl+A -> false. On AZERTY the main-row digits need Shift and report the digit keyCode, so they cannot be typed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/input/keyboard-filter.spec.ts`](defect-repros/input/keyboard-filter.spec.ts)
  Fix: Filter on event.key (digits, separators, minus) instead of keyCode+shiftKey, and let any Ctrl/Meta combination and the other navigation keys through.
- **[input] A minus after digits or a leading fraction separator empties the field and nulls the value** — reproduced.
  input-number.ts:406 blocks a minus only when the view already has one or min >= 0, wherever the caret is. The fraction separator passes through isPeriod at :425. In the deferred reformat, formatViewValue (:620-630) returns null for any text Number() cannot parse ('12-', ','), and setViewValue(null) plus viewToModelUpdate(null) (:474-478) clear the field and the model. Repro (ru-RU): value 12, DASH keydown not prevented, then '12-' gives element '' and control null. In an empty field the COMMA keydown is not prevented and ',' is wiped to ''.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/input/stray-separator-empties.spec.ts`](defect-repros/input/stray-separator-empties.spec.ts)
  Fix: Block a minus unless the caret is at position 0, and keep the previous text/value (or render '0,') when the reformat cannot parse an in-progress entry instead of nulling it.
- **[input] A paste replaces the whole text regardless of caret or selection** — reproduced.
  onPaste (input-number.ts:484-500) parses only the clipboard text into valueFromPaste and does not cancel a valid paste. The browser then inserts it at the caret, and the deferred onInput (:460-465) renders formatNumber(this.valueFromPaste) for inputType 'insertFromPaste', discarding the merged text. Repro: value 12, paste '3' not prevented, element '123' after insertion, then '3' and control 3 after the reformat. Typing at the same spot keeps the rest of the text.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/input/paste-replaces-text.spec.ts`](defect-repros/input/paste-replaces-text.spec.ts)
  Fix: On insertFromPaste, parse the merged view value (or splice the clipboard text into the selection) instead of rendering the clipboard number alone.
- **[input] Overriding groupSeparator without viewGroupSeparator renders a separator the parser does not accept** — reproduced.
  createLocalizedNumberFromParts (input-number.ts:662-676) formats the integer part with Intl.NumberFormat for the active locale id. formatNumberWithLocale (core/formatters/number/formatter.ts:73-75) swaps the group separator only when viewGroupSeparator is set, so the rendered group separator stays Intl's ','. Parsing uses the overridden groupSeparator \['.'\] (normalizeNumber, locale-service.ts:324-338). Repro: en-US plus the override {groupSeparator: \['.'\], fractionSeparator: ','} renders 1234.5 as '1,234,5'. The next keystroke ('1,234,56') cannot be parsed, so the element becomes '' and the control null.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/input/group-override-render.spec.ts`](defect-repros/input/group-override-render.spec.ts)
  Fix: Render with groupSeparator\[0\] when viewGroupSeparator is absent (or derive one from the other), so rendering and parsing always use the same separators.
- **[input] Keystrokes or arrow keys arriving before the deferred reformat settles use stale caret/value** — confirmed in the source.
  onInput (input-number.ts:450-482) records currentValueLength and previousSelectionStart at the keystroke and reformats in setTimeout(0). The caret correction then runs in a microtask with those stale numbers (:552-566). A second keystroke landing before the timer is already in the view when the first correction moves the caret, so later digits go in at the wrong place. e2e.playwright-spec.ts:89-95 records this as INP-BUG-06 ('1234567' renders as '1 234 675' on a loaded runner). stepUp/stepDown (:505, :517) read the committed this.value, which is updated only inside the deferred callback (:477 -> :609-617). An arrow press in that window steps from the old value, the step overwrites the view, and the typed digit is lost. Real timing, so jsdom cannot show it faithfully.
  Fix: Reformat synchronously in the input handler (or flush the pending reformat at the start of the next keydown/input) and derive the caret correction from the live selection.
- **[link] \[tabIndex\]="null", undefined or a non-numeric string becomes NaN: tabindex="NaN", and a pseudo \<span> loses its tab stop, its button role and Enter/Space activation** — reproduced.
  packages/components/link/link.ts:195-197 declares the input with `transform: numberAttribute` and no fallback. Angular's numberAttribute(value, fallback = NaN) returns NaN for null, undefined and 'abc'. hostTabIndex (link.ts:265-269) then returns NaN, and `\[attr.tabindex\]` (link.ts:135) writes "NaN". actsAsButton (link.ts:249) requires `tabIndex() >= 0`, and NaN >= 0 is false, so role() (link.ts:252) gives null and activate() (link.ts:322) returns early. The repro, for null, undefined and 'abc' on `\<span kbqLink \[tabIndex\]=...>`, shows tabindex="NaN", no role, Enter and Space not prevented and 0 clicks. The control case with \[tabIndex\]="0" gives role=button and 2 clicks. Browsers ignore an invalid tabindex, so the span is not focusable either. The same library already guards this case in icon-button.component.ts:99-108 ('numberAttribute gives NaN ... renders no tabindex'). `null` is the usual template idiom for 'leave unset' (`cond ? -1 : null`).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/link/nan-tabindex.spec.ts`](defect-repros/link/nan-tabindex.spec.ts)
  Fix: Use a transform that falls back to the default, e.g. `(value) => numberAttribute(value, 0)` (or map null/undefined to 0 the way icon-button does).
- **[list] Ctrl/Cmd + arrow moves the active option without changing the selection, but still emits selectionChange for the new active option and reports the value to the form control** — reproduced.
  list-selection.component.ts:1192-1193 moves the active option. Because Down is a navigation key, 1213-1223 then calls setSelectedOptionsByKey(activeItem, false, ctrl=true). The ctrl branch at 1008-1011 only checks canDeselectLast and changes nothing, yet 1017-1019 still runs emitChangeEvent + reportValueChange. Repro (multiple="keyboard" with a FormControl holding \['a'\], Ctrl+Down from 'a'): the active index becomes 1 and the selection stays \[true,false,false\]. selectionChange still fires once, for 'b' with selected=false, valueChanges emits \['a'\], and the control becomes dirty. The test passed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/list/ctrl-arrow-emits-change.spec.ts`](defect-repros/list/ctrl-arrow-emits-change.spec.ts)
  Fix: In setSelectedOptionsByKey, skip emitChangeEvent/reportValueChange on the ctrl branch (emit only when shift or autoSelect actually changed the selection).
- **[list] With autoSelect off and noUnselectLast on, a plain click still deselects the only selected option, while Space, Enter and Ctrl+click refuse to** — reproduced.
  setSelectedOptionsByClick has no canDeselectLast guard on the plain (autoSelect=false) branch: list-selection.component.ts:997-998 calls selectionModel.toggle(option) directly. The ctrl branch (988-993) and toggleFocusedOption (1120) are both guarded. noUnselectLast defaults to true outside checkbox mode (300-302). The list-action-button example (list-action-button-example.ts:16) is single selection with \[autoSelect\]="false". Repro: Space and Ctrl+click on the only selected option leave it selected, then a plain click deselects it. The selectionModel becomes empty and the control value is \[\]. The test passed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/list/click-ignores-no-unselect-last.spec.ts`](defect-repros/list/click-ignores-no-unselect-last.spec.ts)
  Fix: Guard the plain-toggle branch of setSelectedOptionsByClick with canDeselectLast(option), as the ctrl branch is.
- **[list] Lists connected by id get kbq-list-option\_drop-forbidden / no-drop cursor while hovered although they accept the drop, and a drop there reports the source previousIndex as currentIndex** — reproduced.
  resolveConnectedLists (list-selection.component.ts:1475-1477) drops string ids, so onOptionDragMoved (1489-1503) never treats an id-connected list as hovered. It toggles DROP\_FORBIDDEN\_CLASS on, which list.scss:118-120 turns into cursor: no-drop. CDK still resolves the id (connectedTo = \['target-list'\]) and accepts the drop. In the target's dropped handler (1394-1414), dropIndex was never set, so currentIndex = target.dropIndex ?? previousIndex (1403) is the source index. CDK's own currentIndex is discarded. Repro: drag 'source 1' (previousIndex 1) over the top of an id-connected target at x 250, y 5. The dragged option gets the forbidden class, and the target's dropped event reports currentIndex 1 where index 0 is correct. The test passed. The cursor itself is CSS and was not observed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/list/id-connected-drop.spec.ts`](defect-repros/list/id-connected-drop.spec.ts)
  Fix: Resolve string ids to KbqListSelection instances (e.g. via CdkDropList registry / dropList.data) for hover and indicator, or fall back to CDK's currentIndex instead of previousIndex for a cross-container drop.
- **[loader-overlay] The overlay gives assistive technology no signal (no aria-busy, no live region, no role or name on the spinner); covered content stays focusable and in the accessibility tree** — reproduced.
  packages/components/loader-overlay/loader-overlay.component.ts:58-68: the host binds only classes, with no role, aria-live or aria-busy. Lines 148-154: ngOnInit only adds `kbq-loader-overlay\_parent` to the parent. It sets no aria-busy, inert or aria-hidden on the parent or its content. loader-overlay.component.html:5 renders `\<kbq-progress-spinner>` without a role or a label. progress-spinner.component.ts:52-56: the spinner host binds only classes and the id, with no role=progressbar and no aria-\*. loader-overlay.scss:22-36: the overlay is only an absolutely positioned layer, so it blocks the pointer and nothing else. loader-overlay.component.spec.ts has no toHaveNoViolations or aria assertions. The repro passes: with the overlay shown, the parent has no aria-busy, and the overlay and spinner carry no role, aria-live or aria-label. The covered button and input have tabIndex 0, are not inert or aria-hidden, and receive focus via .focus() (document.activeElement).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/loader-overlay/no-a11y-signal.spec.ts`](defect-repros/loader-overlay/no-a11y-signal.spec.ts)
  Fix: Set aria-busy on the parent while the overlay is shown (restore it on destroy), make the covered content inert, and give the default indicator role="status" or role="progressbar" with a localized name from kbqInjectA11yLocaleConfiguration().
- **[loader-overlay] Two overlays in one parent: destroying either removes kbq-loader-overlay\_parent while the other is shown; same for a consumer-applied class** — reproduced.
  loader-overlay.component.ts:148-160: each instance adds the class to its parentElement in ngOnInit and calls removeClass unconditionally in ngOnDestroy. There is no reference count, and it never checks whether the class was already there. The repro passes. With two @if-wrapped overlays in one div, hiding the first leaves the second in the DOM but removes `kbq-loader-overlay\_parent` from the div. That drops `position: relative` (loader-overlay.scss:6-8), so the remaining absolutely positioned overlay loses its containing block. In a div that already has the class in its own template, destroying the overlay strips the consumer's class too.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/loader-overlay/shared-parent-class.spec.ts`](defect-repros/loader-overlay/shared-parent-class.spec.ts)
  Fix: Only remove the class if this instance added it and no other overlay remains in the parent (for example, check parent.querySelector(':scope > .kbq-loader-overlay') or keep a per-parent counter).
- **[loader-overlay] .kbq-loader-overlay\_parent { position: relative } has one-class specificity and can override an absolute/fixed/sticky parent** — confirmed in the source.
  loader-overlay.scss:6-8 declares `.kbq-loader-overlay\_parent { position: relative; }` with specificity (0,1,0). The component uses ViewEncapsulation.None (loader-overlay.component.ts:57). The rule lives only in the component stylesheet: it is not in the global theme mixins (\_loader-overlay-theme.scss) or any other global SCSS. Angular appends component styles to \<head> as \<style> elements on the first render, after the global stylesheet \<link>s. A consumer parent positioned by one class in global CSS (`.sidebar { position: fixed }`), or by `:host` (compiled to \[\_nghost-x\], also 0,1,0) in a stylesheet loaded earlier, therefore loses on equal specificity and turns relative while the overlay is shown, which moves it in the layout. jsdom does not apply cascade or specificity reliably, so this cannot be shown in a spec.
  Fix: Lower the rule to zero specificity and apply it only when the parent is statically positioned, for example `:where(.kbq-loader-overlay\_parent) { position: relative }` (still overridable), or set position inline only when getComputedStyle(parent).position === 'static'.
- **[markdown] KbqMarkdown imported on its own, without KbqMarkdownModule, cannot start (no provider for KbqMarkdownService)** — reproduced.
  markdown.service.ts:10 declares `@Injectable()` with no `providedIn`. The only provider is markdown.module.ts:11. markdown.component.ts:42 provides only the scrollbar options, and markdown.component.ts:51 calls `inject(KbqMarkdownService)`. In the repro, a host that imports only `KbqMarkdown` throws an error naming KbqMarkdownService (NG0201) when it is created. The same template rendered through `KbqMarkdownModule` produces the h1. Every example and test imports the module, which is why this was never noticed. I rated it P2, not P1, because it fails loudly at once and importing the module works around it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/markdown/standalone-missing-provider.spec.ts`](defect-repros/markdown/standalone-missing-provider.spec.ts)
  Fix: Make KbqMarkdownService `@Injectable({ providedIn: 'root' })`, or add it to the component's own `providers`, and keep the module provider for compatibility.
- **[markdown] Inserting the alias class before an existing class leaves two class attributes; code blocks lose language-\*, raw HTML elements lose their own classes** — reproduced.
  markdown.service.ts:27-33 inserts `class="kbq-markdown\_\_\<tag>"` right after the tag name and never checks for an existing class. The repro shows `parseToHtml` producing `\<code class="kbq-markdown\_\_code" class="language-bash">` for a ```bash fence. In the DOM, the code element's class is just `kbq-markdown\_\_code` because the HTML parser keeps the first duplicate. A raw `\<a class="custom-link" href="#anchor">` renders with only `kbq-markdown\_\_a`. The existing snapshot already shows the lost `language-bash` (\_\_snapshots\_\_/markdown.component.spec.ts.snap:471-473). Syntax highlighters that look for `language-\*`, and consumer classes on raw HTML, stop working.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/markdown/tag-aliasing.spec.ts`](defect-repros/markdown/tag-aliasing.spec.ts)
  Fix: Merge the alias into an existing class attribute, for example by adding classes through a marked renderer or hook, or by a DOM pass, instead of regex-inserting a second attribute.
- **[markdown] async: true (or an async marked extension) makes marked.parse return a Promise and rendering throws a TypeError** — reproduced.
  markdown.service.ts:21 casts `\<string>marked.parse(markdown, options)`. marked.d.ts (the Marked.parse overloads around lines 632-643) returns `Promise\<string>` when `async: true`, and `async` is part of `MarkedOptions`, which `kbqMarkdownMarkedOptionsProvider` accepts (markdown.component.ts:27). `transform` then calls `.replace` on the Promise (markdown.service.ts:28), so in the repro `service.parseToHtml('# Title', { async: true })` throws a TypeError. The component calls this from a `computed` during change detection (markdown.component.ts:67-71, 98), so nothing renders. A global `marked.use({ async: true })` or an async extension has the same effect (see the global-instance defect).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/markdown/marked-parse-options.spec.ts`](defect-repros/markdown/marked-parse-options.spec.ts)
  Fix: Pass `async: false` explicitly, or reject or omit `async` in the options type, and use a private Marked instance so global async extensions cannot switch it.
- **[markdown] An ordered list starting at another number (start="3") still displays from 1. because the CSS counter ignores start** — confirmed in the source.
  marked's list renderer emits `start="n"` when `n !== 1` (node\_modules/marked/lib/marked.esm.js, list(): `t&&n!==1?' start="'+n+'"'`), and the service only adds the class. markdown.scss:67-82 hides the native markers (`list-style: none`), puts `counter-reset: li` on `.kbq-markdown\_\_ol`, and draws `li:before { content: counter(li) '.'; counter-increment: li }`. The HTML `start` attribute seeds only the built-in `list-item` counter, not the custom `li` counter, so a list that starts at `3.` shows 1., 2., 3. This is common when a list is continued after a code block. jsdom does not render ::before counters, so the repro tests could not show it.
  Fix: Draw the marker from the built-in counter (`content: counter(list-item) '.'`, with the custom counter-reset and counter-increment removed) so `start` and `reversed` are respected.
- **[markdown] Projected content is read only in the browser, so SSR/prerendered pages carry an empty output for projected Markdown** — confirmed in the source.
  markdown.component.ts:61 starts `projectedText` at null. The only writer, `readProjectedText`, is called only inside `afterNextRender` (markdown.component.ts:74-85), which Angular never runs on the server. On the server, `resultHtml` (markdown.component.ts:67-71) is therefore null and `.kbq-markdown\_\_output` is empty, while the source `\<pre>` is `display: none` (markdown.scss:7-9). Prerendered or SSR HTML shows nothing for projected Markdown until the client bootstraps, which also hurts indexing. All markdown docs examples project their content (packages/docs-examples/components/markdown/\*/\*-example.html), so the docs site's prerendered examples are affected. The `markdownText` path is not affected.
  Fix: Also read the projected element's textContent in a hook that runs on the server, and keep the ContentObserver subscription browser-only.
- **[modal] A declarative dialog is registered once, on init, and unregistered when it first finishes closing; later openings are missing from openModals, closeAll() and afterAllClose** — reproduced.
  modal.component.ts:503 calls modalControl.registerModal(this) only in ngOnInit. modal-control.service.ts:75-84 subscribes afterOpen (push into openModals) and afterClose (removeOpenModal). removeOpenModal (:162-174) always calls unregister (:176-186), which unsubscribes both and deletes the map entry. Nothing registers the dialog again, so the second opening's afterOpen pushes nothing, closeAll() (:126-132) iterates an empty openModals, and the second close never reaches afterAllClose.next(). Repro: open and close through \[(kbqVisible)\], which is tracked. Reopen it: openModals.length is 0, closeAll() leaves kbqVisible true, and closing it does not emit afterAllClose again. The existing spec only covers a declarative dialog that is shown from its first render.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/modal/declarative-reregister.spec.ts`](defect-repros/modal/declarative-reregister.spec.ts)
  Fix: Register in the open flow (handleVisibleStateChange(true)) rather than once in ngOnInit, or keep the registration until destroy and only drop the openModals entry on close.
- **[modal] closeAll() closes only the dialogs in openModals, so a dialog still in its opening animation stays open** — reproduced.
  openModals is filled only from the afterOpen subscription (modal-control.service.ts:77), and afterOpen emits after the 300 ms enter animation (modal.component.ts:804-810). closeAll() (:126-132) walks openModals only. Repro: create(), call closeAll() 100 ms later. After the animation kbqVisible is still true and the dialog is then in openModals.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/modal/closeall-opening.spec.ts`](defect-repros/modal/closeall-opening.spec.ts)
  Fix: Have closeAll() close visibleModals() (updated synchronously in setVisible), or the registered dialogs that are visible.
- **[modal] kbqVisible: true passed to the service turns open() into a no-op: no afterOpen, no registry, no scroll lock, no inert page, and the dialog is made inert itself over another shown dialog** — reproduced.
  The model is seeded from the options (modal.component.ts:179). ModalBuilderForService calls open() (modal.service.ts:37), and changeVisibleFromInside returns early when kbqVisible() === visible (modal.component.ts:916), so handleVisibleStateChange never runs: no captureFocus, setVisible, changeBodyOverflow or animateTo, and afterOpen is never emitted. A portal-created component gets no ngOnChanges, so syncVisibleState never runs either. ngAfterViewInit (:524-527) still focuses it, and `hidden` (:328-330) is false, so it is painted. The inert getter (:376-380) compares with topVisibleModal(), which never contains it. Repro: afterOpen spy not called, openModals.length 0, body overflow '' and the page not inert, while the wrap is displayed and the focus is inside. Over a normally opened dialog, the new dialog's container sits under \[inert\] and the one below does not. ModalOptions.kbqVisible (modal.type.ts:87) is public, though its JSDoc says the service manages it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/modal/kbqvisible-option.spec.ts`](defect-repros/modal/kbqvisible-option.spec.ts)
  Fix: Ignore options.kbqVisible on the service path (seed the model with false when KBQ\_MODAL\_OPTIONS is present), or drop kbqVisible from the service options type.
- **[modal] A promise from kbqOkClick or kbqCancelClick that rejects closes the dialog, with the rejection reason as the close result** — reproduced.
  modal.component.ts:741-746: `(result as Promise\<void>).then(handleThen).catch(handleThen)`. The same handler serves success and failure, so a rejection reaches caseClose(reason) (:715). canClose (:704) is `doClose !== false`, so any reason but `false` calls close(reason). Repro: kbqOkClick: () => Promise.reject(new Error('save failed')). After triggerOk() kbqVisible is false and afterClose emits that Error. The same happens for kbqCancelClick with a string reason. A failed async save therefore closes the dialog, and the user's input is lost.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/modal/rejected-handler.spec.ts`](defect-repros/modal/rejected-handler.spec.ts)
  Fix: Give the rejection its own handler that only clears the progress state and keeps the dialog open (optionally rethrowing or reporting the error).
- **[modal] A dialog closed during its opening animation still settles the opening: afterOpen emits after beforeClose, it enters openModals until it finishes closing, and the closing animation is cut short** — reproduced.
  Every animateTo call (modal.component.ts:962-1010) starts its own timer and promise, and nothing cancels the previous one. The enter timer's finish() calls changeAnimationState(null) (:997) and resolves. Then the open branch of handleVisibleStateChange emits afterOpen (:808-810) without checking that the dialog is still visible, and the registry pushes it into openModals (modal-control.service.ts:77). Because animationState is null and kbqVisible is false, `hidden` (:328-330) becomes true and the leave animation is cut off. Repro: close() at t=100. At t=300 the events are \['beforeClose','afterOpen'\], the dialog is in openModals, the container has lost zoom-leave and the wrap is display:none, although the leave timer only settles at t=400, when afterClose emits and the entry is removed. A real browser can also finish the enter promise early through the container's animationend listener, but the ordering bug is the same.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/modal/close-during-opening.spec.ts`](defect-repros/modal/close-during-opening.spec.ts)
  Fix: Cancel the pending animation (clear its timer and listener, reject or ignore its promise) when a new animateTo starts, and skip afterOpen when the dialog is no longer visible.
- **[modal] The progress state of an array footer button is cleared on a plain object once the promise settles, with nothing marking the dialog for check; under zoneless OnPush the spinner stays** — reproduced.
  onButtonClick (modal.component.ts:770-779) sets button.loading = true, then sets it to false in then/catch on the formatted plain object that the footer() computed (:336-340, :1012-1026) returns. It never calls markForCheck. The template reads it through getButtonCallableProp(button, 'loading') (modal.component.html:148) inside the OnPush dialog. The click listener renders the spinner, but promise settlement schedules nothing. Under the suite's exhaustive checkNoChanges, the first unrelated render after resolve failed with NG0100 on `kbq-progress` (true -> false) in KbqModalComponent, which is the check's own signal for this defect. With exhaustive off (as in an application), the repro shows that after resolve and ApplicationRef.tick() the button still has kbq-progress. Only modalRef.markForCheck() + tick removes it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/modal/footer-button-loading.spec.ts`](defect-repros/modal/footer-button-loading.spec.ts)
  Fix: Call this.changeDetector.markForCheck() after setting button.loading in both branches, or keep per-button loading in a signal.
- **[modal] The scroll lock removes the body's inline overflow when the last dialog closes, instead of restoring a value the page had set before** — reproduced.
  changeBodyOverflow (modal.component.ts:1054-1060) sets overflow:hidden while any dialog is visible and otherwise calls renderer.removeStyle(body, 'overflow'). It never records the previous inline value. No other koobiq component writes body overflow (grep), so this hits pages or third-party scroll locks that set body.style.overflow. Repro: body.style.overflow = 'scroll'. During the dialog it is 'hidden'; after close it is '' rather than 'scroll'.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/modal/body-overflow-restore.spec.ts`](defect-repros/modal/body-overflow-restore.spec.ts)
  Fix: Store the body's inline overflow when the first dialog locks it and restore that value when the last one unlocks.
- **[modal] triggerOk(), triggerCancel() and the predefined buttons run the handler or emit kbqOnOk/kbqOnCancel even when the dialog is already closed or closing** — reproduced.
  onClickOkCancel / handleCloseResult (modal.component.ts:703-750) have no visibility check. triggerOk/triggerCancel (:577-583) and the predefined OK and Cancel buttons (modal.component.html:160-173, 196-209) call them directly. Only onClickCloseBtn (:646-650), onClickMask (:658-664) and Escape (:682) check kbqVisible(). Repro: after the first OK click closes the dialog, a second OK click, a Cancel click, triggerOk() and triggerCancel() during the leave animation call the handlers again (ok x3, cancel x2). The close button and Escape are correctly ignored, and triggerOk() after the dialog is gone still calls the handler. In a real browser `.zoom-leave-active` sets pointer-events:none (\_modal-animation.scss:27-31), which mostly blocks a mouse double-click. Keyboard activation of the still-focused OK button and the programmatic API are not blocked, so a handler can run twice (double submit). The existing test "should trigger kbqOkClick/kbqCancelClick" depends on this.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/modal/trigger-after-close.spec.ts`](defect-repros/modal/trigger-after-close.spec.ts)
  Fix: Return early from onClickOkCancel when !kbqVisible() (and optionally while a handler's promise is pending).
- **[navbar] Vertical navbar passes typed characters of a kbqInput field to the typeahead and steals focus** — reproduced.
  vertical-navbar.component.ts:186-196: only TAB and DOWN/UP are handled, and every other key (from any target, kbqInput included) goes to keyManager.onKeydown. navbar.component.ts:113 turns on withTypeAhead() (200 ms). list-key-manager.ts:302-308 sends any one-character key to letterKeyStream, and after the debounce (151-192) it calls setActiveItem. That leads to focus-key-manager.ts:27 activeItem.focus('keyboard') and then navbar-item.component.ts:327-329 focusMonitor.focusVia, which takes DOM focus away from the field. The horizontal navbar (navbar.component.ts:399-401) skips the key manager for kbqInput targets. Repro: 's' typed into a kbqInput inside a kbq-vertical-navbar is not prevented. After 200 ms document.activeElement is the 'Settings' item.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/navbar/vertical-typeahead-input.spec.ts`](defect-repros/navbar/vertical-typeahead-input.spec.ts)
  Fix: Skip keyManager.onKeydown in KbqVerticalNavbar.onKeyDown when the event comes from an editable field, as the horizontal navbar does (ideally any input/textarea/contenteditable, not only kbqInput).
- **[navbar] Navigation and editing keys are prevented in a plain input inside a navbar** — reproduced.
  navbar.component.ts:387-389 and vertical-navbar.component.ts:179-184 call preventDefault for isVerticalMovement or isHorizontalMovement (keycodes.ts:161-167: arrows, PageUp/PageDown, Home, End, Backspace, Delete) unless the target has the 'kbqinput' attribute (navbar.component.ts:404-406). Repro: in both navbars, a plain \<input value="abc"> with the cursor mid-text gets defaultPrevented=true for BACKSPACE, DELETE, LEFT, RIGHT, HOME and END. A kbqInput field in the same navbar keeps the default (control assertion). Related and not reproduced: in the horizontal navbar such an input also feeds Left/Right and letters to the key manager (navbar.component.ts:399-401), which moves focus to other items. Enter/Space typed into a plain input inside a bare \<kbq-navbar-item> click the item and are prevented (navbar-item.component.ts:629-640, 762-769, no target check).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/navbar/plain-input-keys-prevented.spec.ts`](defect-repros/navbar/plain-input-keys-prevented.spec.ts)
  Fix: Detect editable targets generically (input, textarea, select, contenteditable) instead of the kbqinput attribute, and leave their keys alone.
- **[navbar] Clicking into a field inside an item leaves the previous item active** — reproduced.
  The active item is updated only from the item host's own (focus) listener (navbar-item.component.ts:171, 305-315 -> onFocus -> navbar.component.ts:209-216 updateActiveItem). focus does not bubble, so focusing the nested input never reaches it. The blur of the previous item does not reset the active item, because hasFocusedItem() (navbar.component.ts:225-227) is true through the field item's hasFocus (navbar-item.component.ts:210-212, form-field.ts:340-342). Right/Left at the field edges then call setNextItemActive/setPreviousItemActive relative to the stale index (navbar.component.ts:395-398). Repro: items \[First, Second, field, Last\]. Click First, then click into the field: activeItemIndex stays 0. Right in the empty field focuses 'Second' instead of 'Last'.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/navbar/field-focus-stale-active-item.spec.ts`](defect-repros/navbar/field-focus-stale-active-item.spec.ts)
  Fix: Update the active item on focusin of the item (or when the nested element reports focus), not only on focus of the item host.
- **[navbar] The \[kbq-navbar-container\] attribute form gets no kbq-navbar-container class** — reproduced.
  navbar.component.ts:230-236: the KbqNavbarContainer selector is the element 'kbq-navbar-container' only. navbar.component.ts:241 and vertical-navbar.component.ts:70 project '\[kbq-navbar-container\], kbq-navbar-container'. All container layout is keyed on the class (navbar.scss:16-29, vertical-navbar.scss .kbq-navbar-container rules). Repro: \<div kbq-navbar-container> is projected as a child of kbq-navbar but has no kbq-navbar-container class, while the element form has it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/navbar/attribute-container-class.spec.ts`](defect-repros/navbar/attribute-container-class.spec.ts)
  Fix: Change the directive selector to 'kbq-navbar-container, \[kbq-navbar-container\]'.
- **[navbar] A brand on a natively interactive element other than \<a> is treated as decorative** — reproduced.
  navbar-brand.component.ts:198: decorative = !isLink && !nestedElement. isLink is tagName === 'A' only (119-121), and nestedElement covers only projected KbqButton/KbqFormField content children (navbar-item.component.ts:192-194), not the host itself. decorative makes the disabled getter true (navbar-item.component.ts:223-225), so the key manager skips it, and items always render tabindex -1 (254-256). Repro: \<button kbq-navbar-brand> between two items has tabindex=-1 and focusable item disabled === true. Right from the first item goes straight to the last item, so the brand is unreachable from the keyboard.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/navbar/button-brand-decorative.spec.ts`](defect-repros/navbar/button-brand-decorative.spec.ts)
  Fix: Treat natively actionable hosts (BUTTON, A\[href\], INPUT, etc., or role/tabindex set by the consumer) as interactive when deciding decorative.
- **[navbar] A disabled item does not cancel clicks or keyboard activation** — reproduced.
  KbqNavbarItem and KbqNavbarFocusableItem register no click listener (navbar-item.component.ts:159-174, 470-480). Only CSS pointer-events:none (\_navbar-theme.scss:74-78) keeps the mouse away. KbqNavbarItem.onKeyDown (navbar-item.component.ts:762-769) clicks on Enter/Space without checking disabled. The brand, unlike the item, cancels clicks with a capturing listener (navbar-brand.component.ts:182-189, 203-208). Repro: element.click() on \<kbq-navbar-item \[disabled\]="true" (click)=...> runs the handler. A click on a disabled \<a kbq-navbar-item href> reaches the bubbling phase with defaultPrevented=false (the link would be followed), while the disabled brand stops it. Enter on a focused disabled item clicks it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/navbar/disabled-item-click.spec.ts`](defect-repros/navbar/disabled-item-click.spec.ts)
  Fix: Add the brand's capture-phase click guard to every focusable item and skip the synthetic Enter/Space click when the item is disabled.
- **[navbar] Only a window resize re-fits the horizontal navbar** — confirmed in the source.
  navbar.component.ts:316-335: the only triggers for updateExpandedStateForItems are fromEvent(window, 'resize') (debounced, 326-328) and a single afterNextRender. No ResizeObserver/SharedResizeObserver watches the host, although the brand uses one (navbar-brand.component.ts:85, 237-240). The re-fit method is @docs-private (352-357), so consumers have no documented way to trigger it. jsdom cannot show this meaningfully: it has no layout and its ResizeObserver never fires, so a spec would still pass after a fix.
  Fix: Observe the navbar host with SharedResizeObserver (debounced, outside the zone) in addition to or instead of window resize.
- **[navbar] The Ctrl+/ shortcut fires from anywhere, ignores Shift/Alt and does not prevent the default** — reproduced.
  navbar-toggle.component.ts:48 adds a window keydown listener. handleKeydown (69-77) checks only event.ctrlKey and keyCode SLASH/NUMPAD\_DIVIDE. It does not check the target (editable fields), shiftKey/altKey/metaKey or defaultPrevented, and it never calls preventDefault. Repro: Ctrl+/ dispatched on a \<textarea> outside the navbar expands it and the event stays defaultPrevented=false. Ctrl+Shift+/ (Ctrl+?) and Ctrl+Alt+/ toggle it too. Editors that stopPropagation on their own bindings (e.g. Monaco) are not affected, but plain fields and other page shortcuts are.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/navbar/toggle-shortcut-anywhere.spec.ts`](defect-repros/navbar/toggle-shortcut-anywhere.spec.ts)
  Fix: Require exactly Ctrl (no Shift/Alt/Meta), ignore events already defaultPrevented or coming from editable targets, and preventDefault once handled.
- **[notification-center] kbqNotificationCenterPlacement does not move the panel** — reproduced.
  notification-center.ts:771-775: the constructor calls updatePlacementPriority(\['right','rightBottom','rightTop'\]). ngOnChanges at 742-744 only writes `this.placement`. pop-up-trigger.ts:796-802 getPrioritizedPositions() returns the priority list whenever one is set, so `placement` is never consulted. Only the popoverMode setter (notification-center.ts:616-623) replaces the priority. `placement` also feeds updateClassMap (828-832), but nothing binds that class map (see the offset defect), so the input has no visible effect at all. Repro: with \[kbqNotificationCenterPlacement\]="'left'", trigger.placement is 'left', but the FlexibleConnectedPositionStrategy holds exactly 3 positions, all originX 'end' / overlayX 'start' (right side), and none on the left side.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/notification-center/placement-ignored.spec.ts`](defect-repros/notification-center/placement-ignored.spec.ts)
  Fix: When a placement is bound (and popoverMode is off), derive the priority from it or clear placementPriority so POSITION\_PRIORITY\_STRATEGY\[placement\] applies, and reposition an open panel.
- **[notification-center] offset (default 8) is never applied** — reproduced.
  notification-center.ts:286-294: on visibleChange(true) the panel calls applyPopupMargins(renderer, host, 'kbq-notification-center', offset). pop-up-styles.ts:292-325 sets a margin only if the host has a `kbq-notification-center\_placement-\*` class. KbqPopUp.updateClassMap (pop-up.ts:178-193) stores that class in `classMap`, but the panel's host bindings (notification-center.ts:156-163) and its template (notification-center.html) never bind `classMap`. Popover binds it with \[class\]="classMap" in popover.component.html:14. Repro: with \[offset\]="20", instance.offset is 20 and instance.classMap contains 'kbq-notification-center\_placement-right', but the panel element has no placement class and all four margin styles are empty. This is a dead public input. The missing default 8px gap is not visible in jsdom, so the visual effect was not observed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/notification-center/offset-not-applied.spec.ts`](defect-repros/notification-center/offset-not-applied.spec.ts)
  Fix: Bind the class map on the panel host (or apply the offset through the position strategy's offsetX/offsetY) so applyPopupMargins finds the placement class.
- **[notification-center] kbqNotificationCenterPanelClass with several classes throws, and a change after the first open is ignored** — reproduced.
  notification-center.ts:700-708: overlayConfig passes `\[defaultPanelClass, panelClass\]` with the raw string. CDK OverlayRef.attach (node\_modules/@angular/cdk/fesm2022/\_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:799-800) calls \_toggleClasses, which calls element.classList.add(...classes) (line 1021-1025). A token that contains a space throws there. The config is read only once: createOverlay (pop-up-trigger.ts:548-551) returns the existing overlayRef, and attach re-applies `\_config.panelClass` every time. Repro 1: panelClass 'first second', then trigger.show() throws a DOMException named 'InvalidCharacterError' and the panel never opens. Repro 2: open with 'first', close, rebind to 'second', reopen. The pane still has 'first' and never gets 'second'.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/notification-center/panel-class-tokens.spec.ts`](defect-repros/notification-center/panel-class-tokens.spec.ts)
  Fix: Split the input on whitespace before building panelClass, and on change call overlayRef.removePanelClass/addPanelClass instead of relying on the creation-time config.
- **[notification-center] Every close moves focus to the trigger, including outside click and outside scroll** — reproduced.
  notification-center.ts:777-785: ngAfterContentInit calls this.focus() on every visibleChange(false), whatever the reason for the close. closingActions (835-845) includes overlayRef.outsidePointerEvents() and any outside ScrollDispatcher scroll. pop-up-trigger.ts:850-862 hides on them. pop-up-trigger.ts:729-731 focus() focuses the host. Nothing checks where focus currently is, unlike CDK dialog's restore, which acts only when focus is on body or inside the dialog. The panel has no backdrop, so clicking elsewhere is a normal action. Repro: open the panel, then pointerdown, focus and click on an \<input> outside it. After the timers run the panel is closed and document.activeElement is the trigger button, not the input.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/notification-center/close-steals-focus.spec.ts`](defect-repros/notification-center/close-steals-focus.spec.ts)
  Fix: Restore focus only when the active element is inside the panel or is body (or only for Escape and the close button), not after an outside click or scroll.
- **[overflow-items] Container borders are not subtracted from the available space** — reproduced.
