  packages/components/overflow-items/overflow-items.ts:218 and :234 read the size with getBoundingClientRect(), which is the border-box size including borders. :363-366 subtract only paddingStart/paddingEnd (:219-220, :235-236). Border widths are never read, so the available space is too large by border-start + border-end. Repro (layout stubbed to the inline widths): a 102px border-box container with a 1px border on each side (100px content box) and three 34px items (102px) hides nothing. The control has 1px padding on each side instead of the border, and one item is hidden. The overshoot is limited to the border widths. overflow:hidden clips at the padding box, so the item is only visibly clipped when padding-end is smaller than the borders. In the docs examples the 1px border plus xxs padding means the last item eats into the padding.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/overflow-items/border-not-subtracted.spec.ts`](defect-repros/overflow-items/border-not-subtracted.spec.ts)
  Fix: Also subtract borderLeft/RightWidth (borderTop/BottomWidth when vertical) from computedStyle, or measure clientWidth/clientHeight minus padding.
- **[overflow-items] CSS gap between items is not counted** — reproduced.
  overflow-items.ts:221-228 and :237-244: itemSize is rect width (or height) plus margins. :367 sums item sizes, and gap, column-gap and row-gap are never read. Repro (layout stubbed): a 100px container with column-gap 10px and three 30px items (110px in use) hides nothing. The control uses 10px margin-right instead of the gap and hides 'C'. The overflow grows by (visible items - 1) x gap. No in-repo consumer puts a gap on the overflow container: the docs and top-bar examples use layout-margin-right-xs, and the breadcrumbs gap sits on the host and the list, not on .kbq-overflow-items.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/overflow-items/gap-not-counted.spec.ts`](defect-repros/overflow-items/gap-not-counted.spec.ts)
  Fix: Read column-gap (row-gap when vertical) from the container's computed style and add it once between every pair of visible items, the result element included.
- **[overflow-items] Changing wrap, order, alwaysVisible or kbqOverflowItem does not trigger a recalculation** — reproduced.
  overflow-items.ts:270-275: the only triggers are items() (content query, which is structural), reverseOverflowOrder, orientation and resize. wrap (:204) only drives the flex-wrap style (:350-352). order (:109), alwaysVisible (:114) and id (:104) are read only inside a recalculation (:283, :289, :321-322, :361). KbqOverflowItem.hide() checks alwaysVisible only at hide time (:121), and nothing shows an item when the input flips. Repro (layout stubbed, 3x40px in 100px, 'C' hidden): setting alwaysVisible=true on C leaves C hidden (hidden() true, visibility:hidden, hiddenItemIDs {'C'}, no changes emission) until a host resize turns it into {'B'}. Renaming C to 'C2' keeps the stale 'C'. Setting B's order to 10 keeps C hidden, and only a resize hides B. Switching wrap to 'wrap' keeps C hidden.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/overflow-items/stale-after-input-change.spec.ts`](defect-repros/overflow-items/stale-after-input-change.spec.ts)
  Fix: Add wrap and a computed over every item's order/alwaysVisible/id to the merged trigger stream (skip(1) like the others).
- **[overflow-items] Items and the result element are not observed for their own size changes** — confirmed in the source.
  overflow-items.ts:259-268: the ResizeObserver watches only this.element and additionalResizeObserverTargets (document.body by default, :178). Items and the result are never observed. The container's size normally does not depend on its items: it has flex-grow 1 and overflow hidden (:338-340), items do not shrink, and hidden items are absolutely positioned. So an item or result that grows (new text, late content, a web font loading) is not re-measured and gets clipped until something else fires. The exception is a shrink-to-fit or negative-free-space flex context, where the container's own size can follow its content. jsdom has neither ResizeObserver nor layout, so this cannot be shown in a unit test.
  Fix: Also observe each item element and the result element (the items() signal is already available to switchMap over).
- **[overflow-items] \[order\] bound to null, undefined or a non-numeric string becomes NaN and breaks the sort** — reproduced.
  overflow-items.ts:109 `input(null, { transform: numberAttribute })`. The default skips the transform, but any bound value goes through it, and numberAttribute's fallback is NaN. :300 `item.order() ?? index` keeps NaN, because ?? only replaces null and undefined. At :302 the comparator returns NaN, which Array.sort treats as 0, so it stops being consistent. Repro (layout stubbed): A unset, B \[order\]=null/undefined/'abc', C \[order\]=-1, 3x40px in 100px. B.order() is NaN, V8 leaves the order as A, B, C, and C (the lowest order, meant to be hidden last) is hidden first. The control B=1 gives C, A, B and hides B.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/overflow-items/order-null-nan.spec.ts`](defect-repros/overflow-items/order-null-nan.spec.ts)
  Fix: Use a transform that maps null, undefined and NaN to null (e.g. value == null ? null : numberAttribute(value, null)), or treat Number.isNaN(order) as missing in sortItemsByOrder.
- **[overflow-items] show() removes consumer-set inline visibility and position** — reproduced.
  overflow-items.ts:72-76: show() calls renderer.removeStyle for visibility and position unconditionally. :317 calls show() on every item at every recalculation, and :316/:327 hide() and show() on the result. Angular does not write a style binding whose value has not changed, so the consumer's value is lost for good. Repro: an item with \[style.position\]="'relative'" and \[style.visibility\]="'visible'" that was never hidden has both properties at '' after the first recalculation, while the binding still holds 'relative'. Styles set through a class are not affected.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/overflow-items/show-wipes-inline-style.spec.ts`](defect-repros/overflow-items/show-wipes-inline-style.spec.ts)
  Fix: Hide with a class (or save and restore the previous inline values) instead of writing and removing the inline visibility/position.
- **[popover] Leaving a hover-opened panel hides it at once, bypassing kbqLeaveDelay and kbqPopoverPreventClose** — reproduced.
  core/pop-up/pop-up.ts:142-144: when the show timer fires with triggerName 'mouseenter', it calls addEventListenerForHide(). Lines 229-235 then bind a `mouseleave` listener that calls `this.hide(0)` on the panel element. popover.component.ts:156-158 makes that element `.kbq-popover`. KbqPopUp.hide (pop-up.ts:154-172) never checks preventClose or the leave delay; only the trigger's hide()/canHide() do (pop-up-trigger.ts:466-481), and so does the hover hide that waits leaveDelay (488-511). Repro: after a 1000 ms control on the panel, a mouseleave on `.kbq-popover` plus its host closes the panel within 2 ms. A popover with kbqPopoverPreventClose closes the same way, although preventClose held against leaving the trigger. The existing 'kbqTrigger = hover' test (popover.spec.ts:180) dispatches mouseleave only on `kbq-popover-component`, so it never runs this listener. In a browser, the margin gap is inside the host but outside `.kbq-popover`, so moving from the panel back toward the trigger also closes the popover.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/popover/panel-leave-hides-at-once.spec.ts`](defect-repros/popover/panel-leave-hides-at-once.spec.ts)
  Fix: Drop the panel-level zero-delay mouseleave listener for popovers (or route it through trigger.hide()/canHide) so the event-driven hover hide alone owns the delay and preventClose.
- **[popover] A hover-opened confirmation popover emits confirm and stays open** — reproduced.
  popover-confirm.component.ts:207-212: the confirm subscription calls `this.hide()`, which is KbqPopUpTrigger.hide (pop-up-trigger.ts:466-470). Its canHide() (476-481) returns false when triggerName === 'mouseleave' and instance.hovered(). That is exactly the state while the pointer is on the panel, because the trigger's mouseleave listener recorded 'mouseleave' (createListener, 820-830). The close button avoids this by calling close() (popover.component.ts:1113-1117). Repro: hover open, pointer onto the panel, click confirm, then wait 1000 ms. confirm fires once and the panel is still open. A second press fires confirm again. Control: a click-opened confirm closes.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/popover/hover-confirm-stays-open.spec.ts`](defect-repros/popover/hover-confirm-stays-open.spec.ts)
  Fix: Close through `close()` (preventClose-aware, ignores hover state) instead of `hide()` in setupButtonEvents.
- **[popover] A non-click popover with kbqPopoverHasBackdrop ignores backdrop clicks** — reproduced.
  popover.component.ts:1070-1076: closingActionsForClick() returns \[\] unless kbqTrigger includes click, so backdropClick() (1079-1084) is never subscribed. closingActions (1087-1092) is then only the scroll stream. This contradicts the hasBackdrop JSDoc (366-369). Repro: a kbqTrigger="manual" popover with a backdrop, opened through kbqPopoverVisible, stays open after two backdrop clicks. Control: a click popover closes on a backdrop click. The claim is narrower in a real browser. A hover popover still closes through the hover hide once the pointer leaves the trigger and panel (pop-up-trigger.ts:455-461, 488-511). A focus popover closes because a mousedown on the non-focusable backdrop blurs the trigger (blur listener, 667-671). Only manual/program popovers are really trapped, and they expose no output that reports backdrop clicks. The way out is then Escape in the panel, Escape on the focused trigger, or code.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/popover/manual-backdrop-ignores-click.spec.ts`](defect-repros/popover/manual-backdrop-ignores-click.spec.ts)
  Fix: Subscribe to backdropClick() whenever hasBackdrop is set, regardless of the trigger, or fix the JSDoc and expose a backdrop-click output.
- **[progress-bar] Empty aria-label skips the locale fallback: no accessible name without projected text, empty aria-label next to aria-labelledby with it** — reproduced.
  packages/components/progress-bar/progress-bar.component.ts:105 `ariaLabel` is `input\<string | null>(null)` with no normalisation. :126-127 `resolvedAriaLabel = ariaLabel() ?? (text() ? null : locale.progressBar)`, so `''` is kept as is and the fallback is skipped. :124 `resolvedAriaLabelledby` tests truthiness, so `''` still yields the text id. :80-81 write both to the host. Repro run: with `\[aria-label\]="''"` and no text, the host has aria-label="" and no aria-labelledby, and axe reports exactly `aria-progressbar-name`. With a projected text it carries aria-label="" plus aria-labelledby=\<text id>. That case is harmless, because aria-labelledby wins in accname. The input is typed `string | null`, so an empty-string binding passes strict template checks, e.g. an optional label bound as `label ?? ''`. The JSDoc ("when neither is given") promises the fallback.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/progress-bar/empty-aria-label.spec.ts`](defect-repros/progress-bar/empty-aria-label.spec.ts)
  Fix: Treat an empty (or whitespace) aria-label as absent: use `||` instead of `??` in resolvedAriaLabel, so the attribute becomes the locale name, or null when a text is projected.
- **[progress-bar] String value renders an empty bar with aria-valuenow="0" (no number transform, finiteness check does not coerce)** — reproduced.
  progress-bar.component.ts:96 `value = input\<number>(0)` has no transform. :118-122 `Number.isFinite(value) ? clamp : MIN\_PERCENT`: Number.isFinite('30') is false, so percentage is 0. That reaches the width at progress-bar.component.html:15 and aria-valuenow at component.ts:79. Repro run: both a static `value="30"` and a bound string `'30'` give aria-valuenow="0" and width 0%. This is a regression. In the 20.x line (git show 17bc91dd3) the getter was `Math.max(MIN\_PERCENT, Math.min(MAX\_PERCENT, this.value()))`, which coerces '30' to 30. d7b36ea48 introduced the isFinite guard. Triggers: projects without strictTemplates/strictAttributeTypes, and `any`-typed values such as an untyped FormControl or an HTML input value.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/progress-bar/string-value.spec.ts`](defect-repros/progress-bar/string-value.spec.ts)
  Fix: Add `transform: numberAttribute` to the `value` input (or coerce with Number() before the isFinite check) so numeric strings keep working as they did in 20.x.
- **[progress-bar] RTL: indeterminate sweep starts mid-track and leaves to the right** — confirmed in the source.
  progress-bar.scss:71 sets the indeterminate fill to a block of `width: 25%`. In an RTL context, CSS block layout aligns it to the right edge, the same rule that makes the determinate fill (html:15) correctly grow from the right. :57 `transform-origin: top left`. :12-20 keyframes `translateX(-100%) scaleX(1)` to `translateX(400%) scaleX(1.6)`. Nothing mirrors them: no `\[dir=rtl\]` or logical rule exists in progress-bar.scss, \_progress-bar-theme.scss or any other SCSS that mentions progress-bar. I checked the geometry in Playwright's headless Chromium (node -e, no files) on a replica of these rules with a 400px track and linear easing. LTR: the fill goes from -100..0 to off the right edge and is on the track for about 95% of the cycle. RTL: at t=0 it is at 200..300, mid-track; it moves right and is fully off the track from about t=0.35. So in RTL each iteration pops in mid-track, sweeps the wrong way, and leaves the track empty for most of the cycle. That is the 'animation that restarts' look the scss comment at :6-11 warns against. The library ships RTL screenshots for other components (button, select, dropdown, link, button-toggle), so RTL is supported. jsdom cannot show this, so no Vitest repro.
  Fix: Add an RTL variant: under `\[dir='rtl'\]` (or :dir(rtl)) use `transform-origin: top right` and mirrored keyframes (translateX(100%) to translateX(-400%)), or position the fill with logical properties so one keyframe set works both ways.
- **[progress-spinner] The compact arc overstates progress (dash computed for 295 units, compact circumference is ~267)** — reproduced.
  progress-spinner.component.ts:22 MAX\_DASH\_ARRAY = 295 and :92 offset = 295 - p\*295 (in %), used for both sizes; :24-25,82-84 r = 42.5% for compact (default size, :79) and 47% for big; progress-spinner.scss:23 `stroke-dasharray: 295%`; template progress-spinner.component.html:2 viewBox 0 0 100 100, so percentages resolve to user units (normalized diagonal = 100). Visible arc = 295p units over 2\*pi\*42.5 = 267.0, i.e. 1.105\*p of the circle; full from p = 90.5%. The repro spec (passes) reads r and the inline stroke-dashoffset off the rendered DOM: compact at 44 draws 0.486 of the circle, compact at 91 a full circle, big at 44 draws 0.44. The rendered result was also measured in the committed Chromium baseline packages/components/progress-spinner/\_\_screenshots\_\_/01-light.png with a circle fit (scratchpad script C:\Users\Admin\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-Admin--claude-spares-spare-i6M3vF\f575b788-acda-4964-b0d1-fd42460cbecc\scratchpad\spinner\measure.js): compact 44% arc spans -9.5..185.0 deg (predicted with round caps for 48.6%: -8.8..183.8; a correct 44% would end at ~167), big 44% spans -4.2..162.9 deg (predicted 44%: -3.7..161.9). Real consumer hit: file-upload shows upload progress with a determinate default-size spinner (single-file-upload.component.html:23, multiple-file-upload.component.html:45), which looks complete from 90.5%.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/progress-spinner/compact-arc-overstates.spec.ts`](defect-repros/progress-spinner/compact-arc-overstates.spec.ts)
  Fix: Compute the dash length per size from the circle's circumference (2\*pi\*r: ~267 for compact, ~295.3 for big) for both stroke-dasharray and the offset, or use pathLength="100" on the circle and work in percent of the path.
- **[progress-spinner] Assistive technology gets nothing: no progressbar role, aria-value\*, accessible name or text/caption link** — reproduced.
  progress-spinner.component.ts:52-57 host binds only the class, the size/indeterminate classes and id; no role, aria-valuenow/min/max, aria-label/labelledby/describedby; KbqProgressSpinnerText/Caption (:28-43) have no id. Template progress-spinner.component.html:2 only sets focusable=false on the svg. No a11y locale entry exists for the spinner (grep -i spinner in core/locales finds nothing), while progress-bar.component.ts:69-82 sets role=progressbar, aria-valuemin/max/now, aria-label (from the a11y locale) / labelledby and describedby. The repro spec (3 tests, passing) asserts a determinate spinner with text and caption and an indeterminate spinner expose no role, value, name, describedby or live region, and that axe (toHaveNoViolations) reports no violation, so the existing axe tooling would not catch it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/progress-spinner/no-progressbar-semantics.spec.ts`](defect-repros/progress-spinner/no-progressbar-semantics.spec.ts)
  Fix: Mirror kbq-progress-bar: role="progressbar", aria-valuemin/max/now in determinate mode only, an aria-label input with a locale default from kbqInjectA11yLocaleConfiguration(), and aria-labelledby/aria-describedby wired to ids on the text and caption directives; mark the svg aria-hidden.
- **[radio] A button with no value inside a group whose value is null is checked (on init and whenever the group value is cleared); several such buttons leave the last checked; group selected not set on init** — reproduced.
  radio.component.ts:631-636 ngOnInit does `checked = radioGroup.value === this.\_value()`, and both signals default to null (lines 213, 619), so every value-less button is set checked. In the checked setter (382-404) the `radioGroup.value !== this.value` guard at 390 is false (null === null), so group.selected is never set, and the notify at 400 unchecks the earlier siblings, so the last one wins. On a later write of null, updateSelectedRadioFromValue (335-352) runs `radio.checked = this.value === radio.value`, which checks the value-less button and makes it `selected`. The repro passes: three value-less buttons give checked \[false,false,true\] on the instances and the native inputs, with group.selected === null; in a fire/value-less pair, writing 'fire' and then null checks the value-less button. Real usage: packages/components-dev/popover/template.html:429-450 has two value-less buttons in one group. The existing test 'should deselect all of the radio buttons when the group value is cleared' cannot catch this because every button in its fixture has a value.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/radio/valueless-button-checked.spec.ts`](defect-repros/radio/valueless-button-checked.spec.ts)
  Fix: Check a button from the group value only when the button has a bound value (or the group value is non-null), and set group.selected whenever a button becomes checked because it matches the group value.
- **[radio] A button with its own \[name\] inside a group takes the group value but leaves the group-named checked button checked; clicking the old one fires no change** — reproduced.
  The checked setter (radio.component.ts:390-391) sets radioGroup.selected = this. The selected setter (131-137) sets \_selected before writing value, so updateSelectedRadioFromValue (337-341) sees isAlreadySelected and skips the loop that would uncheck the siblings. The only other uncheck path is radioDispatcher.notify(id, this.name) at 400, and the listener at 624-628 matches by name, so with name='custom' the group-named sibling is never unchecked. The template binds \[attr.name\]=name (radio.component.html:13), so the two native inputs are in different native groups and both stay checked. The repro passes. Clicking fire and then the own-named water gives group.value 'water' and selected=water, with checked \[true,true\] on both the instances and the native inputs. A second click on fire's label fires no change (jsdom models this: \_activationBehavior fires change only when the radio was not already checked), so group.value stays 'water' and the user cannot return to fire. The pattern is documented in docs/guides/migration.en.mdx:2539 and used in packages/components-dev/radio/template.html:45,87.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/radio/own-name-two-checked.spec.ts`](defect-repros/radio/own-name-two-checked.spec.ts)
  Fix: When a button inside a group becomes checked, uncheck the group's other checked buttons (or the previously selected one) whatever their name, or document that an own-named button must not sit in a group.
- **[radio] Two buttons with the same value in one group: writing that value ends with no button checked and the group value reset to null, without telling the form control** — reproduced.
  After init, writing 'water' runs the value setter (radio.component.ts:111-124), which runs updateSelectedRadioFromValue (335-352). The loop checks the first 'water' button and makes it \_selected. When it checks the second, notify (400) reaches the first, whose checked=false branch (392-395) sees group.value === its value and sets group.selected = null. The selected setter (131-137) then writes value = null, and the nested updateSelectedRadioFromValue unchecks the second button. Back in the outer loop the second button is no longer checked, so \_selected stays null. Nothing calls controlValueAccessorChangeFn. The repro passes: a \[formControl\] group with buttons fire/water/water, after control.setValue('water'), ends with control.value 'water', group.value null, group.selected null and checked \[false,false,false\]. On init (bindings before content init) the last duplicate wins instead, because isInitialized is false and the button's value setter (416-424) reselects it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/radio/duplicate-value-reset.spec.ts`](defect-repros/radio/duplicate-value-reset.spec.ts)
  Fix: In updateSelectedRadioFromValue, check only the first button that matches (break after a match), or keep the uncheck branch of the checked setter from clearing the group while the group is resolving its own value.
- **[resizer] No touch-action on the handle, so a touch pan cancels the drag** — confirmed in the source.
  The KbqResizer host (resizer.ts:73-79) binds only the `kbq-resizer` class and `cursor`. The directive ships no stylesheet and sets no `touch-action`. A `pointercancel` on the document (resizer.ts:175-177) goes through handleDocumentPointerUp to finishDrag (resizer.ts:243-263), so the drag ends. `preventDefault()` on pointerdown (resizer.ts:194) and `setPointerCapture` (resizer.ts:206) do not stop touch panning. Only `touch-action` does that, and with `auto` the browser claims the gesture and fires `pointercancel`. splitter.scss:70 and dl.scss:81 set `touch-action: none` on their handles. The content panel's handle (content-panel.ts:259-265, styled at content-panel-container.scss:49-56) and the docs example (resizer-overview-example.ts) do not. As a result, touch users cannot resize the content panel or a plain kbqResizer handle. jsdom has no gesture handling; a real touch device would show exactly when the cancel fires.
  Fix: Bind `\[style.touch-action\]` to 'none' on the KbqResizer host while it has a direction, or at least add `touch-action: none` to `.kbq-content-panel-container\_\_panel-resizer`.
- **[resizer] Negative requested size: element keeps the last written size while sizeChange reports negative numbers** — reproduced.
  updateSize (resizer.ts:312-322) computes `this.width + directionX \* (x - this.x)` without a lower bound. It writes `coerceCssPixelValue(width)` (for example '-200px') through Renderer2.setStyle, which sets `el.style.width`. The CSSOM rejects a negative width, so the previous value stays. The repro: a 100px content box is dragged to 150px, then one coalesced move asks for -200. `sizeChange` reports {width: -200, height: 50} and `style.width` stays '150px', so after a fast flick the element stops short of its minimum. Consumers that clamp on their own are not affected: the content panel binds a clamped `\[style.width.px\]`, and the splitter uses disableSizeUpdate. Direct consumers are. jsdom's cssstyle rejects negative widths the same way browsers do (parseMeasurement with restrictToPositive).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/resizer/negative-size.spec.ts`](defect-repros/resizer/negative-size.spec.ts)
  Fix: Clamp the written size at 0 (Math.max(0, …)) before setStyle, and decide whether the reported size should be clamped too.
- **[resizer] Starting size from getBoundingClientRect includes transforms; pointer travel in screen px vs width in local CSS px** — confirmed in the source.
  Under `border-box`, getResizableSize (resizer.ts:225-228) takes the starting size from `getBoundingClientRect()`, which is in viewport pixels after ancestor transforms and CSS `zoom`. updateSize (resizer.ts:318-319) writes that number back as a local CSS `width`/`height`. Inside `scale(0.5)`, the first move (travel ≈ 0) writes half the real CSS size, and the element visibly shrinks and stays that way. In both box models the travel is `event.x/y - this.x/y` (resizer.ts:197-198, 313-314), which is viewport pixels, and it is added to a local CSS length. Under scale s the edge moves s·Δ for a pointer move of Δ, so it does not follow the pointer. jsdom has no layout or transforms, so this cannot be reproduced without stubbing the very values in question.
  Fix: Read the border-box size from offsetWidth/offsetHeight (untransformed) and divide the pointer travel by the element's effective scale (rect.width / offsetWidth).
- **[resizer] Two simultaneous drags with primary pointers of different types leave a resize cursor stuck on the body** — reproduced.
  The drag state lives on each instance: activePointerId and previousBodyCursor (resizer.ts:96-101), and the guard against a second drag (resizer.ts:192) is per instance too. So a mouse (pointerId 1) on handle A and a pen (pointerId 2, also isPrimary) on handle B both start. B's applyDocumentCursor (resizer.ts:278-284) saves A's 'ew-resize !important' as the cursor to restore. When A ends first, restoreDocumentCursor (resizer.ts:291-306) removes the body cursor. When B ends, it puts 'ew-resize !important' back. The repro: after both pointerups, `document.body.style.cursor` is 'ew-resize' with priority 'important'. The cursor stays for the rest of the page's life, because the next drag saves and restores that same value. This needs a mouse and a pen (or a mouse and a finger) dragging at the same time, which is rare.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/resizer/two-drags-cursor.spec.ts`](defect-repros/resizer/two-drags-cursor.spec.ts)
  Fix: Keep the saved body cursor and a drag counter in a shared (root-provided) service, so the original cursor is restored only when the last active drag ends.
- **[scrollbar] scrollToElement/scrollIntoView offset walk skips a non-positioned kbqScrollbarViewport host and runs up to the page** — confirmed in the source.
  getElementOffset (scrollbar.ts:130-142) adds offsetTop/offsetLeft and follows offsetParent. It stops only when `current === ancestor` or when it reaches null. offsetParent is the nearest positioned ancestor (or body/td/table). On a static host the chain never meets the host, so it runs to body/null, and the sum includes the host's own offset from the page or from the positioned ancestor. scrollToElement (:420-426) and scrollIntoView (:432-438) then pass that inflated value to scrollTo. `.kbq-scrollbar-viewport` only sets `isolation: isolate` (scrollbar-viewport.scss:6-8), which does not make the host an offsetParent. kbq-scrollbar is `position: relative` (scrollbar.scss:2), so it is not affected. No library code calls these methods on a kbqScrollbarViewport host. A consumer that reaches the directive through a query on a static element is affected. jsdom always returns offsetParent null and offsetTop 0, so this cannot be shown without layout.
  Fix: Compute the offset from getBoundingClientRect of the target and the host plus the host's scrollTop/scrollLeft, or stop the walk once offsetParent is no longer inside the host.
- **[scrollbar] Under a bare dir="rtl" without the Dir directive, scrollTo/scrollStart/scrollEnd ignore RTL and scrollEnd() does not move** — reproduced.
  The thumb treats a bare `\[dir="rtl"\]` ancestor as RTL (scrollbar.ts:712-714, existing spec 'detects RTL from a bare dir="rtl" ancestor'). The viewport's scrollTo (:389-391) delegates to CdkScrollable.scrollTo (node\_modules/@angular/cdk/fesm2022/scrolling.mjs:328-355). That method reads only `this.dir.value`, and the root Directionality reads only body/html `dir` (\_directionality-chunk.mjs constructor). scrollEnd (:409-411) sends `{ end: 0 }`, so right = 0 and left = scrollWidth - clientWidth, a positive value. Current browsers use the negated RTL scroll axis (0 down to -(max)) and clamp a positive scrollLeft to 0, the start. In the repro, with Directionality 'ltr' and a bare dir=rtl ancestor, scrollEnd() asks for scrollLeft +200 and scrollTo({ start: 50 }) asks for +50 instead of -50. scrollStart() works only because its offset is 0. jsdom does not clamp, so the repro shows the requested offset and the 'does not move' part follows from browser RTL semantics.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/scrollbar/rtl-bare-dir-scroll-end.spec.ts`](defect-repros/scrollbar/rtl-bare-dir-scroll-end.spec.ts)
  Fix: Normalize start/end/right in KbqScrollbarViewport.scrollTo with the same DOM fallback the thumb uses (closest('\[dir="rtl"\]')), or give the CdkScrollable a Directionality that includes it.
- **[scrollbar] A thumb drag or bar press wipes a consumer-set inline scroll-behavior on the scrolling element** — reproduced.
  The drag/press subscriber sets `this.viewportElement.style.scrollBehavior = 'auto'` (scrollbar.ts:677), writes scrollTop/scrollLeft, and then sets it to `''` (:685) without saving the previous inline value. In the repro, a viewport with inline `scroll-behavior: smooth` still has 'smooth' before a bar press and '' after it (scrollTop moved to 100). Later programmatic scrolls and anchor jumps on that element then lose smooth behavior. This affects only consumers who set scroll-behavior inline or through a \[style\] binding, not through a class.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/scrollbar/scroll-behavior-wipe.spec.ts`](defect-repros/scrollbar/scroll-behavior-wipe.spec.ts)
  Fix: Save the previous inline scrollBehavior and restore it instead of clearing it to ''.
- **[scrollbar] A non-numeric kbqScrollbarHideDelay becomes NaN and the hide timer fires on the next task** — reproduced.
  Both hideDelay inputs use `numberAttribute` with no fallback (scrollbar.ts:326-329, :1014-1017), so 'abc', a bare attribute (''), undefined and null all become NaN. The numberAttribute transform accepts `unknown`, so strict templates do not catch `\[kbqScrollbarHideDelay\]="cfg?.delay"`. The track runs `timer(this.hideDelay(), this.scheduler)` (:879). RxJS timer keeps a NaN due time (`NaN \< 0` is false), and asyncScheduler passes it to setInterval, which treats NaN as 0. In the repro, KbqScrollbarViewport.hideDelay() is NaN for all four inputs and the revealed class is gone 1 ms after a scroll. With the numeric 1000 control it is still present.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/scrollbar/nan-hide-delay.spec.ts`](defect-repros/scrollbar/nan-hide-delay.spec.ts)
  Fix: Pass the option as the fallback, numberAttribute(value, options.hideDelay), on both inputs.
- **[search-expandable] A collapse through \[isOpened\] or the isOpened property skips the reset** — reproduced.
  search-expandable.ts:287-294 handles \[isOpened\] by calling the isOpened setter (189-191), which only sets openedState. The reset (control.setValue(''), value.set(''), forced emitValue('')) and the disabled guard live only in setOpened (498-520), which toggle(), the close button and Escape use. Repro: a host with \[(isOpened)\] and \[formControl\] expands the field, types 'query', waits for the debounce, then sets opened=false. The field collapses, but searchControl.value and component.value() stay 'query', even after another debounce. The existing spec test 'should keep the tooltip of the button disabled when a disabled field collapses' (search-expandable.spec.ts:223-233) collapses a disabled field through \[isOpened\], which shows the path also applies while disabled. The docs page says a model value renders the search expanded, so a collapsed field with an applied filter contradicts it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/search-expandable/isopened-collapse-keeps-query.spec.ts`](defect-repros/search-expandable/isopened-collapse-keeps-query.spec.ts)
  Fix: Route an \[isOpened\]/property change after the first one through the same collapse reset as setOpened(false), without emitting isOpenedChange, or document that the parent must clear the control itself.
- **[search-expandable] Every collapse moves focus to the collapsed button, including one started elsewhere** — reproduced.
  search-expandable.ts:390-392 calls restoreFocusToButton whenever the collapsed button appears again after not being rendered. restoreFocusToButton (523-536) calls focusMonitor.focusVia(button, lastFocusOrigin) with no check that focus was inside the component or was lost with the removed input. Repro (two tests): (a) a toolbar button calls ref.toggle() to expand, then gets focus and is clicked again to collapse. document.activeElement moves from the toolbar button to .kbq-search-expandable\_\_button. (b) after \[isOpened\]=true, focus is put on an unrelated button and the parent sets opened=false. Focus is pulled to the collapsed button.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/search-expandable/collapse-steals-focus.spec.ts`](defect-repros/search-expandable/collapse-steals-focus.spec.ts)
  Fix: Restore focus to the collapsed button only when focus was inside the component before the collapse, or was lost to the body when the input was removed.
- **[search-expandable] Writing value while collapsed reaches the control but leaves the field collapsed** — reproduced.
  The value effect (search-expandable.ts:324-332) calls control.setValue(value) with events on, which feeds the debounced pipeline (336-356). That pipeline reaches emitValue/onChange (538-543) and never calls setOpened. Auto-expand exists only in applyValue (447-450), the writeValue path. The forms API does not call writeValue for a view-to-model change, so the field never expands. Repro: on a collapsed \[formControl\] host, component.value.set('written') and advancing 200 ms leaves searchControl.value at 'written' while isOpened is false and only the collapsed button is rendered. The baseline spec records 'A write MUST NOT expand a collapsed field' as current behaviour. Writing value is a legacy and less common path, so this sits at the low end of P2.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/search-expandable/value-write-while-collapsed.spec.ts`](defect-repros/search-expandable/value-write-while-collapsed.spec.ts)
  Fix: Expand the field (like applyValue does) when a non-empty value is written into value while collapsed, or stop forwarding such a write to the bound control.
- **[search-expandable] Every collapse hands '' through the change callback even when nothing was typed** — reproduced.
  setOpened(false) at search-expandable.ts:516 calls emitValue(defaultValue, true). The forced flag skips the 'value !== lastEmittedValue' guard (539), so onChange('') runs even when lastEmittedValue and the control are already ''. Repro: with \[formControl\], clicking the collapsed button and then the close button without typing calls valueChanges once with '' and makes the control dirty. With \[(ngModel)\], ngModelChange fires once with ''. The baseline spec records 'Collapse of an empty field -> marked dirty' as current behaviour. A consumer that reloads on valueChanges reloads on every collapse, and the form turns dirty from open+close alone.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/search-expandable/empty-collapse-emits.spec.ts`](defect-repros/search-expandable/empty-collapse-emits.spec.ts)
  Fix: Force the reset emission only when the bound control may differ from '' (lastEmittedValue !== '' or a pending emission was dropped), and otherwise go through the normal guard.
- **[select] Tag remove control `\<i kbq-icon kbqTagRemove>` is focusable role=button but aria-hidden="true" (axe aria-hidden-focus)** — reproduced.
  select.html:54-60 renders `\<i kbq-icon="kbq-xmark-s\_16" kbqTagRemove \[aria-label\] \[tabIndex\]="isTriggerValueVisible($index) ? 0 : -1">` and binds no aria-hidden. KbqIcon sets `\[attr.aria-hidden\]` on its host (icon.component.ts:44). `defaultAriaHidden = true` (:69) resolves to 'true' when the input is unbound (:73-79), and only KbqIconButton overrides it (icon-button.component.ts:61). KbqTagRemove (tag.component.ts:858-868) adds role=button and tabindex but never touches aria-hidden. The repro passes: both built-in remove controls have role=button, tabindex=0 and aria-hidden=true. An axe `aria-hidden-focus` assertion was tried as well, but under jsdom the rule comes back 'incomplete' rather than as a violation (the controls have no size), so it was dropped. The existing axe tests (select.component.spec.ts:9571-9666) never render tags. tree-select.html:45-50 has the same markup.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/select/tag-remove-aria-hidden.spec.ts`](defect-repros/select/tag-remove-aria-hidden.spec.ts)
  Fix: Opt the icon out of aria-hidden for remove controls (e.g. aria-hidden="false" on the `\<i>`, or have KbqTagRemove override KbqIcon's default), and add an axe/attribute test with selected tags; tree-select needs the same change.
- **[select] Choosing a null-valued reset option emits selectionChange, valueChange and the form change twice (re-entrant deselect), even when already empty** — reproduced.
  selectViaInteraction (option.ts:403-413) sets selected=true and emits. In onSelect (select.component.ts:2487-2517), the null branch (2490-2493) calls option.deselect(), which emits synchronously because the option is now selected (option.ts:337-345). The merged optionSelectionChanges stream (607-633, subscribed at 2468-2477) re-enters onSelect, which takes the null branch again and calls propagateChanges(null). The outer call then propagates a second time at 2493. propagateChanges (2550-2565) emits value, valueChange, onChange and selectionChange without comparing against the previous value. The guard at 2512 never applies because the null option is never in the selection model. Existing tests (select.component.spec.ts:5605-5636) check only the final value. The repro passes: after picking a value and then the null option, selectionChange, valueChange and FormControl.valueChanges each record \[null, null\]. On an already empty select they also record \[null, null\].
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/select/null-option-double-emit.spec.ts`](defect-repros/select/null-option-double-emit.spec.ts)
  Fix: Deselect the reset option without emitting (`option.deselect(false)`) and propagate once, only when the selection or value actually changed.
- **[select] close() during the 101 ms delay of an empty select does not cancel the pending open** — reproduced.
  With noOptions, open() schedules `openPanelTimeout = setTimeout(openPanel, 101)` (select.component.ts:1512-1515). close() returns at :1581 (`if (!this.panelOpen) return;`) before touching the timer. Only open() itself (:1513) and ngOnDestroy (:1404) clear it, and attachPanel (:1530-1533) re-checks neither a cancel flag nor `disabled`. From the same source, a second toggle() during the delay calls open() again and reschedules rather than cancelling. The repro passes: on a select holding only `\<kbq-select-loading>`, open() then close(), advance 101 ms, and panelOpen is true with `.kbq-select\_\_panel` in the overlay.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/select/close-during-open-delay.spec.ts`](defect-repros/select/close-during-open-delay.spec.ts)
  Fix: Clear openPanelTimeout in close() (and in toggle while a delayed open is pending) before the panelOpen early return.
- **[select] With selectAll, the row renders over the loading/error/no-options content; aria-busy is dropped and activating the row does nothing** — reproduced.
  showSelectAll (select.component.ts:965-973) excludes the empty search result, virtual scroll and showPreselectedValues, but not noOptions. The template renders the row (select.html:146-167) ahead of the `\<ng-content />` that projects the state components. isOptionsListBusy is `noOptions && !showSelectAll` (:1191-1193), so aria-busy is removed. toggleSelectAll returns at once when there are no targets (:1420-1423). The repro passes for 'loading', 'error' and 'no-options': the panel shows both the state component and `.kbq-select\_\_select-all`, the listbox has no aria-busy, and clicking the row emits no selectionChange and leaves the control null.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/select/select-all-over-states.spec.ts`](defect-repros/select/select-all-over-states.spec.ts)
  Fix: Add `!this.noOptions` (or no selectAllTargets) to showSelectAll so the row and aria-busy follow the state content.
- **[sidebar] Only input and textarea targets are excluded; typing \[ in a contenteditable editor toggles the sidebar** — reproduced.
  isInput (core/keycodes/keycodes.ts:177-181) returns true only for tagName INPUT or TEXTAREA. handleKeydown (sidebar.component.ts:390-401) toggles on any other target, and the listener sits on the document (377). Repro: a keypress of BracketLeft dispatched on a `contenteditable` div closes the left sidebar. A control test confirms that the same keypress on an \<input> does not. Typing '\[' (or '\]' with a right sidebar) in a rich-text or code editor built on contenteditable (ProseMirror, CodeMirror 6, Quill) collapses the sidebar. `select` and other widgets are not excluded either.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/sidebar/shortcut-filters.spec.ts`](defect-repros/sidebar/shortcut-filters.spec.ts)
  Fix: Also skip editable targets (isContentEditable, select) and events whose defaultPrevented is set, or better, scope the shortcut (see the next defect).
- **[sidebar] Single-character, document-wide shortcut that cannot be turned off or remapped (WCAG 2.1.4); relies on deprecated keypress** — confirmed in the source.
  The constructor (sidebar.component.ts:229) always registers, through afterNextRender, a `keypress` listener on the whole document (375-381). It is removed only on destroy (289-291, 383-388). The only input that affects it is `position` (168), which handleKeydown alone reads (393). The template and host bindings (100-108) never use it, so the shortcut can be disabled only by leaving `position` unset. It cannot be remapped and is not limited to focus inside the sidebar. That fails WCAG 2.1.4 Character Key Shortcuts (Level A) for every page that sets `position`, and AGENTS.md requires WCAG AA. `keypress` is a deprecated event. Rated P2 rather than P3 because it is a Level A failure that hits speech-input users and anyone typing these characters outside text fields. An app can still opt out by not setting `position`.
  Fix: Add an input that turns the shortcut off (or makes it require a modifier / focus within), move it to keydown, and document it.
- **[sidebar] No ARIA state relation, and focus falls to \<body> when the sidebar closes with focus inside the opened content** — reproduced.
  The template (sidebar.component.html:1-5) projects the opened content only while internalState is true. After the transition, waitForStateAnimation sets internalState to false (sidebar.component.ts:368), which detaches the projected opened nodes. Nothing moves focus first: there is no focus handling anywhere in the component. The host bindings (100-108) set no role and no aria-\* attribute. Repro: a focused 'Collapse' button inside the opened content is clicked, or `\[` is pressed on it. The sidebar closes, the button is disconnected and document.activeElement becomes \<body>. The host has no role or aria-\* in either state. The focus loss is the P2 part: keyboard and screen-reader users lose their place with the common 'collapse button inside the sidebar' pattern and with the shortcut. The missing aria-expanded is P3 hygiene, since the sidebar has no toggle control of its own and the shortcut toggles without any announcement.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/sidebar/focus-loss.spec.ts`](defect-repros/sidebar/focus-loss.spec.ts)
  Fix: Before switching the content on close, move focus into the closed content (or the host) when it was inside the opened content. Expose the state, e.g. via an exported id or aria-expanded helper for the app's toggle.
- **[sidebar] Assigning true to opened on an open sidebar captures and persists its pixel width (percentage/inherit widths get frozen)** — reproduced.
  setOpened (sidebar.component.ts:330-355) calls saveWidth whenever openedState() && !toggling() (334-336). That happens before `changed` is computed (338), so a no-op `opened = true` still runs it. saveWidth (418-422) writes `${offsetWidth}px` into params.openedStateWidth, and saveState (354, 299-307) persists it through snapshot (411-416). The capture is not gated on `controlled` (only persistence is, 407), so a bound sidebar is affected too, for example when the app re-asserts \[opened\]=true after the user opened it with the shortcut. Repro: a sidebar declared `width="50%"`, with offsetWidth stubbed to 300. `opened = true` changes the host's inline width from '50%' to '300px' and stores {opened:true,width:'300px'}. One close and reopen also leaves it at '300px'. This close capture is deliberate (saveState JSDoc 296-297), but it means a percentage or inherit sidebar stops following its container after one close.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/sidebar/redundant-open-width.spec.ts`](defect-repros/sidebar/redundant-open-width.spec.ts)
  Fix: Capture the width only on an actual open-to-closed change, and only when it differs from the declared width (or keep the declared unit unless the width was changed from outside, e.g. by a splitter).
- **[sidebar] Widths are read once: a bound \[width\] has no effect after init and content added later gets the default widths** — reproduced.
  params are built from the content directives' inputs only in ngAfterContentInit (sidebar.component.ts:237-247). The width inputs of KbqSidebarOpened and KbqSidebarClosed (55-57, 68) and the contentChild signals (195, 200) are not read anywhere else, and nothing reacts to their changes. Repro: changing a bound `\[width\]` from '200px' to '300px' leaves the host at '200px'. Opened content shown later by an @if with width="300px" is projected and openedContent().width() returns '300px', but the host stays at 'inherit'.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/sidebar/widths-read-once.spec.ts`](defect-repros/sidebar/widths-read-once.spec.ts)
  Fix: Derive the declared widths with computed() from the content queries and inputs, and keep the captured or restored width as a separate override.
- **[sidepanel] Right or middle click on the panel underneath closes the panel above it in a non-modal stack** — reproduced.
  sidepanel-ref.ts:126-134 merges overlayRef.outsidePointerEvents() into the close stream, filtered only by isClickInsideSamePositionStack (sidepanel-ref.ts:181-187), which looks at the target and never at the button or event type. CDK OverlayOutsideClickDispatcher (node\_modules/@angular/cdk/fesm2022/\_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:482) listens to click, auxclick and contextmenu on body and feeds all three to \_outsidePointerEvents (lines 505-522). The repro shows a contextmenu event (button 2) and an auxclick event (button 1) on content of the lower non-modal panel each close the upper panel (openedSidepanels becomes \[lower\]). In real layouts the lower panel is reachable outside the indent strip when it is wider than the panel above it. This contradicts sidepanel.en.mdx:22 ('closes on a left mouse button click only'). In a modal stack the top panel's transparent backdrop covers the lower one, so the target is the backdrop and the filter rejects it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/sidepanel/aux-click-closes-upper.spec.ts`](defect-repros/sidepanel/aux-click-closes-upper.spec.ts)
  Fix: In the outsidePointerEvents branch, also require event.type === 'click' (or event.button === 0).
- **[sidepanel] Closing the middle panel of a same-position stack of three or more leaves the panel under it hidden** — reproduced.
  removeOpenSidepanel (sidepanel.service.ts:483-492) calls getLowerSidepanelsWithSamePosition(index) (568-577). That function ignores the index except to read the position and always returns the panels at length-2 and length-3 of the stack. When the middle panel B of \[A,B,C\] closes, 'lower' is B itself, which is already Hidden, so setAnimationState skips it (467). A stays BottomPanel (opacity 0, sidepanel-container.component.ts:238-248). updateIndents (506-512) still gives C the indent strip. updateAnimationState on beforeClosed returns early because B is not last (446-449). The repro checks that after middle.close() the stack is \[bottom, top\], top still has .kbq-sidepanel-indent and bottom's host style.opacity is '0'. Bottom becomes opacity '1' only after the top panel closes. Related case from the same lookup: closing the bottom panel of three promotes the middle one to Visible, not Lower, under the top panel.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/sidepanel/middle-close-hides-lower.spec.ts`](defect-repros/sidepanel/middle-close-hides-lower.spec.ts)
  Fix: Pick the panels to promote relative to the closed panel's position within its same-position stack (the one directly below it becomes Lower or Visible, the next one BottomPanel), not from the end of the stack.
- **[sidepanel] A multi-class or empty backdropClass makes open() throw and leaks an attached overlay, backdrop and scroll block** — reproduced.
  sidepanel-ref.ts:47 runs overlayRef.backdropElement?.classList.add(config.backdropClass ?? 'kbq-overlay-dark-backdrop') in the KbqSidepanelRef constructor. assignDefined (sidepanel.service.ts:75-83) keeps '' because it is not undefined. DOMTokenList.add('a b') throws InvalidCharacterError and add('') throws SyntaxError. The constructor runs at service.ts:187, after createOverlay (185) and attachContainer (186). CDK attach (\_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:774-808) has already attached the host, the container, the backdrop, the scroll strategy and the keyboard and outside-click registrations. openedSidepanels.push (209) is never reached, so closeAll() cannot reach the panel and nothing subscribes to its backdrop or Escape. The repro checks both 'custom-backdrop kbq-overlay-dark-backdrop' and '': open() throws, one kbq-sidepanel-container and one .cdk-overlay-backdrop stay in the overlay container, openedSidepanels is empty, and both are still there after closeAll(). In a browser the leftover backdrop has no dark class but still catches clicks, and the page scroll stays blocked. Only modal panels are affected, because backdropElement is null without a backdrop.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/sidepanel/backdrop-class-leaks-overlay.spec.ts`](defect-repros/sidepanel/backdrop-class-leaks-overlay.spec.ts)
  Fix: Split backdropClass on whitespace and drop empty tokens before classList.add (or pass it to OverlayConfig.backdropClass), and dispose the overlay if constructing the ref fails.
- **[skeleton] toColumns passes any object through; \[columns\]="null" reaches the grid-template computation, which spreads it and throws** — reproduced.
  skeleton-presets.ts:194-195: `typeof value === 'object' ? value : ...` returns null unchanged (typeof null === 'object'); skeleton-presets.ts:264-266 then spreads `...this.columns()` and throws. Reproduced: `\<kbq-skeleton-table \[columns\]="columns()" />` with a null value makes fixture.detectChanges() throw TypeError 'this.columns is not a function or its return value is not iterable' (test passes). Control case in the same spec: undefined goes through numberAttribute -> NaN -> range() and renders one column without throwing, so only null crashes. Realistic with `columns$ | async` (null before the first emission) in an app without strictTemplates; strict templates reject null at compile time.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/skeleton/null-columns.spec.ts`](defect-repros/skeleton/null-columns.spec.ts)
  Fix: Use `Array.isArray(value) ? value : equalColumns(numberAttribute(value))` in toColumns so null and other non-arrays fall back to the count path.
- **[split-button] The split button overwrites the dropdown's xPosition with 'before'** — reproduced.
  split-button.ts:153-160: an effect that reads buttons(), dropdownTrigger(), the trigger's dropdown() and panelAutoWidth() calls updateDropdownParams(). At split-button.ts:200 that does `dropdownTrigger.dropdown().xPosition.set('before')` without any condition. xPosition is a model() (dropdown.component.ts:165), and Angular runs a view's effects after its template update (refreshView: executeTemplate, then runEffectsInView), so the consumer's binding is applied first and then replaced. The repro passes three tests: (1) with `\[xPosition\]="'after'"` bound, dropdown.xPosition() reads 'before'; (2) a trigger outside the split button that shares the dropdown sees 'before' too (it is the same instance, and dropdown-trigger.directive.ts:769 reads it on every open); (3) after the consumer changes the binding to 'center', toggling panelAutoWidth sets it back to 'before'. This contradicts split-button.en.mdx:35 (and ru:35), which says positioning "is configured the same way as in the Dropdown component".
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/split-button/xposition-overwrite.spec.ts`](defect-repros/split-button/xposition-overwrite.spec.ts)
  Fix: Set 'before' only as a default: skip the write when the consumer bound or configured xPosition, or pass a position override to the trigger instead of writing the shared dropdown's model.
- **[split-button] panelAutoWidth measures the host box, which can be wider than the visible buttons** — confirmed in the source.
  split-button.ts:204 sets widthOrigin to the host element, and dropdown-trigger.directive.ts:926 measures it on open. split-button.scss:120 makes the host `display: flex`, a block-level box, so it takes its container's width or an explicit width. The nested buttons do not grow, so the host can be wider than the visible control. The committed baseline \_\_screenshots\_\_/05-light.png (E2eSplitButtonDropdown, e2e.ts:240-242, host width 240px, 2x DPR) shows it. The visible control spans about x=12-98 CSS px. The panel is 240px wide and starts at the chevron's left edge (about x=67). The forced 'before' position (split-button.ts:200; xPositions.before at dropdown-trigger.directive.ts:112) would put the panel's end at the chevron's end, which needs 240px to the left. That does not fit, so CDK falls back to the start/start pair and the panel is anchored to the chevron, not lined up with the control. jsdom has no layout. The docs example avoids this only because its host sits in a centred flex container (docs-examples split-button-menu-width).
  Fix: Make the host shrink to its content (e.g. `display: inline-flex`, or `width: fit-content`), or measure the span from the first to the last button instead of the host box.
- **[splitter] Enter, Home and End act only on the panel before the separator; a trailing collapsible panel cannot be collapsed or expanded from the keyboard** — reproduced.
  splitter.ts:933 always takes `panel = this.panels()\[index\]`, the panel before the separator. Home (952-960), End (961-972) and Enter (973-976) only check that panel's `collapsible`/`collapsed`. The arrows go through resizeBy (1079-1081), which uses minSizes/maxSizes (698-709). An open panel is held at its expanded minimum there, and a collapsed panel is pinned to its strip. Only a drag lets a panel cross the gap: freePanels (682-688) is true only while dragBoundary is set, and pinsFor runs only from handleResizeTo (872-880). The repro passes on a 600px splitter whose second panel has minSize=100, collapsible and collapsedSize=40. Every handled key leaves that panel open at \[500,100\]. Once the host collapses it, Enter, End, Home and ArrowLeft leave it collapsed at \[560,40\]. The Terminal panel of splitter-collapsible-example.ts is such a panel; that example also has a host toggle. This fails WCAG 2.1.1: a mouse drag can do it, the keyboard cannot.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/splitter/trailing-collapsible-keyboard.spec.ts`](defect-repros/splitter/trailing-collapsible-keyboard.spec.ts)
  Fix: Make Enter/Home/End also reach the collapsible panel after the separator when the panel before it is not collapsible (or toggle whichever adjacent panel is collapsed), and let the arrows carry a collapsible neighbour across its gap like a drag does.
- **[table] A multi-row \<thead>: every header th sticks at top: 0, so the second row slides over the first** — confirmed in the source.
  packages/components/table/table.scss:39-44 gives every `.kbq-table\_sticky-header > thead > tr > th` `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 1` and has no per-row offset. In a two-row header (for example grouped columns), both rows stick to the same top edge. The second row's cells come later in tree order and have the same z-index, so they paint over the first row. Meanwhile table.component.ts:86-88 publishes the height of the whole `\<thead>` (kbqGetElementHeight(head)), so the scroll margin (table.scss:51-54) covers both rows while only one row of the header stays visible. The bug is in sticky layout, which jsdom does not do. The docs (table.en.mdx, Borders) mention tables with complex structures, and nothing documents a single-row-only limit.
  Fix: Offset each header row after the first by the rows above it, for example by publishing per-row offsets or by making `thead` itself the sticky element instead of the `th` cells.
- **[table] A td in the \<thead> (e.g. an empty corner cell) gets no header line, does not stick and has no background** — confirmed in the source.
  Every header rule selects `th` only: the header line in table.scss:24-29, the sticky positioning in table.scss:40-44 and the pinned background in \_table-theme.scss:8-10 and :32-34. Only the color and typography are set on `thead` itself (\_table-theme.scss:5-6, :48-53), so a `td` inherits those and nothing else. With `stickyHeader`, the `th` cells stay pinned while the `td` corner cell scrolls away, which leaves a hole in the pinned header that body cells scroll through. Even without `stickyHeader`, the header line has a gap under that cell. HTML allows a `td` in a header row, and axe's empty-table-header rule pushes authors to use `\<td>` rather than an empty `\<th>` for a corner cell. The bug is in CSS layout, which jsdom does not do.
  Fix: Change the thead rules (border, sticky, background) from `> tr > th` to `> tr > :is(th, td)`.
- **[table] A \<thead> added to or replaced in an already-rendered table is not observed while stickyHeader stays on** — reproduced.
  In table.component.ts:77-91 the effect reads `this.stickyHeader()` as its only signal; `this.elementRef.nativeElement.tHead` (line 78) is a plain DOM read and does not make the effect run again. Reproduced with two tests. Test 1: a `\<thead>` behind `@if` inside `\<table kbq-table stickyHeader>` is turned on after the first render. The table carries `kbq-table\_sticky-header`, yet `observer.observed` stays empty and `--kbq-table-size-sticky-header-height` stays ''. Test 2: an `@if/@else` swaps the `\<thead>`. The observer stays subscribed only to the old, detached head, and the new head's height (64) is never published; jsdom keeps the old 42px. In a real browser, ResizeObserver reports the removed element at 0, so the value would turn to 0px, which is still wrong. The line 76 comment admits a swapped `\<thead>` is unsupported, but neither the docs nor the JSDoc say so. The result is that a focused control can be scrolled under the pinned header (WCAG 2.4.11).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/table/thead-swap.spec.ts`](defect-repros/table/thead-swap.spec.ts)
  Fix: Re-read `tHead` when the table's children change, for example with a MutationObserver or a `contentChild` of the thead that feeds the effect, or document that the `\<thead>` must be static.
- **[tabs] The arrow keys ignore right-to-left layouts** — reproduced.
  paginated-tab-header.ts:461-463 builds the FocusKeyManager withHorizontalOrientation(getLayoutDirection()) and updates it on dir change (line 485). handleKeydown (lines 571-574) never passes the event to keyManager.onKeydown: it calls setNextItemActive for RIGHT\_ARROW and setPreviousItemActive for LEFT\_ARROW directly. The repro puts the group under \<div dir="rtl">. The header reports 'rtl' and has the kbq-tab-header\_rtl class, yet Right moves focus from 1 to 2 (the next tab in document order, drawn on the left) and Left moves it back. The configured key manager's own onKeydown(RIGHT) moves to 0. The same code serves KbqTabNavBar.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tabs/rtl-arrow-keys.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tabs/rtl-arrow-keys.spec.ts)
  Fix: Swap the horizontal arrow mapping when getLayoutDirection() is 'rtl', or pass the horizontal arrow keys to keyManager.onKeydown.
- **[tabs] Removing the selected tab from the middle of the list shows the tab that takes its index without emitting selectedTabChange, selectedIndexChange or activeTabChange, and leaves that tab's isActive false** — reproduced.
  tab-group.component.ts:346-364: after the removal, getTabIndexToSelect clamps the old number to the tab now at that index, so indexToSelect equals selectedIndexState. The loop then looks for a tab with isActive, finds none (the active one was destroyed) and does nothing. ngAfterContentChecked (line 389) sees no index change, so it emits nothing. It also skips the Promise at lines 398-399, the only place isActive is written. Angular Material has a fallback here for an active tab that was swapped out; this port lacks it. The repro starts with \[A,B,C\] and B selected, then removes B. C becomes the selected label and the active body ('Content C'), no event fires, and no tab has isActive true. A follow-up add or remove then cannot find the active tab to keep it selected.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tabs/remove-selected-tab.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tabs/remove-selected-tab.spec.ts)
  Fix: In the tabs.changes handler, when no tab is active but tabs\[indexToSelect\] exists, set its isActive and emit selectedTabChange in a microtask, as Material does.
- **[tabs] Adding or removing tabs keeps the selected tab at a new index without emitting selectedIndexChange or activeTabChange** — reproduced.
  tab-group.component.ts:351-361 moves the selection to the active tab's new index by writing selectedIndexState and activeTab silently. The comment at lines 356-358 says this is deliberate, to avoid consumer loops (inherited from Material). ngAfterContentChecked then sees no change (line 389), so neither output fires. The repro starts with \[(selectedIndex)\]=2 on \[A,B,C,D\] (C selected). Removing A keeps C selected, but the group's index moves to 1 with no event while the host still holds 2. Assigning 2 again is not an input change, so D stays unreachable through the binding. Inserting X before A moves C to index 3 while the host stays on 2. The existing tests (tab-group.spec.ts:469-494) check only the group's own selectedIndex, never the bound host value.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tabs/index-desync-on-add-remove.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tabs/index-desync-on-add-remove.spec.ts)
  Fix: Emit selectedIndexChange (and the numeric activeTabChange) asynchronously when tabs.changes moves the selected tab to a new index, guarding against re-entry instead of suppressing the event.
- **[tabs] With activeTab bound to a string, clicking a tab that has no tabId selects the first enabled tab instead of the clicked one and emits null** — reproduced.
  tab-group.component.ts:523-524 in string mode assigns this.activeTab = this.tabs.get($event)?.tabId() || null. A tab without tabId gives null, so the activeTab getter falls through to getDefaultTab, the first enabled tab (lines 241-242, 682-683). The Promise at lines 404-409 then emits attributeToSelectBy, which is null. The repro binds \[(activeTab)\]='second' and clicks the third label, which has no tabId. 'First' becomes selected and the host's activeTab becomes null. After that the group has silently left string mode.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tabs/string-active-tab-without-id.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tabs/string-active-tab-without-id.spec.ts)
  Fix: In string mode, fall back to the clicked index (or ignore the click) when the tab has no tabId, rather than assigning null.
- **[tags] In a kbq-form-field, a plain click on a tag of a list without a tag input moves focus to the first enabled tag instead of the clicked one** — reproduced.
  tag.component.ts:658-689: handleClick stops propagation only on a Ctrl/Meta/Shift click (:665-679), so a plain click bubbles up to form-field.html:16 `(click)="onContainerClick($event)"`. That calls form-field.ts:412-417, then tag-list.component.ts:652-654 onContainerClick, then focus() (:659-663), then focusFirstTag (:665-686). With no tagInput, focusFirstTag calls keyManager.setActiveItem(first enabled index), and core FocusKeyManager.setActiveItem (core/a11y/key-manager/focus-key-manager.ts) always focuses the active item. Repro: focus tag 3 (activeItemIndex 2, as a browser does on mousedown), then click it. activeElement becomes tag 1 and activeItemIndex becomes 0. The same list outside a form field keeps focus on tag 3. As a result, a following Delete or Space acts on the first tag. No docs example puts a tag list without a tag input in a form field, so this needs that specific setup.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tags/container-click-focus.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tags/container-click-focus.spec.ts)
  Fix: Make KbqTagList.onContainerClick ignore clicks whose target is inside a tag, or skip refocusing when a tag already has focus.
- **[tags] In an empty tag input of a list with no enabled tag, Shift+Tab has its default prevented while nothing takes focus** — reproduced.
  tag-input.ts:258-275: when the input is empty, every key goes to `\_tagList.keydown(event)` and then has its propagation stopped. In tag-list.component.ts:739-749, an empty input target with Shift+Tab calls keyManager.setLastItemActive() and then preventDefault() unconditionally. core list-key-manager.ts:443-460 setActiveItemByIndex returns without activating anything when the list is empty or every item is skipped as disabled. Repro: with no tags, and again with two disabled tags, Shift+Tab gives defaultPrevented === true and focus stays in the input. A keyboard user cannot tab backwards out of an empty tag field, which is the usual starting state of a form. Tab forward still works.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tags/shift-tab-empty-input.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tags/shift-tab-empty-input.spec.ts)
  Fix: Call preventDefault (and return) only when the key manager actually activated a tag; otherwise let Shift+Tab through.
- **[tags] While a tag is edited, Left, Shift+Tab and Ctrl/Cmd+A in an empty edit input bubble to the list, which treats the edit input as the tag input** — reproduced.
  tag.component.ts:177-200: KbqTagEditInput stops propagation only for Escape, Enter, Backspace, Space and Delete. KbqTag.handleKeydown (:693) returns early while editing but does not stop the event. The keydown therefore reaches the list host listener (tag-list.component.ts:108), and keydown (:724-757) uses isInputEmpty(target) (:865-869), which accepts any empty \<input>. For Left or Shift+Tab it calls setLastItemActive + preventDefault; for Ctrl/Cmd+A it calls selectAll + setLastItemActive. Moving focus to another tag fires the edited tag's focus-monitor blur, which calls cancelEditing('blur') (:838). Repro (editing the first of 4 tags, edit input emptied): Left and Shift+Tab move focus to the last tag and editChange reports {type:'cancel', reason:'blur'}. Ctrl+A selects all four tags and also moves focus to the last tag.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tags/edit-input-keys.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tags/edit-input-keys.spec.ts)
  Fix: In KbqTagList.keydown, check for the registered tag input element rather than any empty input, or stop these keys in KbqTagEditInput.
- **[tags] User changes are misdetected: a handler that writes the form control leaves it pristine with no change/valueChange, autocomplete-option tags are never flagged, and a rejected tag's latch turns a later code change into a user change** — reproduced.
  tag-list.component.ts:540-575: after tags.changes, a microtask propagates only when pendingUIChange is set and hasTagsValueChanged() (:930-940) is true. writeValue (:622-624) has already set `value` to what the handler wrote. (a) Repro of the pattern in the tag-input/tag-autocomplete \*-with-form-control-validators examples: a handler does control.setValue(\[...value, v\]) with a max-2 validator. The control value becomes \['a','b','c'\] and is invalid, but dirty stays false, change and valueChange never emit, and errorState() stays false under ShowOnControlDirtyErrorStateMatcher. (b) notifyPendingTagChange is called only from tag-input.ts:405-407 (typed, blur and paste), alongside the latches on remove (:984-986) and drop (:1010). The autocomplete optionSelected path never sets it. Repro with tags kept in their own array: the picked option renders, but control.value stays \['a'\] and change is not emitted. A typed tag in the same fixture does update the control and marks it dirty. (c) The latch is set before the consumer decides, and a rejected tag fires no tags.changes, so the latch survives. Repro: after a rejected tag, `tags.set(\[...\])` in code marks the control dirty and emits change once. The same change with no prior rejection leaves it pristine. The existing test at tag-input.spec.ts:873 covers only a later control.setValue, which the value guard hides.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tags/pending-ui-change.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tags/pending-ui-change.spec.ts)
  Fix: Base the dirty/change decision on a snapshot taken at the UI action (and clear the latch when no tags.changes follows in the same turn) rather than on the written value, and notify on autocomplete option selection.
- **[tags] The tag list has no role, aria-invalid, aria-required or aria-describedby, and tags have no role or aria-selected, so error and selection state never reach assistive technology** — confirmed in the source.
  The host bindings at tag-list.component.ts:96-109 and tag.component.ts:229-250 carry no role, aria-invalid, aria-required or aria-selected, so selection and error state are not exposed. The skipped 'error messages' suite (tag-list.component.spec.ts:1179-1183) records this. Correction to the finding: KbqFormField does write aria-describedby onto the kbq-tag-list host (form-field.ts:381-384 and 484-506, kbqSetDescribedByIds on controlElementRef). That host has no role and, with a tag input, no tabindex (:333-335), so nothing focusable references the hints or errors. A kbqInput on the tag input renders its own aria-invalid (input/input.ts:61) from its own absent control. Second correction: the list adds no validator, but Angular's RequiredValidator (selector :not(\[type=checkbox\])\[required\]\[formControl|formControlName|ngModel\]) does attach when `required` sits next to a forms directive. Rated P2 rather than polish because screen-reader users get neither selection nor validity.
  Fix: Give the list and tags proper composite-widget semantics (as with a grid or listbox), with aria-selected on tags, and forward aria-invalid, aria-required and aria-describedby to the focusable tag input when one is registered.
- **[textarea] The clamp at the row limit is maxRows times the line height, without the vertical padding** — reproduced.
  textarea.component.ts:343 sets the height to scrollHeight (which includes the padding) + diff + the free row. :347 derives rowsCount from it. :351-353 then clamp the min-height at the limit to `maxRows \* lineHeight`. The paddingTop/paddingBottom measured at :290 are ignored there. textarea.scss:17 sets `box-sizing: border-box`, so min-height includes the 5px padding from textarea-tokens.scss:57. The repro uses lineHeight 20, padding 5 and maxRows 5. At 4 lines the min-height is 110px. At the 5th line it is 100px: the field shrinks by 10px and holds only 4.5 lines. The baseline \_\_screenshots\_\_/01-light.png shows the maxRows field with 4 full lines and a 5th cut by the border. It also hits filter-bar/pipes/pipe-text.html:29 (\[freeRowsHeight\]="0" \[maxRows\]="5"), where the 6th line shrinks the field.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/textarea/max-rows-clamp.spec.ts`](defect-repros/textarea/max-rows-clamp.spec.ts)
  Fix: Clamp to maxRows \* lineHeight plus the vertical padding (and the border diff), e.g. reuse minHeight - lineHeight + maxRows \* lineHeight.
- **[textarea] Switching canGrow off does not clear the minimum height or the row-limit state** — reproduced.
  The effect at textarea.component.ts:264-271 schedules grow() when canGrow changes. grow() returns at :328 when !canGrow(), so neither the inline style.minHeight written at :351 nor rowsCount (:211) is reset. maxRowLimitReached (:185-189) stays true from the stale rowsCount. The host then gets both kbq-textarea-resizable and kbq-textarea\_max-row-limit-reached (:47-48). In textarea.scss:29-40 the later `resize: unset` beats `resize: vertical`, so no handle appears. The inline min-height also overrides the class min-height at :31. Repro case 1, no maxRows: min-height stays 220px after canGrow is set to false. Case 2, maxRows 3: min-height stays 60px, maxRowLimitReached() stays true and both classes stay.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/textarea/can-grow-off.spec.ts`](defect-repros/textarea/can-grow-off.spec.ts)
  Fix: When canGrow is off, reset rowsCount to 0 and remove the inline min-height (in grow's early return or in the canGrow effect).
- **[textarea] The tab group is looked up only up to the host of the declaring template, so a textarea inside a component in a tab does not re-measure after the tab animation** — reproduced.
  textarea.component.ts:67 calls `inject(KBQ\_PARENT\_ANIMATION\_COMPONENT, { optional: true, host: true })`, and the token is provided only by KbqTabGroup (tab-group.component.ts:141). With `host: true` the lookup stops at the host element of the component whose template declares the textarea. In a wrapper component placed in a kbq-tab, `parent` is null and the animationDone subscription at :227-232 is never made. tab-group.component.ts:487-490 emits animationDone. In the repro, removeTabBodyWrapperHeight() re-runs ngOnInit once for a textarea placed directly in the tab and never for one inside a child component. The flag dates back to the init commit. The visible cost is whatever the re-measure exists to fix; that was not established in a browser.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/textarea/tab-nested.spec.ts`](defect-repros/textarea/tab-nested.spec.ts)
  Fix: Drop `host: true` from the KBQ\_PARENT\_ANIMATION\_COMPONENT injection (keep `optional`).
- **[time-range] Focus falls to \<body> after the popover closes (Apply, Cancel, Escape), because focus is restored to the non-focusable kbq-time-range\_\_trigger wrapper** — reproduced.
  time-range.ts:73-89 puts kbqPopover on \<kbq-time-range-title>. That host has no tabindex and no host bindings (time-range-title.ts:20-39); the focusable element is the inner \<a kbqLink pseudo> (time-range-title.ts:32, which gets tabindex/role from link.ts:132-135). Every close path goes through popover.component.ts:190-195 to trigger.restoreFocus() (popover.component.ts:1100-1104), which calls focusMonitor.focusVia(getNativeElement()), i.e. the wrapper. focus() on a non-focusable element does nothing, so focus stays on the footer button and drops to \<body> once the overlay detaches. Repro: focus the link, open, focus Cancel, click Cancel, settle. The popover is gone, the wrapper has no tabindex, and document.activeElement is body, not the link. The test passes.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/time-range/focus-lost-on-close.spec.ts`](defect-repros/time-range/focus-lost-on-close.spec.ts)
  Fix: Restore focus to the focusable element inside the trigger (the link or the custom title control), e.g. by putting kbqPopover on that element or overriding the restore target.
- **[time-range] With showRangeAsDefault on and `range` not listed, a manual range the user applied is replaced by the first type when written back** — reproduced.
  showRangeAsDefault defaults to true (time-range.ts:153), so the editor offers the range option (time-range-editor.ts:130-132, html:24-31). Apply emits {type:'range',...} (time-range.ts:284-292). Writing that value back goes through writeValue and then checkAndCorrectTimeRangeValue (time-range.service.ts:222-225). That function keeps a value only if the non-empty list includes its type, so it falls back to getTimeRangeDefaultValue (the first type) and emits valueCorrected. Repro: types \['lastHour','last24Hours'\]; select range, Apply, then control.setValue({...applied}). Afterwards value().type is 'lastHour', valueCorrected emitted \['lastHour'\], and the bound control still holds 'range' (control and component out of sync). The test passes.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/time-range/unlisted-range-round-trip.spec.ts`](defect-repros/time-range/unlisted-range-round-trip.spec.ts)
  Fix: Treat 'range' as an allowed type in correction (component and editor) whenever showRangeAsDefault makes it selectable.
- **[time-range] Empty availableTimeRangeTypes: post-init preset kept with disabled range fields and no options; the one-time switch to range is a dirty user change not reported via valueCorrected; showRangeAsDefault=false leaves the editor empty** — reproduced.
  (a) With an empty list, time-range.service.ts:223 keeps any type. The switch to range happens only when the list changes (toObservable at time-range.ts:211-213, handler at 352-368), so a later control.setValue({type:'lastHour'}) is kept. In the editor, applyValue (time-range-editor.ts:282) sets type 'lastHour', and syncRangeControlsDisabledState (time-range-editor.ts:560-569) disables the from/to fields. No radio-group is rendered because types.length is 0 (html:9). Repro: type 'lastHour', fromDate and toTime disabled, no radio inputs, range block present. (b) handleAvailableTypesChange calls this.onChange(...) (time-range.ts:367), and the forms layer then marks the control dirty. valueCorrected is never emitted for 'range'. Repro: control.value.type 'range', dirty true, no 'range' in the corrections. (c) isRangeVisible = showRangeAsDefault || types.includes('range') (time-range-editor.ts:130-132) is false and there are no types, so the editor renders nothing. Repro: 0 inputs and an empty container. All three tests pass.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/time-range/empty-types.spec.ts`](defect-repros/time-range/empty-types.spec.ts)
  Fix: Correct written values against the effective list (\['range'\] when empty) in writeValue, emit valueCorrected instead of onChange for that switch, and never render an empty editor.
- **[time-range] valueCorrected at init: reported twice for an initial value needing correction; under ngModel a correction of null is reported before the model's own value arrives** — reproduced.
