  ngOnInit calls writeValue(ngControl.value) (time-range.ts:216-219), and the forms layer's setUpControl writes the same unchanged value again. wasCorrected compares the written value with the corrected one (time-range.ts:241-243, 269-277), so both writes emit. Repro: an initial {type:'currentYear'} with the list \['lastHour','last24Hours','range'\] gives corrections \['lastHour','lastHour'\]. With \[(ngModel)\], NgModel first writes null synchronously and only later writes the model through a promise. wasCorrected(null) returns nonNullable (time-range.ts:270), so the default 'lastHour' is reported. Repro: value() ends up equal to the valid model {type:'last24Hours',...}, yet the corrections list is non-empty and every entry is 'lastHour'. Both tests pass.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/time-range/value-corrected-init.spec.ts`](defect-repros/time-range/value-corrected-init.spec.ts)
  Fix: Emit a correction only once per distinct written value (e.g. remember the last reported input) and skip the initial null write of NgModel.
- **[time-range] Built-in label of a duration preset is computed from the stored startDateTime to now: stale start shows a different duration; future or unparseable start makes the formatter throw during render** — reproduced.
  time-range-title.ts:111-119 formats dateFormatter.duration(deserialize(startDateTime), today(), \[unit\]). checkAndCorrectTimeRangeValue keeps any truthy startDateTime (time-range.service.ts:227). DateFormatter.duration throws 'Invalid date' for an invalid date or for start > end (node\_modules/@koobiq/date-formatter/index.esm.js:2795-2799, checkDates). Repro: {type:'lastHour', startDateTime: now-3h} renders 'последние 3 часа' while value().type is 'lastHour'. A start one minute in the future, or 'not-a-date', makes fixture.detectChanges() throw 'Invalid date'. All three tests pass.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/time-range/start-label.spec.ts`](defect-repros/time-range/start-label.spec.ts)
  Fix: Label duration presets from their type's configured units (as the editor options do), or recompute startDateTime on write, and never let the trigger's computed throw.
- **[timepicker] A time typed into an empty field takes its milliseconds (and in HH:mm its seconds) from the current time; typing exactly max fails kbqTimepickerHigherThenMax** — reproduced.
  timepicker.directive.ts:931 `const date = this.value() || this.dateAdapter.today()`. In HH:mm, '15:00' matches HOURS\_MINUTES\_SECONDS\_REGEXP (seconds group optional, timepicker.constants.ts:25), so :949-952 take `seconds = getSeconds(date)`. :973 always takes `getMilliseconds(date)`. maxValidator :995-1003 compares with compareDateTime, which goes down to milliseconds. The validation example (timepicker-field-validation-example.ts) sets max = today().startOf('hour').plus({hours: 1}). Repro with today() mocked to 14:23:47.123 and max 15:00:00.000: typing '15:00' leaves the input showing '15:00', the control holds 15:00:47.123 and has kbqTimepickerHigherThenMax. In HH:mm:ss, '15:00:00' still carries .123 ms and fails max. When the field already has a value, these parts come from the previous value instead.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/timepicker/now-millis-fail-max.spec.ts`](defect-repros/timepicker/now-millis-fail-max.spec.ts)
  Fix: Build the typed time with zero milliseconds, and zero seconds in HH:mm when starting from today(), or compare against min/max only at the precision of the format.
- **[timepicker] Pasted 12-hour times: 12 pm becomes the next day's 00:xx, 12 am moves a day, hours 13-19 are accepted, '1:30PM' is read as 01:30** — reproduced.
  AM\_PM\_FORMAT\_REGEXP (timepicker.constants.ts:28) `^(\[0-1\]?\[0-9\]):(\[0-5\]?\[0-9\]) (\[ap\]\[m\]?$)` accepts hours 0-19 and requires a space. getDateFromTimeString :942-948 adds 12 for every 'p' (12 pm becomes 24) and for '12 a' (also 24 instead of 0). createDateTime :966-974 then uses Luxon set(), which rolls hour 24 over to 00 of the next day. '1:30PM' fails the AM/PM regex, so formatUserPaste :613-627 drops 'PM' and returns 01:30:00. Repro starting from 2020-01-10T08:00: '12:30 pm' gives 2020-01-11T00:30 (input '00:30'); '12:30 am' gives 2020-01-11T00:30; '15:00 am' is accepted as 15:00; '13:00 pm' gives 2020-01-11T01:00; '1:30PM' gives 01:30. The existing '12:3 aM' unit test checks only the time, so the day shift goes unnoticed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/timepicker/12h-paste.spec.ts`](defect-repros/timepicker/12h-paste.spec.ts)
  Fix: Map 12 pm to 12 and 12 am to 0, limit 12-hour hours to 1-12, and allow an optional space before am/pm.
- **[timepicker] Pasted text without digits or with more than three digit groups is written as is; the value stays and no kbqTimepickerParse error appears** — reproduced.
  When the text has no hours group or does not match the generic regex at all (4+ digit groups), formatUserPaste :617-621 writes the raw text into the input. onPaste :477-481 then returns without calling onChange. On blur, onBlur :465-469 calls onInput, which sets lastValueValid=false (:494). For non-empty text, onInput :516-522 returns without onChange or any re-validation, so parseValidator :979-983 never runs. Repro for 'abc' and '1:2:3:4' after focus, paste and blur: the input shows the text, the control stays VALID with the old 10:30 value and errorState() is false. A manual updateValueAndValidity() then reports kbqTimepickerParse, so only the missing re-validation hides it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/timepicker/garbage-paste.spec.ts`](defect-repros/timepicker/garbage-paste.spec.ts)
  Fix: Re-run validation (validatorOnChange / updateValueAndValidity) whenever the text cannot be parsed, on paste and on blur.
- **[timepicker] Without a form control, clearing the text and leaving the field keeps the previous time in value()** — reproduced.
  Backspace and Delete are a no-op in onKeyDown :543-549. On blur, onBlur :465-469 calls onInput, and onInput :516-519 only calls onChange(null) for empty text, never setValue(null). onChange is noop without forms (:338), so valueState and the public value() (:250) keep the old time. With a form control, the raw valueChanges subscription (:1053) happens to reset it. Repro with `\[value\]` bound to 2020-01-10T10:30: after clearing and blur, the input is '' and empty() is true, but value() is still 10:30. A following Up arrow renders '11:30', so the stale value comes back.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/timepicker/clear-without-form.spec.ts`](defect-repros/timepicker/clear-without-form.spec.ts)
  Fix: Set the value to null (setValue(null)) in onInput when the text is empty, not only report null to onChange.
- **[timepicker] value() takes the bound control's raw valueChanges (an ISO string stays a string, arrow keys then break); the subscription is never unsubscribed** — reproduced.
  setControl :1053 `this.control.valueChanges.subscribe((value) => this.valueState.set(value))` has no deserialize and no takeUntilDestroyed. FormControl.setValue calls writeValue (:592-594, which deserializes and renders) before emitting valueChanges, so the raw value overwrites the parsed one. The adapter supports ISO strings (deserialize, also used by min/maxValidator :986/:996). Repro: control.setValue('2024-05-17T10:30:00') renders '10:30', but typeof value() is 'string'. Up then throws, because incrementTime :798-836 reads getHours(string) as undefined, which becomes NaN, and Luxon rejects it. This differs from the description: the arrow keys throw rather than compute an invalid date. Leak repro: on destroy the form field releases its own valueChanges subscription but the timepicker's stays. The destroyed instance's value() keeps receiving control values, and each new instance adds one more leaked subscription. The leak alone would be P3.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/timepicker/raw-control-value.spec.ts`](defect-repros/timepicker/raw-control-value.spec.ts)
  Fix: Pipe the subscription through takeUntilDestroyed and store getValidDateOrNull(dateAdapter.deserialize(value)), or drop it since writeValue already covers model writes.
- **[timezone] KbqTimezoneSelect re-declares the providers of KbqSelect but not KBQ\_PARENT\_POPUP; tooltips in its panel (including the overflow tooltip) never get the parent-closed signal** — reproduced.
  timezone-select.component.ts:79-100 re-declares KbqFormFieldControl, the cleaner, KBQ\_OPTION\_PARENT\_COMPONENT, kbqSiblingPopupProvider and KbqSelectHiddenItemsMeasurer, but not `{ provide: KBQ\_PARENT\_POPUP, useExisting: ... }`, which KbqSelect has at select.component.ts:244. Providers are not inherited by a decorated subclass, which the comment at :94-95 itself says. tooltip.component.ts:305 injects KBQ\_PARENT\_POPUP as optional and gets null. The only hide-on-close hook, at :891 (`this.parentPopup?.closedStream...subscribe(() => this.hide())`), is reached through timezone-option.directive.ts:337, but it does nothing here. The panel closes and detaches the option from the document, so no mouseleave fires. The repro passes: after select.close() the select's closedStream emitted once and the option is disconnected, yet tooltip.isOpen is still true and `.kbq-tooltip\_\_content` is still in the DOM. The CONTROL test in the same file shows kbq-select hides the tooltip in the same flow. Users see this when they hover an option whose city list overflows for 400 ms or more and then click it or press Escape: the hint stays on screen after the panel is gone.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/timezone/parent-popup.spec.ts`](defect-repros/timezone/parent-popup.spec.ts)
  Fix: Add `{ provide: KBQ\_PARENT\_POPUP, useExisting: KbqTimezoneSelect }` to the providers of KbqTimezoneSelect.
- **[timezone] With foldDiacritics, the highlight folds diacritics but the city list is narrowed without folding, so an option found through a folded city shows an empty city list** — reproduced.
  timezone-option.component.html:22-28 pipes `timezone.cities | citiesByFilter: highlightText()` before `kbqHighlightBackground: ... : foldDiacritics()`. citiesByFilter (cities-by-filter.pipe.ts:10) calls filterCitiesBySearchString, which builds a plain case-insensitive RegExp with no folding (timezone.utils.ts:234-239), and returns '' when nothing matches. The search example finds zones with createSearchPredicate, which folds (core/search/search-base.ts:121-136; example :116-123). tooltipViewValue (timezone-option.component.ts:83-85) uses the same unfolded narrowing. The repro passes. For a zone with city 'Brasilia' and cities 'Rio de Janeiro, São Paulo', the folded predicate matches the query 'sao', yet the rendered `.kbq-timezone-option\_\_cities` is empty, has no \<mark>, and tooltipViewValue lacks 'São Paulo'. In the CONTROL test, 'paulo' keeps and marks 'São Paulo'. The example data is ASCII-only, so this hits consumer data with diacritics (São Paulo, Zürich, Cyrillic ё).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/timezone/fold-diacritics-cities.spec.ts`](defect-repros/timezone/fold-diacritics-cities.spec.ts)
  Fix: Pass foldDiacritics into filterCitiesBySearchString/citiesByFilter and match with the folded search helpers (normalizeSearchValue/findSearchMatchRanges) when it is set.
- **[title] ngAfterViewInit override skips the tooltip trigger's version, so a kbq-title tooltip does not close with a parent pop-up (KBQ\_PARENT\_POPUP)** — reproduced.
  title.directive.ts:202-256 overrides ngAfterViewInit and never calls super.ngAfterViewInit(). The only subscription to parentPopup.closedStream is in the base hook at tooltip.component.ts:890-892, and the comment at tooltip.component.ts:813-815 says KbqTitleDirective skips it. select.component.ts:244 and tree-select.component.ts:218 provide KBQ\_PARENT\_POPUP; KbqOptionTooltip calls super (select-option.directive.ts:79-80). Repro: a fake parent pop-up wraps a kbq-title and a plain kbqTooltip. When closedStream emits, the control kbqTooltip closes but the title tooltip stays open (isOpen true, .kbq-tooltip still in the overlay). Browser caveat: if the browser reports a 0x0 ResizeObserver entry for the detached measured element, the debounced resize path (title.directive.ts:217-220) would hide the tooltip about 100 ms late instead of never; only a real browser can show which.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/title/parent-popup-close.spec.ts`](defect-repros/title/parent-popup-close.spec.ts)
  Fix: Call super.ngAfterViewInit() at the start of KbqTitleDirective.ngAfterViewInit.
- **[title] Mutations are throttled without a trailing emission, so a second content change within 100 ms is dropped** — reproduced.
  title.directive.ts:222-228 uses throttleTime(this.debounceInterval) with no config. In rxjs 7.8.2 that means leading: true, trailing: false (node\_modules/rxjs/dist/esm/internal/operators/throttle.js:6). Repro uses the real ContentObserver/MutationObserver: with the tooltip open on 'Alpha', the text changes to 'Beta' (handled) and to 'Gamma' 50 ms later. After 1 s the host shows 'Gamma' but directive.content and the open tooltip still read 'Beta'. Only the next mouseenter or focus (handleElementEnter, :260-263) refreshes the text. The overflow verdict goes stale the same way.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/title/throttle-no-trailing.spec.ts`](defect-repros/title/throttle-no-trailing.spec.ts)
  Fix: Use throttleTime(100, asyncScheduler, { leading: true, trailing: true }) or debounce the mutation stream.
- **[title] Default content is the container textContent, so several #kbqTitleText elements separated only by markup whitespace run together** — reproduced.
  viewValue is (this.parent.textContent || '').trim() (title.directive.ts:123-125), and resolvedContent falls back to it (:164-166). Angular's default preserveWhitespaces: false removes whitespace-only text nodes between elements. The title-multiple-text docs example (packages/docs-examples/components/title/title-multiple-text/title-multiple-text-example.ts:12-14) therefore produces 'Status:A long value…'; the visible 4px gap comes only from CSS. Repro, with the example's markup copied into a test host: directive.content and the rendered tooltip text are both 'Status:A long value that does not fit and is truncated with an ellipsis'.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/title/multi-text-joined.spec.ts`](defect-repros/title/multi-text-joined.spec.ts)
  Fix: Build the default content from the #kbqTitleText elements joined with a space, or give the example explicit content or an &ngsp; separator.
- **[toast] Toast in a consumer-hosted kbq-toast-container is not in the root service records: close/Escape only emit read, hover pauses the root stack** — reproduced.
  toast-container.component.ts:254 provides KBQ\_TOAST\_STACK as useExisting KbqToastService (the root service). createToast (294-299) registers nothing with the service. close() (toast.component.ts:152-155) emits stack.read and calls stack.hide(id), which finds no record and returns (toast.service.ts:276-281). Escape takes the same path (toast.component.ts:76). Hover calls stack.setHovered (toast.service.ts:327-339), which adds the id to the root hoveredToasts and pauses the root heartbeat (184-186). Repro: the close button and Escape left the hosted toast on screen with its view not destroyed, while service.read emitted its data. Hovering the hosted toast set service.hovered() to true and kept a 1000 ms root toast alive for 10 s.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/toast/hosted-container-stack.spec.ts`](defect-repros/toast/hosted-container-stack.spec.ts)
  Fix: Give a hosted container its own stack that removes its own views on hide() and scopes hover/focus to it, instead of borrowing the root service.
- **[toast] Escape closes a toast that has no close button, bypassing its Cancel action** — reproduced.
  toast.component.ts:76 binds '(keydown.esc)': 'close()' unconditionally, whatever closeButton is (115). toast.en.mdx:49 recommends a progress toast without the x button and with a Cancel action, and :63 says the x button 'only hides the toast'. Escape does exactly that hide. Repro: a toast with closeButton: false and a Cancel action; Escape pressed while Cancel is focused removed the toast (service.toasts is empty) and the Cancel handler never ran. The operation goes on with no visible UI left to cancel it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/toast/escape-closes-undismissible.spec.ts`](defect-repros/toast/escape-closes-undismissible.spec.ts)
  Fix: Close on Escape only when the toast is dismissible (closeButton truthy), or make Escape dismissal configurable.
- **[toggle] Static tabIndex, aria-label and aria-labelledby stay on the \<kbq-toggle> host as well as reaching the checkbox** — reproduced.
  toggle.component.ts:58-66: the host object binds only class, id and state classes. No \[attr.tabindex\], \[attr.aria-label\] or \[attr.aria-labelledby\] null binding clears what Angular writes there from static template attributes. The inputs that take those attributes are toggle.component.ts:81-82 (aria-label, aria-labelledby) and 181-184 (tabIndex), and the template forwards them to the checkbox at toggle.component.html:10,11,19. The repro passes. \<kbq-toggle aria-label="Notifications"> leaves aria-label on the host. aria-labelledby does the same. \<kbq-toggle tabIndex="5"> leaves tabindex="5" on the host (host.tabIndex === 5) and on the input, so there are two sequential tab stops. FocusMonitor watches the host with checkChildren (toggle.component.ts:247-248), so focus on the host also gets the cdk-keyboard-focused styling (\_toggle-theme.scss:32-47), but Space does nothing there. The bound forms \[aria-label\] and \[tabIndex\] leave nothing on the host (control test). The axe part is weaker than the finding says. axe 4.12.1 puts aria-prohibited-attr on the host under 'incomplete' (needs review), not under violations, because the inner checkbox carries the same name as subtree text (axe.js:27505-27511). toHaveNoViolations() therefore would not catch it.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/toggle/static-attrs-on-host.spec.ts`](defect-repros/toggle/static-attrs-on-host.spec.ts)
  Fix: Add '\[attr.tabindex\]': 'null', '\[attr.aria-label\]': 'null' and '\[attr.aria-labelledby\]': 'null' to the host bindings, as Material's checkbox does.
- **[tooltip] A cancelled show counts for the instant-show window (pinned tooltips shown from code count too)** — reproduced.
  KbqTooltipComponent.show (tooltip.component.ts:165-175) calls delayTracker.markShown() right after super.show(delay), and KbqPopUp.show only schedules the visibility timer (pop-up.ts:129-145). A mouseleave before the enter delay clears that timer (pop-up.ts:155-157), but the timestamp is already recorded, so shouldSkipEnterDelay() (tooltip.component.ts:116-118) lets the next tooltip skip its delay. The repro passes: the pointer stays 100 ms on the first trigger, which never emits visibleChange(true), and the second tooltip is open 1 ms after mouseenter instead of after 400 ms. A second test shows that a manual tooltip shown and hidden from code also opens the window; that part is arguably by design. Effect in normal use: once the pointer has crossed any trigger, tooltips appear instantly or flash while the pointer sweeps across a toolbar.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tooltip/cancelled-show-window.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tooltip/cancelled-show-window.spec.ts)
  Fix: Call markShown() when the pop-up actually becomes visible (on the instance's visibleChange(true)), not when the show is scheduled.
- **[tooltip] Clearing the content of an open tooltip leaves an empty tooltip on screen until it is closed** — reproduced.
  The content setter (tooltip.component.ts:413-417) calls updateData() (1102-1120), which pushes '' into the open instance and re-renders it. The empty-content guards exist only on the way in: KbqTooltipTrigger.show (956-958) and KbqTooltipComponent.show (166-168). Nothing hides an already open tooltip. The repro passes: after content='' and 1 s, trigger.isOpen is true and .kbq-tooltip.kbq-tooltip\_visible is still in the overlay with empty text, which is a padded empty box. This happens, for example, with a pinned validation hint whose message is cleared while the hint is shown.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tooltip/empty-content-open.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tooltip/empty-content-open.spec.ts)
  Fix: Hide the tooltip in the content setter or updateData() when the new content is empty.
- **[tooltip] show() or kbqVisible right after a pointer-originated focus is swallowed by the keyboard-focus gate** — reproduced.
  The gate in KbqTooltipTrigger.show (tooltip.component.ts:971-973) reads triggerName. Only the DOM listeners set it (createListener, pop-up-trigger.ts:820-829, and the keydown listener at 680), so a programmatic show() or updateVisible() (356-368) after a mouse focus still sees 'focus' with lastFocusOrigin='mouse' and returns. updateVisible sets visible=true before calling show(), so kbqVisible stays latched at true and binding true again does nothing. The repro passes. On a button with the default triggers, after focusVia(button,'mouse') setting \[kbqVisible\]=true leaves isOpen false, no .kbq-tooltip and tooltipVisible === true. trigger.show(0) is also swallowed, and the same call opens the tooltip once a blur has changed triggerName. A typical case is a 'Copied' hint opened from a click handler.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tooltip/show-after-pointer-focus.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tooltip/show-after-pointer-focus.spec.ts)
  Fix: Apply the focus-origin gate only in the focus listener path (or reset triggerName in programmatic show/updateVisible).
- **[top-bar] Container that is itself the kbqOverflowItems host spaces its items twice (gap: unset overridden by the placement rules)** — confirmed in the source.
  I compiled packages/components/top-bar/top-bar.scss with sass. The CSS keeps the source order. First comes `.kbq-top-bar .kbq-top-bar-container.kbq-overflow-items, ...:has(> .kbq-overflow-items) { gap: unset }` (top-bar.scss:53-59), specificity (0,3,0). Later comes `.kbq-top-bar .kbq-top-bar-container.kbq-top-bar-container\_end { gap: var(--kbq-top-bar-container-end-gap) }` (top-bar.scss:74-78), also (0,3,0). The later rule wins, so `gap: unset` never applies, and the same holds for container\_start (top-bar.scss:66-72). In the host form, the mixin rule `.kbq-top-bar-container.kbq-overflow-items > .kbq-overflow-item:not(:last-of-type) { margin-inline-end: var(--kbq-top-bar-container-end-gap) }` (top-bar.scss:8-11, 61-64) still applies, and KbqOverflowItems sets `display:flex` inline (overflow-items.ts:339). Visible items in an end container are then separated by gap + margin, twice `--kbq-size-s`. The start container is affected only when `--kbq-top-bar-container-start-gap` is non-zero, because its default is 0. Hidden items are `position:absolute` (overflow-items.ts:61-62) and take no gap. Every example and e2e scenario uses the wrapper form (e2e.ts:63,170,234; docs-examples/top-bar/\*), so no one renders this form. jsdom cannot show it: unit tests load no component styles, jsdom does no layout, and its cascade ignores specificity (jsdom style-rules.js:177).
  Fix: Raise the overflow-host rule above the placement rules (move it after them or increase its specificity) so that `gap: unset` wins for the `.kbq-overflow-items` host form.
- **[tree] A plain click, and a vertical key with autoSelect, clear the selection model and then select: the form gets undefined/\[\] first, and re-selecting the selected node reports twice, emits selectionChange and dirties the control** — reproduced.
  tree-selection.component.ts:1121-1123 (click) and 1094-1098 (key) call selectionModel.clear() then toggle(); bindSelectionModel 808-813 calls reportValue()/onChange on every `changed` emission, so the form receives getSelectedValues() after the clear (undefined in single, \[\] in multiple) and then the new value. Lines 1057-1064 run setSelectedOptionsByKey even when the key manager could not move (Down on the last row), and emitChangeEvent runs unconditionally (1098, 1128). Angular's onChange marks the control dirty. Repro (3 passing tests): a click on Documents gives valueChanges \[undefined, 'Documents'\]; a click on the selected Pictures gives \[undefined, 'Pictures'\] with selectionChange x1 and dirty=true; Down on the selected last row gives \[undefined, 'Applications'\] and dirty=true.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree/click-double-report.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree/click-double-report.spec.ts)
  Fix: Use selectionModel.setSelection(option.data), which emits once and not at all when nothing changes, and emit selectionChange only when it reports a change.
- **[tree] An empty query reaches the default matcher as null and becomes /null/gi, so clearing the search leaves the tree filtered to nodes containing "null"** — reproduced.
  flat-tree-control.filters.ts:18 `const viewValue = value || null`; flat-tree-control.ts:9-11 builds RegExp(escapeRegExp(secondViewValue), 'gi'), and escapeRegExp returns falsy input unchanged (core/highlight/highlight-base.ts:8-14), so RegExp(null) is /null/gi. filterNodes publishes `value || result` (flat-tree-control.ts:136), so a non-empty result keeps KbqTreeFlatDataSource on filterHandler (flat-data-source.ts:186-191). base-tree-control.ts:36-58 also stays locked on expand/collapse/toggle. Repro (passing): with a 'Nullable' node, filterNodes('doc') then filterNodes('') leaves filterValue = \[Types, Nullable\], the data source renders only \['Types','Nullable'\] and collapse() is a no-op. A contrast test without such a label shows the full tree again.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree/empty-query-null.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree/empty-query-null.spec.ts)
  Fix: Treat an empty or null query as no filter: return every node from FilterByViewValue, or have defaultCompareViewValues return true for an empty query, so filterNodes publishes an empty filter value.
- **[tree] With autoSelect off, a plain click on the only selected node deselects it although noUnselectLast is on** — reproduced.
  tree-selection.component.ts:1124-1126: the plain-click branch with autoSelect off calls selectionModel.toggle() without canDeselectLast. The Ctrl branch (1114-1118) and Space/Enter (1178) apply the guard. noUnselectLast defaults to true outside checkbox mode (398-400). Affected examples are single selection with \[autoSelect\]="false": tree-action-button-example.ts:119, tree-selection-separate-from-focus-example.ts:123, tree-toggle-on-click-example.ts:106. Repro (passing): with autoSelect=false and noUnselectLast=true, clicking the selected Pictures empties the selection and the control value becomes undefined. A contrast test shows Space on the same node keeps it selected.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree/click-ignores-no-unselect-last.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree/click-ignores-no-unselect-last.spec.ts)
  Fix: Return early from the plain-click toggle branch when canDeselectLast(option) is false, as the Ctrl branch does.
- **[tree] The keyboard does not check whether the active option or the tree is disabled (Space/Enter, vertical keys, custom selectAllHandler)** — reproduced.
  handleKeydown (tree-selection.component.ts:984-1065) never checks `disabled`. toggleFocusedOption 1176-1181 checks only selectable(), and setSelectedOptionsByKey 1094 checks only autoSelect && selectable(). On a disabled tree every option is disabled, so ListKeyManager.setActiveItemByIndex cannot move, but 1057-1064 still re-selects the current active item. Only the click path (tree-option.component.ts:608) and selectAllOptions (1257) check disabled. A custom selectAllHandler is called at 992-993 whatever the state. The option keeps DOM focus when the tree is disabled, so its keydown still bubbles to the host listener. Repro (2 passing tests): after disabling the tree with Pictures active, Space selects Pictures and emits selectionChange. With Documents selected and Pictures active, Down on the disabled tree moves the selection to Pictures.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree/keyboard-ignores-disabled.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree/keyboard-ignores-disabled.spec.ts)
  Fix: Return from handleKeydown's selecting branches (and before calling selectAllHandler) when the tree is disabled, and skip the toggle/autoSelect when the active option is disabled.
- **[tree] Padding corrections are pixel amounts applied in the indent's units (first level loses 2, a row without toggle gains 24)** — reproduced.
  padding.directive.ts:48-56 adds iconWidth (24, meant as px) and subtracts border (2, px) to parsedIndent().value, and 97 appends the indent's own units. The existing padding.directive.spec.ts:127-133 already pins `${10 - 2}em` = 8em for the toggle case. Repro (passing): with kbqTreeNodePaddingIndent='10em', rows with a toggle get \['8em','20em','30em'\] and rows without one get \['32em','44em','54em'\]. The 24px toggle room becomes 24em, and the root row is 2em short instead of 2px.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree/padding-units.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree/padding-units.spec.ts)
  Fix: Keep the pixel corrections in px, e.g. emit calc(\<level\*value>\<units> + \<extra>px), or convert only when units are px.
- **[tree] The Shift-click anchor is the option active before the last keyboard/programmatic move, not the one active now** — reproduced.
  selectActiveOptions (tree-selection.component.ts:1135-1138) anchors on keyManager.previousActiveItemIndex. ListKeyManager.setActiveItem (list-key-manager.ts:217-227) sets it to the index being left on every move, so after Down from row 1 to row 2 it points at row 1. The pointer focus moves the active index through updateActiveItem (1607) without re-anchoring, and the Shift branch of setSelectedOptionsByClick (1108-1113) does not re-anchor either. Repro (passing, with focusin dispatched before the click as a browser does): in multiple="keyboard", click Pictures, then Down (selection becomes Documents; previousActiveItemIndex stays 1), then Shift+click Applications. The range runs from the now-deselected Pictures, the noUnselectLast guard (1156) skips every row, and the selection stays \['Documents'\] instead of Documents..Applications. The same anchor code exists in list-selection.component.ts:1028 and select.component.ts:1952.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree/shift-click-stale-anchor.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree/shift-click-stale-anchor.spec.ts)
  Fix: Keep a dedicated range anchor that is set to the active index after every non-Shift move or click, and use it instead of previousActiveItemIndex.
- **[tree-select] Tag remove control `\<i kbq-icon kbqTagRemove>` is aria-hidden while focusable (axe aria-hidden-focus)** — reproduced.
  tree-select.html:45-50 puts kbqTagRemove on `\<i kbq-icon=...>` with \[tabIndex\]="0". KbqIcon binds \[attr.aria-hidden\] to resolvedAriaHidden() (icon/icon.component.ts:44,72-79), and with no explicit input that falls back to defaultAriaHidden=true. KbqTagRemove adds role="button" and a tabindex on the same host (tags/tag.component.ts:857-871). The repro renders a multiple tree-select with two values; each remove icon has role=button, tabindex=0 and aria-hidden="true", so a Tab stop is hidden from assistive technology (WCAG 4.1.2). Note: under jsdom axe's aria-hidden-focus rule reports nothing for it (no layout), so an axe test that renders tags under jsdom would not catch it either. KbqSelect uses the same markup (select/select.html:54-59).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree-select/tag-remove-hidden.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree-select/tag-remove-hidden.spec.ts)
  Fix: Put a static aria-hidden="false" on the remove `\<i kbq-icon>` (KbqIcon reflects an explicit value), or have KbqTagRemove opt its host out of the icon's aria-hidden default.
- **[tree-select] Single-mode pick clears the model then selects: reports undefined to the form first; re-picking the selected option re-emits selectionChange and marks dirty** — reproduced.
  tree-selection.component.ts:1121-1123 (click) and 1094-1098 (setSelectedOptionsByKey) run selectionModel.clear() and then toggle(). Each emits `changed`, and tree-select.component.ts:1036-1042 calls reportValue() on every change. Repro results: ArrowDown reports \[undefined,'Banana'\]. Clicking the selected 'Apple' reports \[undefined,'Apple'\], emits selectionChange once and sets control.dirty. Clicking another option without a preceding mouseenter (a programmatic click) is worse: \[undefined,'Banana',undefined,'Banana'\] and two selectionChange events. The click's userInteraction closes the panel first (1109-1115). setSelectedOptionsByClick then moves the key manager (tree-selection 1108-1110), and the tree-select's keyManager.change handler takes a move while closed as navigation and calls selectViaInteraction() again (tree-select.component.ts:1895-1905). A real mouse hover first sets the active item silently through updateActiveItem, so a real mouse click gets the single \[undefined, value\] pair.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree-select/single-pick-reports-undefined.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree-select/single-pick-reports-undefined.spec.ts)
  Fix: Drop the redundant clear() before toggle in single mode (a single SelectionModel already replaces), skip re-selecting an already selected node, and stop the closed-panel keyManager.change branch from re-entering selectViaInteraction during a click.
- **[tree-select] PageUp/PageDown in the search field move DOM focus onto the option and leave it there** — reproduced.
  The PAGE\_UP/PAGE\_DOWN branches (tree-select.component.ts:1663-1670) move the key manager. FocusKeyManager.setActiveItem focuses the option, and keyManager.change also calls scrollActiveOptionIntoView, which runs activeItem.focus() (1893-1894, 1966-1968). Unlike the LEFT/RIGHT branch (1648-1652) and the catch-all branch (1714-1718), these branches never call search.focus(). KbqSelectSearch lets PageUp/PageDown bubble (core/select/common.ts:151-153). Repro: the page size is emulated with keyManager.withScrollSize(2) because jsdom measures 0. PageDown in the search field makes option\[2\] the activeElement, while a control test shows ArrowDown returns focus to the search field.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree-select/search-loses-focus.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree-select/search-loses-focus.spec.ts)
  Fix: Refocus the search field after PAGE\_UP/PAGE\_DOWN the way the arrow branches do.
- **[tree-select] Hovering an option gives it DOM focus, taking focus out of the search field** — reproduced.
  KbqTreeOption (mouseenter) runs onMouseenter, then focus('mouse'), then kbqFocusAndReveal, which calls element.focus() (tree/tree-option.component.ts:136,645-649,509-525; core/utils/dom.ts:22-34). The tree-select forces tree.inSelect=true (tree-select.component.ts:1092), and nothing refocuses the search on hover. Repro: with the search field focused, a mouseenter on option\[3\] makes that option document.activeElement. KbqSelect avoids this with an ActiveDescendantKeyManager and setActiveItem on hover (select.component.ts:379,2444; core/option/option.ts:436-442). In single mode the next letter typed then selects the hovered option (defect 5).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree-select/search-loses-focus.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree-select/search-loses-focus.spec.ts)
  Fix: Highlight hovered options without moving DOM focus while a search field is present (e.g. updateActiveItem + kbq-focused class), or return focus to the search after hover.
- **[tree-select] Footer slot projects `kbq-select-footer,\[kbq-tree-select-footer\]`: attribute form `\[kbq-select-footer\]` not rendered; `\[kbq-tree-select-footer\]` gets no directive/styles; footer controls unreachable by Tab and Enter/Space prevented** — reproduced.
  tree-select.html:135 uses select="kbq-select-footer,\[kbq-tree-select-footer\]", while KbqSelectFooter's selector is 'kbq-select-footer, \[kbq-select-footer\]' (core/select/common.ts:58-62) and KbqSelect projects both forms (select/select.html:171). The template has no catch-all ng-content. Repro: `\<div kbq-select-footer>` is absent from the document with the panel open; the element form renders with kbq-select\_\_footer (control test); `\<div kbq-tree-select-footer>` renders without that class. Enter on a focused footer button is defaultPrevented and toggles the active option to \['Apple'\] (panelKeydownHandler ENTER branch 1671-1679, then tree.handleKeydown, then toggleFocusedOption, tree-selection 1030-1031), and Tab on it closes the panel (1637-1642). The dev app's `\<div kbq-select-footer class="dev-custom-footer">` (components-dev/tree-select/template.html:51,342) is therefore not rendered.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree-select/footer-projection.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree-select/footer-projection.spec.ts)
  Fix: Project `kbq-select-footer,\[kbq-select-footer\]` like KbqSelect, and ignore Enter/Space/Tab in panelKeydownHandler when the event target is inside the footer.
- **[tree-select] Writing null/undefined to a tree whose getValue returns that value for a node selects that node instead of clearing** — reproduced.
  setSelectionByValue passes \[value\] to tree.setOptionsFromValues even for null/undefined in single mode (tree-select.component.ts:1869-1870). That method resolves each value with treeControl.hasValue, a strict-equality find (tree-selection.component.ts:1382-1391; flat-tree-control.ts:119-121). Only a null written before content init is dropped (1103-1105). The tree's own writeValue treats null/undefined as a clear (tree-selection 1322-1332). Repro with nodes {value:null} and {value:undefined}: control.reset() selects 'Not chosen' and shows it in the trigger, and setValue(undefined) selects 'Unset'. The skipped describe 'when resetting the value by setting null or undefined' in the existing spec covers the same path.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree-select/null-write-selects-node.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree-select/null-write-selects-node.spec.ts)
  Fix: In applyPendingValue/setSelectionByValue clear the model for null/undefined in single mode instead of resolving them as node values.
- **[username] type="inherit" overrides the display of the stacked (and text) layout** — confirmed in the source.
  In the compiled CSS (dist/scss-compiled/username/username.css), the stacked rule is `.kbq-username.kbq-username\_stacked:not(:has(>.kbq-username\_\_custom-view))` and the inherit rule is `.kbq-username.kbq-username\_inherit:not(:has(>.kbq-username\_\_custom-view))`. Both have specificity (0,3,0), and the custom-view variants are also equal. The inherit rule (username.scss:77-82) comes after the stacked rule (username.scss:37-46) and the text rule (username.scss:61-65), so its `display: inherit` wins over `display: flex; flex-flow: column` and over `display: inline`. Only the inline rule opts out, via `:not(.kbq-username\_inherit)` at username.scss:48. The truncate rule (username.scss:67-75) still targets the stacked parts. `canTruncate()` (username.ts:166) is true for stacked, so `kbq-title` stays attached (username.html:7-8, 17-18). Under a block parent the host becomes a block, and the primary and secondary spans are inline boxes on one line. `overflow` and `text-overflow` do not apply to inline boxes, so no ellipsis appears. The docs playground offers this combination (username-playground-example.ts:75-76). e2e.ts:90 renders `inherit` only with `inline`. This is a CSS cascade issue, which jsdom cannot show.
  Fix: Restrict the `display: inherit` override to inline mode, for example `.kbq-username\_inherit.kbq-username\_inline`, or exclude `\_inherit` only where intended, so that stacked keeps its flex column.

## P3

- **[accordion] Trigger and content id are host bindings, so a consumer-written id is replaced** — confirmed in the source.
  accordion-trigger.directive.ts:9 `'\[attr.id\]': 'triggerId'` and accordion-content.directive.ts:24 `'\[attr.id\]': 'contentId'` are unconditional host attribute bindings to generated values (`${item.id}-trigger` / `-content`, :46-53 / :82-89). A host binding runs after the static attribute is set and overwrites it, so `\<button kbq-accordion-trigger id="x">` ends up with the generated id. There is no `id` input. aria-controls, aria-labelledby and the header's labelledBy (accordion-header.ts:42-44) all reference the generated ids, which is why the override exists. jsdom could show this; I did not reproduce it because the repro budget is reserved for P0–P2.
  Fix: Accept an optional `id` input on the trigger and content directives (falling back to the generated id) and derive aria references from the effective ids.
- **[accordion] aria-controls dangles for an item without content; aria-labelledby dangles for content without a trigger** — confirmed in the source.
  accordion-trigger.directive.ts:15 binds `aria-controls` to `contentId` unconditionally, even when `item.content()` (accordion-item.ts:52) is undefined. accordion-content.directive.ts:32 binds `aria-labelledby` to `triggerId` unconditionally, even with no trigger. By contrast, the header already guards this: accordion-header.ts:43 `this.item.trigger()?.triggerId ?? null`. The missing-content case (AccordionMissingContent, accordion.spec.ts:2668) only asserts that nothing throws. Once such an item is expanded (aria-expanded=true), the reference points at a nonexistent id. I did not reproduce it (P3).
  Fix: Bind aria-controls to `item.content() ? contentId : null` and aria-labelledby to `item.trigger() ? triggerId : null`, as the header does.
- **[accordion] collapsible has no boolean transform, unlike disabled** — confirmed in the source.
  accordion.ts:183 `readonly collapsible = input(true);` has no transform, while accordion.ts:162 gives `disabled` `transform: booleanAttribute`. With strictTemplates (strictAttributeTypes), a static `collapsible="false"` is a TS2322 template error. Without it, the input receives the string "false", so `!this.accordion.collapsible()` at accordion-trigger.directive.ts:57 is false and collapse stays allowed, the opposite of what was written. The repo itself only binds `\[collapsible\]="false"` (accordion.spec.ts:2502). This is a compile-time or typing inconsistency, so I did not reproduce it.
  Fix: Use `input(true, { transform: booleanAttribute })` (an API golden-file change).
- **[accordion] A disabled trigger stays in the Tab order while arrow keys and Home/End skip it** — confirmed in the source.
  A disabled item's trigger gets only `aria-disabled` (accordion-trigger.directive.ts:16), not `disabled` or a negative tabindex. The test at accordion.spec.ts:1304 asserts there is no HTML disabled attribute, so a native button stays tabbable. The key manager is created without a custom skipPredicate (accordion.ts:275), and the installed CDK default is `\_skipPredicateFn = item => item.disabled` (node\_modules/@angular/cdk/fesm2022/\_list-key-manager-chunk.mjs:28). Arrows and Home/End therefore skip the item that Tab reaches. Both behaviours are allowed by WAI-ARIA, but mixing them is inconsistent: a11y polish, not a functional break.
  Fix: Pick one policy: either `withAllowedDisabledItems`/skipPredicate(() => false) to keep disabled headers arrow-reachable, or remove them from the Tab order.
- **[actions-panel] The panel has role="dialog" without an accessible name, and the config has no option to set one** — reproduced.
  The role comes from CdkDialogContainer's host bindings: attr.role=\_config.role defaults to 'dialog' (dialog.mjs:38, 267), and aria-label / aria-labelledby come from \_config.ariaLabel / \_ariaLabelledByQueue, both null or empty (dialog.mjs:54-55). KbqActionsPanelConfig (actions-panel-config.ts:28-108) declares no ariaLabel, ariaLabelledBy or role, and nothing in the container fills \_ariaLabelledByQueue. The repro shows role=dialog with no aria-label or aria-labelledby, and axe reports `aria-dialog-name`. One runtime escape hatch exists: openDialog spreads `...config` into CDK's DialogConfig (actions-panel.ts:177-178), so an `ariaLabel` passed through a type cast would work, but it is not part of the typed API. Rated P3 (a11y polish), although it fails the repo's own rule that components pass every axe check.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/actions-panel/dialog-without-name.spec.ts`](defect-repros/actions-panel/dialog-without-name.spec.ts)
  Fix: Add ariaLabel/ariaLabelledBy to KbqActionsPanelConfig with a locale default, or drop role=dialog for a fitting role such as toolbar or region.
- **[actions-panel] A replacement panel closed before the replaced one finishes closing never emits or completes beforeOpened/afterOpened** — reproduced.
  When a panel is replaced, open() defers `actionsPanelRef.open()` until the replaced panel's afterClosed (actions-panel.ts:145-149). Closing the replacement first sets its containerInstance to null (actions-panel-ref.ts:87). The deferred open() then returns early at `if (!this.containerInstance) return;` (actions-panel-ref.ts:68-70), so beforeOpened never emits or completes. afterOpened waits for animationDone('visible') (actions-panel-ref.ts:94-104), which never comes because the entrance never starts. In the repro, the service's close() runs while the replaced panel is still exiting, and the replacement emits only \['beforeClosed', 'afterClosed'\]. This contradicts the afterOpened JSDoc ('or once closing cuts its entrance short'), and `firstValueFrom(ref.afterOpened)` would hang. Rated P3 because the panel never opened and the window is a rare race.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/actions-panel/replacement-never-opened.spec.ts`](defect-repros/actions-panel/replacement-never-opened.spec.ts)
  Fix: Complete beforeOpenedSubject and afterOpenedSubject (or emit them) in close() when the panel never started opening.
- **[alert] The `closed` listener sits on the close control's stretched wrapper, so a click in the empty column under the close button emits `closed`** — confirmed in the source.
  alert.component.html:21-25: `(click)="closed.emit()"` is bound to `div.kbq-alert\_\_close-button`, not to the projected control. alert.component.scss:6-8 makes `.kbq-alert` `display: flex` (row) and sets no `align-items`. The wrapper rules (scss:71-79 normal, 124-132 compact) set only margin-top/margin-left. A grep finds no `align-self`/`align-items` for the wrapper anywhere, in alert or in other packages. With the default `normal`/stretch alignment, the wrapper runs from margin-top (10px token, alert-tokens.scss:17/36) to the bottom of the alert's content box, and it is as wide as the control. Any click in that strip below the button bubbles to the wrapper's listener and emits `closed`. jsdom does no layout or hit-testing, so it cannot show this. Rated P3: the extra hit area is a narrow column directly under the close control, so accidental dismissal is a near miss on the control.
  Fix: Move the `(click)` onto the projected control (e.g. a host listener in KbqAlertCloseButton reporting to the alert through a narrow token), or give the wrapper `align-self: flex-start`.
- **[alert] `kbq-alert\_\_button-stack\_has-button` follows any `kbq-button` at any depth of the content, not only one in the control slot** — reproduced.
  alert.component.ts:112 `button = contentChild(KbqButton)` keeps the default `descendants: true`, and it is not scoped to `\[kbq-alert-control\]`. alert.component.html:15 binds `\[class.kbq-alert\_\_button-stack\_has-button\]="button()"`. Repro: with a link-only control (`\<a kbq-alert-control>`), the modifier is applied (1) when the close control is `\<button kbq-button kbq-alert-close-button>` and (2) when a `kbq-button` sits inside a `\<span>` in the text. A control test with no kbq-button shows the modifier absent, so the assertion can fail. All 3 tests passed. The effect is the wrong button-stack padding-top (`--kbq-size-m` instead of the button-stack-padding-top token, scss:67-69/120-122). Visual only.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/alert/has-button-any-depth.spec.ts`](defect-repros/alert/has-button-any-depth.spec.ts)
  Fix: Derive the modifier from buttons inside the projected `\[kbq-alert-control\]` elements, e.g. a contentChildren of KbqAlertControl that each report a nested KbqButton, rather than from a content-wide KbqButton query.
- **[alert] The re-tint compares the icon's color with the last auto-assigned color, so a consumer color equal to it, set later, is overwritten on the next `alertColor` change** — reproduced.
  alert.component.ts:155-163: the effect re-tints when `icon.color() === this.lastAutoColor` (lastAutoColor at :147) and does not record whether the alert or the consumer set that value. core/common-behaviors/color.ts:49-61: a new `\[color\]` input resets `colorSetInCode`, but `color()` keeps the same value ('error'), so nothing changes visibly. The next `alertColor` change then matches lastAutoColor and calls `icon.color.set(...)`. Repro: (1) on the same icon, with `\[color\]` going from undefined to 'error' after the auto-tint to error, switching alertColor to warning turns the icon `kbq-warning`; (2) replacing the tinted icon with one bound to `\[color\]="'error'"` gives the same result. A control test shows that an explicit color present from the start is kept. All 3 tests passed. Rated P3: it needs a consumer color equal to the last tint, set after it, and the result matches the alert's new status.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/alert/retint-consumer-color.spec.ts`](defect-repros/alert/retint-consumer-color.spec.ts)
  Fix: Track ownership instead of the value: only re-tint while the icon's `colorInput()` is unset (or remember the icon instance and reset lastAutoColor when it changes or when its input is bound).
- **[alert] The dev-mode warning covers only one icon of each kind; two `kbq-icon` or two `kbq-icon-item` render side by side, only the first is tinted, and nothing warns** — reproduced.
  alert.component.ts:105/107 are single `contentChild` queries, so the first icon of each kind wins. alert.component.html:3 `\<ng-content select="\[kbq-icon-item\],\[kbq-icon\]">` projects every top-level match into the slot. The warning at :173 fires only when both `this.icon()` and `this.iconItem()` exist. Repro with alertColor 'error': two `kbq-icon` render in `.kbq-alert\_\_icon`, the first is `kbq-error`, the second stays `kbq-empty`, and a `console.warn` spy is never called. The same holds for two `kbq-icon-item`. 2 tests passed. This is a gap in the dev guard against consumer misuse, so it is hygiene.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/alert/same-kind-icons-no-warning.spec.ts`](defect-repros/alert/same-kind-icons-no-warning.spec.ts)
  Fix: Use contentChildren for both kinds and warn when their combined count exceeds one.
- **[app-switcher] backdropClass has no effect, because no backdrop is rendered** — confirmed in the source.
  hasBackdrop is a private field set to false (app-switcher.ts:657) and nothing ever reassigns it. overlayConfig passes hasBackdrop: false together with backdropClass (787-793), and the CDK creates a backdrop element only when hasBackdrop is true. So the backdropClass input (839, 866-868) and its default only change a field nothing reads. The existing spec checks only how the input maps to the field (app-switcher.spec.ts:394-400). jsdom could show this too, but it is P3 and outside the repro budget.
  Fix: Deprecate or remove backdropClass, or document it as a no-op, since the switcher intentionally renders no backdrop.
- **[app-switcher] The trigger gets no aria-haspopup or aria-expanded** — confirmed in the source.
  The KbqAppSwitcherTrigger host (app-switcher.ts:626-636) binds only class.kbq-app-switcher\_open, class.kbq-active, keydown and touchend. The KbqPopUpTrigger base (core/pop-up/pop-up-trigger.ts) sets no ARIA attributes, imperatively or otherwise. By contrast, KbqDropdownTrigger binds \[attr.aria-expanded\] (dropdown/dropdown-trigger.directive.ts:145). The docs examples add only aria-label, so the open state is never conveyed to assistive technology. jsdom could show the missing attributes, but this is P3 (pattern conformance, no axe rule fails) and outside the repro budget.
  Fix: Bind \[attr.aria-haspopup\]="'menu'" and \[attr.aria-expanded\]="isOpen" on the trigger host.
- **[app-switcher] The default grouping treats a type of 0 as no type and merges types 1 and '1' into one group** — confirmed in the source.
  defaultGroupBy (app-switcher.ts:121-145) tests `if (!app.type)` at :126, so the numeric type 0 is pushed to untyped like ''. It then keys groups by `app.type.toString()` at :129, so 1 and '1' fall into one group. KbqAppSwitcherApp.type is declared `string | number` (app-switcher.ts:79). The docs example uses type '' to mean no type, so the falsy check is intended for '' but also catches 0. The only effect is presentational: apps of type 0 appear as flat rows instead of a group. The 1/'1' merge is arguably acceptable. jsdom could show this trivially, but it is P3 and outside the repro budget.
  Fix: Check `app.type === undefined || app.type === null || app.type === ''` instead of a falsy test; optionally key groups by typeof+value if 1 and '1' must stay apart.
- **[autocomplete] Focusing a field with an unbound kbqAutocomplete throws a TypeError instead of the missing-panel error** — confirmed in the source.
  autocomplete-trigger.directive.ts:616-619: handleFocus evaluates `!this.panelOpen && this.canOpen() && this.autocomplete().openOnFocus()`. panelOpen short-circuits on overlayAttached() being false (182-184) and canOpen() is true, so with no panel bound, `undefined.openOnFocus` throws a TypeError. open(), handleClick and handleInput all go through attachOverlay (805-810), which throws getKbqAutocompleteMissingPanelError(). The spec's AutocompleteWithoutPanel fixture (autocomplete.spec.ts:3916-3928) binds an undefined panel but never focuses the field. This only affects how a misconfiguration is reported, so no repro was run.
  Fix: Guard handleFocus for a missing panel (throw getKbqAutocompleteMissingPanelError or return) before reading openOnFocus.
- **[autocomplete] Clearing the field deselects the option only when kbq-autocomplete sits inside the field's kbq-form-field** — reproduced.
  autocomplete.component.ts:113 injects KBQ\_FORM\_FIELD with {host: true, optional: true} from the panel's own element, so it finds only an ancestor kbq-form-field. The deselect on an empty value (267-276) is wired only through that parentFormField. The trigger never deselects on an empty input (handleInput, autocomplete-trigger.directive.ts:580-604). Repro: after clicking Alabama and typing '' into the field, the option is deselected when the panel is inside the form field (control test) but stays selected when the panel is a sibling of the form field. All docs examples put the panel inside, so the visible effect (a stale kbq-selected option on reopen) needs a placement the unit fixtures use but the docs do not show.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/autocomplete/cleared-field-keeps-selection.spec.ts`](defect-repros/autocomplete/cleared-field-keeps-selection.spec.ts)
  Fix: Clear the selection from the trigger, which already knows its own value and form field, instead of from the panel's host-scoped form-field injection.
- **[autocomplete] aria-autocomplete="both" even where no inline hint can be drawn** — confirmed in the source.
  The host binding at autocomplete-trigger.directive.ts:148 is `exposesPanel ? (textMode() && inlineHint() ? "both" : "list") : null`, which depends only on the inputs. resolveInlineHint (1179-1204) returns '' when there is no form field (`!this.formField`, 1187) or when kbqIsTextLaidOutFromStart(getComputedStyle) is false (right-to-left or centred/end-aligned text, 1199-1203). In those cases the field announces inline completion it never shows. This is a11y polish, so no repro was run.
  Fix: Compute aria-autocomplete from the same conditions as resolveInlineHint (form field present, text laid out from the start), falling back to "list".
- **[badge] An outlined badge in a solid color gets no theme rule (border drawn in inherited text color)** — confirmed in the source.
  \_badge-theme.scss:71-95: the `.kbq-badge-outline` block has rules only for the five fade colors and `disabled`. There are none for contrast, theme, success, warning or error, and badge-tokens.scss has no `outline-fade-off-\*` tokens. The base `.kbq-badge` (badge.component.scss:16 `border-style: solid`, :23 `border-width`) sets no border-color, so the border falls back to currentColor. The text color is inherited and the background stays transparent. So `\<kbq-badge outline badgeColor="success">` renders unthemed: inherited text color with a currentColor border. The badge.component.ts:118-136 inputs are independent, and nothing warns or documents this. The e2e `outlineColors` (e2e.ts:51-58) and the docs examples cover only fade and disabled. jsdom does not apply the theme CSS, so this is confirmed from source only. It is an unsupported design-system combination rather than a broken one, hence P3.
  Fix: Document that `outline` supports only the fade colors and `disabled`, or warn in dev mode / fall back to the matching fade color for an outlined solid color.
- **[badge] Recomputation with one child node or fewer removes host classes but not kbq-icon\_left / kbq-icon\_right from the remaining icon** — reproduced.
  badge.component.ts:74-75 always remove the host modifiers. The per-icon removal of `kbq-icon\_left` / `kbq-icon\_right` (:84-85) runs only inside `if (icons.length && filteredNodesWithoutComments.length > 1)` (:80). Take two adjacent icons with the first behind `@if`: the first gets the left class and the second the right class. When the first is removed, the effect reruns because the icon set changed. The repro shows that the host loses `kbq-badge-icon\_right` / `kbq-badge-icon\_left`, yet the lone icon keeps `kbq-icon\_right`. That class still matches `.kbq-badge .kbq-icon\_right { margin-left }` (badge.component.scss:34-36), so an icon-only badge is left with an asymmetric 2-4px margin. The only trigger is an icon-only badge whose icon set shrinks, hence P3.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/badge/single-node-stale-icon-class.spec.ts`](defect-repros/badge/single-node-stale-icon-class.spec.ts)
  Fix: Remove the icon modifiers from every icon before the `length > 1` check, not inside it.
- **[badge] KbqBadgeModule imports A11yModule and PlatformModule that the badge does not use** — confirmed in the source.
  badge.module.ts:1-2 and :7-8 import `A11yModule` and `PlatformModule` into `imports`, but they are not in `exports` (:11-15). badge.component.ts imports nothing from @angular/cdk, and none of KbqBadge, KbqBadgeCaption or KbqBadgeCssStyler uses them. The coupling is stale. Its only effect is that A11yModule's constructor runs (it applies the CDK high-contrast body classes) wherever KbqBadgeModule is instantiated, which is benign because other modules import A11yModule too.
  Fix: Drop A11yModule and PlatformModule from the KbqBadgeModule imports.
- **[breadcrumbs] Collapsed templated item with \<a kbq-button kbqBreadcrumb> is rendered inside the row's \<a kbq-dropdown-item>: link inside link** — confirmed in the source.
  breadcrumbs.html:49-57: every hidden item becomes `\<a kbq-dropdown-item \[routerLink\]=...>`. When the item has a customTemplateRef, the row renders that template inside itself through ngTemplateOutlet (line 56). The docs example breadcrumbs-custom-template-example.ts:21-28 and the unit fixture CollapsibleCustomViewBreadcrumbs (breadcrumbs.spec.ts:988) put an `\<a kbq-button kbqBreadcrumb \[routerLink\]>` in that template, so the result is `\<a>\<a href>`, which the HTML content model forbids. The existing spec already shows the nesting in jsdom: breadcrumbs.spec.ts:678-694 asserts that a `\[kbq-dropdown-item\]` anchor contains `.custom-breadcrumb`, which is an anchor. Practical impact is small. The inner link holds tabindex -1 because the roving value wins and the copy does not register (roving-focus-group.directive.ts registerItem guard). A click on the inner link runs both RouterLink.onClick handlers, since RouterLink does not check defaultPrevented (node\_modules/@angular/router/fesm2022/\_router\_module-chunk.mjs:255-277), so navigateByUrl is called twice, normally with the same URL. Rated P3 (invalid markup and AT semantics), so it was not reproduced, as the P0–P2 rule requires.
  Fix: Render a templated hidden item's row from `text`/`routerLink` (or a non-interactive wrapper such as a `div kbq-dropdown-item`) instead of wrapping consumer markup in an anchor.
- **[breadcrumbs] kbqBreadcrumb injects the trail's roving-focus group without optional and throws outside kbq-breadcrumbs** — confirmed in the source.
  KbqBreadcrumbButton (`\[kbq-button\]\[kbqBreadcrumb\]`, breadcrumbs.ts:83-92) applies RdxRovingFocusItemDirective as a host directive. That directive does `protected readonly parent = inject(RdxRovingFocusGroupDirective);` with no `optional` (roving-focus-item.directive.ts:35). The group is provided only as a host directive of KbqBreadcrumbs (breadcrumbs.ts:186). An element outside a trail, whose injector chain has no KbqBreadcrumbs, therefore fails with NG0201 (No provider for RdxRovingFocusGroupDirective), which names a @docs-private class. The JSDoc only says the directive takes part in the roving focus, and the overview pages (breadcrumbs.en/ru.mdx) never mention `kbqBreadcrumb` or this requirement. Every in-repo usage sits inside a trail, including the top-bar example (top-bar-breadcrumbs-adaptive-example.ts:69-79). Rated P3 (undocumented coupling), so it was not reproduced, although jsdom could show it.
  Fix: Inject the group with `{ optional: true }` and skip roving registration/keyboard handling when absent, or document that kbqBreadcrumb must live inside kbq-breadcrumbs.
- **[breadcrumbs] Expand button has no hidden state before the first overflow measurement (zero-delay timer), so a trail with nothing to collapse may show it for a frame** — confirmed in the source.
  The result element starts visible. ElementVisibilityManager.hiddenState is `signal(false)` (overflow-items.ts:42), and no CSS hides `.kbq-overflow-items-result` by default (breadcrumbs.scss has no such rule). The first `result.hide()` comes from getHiddenItems (overflow-items.ts:316), which runs only after `toObservable(this.items)` (271) passes `debounce(() => timer(this.debounceTime()))` (278, default 0). KbqBreadcrumbs.enforceMaxVisible, the other hide path (breadcrumbs.ts:325), subscribes to `dir.changes` (296), which is emitted from the same debounced callback, and to `max` with skip(1). So between the first render and that macrotask the expand button is laid out and visible. The existing spec relies on this ('flush KbqOverflowItems debounceTime(0)', breadcrumbs.spec.ts ~585). Whether the browser paints a CSR frame in that window depends on timing. Under SSR it does not depend on timing: setupObservers returns early when not in the browser (overflow-items.ts:257), so the prerendered HTML, such as the docs site's, always contains the visible expand button until hydration and the client timer. jsdom cannot show the painted flash.
  Fix: Start KbqOverflowItemsResult hidden (initial hidden state or a default visibility:hidden style) and reveal it only when a measurement or max hides something.
- **[button] A group color set in code to contrast-fade (the group default) is not propagated, while the same value bound through the input is** — confirmed in the source.
  button-group.ts:51-55: propagatedColor is undefined when colorInput() is unset and color() equals defaultColor(), which is contrast-fade (set at 103). color.set() writes colorSetInCode (core/common-behaviors/color.ts:21-28, 58-61), so color() reads contrast-fade, the same as the default. setColorFromGroup(undefined) then lets each button fall back to its style default (button.component.ts:629-631, 90-94): transparent becomes contrast. Bound through the input, colorInput() is truthy and contrast-fade is propagated. The private JSDoc at button-group.ts:46-49 states this exception. There is no visual effect: \_button-theme.scss:87-97 paints transparent with the same transparent/contrast-fade-on tokens with or without kbq-contrast, and has no kbq-contrast-fade branch. Only the host class (kbq-contrast instead of kbq-contrast-fade) and button.color() differ. jsdom could show the class; not reproduced because it rates P3.
  Fix: Decide propagation from whether a color was bound or set in code, not by comparing the value with the group default.
- **[button] kbq-progress is purely visual: no aria-busy, does not disable the button, lets clicks through** — confirmed in the source.
  .kbq-progress is a global CSS utility (core/styles/common/\_animation.scss:42-63; button.scss:6): an ::after overlay with no pointer-events change. KbqButton never reads it: no aria-busy in the host bindings (button.component.ts:315-323), and disabled comes only from ownDisabled or groupDisabled (633-635). button.en.mdx:30-33 only says to add class="kbq-progress"; it does not mention disabling the button or announcing the state. Consumers differ. filter-bar/filter-save-popover.ts:79-80 also binds \[disabled\]. modal.component.html:148/163/171 does not, and modal.component.ts:702-746 has no guard while okLoading is set, so a second click on OK runs the handler again. This may be intended, so P3 (a11y polish and docs).
  Fix: Document that a loading button needs \[disabled\] and aria-busy from the consumer, or add a loading input that sets them.
- **[button] KbqButton imported without KbqButtonCssStyler fails with a bare missing-provider error; the opposite mistake gets a dev-mode warning** — confirmed in the source.
  button.component.ts:330 'protected styler = inject(KbqButtonCssStyler)' is not optional. The styler is a separate directive on the same selector (127-135) and is not a hostDirective of KbqButton, so 'imports: \[KbqButton\]' alone throws NG0201 (No provider for KbqButtonCssStyler) when the button is created. The reverse case warns 'KbqButtonCssStyler should be imported together with KbqButton.' (190-194, tested at button.component.spec.ts:1123-1141). jsdom could show the error; not reproduced because it rates P3 (DX inconsistency).
  Fix: Inject the styler with { optional: true } and throw a dev-mode error that names the fix (import KbqButtonModule or both classes).
- **[button-toggle] The array check accepts falsy non-arrays (`0`, `false`) in a multiple group** — confirmed in the source.
  setSelectionByValue (488) throws only for `value && !Array.isArray(value)`, so 0, false and '' pass. After init, line 498 computes `value ?? \[\]`, which is 0 or false, and line 499 calls `values.includes(...)` for any toggle with a non-null value. That throws `TypeError: values.includes is not a function` instead of 'Value must be an array in multiple-selection mode.'. ('' does not throw: String.prototype.includes runs instead.) Before init, rawValue keeps the scalar. isPrechecked (433) falls back to `toggle.value === rawValue` when rawValue is not an array, so a toggle with value 0 or false is preselected and the group reports \[0\]. Not reproduced: the input is invalid anyway and the impact is a wrong error message, which is P3, outside the repro budget.
  Fix: Check `value != null && !Array.isArray(value)` in multiple mode so falsy scalars hit the documented error before and after init.
- **[button-toggle] RTL key swap follows CDK Directionality while layout follows CSS `dir`** — confirmed in the source.
  Keys: the toggle injects the optional CDK Directionality (569) and swaps Left and Right only when `directionality.value === 'rtl'` (860). Layout: the group is an inline-flex row (button-toggle.scss:10-11), whose order follows the inherited CSS `direction`. The hit areas use logical inset-inline-start/end (175, 179). So a `dir="rtl"` attribute that no CDK `Dir` directive picks up mirrors the layout, but Left still moves to the previous toggle in DOM order, which now sits to its right. Examples: a template that does not import BidiModule, or a dir set on \<html> after the root Directionality has read it. The visual mismatch depends on CSS layout, which jsdom does not render. This is the same contract as every CDK key manager, hence P3.
  Fix: Document that RTL needs the CDK `Dir` (or a Directionality provider), or read the computed `direction` of the group at keydown.
- **[checkbox] Projected kbq-hint sits inside the \<label>, so it joins the accessible name; no aria-describedby** — confirmed in the source.
  checkbox.html:1 opens `\<label class="kbq-checkbox\_\_layout" \[attr.for\]="inputId()">`, which wraps the whole template, including `\<ng-content select="kbq-hint" />` at checkbox.html:35. Accname 1.2 builds the name of a labelled input from all text descendants of its \<label>, so the hint text becomes part of the name. The input (checkbox.html:8-23) has no aria-describedby. KbqHint generates an id 'Referenced by the aria-describedby of the form field control' (form-field/hint.ts:22-23), which the checkbox never references. kbq-toggle does bind `\[attr.aria-describedby\]="hintId"` (toggle.component.html:8). jsdom could show this through an accessible-name computation, but it was not reproduced because P3 is outside the repro scope.
  Fix: Move the hint slot outside the \<label>, or wrap it in an element with an id, and point the input's aria-describedby at it, as kbq-toggle does.
- **[checkbox] checked + indeterminate: aria-checked is 'true' while the native input is indeterminate and the mixed mark is shown** — reproduced.
  checkable.ts:90-92: getAriaChecked() returns 'true' whenever checked, before it looks at indeterminate. checkbox.html:12 binds it as aria-checked and checkbox.html:18 sets the native indeterminate. checkbox.scss:73-81: `.kbq-indeterminate` hides the checkmark and shows the mixed mark, overriding `.kbq-checked`. Repro with `\<kbq-checkbox checked indeterminate>`: input.checked and input.indeterminate are true, aria-checked is 'true', and axe-core's `aria-conditional-attr` rule (check aria-conditional-checkbox-attr: aria-checked must equal the native state, 'mixed') reports the input. The existing spec pins this value (checkbox.component.spec.ts:126). This breaks the repo's 'must pass all AXE checks' rule in a reachable state. What a screen reader actually announces depends on whether the browser prefers the native state over aria-checked, which only a real browser and AT can settle. If a browser honours aria-checked here, this rises to P2.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/checkbox/aria-checked-conflict.spec.ts`](defect-repros/checkbox/aria-checked-conflict.spec.ts)
  Fix: Drop aria-checked from the native checkbox, since the native checked and indeterminate state already expose it, or make it 'mixed' whenever indeterminate is true.
- **[checkbox] Binding \[clickAction\]="undefined" replaces the token's value, so the token is ignored for that checkbox** — confirmed in the source.
  checkbox.ts:99-101: the token value is only the default of a plain `input\<KbqCheckboxClickAction>()`, which has no transform. A bound `undefined` replaces it, and resolveClick(undefined) (checkable.ts:109-118) behaves as 'check-indeterminate'. That matches the type doc ('undefined: Same as check-indeterminate', checkbox-config.ts:9), but it differs from the nullish fallback the `id` input uses (checkbox.ts:79-81). A wrapper component that forwards an optional clickAction silently overrides an app-wide KBQ\_CHECKBOX\_CLICK\_ACTION. It is jsdom-showable but was not reproduced because of its P3 severity.
  Fix: Add a transform that maps `undefined` to the injected token value, or document that binding `undefined` means 'check-indeterminate', not 'use the token'.
- **[clamped-text] With `hiddenThreshold` at 0 or below, `hasToggle()` is true when nothing is hidden and the trigger reads "Show 0 more"** — confirmed in the source.
  clamped-list.ts:46 sets exceededItemCount = items.length - collapsedVisibleCount, and :48 sets hasToggle = exceededItemCount >= hiddenThreshold, with no lower bound on either. With hiddenThreshold 0 and items.length == collapsedVisibleCount, 0 >= 0 is true, so the consumer's `@if (hasToggle())` renders a trigger. showMoreCountText (54-56) then reads 'Show 0 more', and visibleItems (50-52) is the whole list in both states, so the toggle changes nothing visible. A negative threshold also matches lists shorter than collapsedVisibleCount and gives 'Show -2 more'. This is plain signal arithmetic. No Vitest run was spent on it because it is a P3 misconfiguration edge and the repro budget was kept for P0-P2.
  Fix: Require exceededItemCount() > 0 in hasToggle (or clamp hiddenThreshold to at least 1).
- **[clamped-text] The kbq-clamped-text toggle changes its accessible name between Expand and Collapse while also exposing aria-expanded** — confirmed in the source.
  clamped-text.ts:66-67 renders the name as collapsedState() ? openText : closeText ('Expand'/'Collapse' in en-US, core/locales/en-US.ts:134-135). The same element carries KbqClampedListTrigger, which binds aria-expanded (clamped-list.ts:80, 95-101). The accessibility tree therefore holds both the state in the name and in aria-expanded, for example 'Collapse, button, expanded'. The clamped-list examples follow the same pattern. The double state is certain from the source. Whether announcing it twice counts as a defect is the design question the finding itself raises, so this is rated a11y polish.
  Fix: Keep a static label (e.g. the open text) with aria-expanded, or drop aria-expanded and rely on the changing label; pick one convention for both containers.
- **[code-block] hideTabsChange is declared but never emitted, so \[(hideTabs)\] never writes back** — confirmed in the source.
  `hideTabsChange = output\<boolean>()` (code-block.ts:339) has no `.emit` anywhere in the entry point; grep finds only the declaration, a comment (code-block.ts:324) and the spec listener (code-block.spec.ts:123). The component never writes `hideTabs` itself: the single-unnamed-file rule is derived in `tabsHidden` (code-block.ts:329-333). Only a consumer calling `hideTabs.set()` through the template ref changes it, and that is not emitted either. The output is dead API, and its JSDoc promises two-way binding. Classed P3, so not reproduced.
  Fix: Drop or deprecate the output and its two-way-binding JSDoc, or emit it wherever `hideTabs` is written internally.
- **[code-block] The copy tooltip sits on a wrapper div that never receives focus, so keyboard users see neither 'Copy' nor 'copied', and the copy is not announced** — confirmed in the source.
  code-block.html:65-76 puts `kbqTooltip` on a plain `\<div>` with no tabindex around the copy button. The pop-up trigger binds its focus trigger as a `focus`/`blur` listener on its own host element (core/pop-up/pop-up-trigger.ts:667-670, 840-841). `focus` does not bubble, so focusing the inner button never opens the tooltip. `aria-describedby` is also written on the div, not on the button (tooltip.component.ts:1250-1261). copyCode() only swaps the tooltip content to copiedTooltip (code-block.ts:625-627), while the button's aria-label stays bound to copyTooltip (code-block.html:69), and there is no live region. The download, soft wrap and link buttons carry kbqTooltip on the button itself, so they do get it on keyboard focus. Classed P3, so not reproduced.
  Fix: Put the tooltip trigger on the button itself, or announce the copied state through a live region (for example LiveAnnouncer) and keep the tooltip in sync on keyboard focus.
- **[code-block] downloadCode() never revokes the object URL it creates and clicks an anchor not attached to the document** — confirmed in the source.
  code-block.ts:655-667 calls URL.createObjectURL(blob), sets href and download on a detached `\<a>` and calls click(). No URL.revokeObjectURL exists anywhere in the entry point, so every download keeps its blob alive until the document unloads, which is a small leak. Clicking a detached anchor does download in current Chromium, Firefox and Safari, so that half has no visible effect today and only matters for old Firefox. Classed P3, so not reproduced.
  Fix: Revoke the URL after the click (for example in a setTimeout), optionally appending the anchor to the document and removing it afterwards.
- **[code-block] Enter on 'Show all' decides whether to move focus before the expanded layout renders, so expanding always moves focus to the code area, which then gets tabIndex -1** — confirmed in the source.
  code-block.html:127 calls onViewAllEnterKeydown (code-block.ts:600-607). It calls toggleViewAll(), which sets viewAll synchronously (code-block.ts:432-440), so calculatedMaxHeight is already null, and then reads canCodeContentBeFocused (code-block.ts:352-354). hasScroll (code-block.ts:591-593) reads scrollHeight/clientHeight from the DOM before change detection has removed the max-height style or added `kbq-code-block\_view-all`, so the area is still collapsed. The button only exists when the pre is taller than maxHeight (code-block.ts:587), so scrollHeight > clientHeight is always true and focusVia(main, 'keyboard') always runs. After the render, the afterEveryRender hook (code-block.ts:399-406) measures the expanded layout and writes tabIndex -1 unless the code also overflows horizontally, so focus sits on a non-tabbable area with the keyboard focus ring. Space (click path) does not move focus, which is inconsistent. This depends on real layout timing, so jsdom cannot show it without faking layout.
  Fix: Measure in afterNextRender after the toggle and move focus only if the expanded content really scrolls.
- **[code-block] With tabs hidden the code area keeps role=tabpanel with no tab list and no accessible name; with one named file the only tab has aria-selected=false and no tab stop, and the panel has no aria-labelledby** — confirmed in the source.
  The main area always carries kbqTabNavPanel (code-block.html:96-102), whose host sets a static `role: 'tabpanel'` and `\[attr.aria-labelledby\]: activeTabId` (tabs/tab-nav-bar.ts:439-447). With tabs hidden, `@if (!tabsHidden())` removes the nav bar (code-block.html:2), so nothing sets activeTabId and the panel has no name. If the tabs were visible first and are then hidden, activeTabId keeps pointing at a removed id. With one file, `\[active\]="$count > 1 && ..."` (code-block.html:9) is false, so ariaSelected is 'false' (tab-nav-bar.ts:315-318) and tabIndex is -1 because a panel is linked (tab-nav-bar.ts:300-303). updateActiveLink finds no active item and never sets activeTabId (tab-nav-bar.ts:190-215), so the panel has no aria-labelledby. Classed P3, so not reproduced.
  Fix: Bind the tabpanel role and aria-labelledby only while tabs are shown, and mark the single tab active (or render it without tab semantics).
- **[code-block] The 'Show all' button exposes its state only through its text, with no aria-expanded** — confirmed in the source.
  code-block.html:121-136: the view-all button has no aria-expanded or aria-controls binding; only the text switches between viewAllText and viewLessText (and the chevron icon). The label change does convey the state, so this is polish against the disclosure pattern rather than a hard failure.
  Fix: Bind \[attr.aria-expanded\]="viewAll()" (and aria-controls to the code area id).
- **[code-block] With an empty files list the tab bar renders without tabs and the copy button stays, doing nothing** — confirmed in the source.
  tabsHidden is `hideTabs() || (files.length === 1 && !files\[0\].filename)` (code-block.ts:329-333), which is false for \[\], so the nav bar renders (code-block.html:2-24) with an empty @for.
