Its role is 'tablist' because a panel is linked (tab-nav-bar.ts:127-129), which leaves an empty tablist that axe's aria-required-children flags. The copy button renders whenever canCopy (default true, code-block.ts:240; code-block.html:64-77), and copyCode/downloadCode return early when activeFile() is undefined (code-block.ts:619-621, 656-658). The existing test 'should render nothing rather than crash on an empty file list' only checks that no \<pre> exists. This is an edge case, since `files` is meant to be required (TODO at code-block.ts:254).
  Fix: Treat an empty files list as tabs hidden and hide or disable the copy and download actions when there is no active file.
- **[content-panel] Resize handle has no separator role, tabindex or keyboard alternative** — confirmed in the source.
  The handle (content-panel.ts:260-265) is a bare div with \[kbqResizer\] and (dblclick), with no role, tabindex or aria-value\* attributes. The KbqResizer host only sets a class and a cursor (resizer.ts:73-78), and it subscribes only to pointerdown, pointermove, pointerup, pointercancel and lostpointercapture (resizer.ts constructor, afterNextRender block); there is no keydown handling. The double-click reset is pointer-only as well, so the width cannot be changed from the keyboard or without dragging (WCAG 2.1.1, 2.5.7). The panel stays usable at its default width.
  Fix: Give the handle role="separator", aria-orientation="vertical", aria-valuenow/min/max and tabindex="0", and handle Arrow, Home and End keys to step widthState within bounds, saving through the existing debounce.
- **[content-panel] Layout fixed to the right edge, no RTL adaptation or RTL screenshot** — confirmed in the source.
  These styles and bindings use physical properties: - content-panel-container.scss:28-29: the panel is placed with `right: 0`. - content-panel-container.scss:52: the resizer sits on the physical left edge. - content-panel-container.scss:59-69: the slide keyframes use translateX(100%). - content-panel.ts:242 binds `margin-right`, and content-panel.ts:262 fixes the resizer direction to \[-1, 0\]. - content-panel.scss:85 inherits only the right border radii. Nothing in the component dir references Directionality, `dir` or logical properties, and \_\_screenshots\_\_ holds only 01-light and 01-dark. Under dir="rtl" the panel still works on the physical right and the content still makes room for it there; the layout is simply not mirrored.
  Fix: Use logical properties (inset-inline-end, margin-inline-end, an inline-start resizer), mirror the slide keyframes and the resizer direction from Directionality, and add an NN-rtl screenshot.
- **[datepicker] 2. ARIA gaps that no axe test covers** — reproduced.
  The input's host bindings (datepicker-input.directive.ts:310-324) have no aria-haspopup, aria-expanded or aria-controls. `\[attr.aria-expanded\]` sits on the toggle host (datepicker-toggle.component.ts:49), which has no role. Day `\<td>`s carry no role, aria-selected or aria-disabled: selected and disabled are only the CSS classes kbq-selected and kbq-disabled (calendar-body.html:12-26). The table (month-view.html:1) has no caption, aria-label or aria-labelledby. The repro asserts every one of these. It also runs axe-core's aria-allowed-attr rule, which does NOT report the role-less aria-expanded, so axe cannot catch this gap.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/datepicker/keyboard-a11y.spec.ts`](defect-repros/datepicker/keyboard-a11y.spec.ts)
  Fix: Add aria-haspopup/aria-expanded/aria-controls to the input, render the toggle as a real button (or role=button), and give the table an accessible name with gridcell, aria-selected and aria-disabled on the cells.
- **[datepicker] 7. An unusable dateInput format builds an Error that is never thrown** — confirmed in the source.
  datepicker-input.directive.ts:1627-1629 calls `Error(`Can' t use this format: ${format}`)` without `throw`. A format with a separator but fewer than three parts (e.g. 'MM.yyyy') leaves a DateDigit null and construction carries on. Later code dereferences `this.thirdDigit!` (e.g. :1154, :1217) and fails with an unrelated TypeError on a keystroke. A format without any separator already throws a TypeError earlier, at setFormat :1012. The diagnostic is lost, and the input does not misbehave silently.
  Fix: `throw new Error(...)` (and fix the message typo).
- **[datepicker] 10. Unparseable text of three non-digit groups sets the model to null on blur** — reproduced.
  parseOnBlur (datepicker-input.directive.ts:858-905): text that does not split into three groups only sets valueState to null without cvaOnChange (:872-877). The later updateValueAndValidity re-emits the old control value, so the model is kept with kbqDatepickerParse. Three groups that parse to NaN reach createDateTime null and `cvaOnChange(null)` (:897-905). Letters cannot be typed (:785-788) but can be pasted, because onPaste shows unmatched text raw (:946-956). Repro: pasting 'abc' and blurring keeps 2020-01-15 with a parse error, while pasting 'aa.bb.cccc' and blurring sets the model to null with a parse error.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/datepicker/three-groups-null.spec.ts`](defect-repros/datepicker/three-groups-null.spec.ts)
  Fix: Handle every unparseable text the same way in parseOnBlur (either keep the model or always clear it).
- **[datepicker] 11. Calendar header: minimum Feb 1900; same-year bounds leave later months enabled; unset bounds ignored** — reproduced.
  calendar-header.component.ts:102 `createDate(defaultMinYear, 1)` is 1900-02-01 (0-based month). Repro: header.minDate is 1900-02-01, previousDisabled is true on Feb 1900 and January is disabled in the month list. In updateMonthOptions (:263-269) the if/else-if applies only the min branch when min and max share a year. Repro with min 2020-03-05 and max 2020-06-20: only Jan and Feb are disabled. Picking a later month is then clamped back by the calendar's activeDate setter, so no out-of-range date can be selected. The minDate/maxDate setters return on a falsy value (:76-79, :92-95). Repro: after both bounds become null, years stays \[2020\] and the old limits stay. The header is recreated on each datepicker open, so this last case matters only while the panel is open or for a long-lived kbq-calendar.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/datepicker/header-bounds.spec.ts`](defect-repros/datepicker/header-bounds.spec.ts)
  Fix: Use createDate(1900, 0), apply both bounds when they share a year, and fall back to the defaults when a bound becomes null.
- **[divider] A negative length passes the parser and is written as e.g. -5px, which the browser rejects, so the divider silently falls back to its layout length** — reproduced.
  utils.ts:120-123 (`kbqOptionalNumberAttribute`) returns any finite number, negatives included. divider.component.ts:69-71 stores -5, :83/:89 pass it to `\[style.height.px\]` / `\[style.width.px\]` (:42-43), and Angular writes '-5px'. CSSOM rejects a negative width or height, so the write is dropped. The repro confirms that `length="-5"` gives `length() === -5` with an empty inline width (horizontal) and an empty inline height (vertical), the same fallback as an unparsable value. A stronger edge case, also reproduced: after a runtime change from 16 to -5 the inline width stays '16px', because the rejected write leaves the previous value in place. An unparsable value such as 'tall' maps to null, so Angular removes the style and clears it. The input is invalid by contract, so the impact is limited to bound or computed lengths that turn negative.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/divider/negative-length.spec.ts`](defect-repros/divider/negative-length.spec.ts)
  Fix: Treat a negative value as no length in the `length` transform (e.g. `parsed >= 0 ? parsed : null`), so it clears the inline style like an unparsable one.
- **[divider] The divider always writes role, replacing a caller's role, and removes a caller's aria-orientation from horizontal and decorative dividers** — reproduced.
  divider.component.ts:37 binds `\[attr.role\]` dynamically to 'presentation' or 'separator'. Host bindings run after the parent template has set its static attributes, so a caller's `role` is overwritten. A static host attribute would have let the template attribute win. divider.component.ts:39 and :77 bind `aria-orientation` to null unless the divider is vertical and not decorative. The first binding pass writes that null and removes the attribute. The repro passes for all four cases: `role="none"` becomes 'separator', `role="separator" decorative` becomes 'presentation', and the caller's `aria-orientation="horizontal"` on a horizontal divider and `aria-orientation="vertical"` on a decorative vertical one are both removed. The practical impact is small. Horizontal is already the ARIA default for a separator, removing the attribute on a decorative divider is correct, and `decorative` covers the presentation case. The real loss is that a consumer cannot give the host another role.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/divider/caller-aria-overridden.spec.ts`](defect-repros/divider/caller-aria-overridden.spec.ts)
  Fix: Read a caller-supplied role (e.g. via `HostAttributeToken('role')`) and prefer it, or document that the divider owns `role` and `aria-orientation`.
- **[dl] Restoring the default column ratio (dtWidth null) is only possible with a double-click; the keyboard has no way to do it (WCAG 2.1.1)** — confirmed in the source.
  dl.component.ts:329-347 handleResizeKeydown reacts only to Home, End, ArrowLeft and ArrowRight, and each one passes a number to setDtWidth (:377-385), which never writes null. The only null write by the component is writeDtWidth(null) in handleResizeDblClick (:354-356), reached from the (dblclick) listener (:100). Keyboard users can reach any width between the bounds but cannot return to the fluid default ratio that follows the container.
  Fix: Add a separator key (for example Enter, as in the APG window splitter, or Escape/Delete) that writes null like the second double-click, and document it.
- **[dl] The second double-click sets the col-resize cursor without checking the bounds** — confirmed in the source.
  dl.component.ts:354-357 writes null and then hardcodes resizeCursor.set('col-resize'), skipping updateResizeCursor (:397-415). updateResizeCursor would return 'default' when max \<= min (:404), and the grow cursor when the resting width is at or below the minimum (:411), which is the normal case with the default minimum, since that minimum is the term's own resting width. The cursor is recomputed only by updateLayout (:369) on the next resize, or by the minimum-change effect (:297), so until then it contradicts the 'Separator cursor' requirement. This is cosmetic, so no repro was written.
  Fix: After writing null, recompute the cursor with updateResizeCursor() once the resting layout has settled (for example after the next render), instead of hardcoding col-resize.
- **[dl] An unparsable verticalBreakpoint becomes NaN, so the automatic layout always stays horizontal** — confirmed in the source.
  dl.component.ts:130 uses transform: numberAttribute with no fallback. Angular's numberAttribute returns NaN for '', 'abc', null and undefined bindings. autoVertical (:200-204) then computes width \<= NaN, which is always false, so isVertical (:207) stays false for every width. The other numeric inputs (dtWidth :143-146, dtMinWidth/ddMinWidth :161-164) were moved to kbqOptionalNumberAttribute precisely to avoid NaN, so this input is the inconsistent one. Misuse or edge input only, so no repro was written.
  Fix: Coerce with a fallback to the default, e.g. transform: (v) => numberAttribute(v, 400), or treat NaN as not set.
- **[dropdown] The 8px horizontal shift of a nested panel is not mirrored for RTL (gap instead of overlap), and the both-axes fallback leaves a gap in LTR** — confirmed in the source.
  dropdown-trigger.directive.ts:780-789 sets offsetX = NESTED\_PANEL\_LEFT\_PADDING (8) for nested panels. The positions at 805-831 use -offsetX for the primary position, +offsetX for the X fallback and -offsetX for the both-axes fallback, all independent of this.dir. The CDK adds offsetX literally with no direction mirroring (node\_modules/@angular/cdk/fesm2022/\_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:1382-1385, 1710-1712), while 'start'/'end' do mirror. Result: in LTR the panel overlaps the trigger item by 8px (primary and X fallback), but in RTL both leave an 8px gap. e2e.playwright-spec.ts:124-146 asserts that RTL gap as the expected result (triggerBox.x == panelRight + 8; panelLeft == triggerRight + 8), unlike the LTR tests at 100-121. The fourth position (panel left of the trigger in LTR, shifted by -8) leaves a gap in LTR and has no test. This is layout only, so jsdom cannot show it.
  Fix: Negate the nested offsetX when dir is 'rtl', and give the both-axes fallback the same sign as the X fallback.
- **[dropdown] Explicit offsetY/offsetX are applied unchanged to fallback positions, so a fallback panel is pushed into the trigger** — confirmed in the source.
  dropdown-trigger.directive.ts:802-803: resolvedFallbackOffsetY = this.offsetY() ?? (overlayFallbackY === 'top' ? offsetY : -offsetY). Only the computed default flips sign for the fallback, as the existing 'y-position offsetY' tests at dropdown.spec.ts:1194-1239 show. An explicit offsetY is used as given. With yPosition 'below' and offsetY=8, the above-fallback (overlayY bottom at originY top) moves the panel 8px down into the trigger instead of 8px up. offsetX is also passed unchanged to the X fallback (line 813): navbar-item sets offsetX -8 with overlapTriggerX false (navbar-item.component.ts:793-796), so its X fallback (panel to the left) moves further left and opens a gap. Panel placement is layout, which jsdom does not compute.
  Fix: Mirror an explicit offset for the fallback positions (negate offsetY on the flipped Y axis and offsetX on the flipped X axis) or document it as literal.
- **[dropdown] Changing xPosition or yPosition while the panel is open moves the position classes at once but not the panel** — confirmed in the source.
  The validation effect (dropdown.component.ts:365-378) clears positionOverride on every position change, so classList (189-206) switches to the new input at once. The existing tests at dropdown.spec.ts:997-1027 assert exactly that while the panel is open. setPosition() is called only from openPanel (dropdown-trigger.directive.ts:418) and updatePosition (394), so the strategy keeps the old positions and the panel stays where it was until it is reopened or updatePosition() is called. The kbq-dropdown-before/after/above/below classes are not used by any library SCSS (grep), and transform-origin follows the real position through withTransformOriginOn. The mismatch therefore only affects consumer styling keyed on those classes.
  Fix: Either reposition an open panel when a position input changes, or keep the override until the next position change from the strategy.
- **[dropdown] navigationWithWrap is read once per key manager, so a later change is ignored and the search/non-search managers can disagree** — reproduced.
  configureKeyManager (dropdown.component.ts:815-817) reads untracked(this.navigationWithWrap) only when a manager is built. initKeyManager (783-808) builds each manager once with ??=, and nothing reacts to navigationWithWrap afterwards. The ActiveDescendantKeyManager is built only when inSearchMode first becomes true (effect at 343-356), with the value at that moment, so the two modes can differ. The repro shows a wrap set from the start makes Up on the first item go to 'Last' (control). Switched on after init, it leaves focus on 'First'; switched off after init, it still wraps to 'Last'. dropdown().navigationWithWrap() reports the new value in both cases.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/dropdown/navigation-wrap-change.spec.ts`](defect-repros/dropdown/navigation-wrap-change.spec.ts)
  Fix: Apply withWrap(navigationWithWrap()) to both existing managers from an effect instead of only at construction.
- **[dynamic-translation] Slot names go into a regular expression without escaping** — confirmed in the source.
  packages/components/dynamic-translation/dynamic-translation.ts:96-104 interpolates the raw slot names into `new RegExp(...join('|'))` with no escaping. I ran the same expression in node (scratchpad dt-regex-check.js). With name 'a.b', `\[\[axb\]\]` matches with slotName 'axb'. `slotTemplatesByName\['axb'\]` is undefined, so the template is null (:119), ngTemplateOutlet renders nothing and the marker disappears. With names \['a|b','c'\], `\[\[a|b:q\]\]` does not match and stays literal, while `\[\[a:q\]\]` matches as the unregistered name 'a' and disappears. The names 'a(b', 'a\[b' and 'a)b' throw SyntaxError ('Unterminated group', 'Unterminated character class', 'Unmatched )'). The throw happens inside the `parsedSlots` computed (:88-90), so the render throws. Not run in jsdom: the names are developer constants and the failure shows at once during development, so it rates P3.
  Fix: Escape each name before joining, e.g. name.replace(/\[.\*+?^${}()|\[\\]\\\]/g, '\\$&').
- **[dynamic-translation] With no slot template registered, \[\[\]\] and \[\[:text\]\] disappear instead of staying as text** — confirmed in the source.
  dynamic-translation.ts:96-104: with no registered names, `Object.keys({}).join('|')` is '' and the pattern becomes `\\[\\[()(?::(.+?))?\\]\\]`. In node, `\[\[\]\]` and `\[\[:text\]\]` match with slotName ''. :119 then gives template null, so nothing renders. `\[\[link:foo\]\]` stays literal, because after the empty group `l` matches neither ':' nor '\]\]'. This is a minor inconsistency on unlikely input; not run in jsdom.
  Fix: Return the text as a single text part when no slot name is registered, or never build an empty alternative.
- **[dynamic-translation] A line break inside a context leaves the whole marker as literal text** — confirmed in the source.
  dynamic-translation.ts:104 builds the RegExp with only the 'g' flag, so `.` in `(.+?)` does not match \n or \r. The optional context group fails, and `\\]\\]` cannot follow the name directly, so nothing matches. In node, '\[\[a:line1\nline2\]\]' and '\[\[a:line1\r\nline2\]\]' both give no match, so the marker is rendered as plain text by the text branch (:126-130). It is unambiguous but rare in translation strings; not run in jsdom.
  Fix: Add the 's' flag or use `\[\s\S\]` in the context group.
- **[dynamic-translation] The helper's elements are created only in the browser, so SSR/prerendered output holds empty placeholders** — confirmed in the source.
  dynamic-translation.ts:172 is the only place replaceSlots runs. afterNextRender returns NOOP\_AFTER\_RENDER\_REF when ngServerMode is set (node\_modules/@angular/core/fesm2022/\_debug\_node-chunk.mjs:4532-4533). The server HTML therefore keeps `\<span data-slot-name data-slot-content>` with no text until the client app boots. jsdom runs without ngServerMode, so it cannot show this. The helper is @docs-private and the client corrects the output, hence P3.
  Fix: Render the slot elements during change detection (template or Renderer2 in a render-time hook) instead of afterNextRender, or document the helper as client-only.
- **[ellipsis-center] minVisibleLength and charWidth have no numberAttribute transform, unlike debounceInterval** — confirmed in the source.
  ellipsis-center.directive.ts:92 is `input\<number>(0)` and :99 is `input(7)`, with no transform. :105 gives `debounceInterval` the `numberAttribute` transform. A static `minVisibleLength="10"` passes the string "10". At run time it still works only through coercion: `length >= this.minVisibleLength()` at :201 and `Math.max(this.charWidth(), ...)` at :206. A non-numeric `charWidth` gives NaN, then `slice(0, NaN)`, so the start half comes out empty. With `strictTemplates: true` (repo tsconfig.json:118, also the Angular CLI default), `strictAttributeTypes` rejects a static attribute bound to a `number` input with TS2322. Every in-repo use binds with brackets: file-upload html, the breadcrumbs example and e2e.ts. The same applies to `ignoreTooltipPointerEvents` (:76), which has no booleanAttribute: a static `="false"` would be the truthy string.
  Fix: Add `{ transform: numberAttribute }` to both inputs (and `booleanAttribute` to ignoreTooltipPointerEvents), then approve the API golden file.
- **[ellipsis-center] Changing minVisibleLength or charWidth alone does not re-split, and a later debounceInterval is never applied** — confirmed in the source.
  ngOnChanges (ellipsis-center.directive.ts:118-124) calls `refresh()` only for `kbqEllipsisCenterInput`. No effect reads `minVisibleLength` or `charWidth`. They are read only inside the timer callback of `refresh()` (:201, :206), so a change of either one waits for the next text change or host-width change. `debounceTime(this.debounceInterval())` at :143 is evaluated once in ngAfterViewInit, so later values are ignored. The archived openspec spec records both as shipped behaviour. All in-repo consumers bind constants, so the impact is limited to dynamic bindings. Rated P3, so no repro, although jsdom could show it.
  Fix: Trigger `refresh()` from an effect (or ngOnChanges) on minVisibleLength/charWidth, and switch to `debounce(() => timer(this.debounceInterval()))` to read it per event.
- **[ellipsis-center] Content already inside the host is kept beside the halves, unaccounted for in the split, and makes the open tooltip link via aria-describedby** — reproduced.
  refresh() (ellipsis-center.directive.ts:168-183, :235-236) removes only the previous `.kbq-ellipsis-center\_data-text-\*` spans and appends new ones, so other children stay. The overflow test (:196-197) compares only the start span's `scrollWidth` with the host's `clientWidth`. The tail length (:207) uses `clientWidth / 2`, ignoring the width of other content. The repro shows the `\<b>` child kept before both halves. It also shows the tail is the same length as in an empty host of the same width. With the tooltip open, the host gets `aria-describedby` set to the tooltip id, because `describesHostText` (tooltip.component.ts:1293-1295) compares `content` with the host's `textContent` ("Draft:" + text). A control host without children gets no `aria-describedby`. The layout results cannot be shown in jsdom: the end half overflowing when the other content is wider than about half the host, and a text that fits alone but not together with the other content being cut at the end with no tooltip. Rated P3 because the overview (ellipsis-center.en.mdx, Usage) tells consumers to leave the element empty, and no in-repo consumer puts content inside.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/ellipsis-center/host-content-describedby.spec.ts`](defect-repros/ellipsis-center/host-content-describedby.spec.ts)
  Fix: Either clear foreign children on render, or measure against the space they leave (`clientWidth` minus their widths) and document the restriction.
- **[ellipsis-center] ignoreTooltipPointerEvents defaults to true, so the tooltip is not hoverable (WCAG 1.4.13), and the non-focusable host leaves keyboard users no way to see the full text** — confirmed in the source.
  ellipsis-center.directive.ts:76 overrides the default to `true`. tooltip.component.ts:1237 then adds `cdk-overlay-pane\_ignore-pointer-events`, which core/styles/common/\_overlay.scss:81-82 turns into `pointer-events: none !important`. The pane never receives mouseenter, so its `hovered()` stays false. The hide on mouseleave (pop-up-trigger.ts:480/491) therefore proceeds with the default `leaveDelay` of 0 (tooltip.component.ts:452), and the tooltip vanishes as soon as the pointer leaves the host towards it. The base tooltip JSDoc (tooltip.component.ts:328-337) ties its hoverable `false` default to WCAG 1.4.13. The directive's host binding (:46-48) adds only a class, with no tabindex or role, so the focus trigger cannot fire for keyboard users. The full text stays in the DOM, so screen readers are unaffected. The opt-out is deliberate (JSDoc :71-75, schematics tooltip-pointer-events-and-types/data.ts:50), so whether it fails conformance is an accessibility review decision, not a code bug.
  Fix: Have accessibility review decide; options are defaulting to hoverable with a leave delay, or documenting that consumers should make the host focusable.
- **[empty-state] kbq-empty-state\_has-icon follows a descendant content query, not what is projected** — reproduced.
  packages/components/empty-state/empty-state.component.ts:154 `icon = contentChild(KbqEmptyStateIcon)`: a signal contentChild defaults to descendants: true and matches declarations, not projection. Line 138 binds `\[class.kbq-empty-state\_has-icon\]` to `!!icon()`. The template (empty-state.component.html:1) projects only top-level `\[kbq-empty-state-icon\]` children. The repro passes for two cases. (1) `\<div class="wrapper">\<img kbq-empty-state-icon>\</div>` is not rendered at all (neither .wrapper nor .kbq-empty-state-icon is in the host), yet the host carries has-icon together with align-center. (2) An icon nested inside `\[kbq-empty-state-text\]` sets has-icon while the illustration slot is empty. empty-state.scss:69-71 and 98-100 then add margin-bottom = image-addon-height to a centred normal or big state (--kbq-size-xxl / --kbq-size-6xl, empty-state-tokens.scss:8,16; compact is 0), which shifts the content up with no illustration. Rated P3, not P2: it only happens with markup the component does not support (the slot directive placed outside the slot position), and in case 1 the consumer already sees a missing illustration.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/empty-state/has-icon-unprojected.spec.ts`](defect-repros/empty-state/has-icon-unprojected.spec.ts)
  Fix: Query only direct content children: `contentChild(KbqEmptyStateIcon, { descendants: false })`. The existing `@if (withIcon())` fixture still has to pass. A two-root `@if` would stay matched.
- **[empty-state] empty-state-content example: decorative illustration has alt="Empty state" and does not use NgOptimizedImage** — confirmed in the source.
  packages/docs-examples/components/empty-state/empty-state-content/empty-state-content-example.ts:23 and :43 give both purely decorative \<img> illustrations `alt="Empty state"`. The image gets an accessible name that adds nothing next to the h2 title ("Нет групп"), and it is English in an otherwise Russian example. WCAG H67 asks for alt="" on decorative images. Lines 21-27 and 41-47 use a plain \<img> with absolute koobiq.io src/srcset (built at lines 68-72) rather than NgOptimizedImage, which AGENTS.md asks for on static images. This is hygiene in a docs example. axe does not flag it because a non-empty alt is valid.
  Fix: Use alt="" on both illustrations, and switch to NgOptimizedImage (ngSrc/ngSrcset) or record why the plain img stays.
- **[file-upload] A file input given no id gets the string "null" (folder input of 'folder'/'mixed' uploaders, bare kbqFileLoader without `for`; same for `accept`)** — confirmed in the source.
  primitives/file-picker.ts:56 binds `\[id\]="innerFor()"` and :54 `\[accept\]="innerAccept()"` as DOM properties. innerFor (:101) = `fileUploadContext?.id() ?? this.for()`, and both default to null (:25, :76). innerAccept (:99) = context accept ?? loader accept, both null by default (:31, :74). The uploaders forward only `id` and `disabled` to the context (single-file-upload.component.ts:88, multiple-file-upload.component.ts:90), so context.accept() is always null. The folder loaders bind neither `\[for\]` nor `\[accept\]` (single-file-upload.component.html:89-97, multiple-file-upload.component.html:124-132), unlike the browse-link loaders (single html:73/77, multiple html:107/111). Angular's setDomProperty calls `renderer.setProperty(el, name, value)`, which runs `el\[name\] = value` (node\_modules/@angular/platform-browser/fesm2022/\_dom\_renderer-chunk.mjs:627). `id` and `accept` are non-nullable DOMString reflecting attributes, so assigning null stores "null". Two folder/mixed uploaders without `id` therefore share id="null". Browsers ignore the invalid accept token "null", so there is no functional filtering impact and nothing depends on the input id (label association is by nesting, refocus uses viewChild). No existing test covers the folder input.
  Fix: Bind `\[attr.id\]` / `\[attr.accept\]` in the KbqFileLoader template (or give the folder loader its own generated id) so null removes the attribute.
- **[file-upload] A static `id` on the uploader host stays on the host and is forwarded to the file input (and in 'mixed' mode also to the folder input)** — confirmed in the source.
  The uploaders expose KbqFileUploadContext's `id` input through hostDirectives (single-file-upload.component.ts:88, multiple-file-upload.component.ts:90). Angular's setupInitialInputs (node\_modules/@angular/core/fesm2022/\_debug\_node-chunk.mjs:7970-8017) feeds a static attribute into a matching host-directive input, while setUpAttributes still renders every static attribute on the element. So `\<kbq-file-upload id="avatar">` keeps id="avatar" on the host and also sets context.id()='avatar'. KbqFileLoader.innerFor (primitives/file-picker.ts:101) prefers context.id() for every loader inside, including the folder loader that binds no `\[for\]`. The file input gets id="avatar" (and in 'mixed' mode the folder input does too, so even a bound `\[id\]` lands on two inputs). The result is duplicate ids in the document: getElementById and an external `\<label for>` resolve to the non-labelable host. The inputs are still labelled by their wrapping \<label>, so this is hygiene rather than a broken control.
  Fix: Give the inputs derived ids (e.g. `${id}-input`, `${id}-folder-input`) or bind the host's `\[attr.id\]` to null when the id is consumed.
- **[file-upload] The single uploader sets `hasError` only when the control status changes (distinctUntilChanged), so an invalid file replacing another invalid one keeps hasError unset; the flag is written onto the consumer's item** — reproduced.
  single-file-upload.component.ts:289-299 subscribes to `ngControl.statusChanges.pipe(distinctUntilChanged())` and sets `file.hasError = status === 'INVALID'` on the current item (:295). Replacing an invalid file with another invalid one under a sync validator emits INVALID again. distinctUntilChanged drops that emission, so the new item's hasError stays undefined. Repro with a validator rejecting `.exe`: (1) a user selects first.exe (hasError true), then drops second.exe. The control is invalid, hasError is undefined, and `kbq-error` is still shown only because `invalid` (:197-198) also ORs errorState after the touch. (2) Two programmatic setValue calls with invalid items on an untouched control: `kbq-error` is shown after the first and disappears after the second although the control stays INVALID. (3) setValue(item) mutates the consumer's own object (item.hasError === true). With async validators the PENDING step breaks the run of identical statuses, so the flag is set. The user-visible error styling in the normal user flow is preserved by errorState, hence P3.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/file-upload/single-has-error-distinct.spec.ts`](defect-repros/file-upload/single-has-error-distinct.spec.ts)
  Fix: Derive the item's error from the current control status on every value/status change (drop distinctUntilChanged or also react to value changes), ideally without mutating the consumer's item.
- **[filter-bar] The select and multiselect pipes read filterBar!.internalTemplatesChanges while being constructed and throw a TypeError outside a kbq-filter-bar** — confirmed in the source.
  pipe-select.ts:60 (a field initializer) and pipe-multi-select.ts:169 (the constructor) do `merge(this.filterBar!.internalTemplatesChanges, ...)`. base-pipe.ts:54 injects KBQ\_FILTER\_BAR\_HOST with `{ optional: true }`, so outside a bar this is null and the dereference throws `TypeError` during construction. The impact is small: KbqPipeState (pipe-state.ts:18) and KbqPipeButton (pipe-button.ts:55) inject KBQ\_FILTER\_BAR\_HOST without `optional`, and `\[kbqPipeState\]` sits on the trigger of every pipe template except `input`. So text, date, datetime, tree-select, multi-tree-select and readonly pipes fail outside a bar too, with a NullInjectorError instead. The real issue is the inconsistency with the optional injection, not a lost use case. Not reproduced because it is below the P2 repro threshold; the null dereference is deterministic.
  Fix: Use `this.filterBar?.internalTemplatesChanges ?? of(null)`, or make the bar a required dependency of every pipe and drop the pretence of `optional: true`.
- **[filter-bar] The text pipe never resets its textarea from the value; abandoned text survives closing the popover** — confirmed in the source.
  pipe-text.ts:60 holds the draft in a component-level FormControl. It is written from `data.value` only in ngOnInit (:62-64), on apply (:80-90, where it then equals the value) and on clear (:100-104 `control.reset()`). The visibleChange subscription (:69-77) neither resets nor reseeds it on close or open, and the popover content (pipe-text.html:27-31) re-binds `\[formControl\]="control"` on every open, so typed-then-abandoned text reappears with Apply enabled while `data.value` is unchanged. The date pipes behave differently: pipe-date-base.ts resets to list mode on close and rebuilds the form through initFormGroup() in showPeriod(). Not reproduced because it is below the P2 repro threshold; jsdom could show it.
  Fix: Reseed the control from data.value when the popover closes without applying, or when it opens (control.setValue(this.data.value)).
- **[filter-bar] numberOfSelectedLeaves, which feeds the multi-tree-select count badge, counts every selected node, branches included** — confirmed in the source.
  pipe-multi-tree-select.ts:77-79 filters `selectionModel.selected` only against `kbqTreeSelectAllValue`. Branch nodes are in that model: a clicked branch is selected, and toggleParents (:359-377) selects a parent once all its descendants are. So the badge (pipe-multi-tree-select.html:54-60) shows parents + leaves. A branch with a single child selected even reads as 2 nodes and shows the badge "2" instead of the name (html:50). This matches the length of the stored value, which also holds branch values; it matches kbq-tree-select's own per-node tags (tree-select.component.ts:1839); and it matches the baseline spec ("count badge of selected nodes"). Only the getter's name says leaves. The existing test (pipe-multi-tree-select.spec.ts:680-704) recomputes the same formula, so it cannot fail. Counted as a defect at P3 because the intended count (nodes vs leaves) is a design decision not settled by the code.
  Fix: Decide the intended count: either filter out expandable nodes (treeControl.isExpandable) or rename the getter to reflect that it counts nodes.
- **[filter-bar] Escape in the saved-filter search calls closePopover(), which hides the (closed) save popover and moves focus to the trigger; closing the menu is left to the dropdown** — confirmed in the source.
  filters.ts:242-247 calls closePopover() on Escape without stopping the event. closePopover (:360-362) calls KbqFilterSavePopover.close (filter-save-popover.ts:229-236): hide() on a popover that is not open, then `closed.emit(true)` → onSavePopoverClosed → restoreFocus (filters.ts:211-223), which focuses the main button synchronously. The event then bubbles to the dropdown panel, whose handleKeydown (dropdown.component.ts:581-586) closes the menu. With an empty query the user sees the expected result through two redundant paths. The existing spec (filters.spec.ts:1098-1115) only checks that closePopover was called. A likely consequence, read from the source and not run: with text in the search, the form-field cleaner's Escape-to-clear (cleaner.ts:170-173) requires the input to be focused, and focus has already moved away, so Escape closes the whole menu instead of clearing the query first. That differs from the library convention stated in dropdown-search.ts:82 (first Escape clears, second closes). Without that consequence this would be P4.
  Fix: Drop the closePopover() call for Escape and let the cleaner and the dropdown handle it (or only close when the query is empty).
- **[flag] A label made only of spaces gives role="img" with a blank aria-label (axe role-img-alt)** — reproduced.
  packages/components/flag/flag.ts:92 `this.label() ? 'img' : null`, :98 `this.label() || null` and :104 `!this.label()` only test truthiness, so ' ' is treated as a name and nothing is trimmed. The empty string is covered by flag.spec.ts:343, but whitespace is not. In the repro, `\[label\]="' '"` gives role="img", aria-label=" " and no aria-hidden, and axe's role-img-alt rule reports a violation on the host (the spec asserts `.not.toHaveNoViolations` limited to that rule, and it passes).
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/flag/whitespace-label.spec.ts`](defect-repros/flag/whitespace-label.spec.ts)
  Fix: Trim the label once, for example `computed(() => this.label()?.trim() || null)`, and derive all three attributes from it.
- **[flag] Host bindings drop a consumer's static role/aria-label; a flag named via aria-labelledby is hidden** — reproduced.
  packages/components/flag/flag.ts:44-46 bind `\[attr.role\]`, `\[attr.aria-label\]` and `\[attr.aria-hidden\]` to computeds that return null without `label` (:92, :98). Angular removes an attribute when its bound value is null, so the consumer's static attribute is removed on the first update. The repro renders `\<kbq-flag role="img" aria-label="Germany">`: role and aria-label are both removed and aria-hidden="true" is set. It also renders `\<kbq-flag aria-labelledby="flag-name">`: aria-labelledby is kept but aria-hidden="true" hides the flag. This matches the documented fail-safe (flag.en.mdx Accessibility section, JSDoc of `label` at :81-85, design Decision 5). So `label` really is the only way to name a flag, and other naming routes are discarded silently.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/flag/consumer-aria-dropped.spec.ts`](defect-repros/flag/consumer-aria-dropped.spec.ts)
  Fix: Either document that `label` is the only naming route and that role/aria-label on the host are overwritten, or skip the fail-safe when the host already carries aria-label/aria-labelledby (via HostAttributeToken).
- **[flag] An invalid aspectRatio such as "4:3" makes aspect-ratio invalid at computed-value time and an svg flag collapses to zero width** — confirmed in the source.
  flag.ts:40 writes the input verbatim into `--kbq-flag-aspect-ratio`, and flag.scss:10 reads it with `aspect-ratio: var(--kbq-flag-aspect-ratio)`. A custom property accepts '4:3', but after substitution the declaration is invalid at computed-value time, so aspect-ratio falls back to `auto`. The width then comes from the content alone: height 1em (flag.scss:9), inline-block, and the svg carriers are `position: absolute` (flag.scss:30-31) inside a `display: contents` slot (:43-44), so they add no width. I checked this in the built-in Chromium 152 pane with the same CSS. `--kbq-flag-aspect-ratio: 4 / 3` gives a 32x24 box. '4:3' computes `aspect-ratio: auto` and gives a 0x24 box, so the svg flag is invisible. With a projected \<img> the box takes the image's intrinsic ratio, so the flag stays visible but uncropped. The docs prose writes ratios as '4:3' (doc finding 5), but the input contract asks for a CSS value, so this needs invalid input.
  Fix: Say in the JSDoc/docs that the colon form is invalid, and optionally warn in dev mode when `CSS.supports('aspect-ratio', value)` is false; a var() fallback cannot help because it only covers an unset variable.
- **[flag] Crop rules match every descendant img/svg, not only the flag's carrier** — confirmed in the source.
  flag.scss:19-24 (`img`) and :30-39 (`svg`) are descendant selectors under `.kbq-flag`. The nested-\<svg> part is real. I checked it in Chromium 152 (built-in pane) with an outer `\<svg viewBox="0 0 30 20">` containing an inner `\<svg x=0 y=0 width=10 height=10>`. The inner svg picks up `transform: translate(-50%,-50%)` (computed matrix(1,0,0,1,-15,-10), half of the outer viewBox) and is drawn 18px left and 12px above the flag's top-left corner, where overflow:hidden (:12) clips it. A control outside .kbq-flag draws it at the corner. The \<img> part of the claim is REFUTED. An img inside a \<picture>, an inline \<span>, or even a block \<div> wrapper filled the 36x24 host exactly in Chromium: an inline wrapper is not a containing block, so width/height:100% resolve against the host. None of the 265x2 flags in country-flag-icons (3x2 and 1x1, files and string exports) contain a nested \<svg>, so this needs hand-made consumer markup.
  Fix: Exclude nested SVGs from the svg rule, for example `svg:not(svg svg)`, or scope it to the outermost svg.
- **[form-field] The password toggle combines aria-pressed with a label that flips between Show password and Hide password** — confirmed in the source.
  password-toggle.ts:69-71 renders `role="button"` with `\[attr.aria-label\]="accessibleName"` and `\[attr.aria-pressed\]="!hidden"`. password-toggle.ts:184-188 switches the name to `hidePassword` ('Hide password', en-US.ts:20) while the password is shown, and in that state `aria-pressed` is "true". The result is a toggle announced as 'Hide password, toggle button, pressed', which reads as a contradiction; per WAI-ARIA APG a toggle button keeps a constant label. The attribute pair is deterministic; only the announcement itself needs AT. Not reproduced because it is P3.
  Fix: Keep a constant label ('Show password') with aria-pressed, or drop aria-pressed and keep the flipping action label.
- **[form-field] The hints and errors of a kbq-fieldset are not referenced by any aria-describedby** — confirmed in the source.
  fieldset.ts:46-50 projects kbq-error/kbq-hint into its own hint area, but the host (fieldset.ts:55-59) sets only `role="group"` and `aria-labelledby`, with no `aria-describedby`. KbqFormField links only its own content children (form-field.ts:212, 231-232) in `updateDescribedByIds` (form-field.ts:484-507). Those queries cannot reach hints that are siblings in the fieldset, so neither the group nor its controls point to them. Example: form-fieldset-overview-example.ts projects 'Hint for the first field' and 'Hint for the second field' this way, and no aria-describedby points to either. jsdom could show the missing attribute, but it is P3, so not reproduced.
  Fix: Give the fieldset host `aria-describedby` built from its projected hints and errors (contentChildren of KbqHint/KbqError ids).
- **[icon] An icon button disabled from the start is focus-monitored anyway** — confirmed in the source.
  icon-button.component.ts:137 registers `effect(() => disabledSignal() ? stopFocusMonitor() : runFocusMonitor())`. It is a view effect on the parent LView (createViewEffect, @angular/core fesm2022/\_effect-chunk2.mjs:2899-2900). In refreshView (\_debug\_node-chunk.mjs:5748ff) runEffectsInView (:5793) runs after the pre-order hooks, where ngOnChanges sets `disabled=true` (:127-131), and before the view hooks (:5822-5831). So the effect calls stopFocusMonitor (a no-op), and then ngAfterViewInit (:140-141) calls runFocusMonitor unconditionally. Nothing re-runs the effect until `disabled` changes. A button disabled later is unmonitored instead, so the two are inconsistent. The cdk-keyboard-focused ring (\_icon-button-theme.scss:93) can show on a focusable disabled host, such as `\<a href>` (see the anchor defect). jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because the triage keeps repros for P0-P2.
  Fix: In ngAfterViewInit, start monitoring only when `!this.disabled`, or let the effect alone own monitoring (e.g. afterNextRender).
- **[icon] A resolver that throws makes getNamedSvgIcon throw synchronously** — confirmed in the source.
  icon-registry.ts:137-138 calls each resolver directly in getNamedSvgIcon, outside any `defer`, so a throwing resolver escapes at the call site. The same holds for a non-string answer, where `result.trimStart()` at :149 throws. This contradicts the class comments at :115-116 ("every failure of this class has to reach the caller through the same channel") and :325-326. KbqIcon is safe only because it wraps the call in `defer` (icon.component.ts:247-252). The existing test 'should survive a resolver that throws' (icon.component.spec.ts:472) covers only that path. No other caller exists in the repo, so only direct consumers of the public KbqIconRegistry.getNamedSvgIcon are affected. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because it is P3.
  Fix: Wrap the resolver loop (or the whole body of getNamedSvgIcon) in `defer` so that every failure becomes an error notification.
- **[icon] getNamedSvgIcon accepts a markup name but sanitizes it as untrusted text, so it never yields an SVG** — confirmed in the source.
  icon-registry.ts:114-120: a name starting with `\<` is passed to `sanitizer.sanitize(SecurityContext.HTML, name)`. `name` is a plain string, so it can never be trusted SafeHtml. Angular's HTML sanitizer allow-list has no SVG elements, so `\<svg>`/`\<path>` are stripped (with a dev-mode 'sanitizing HTML stripped some content' warning), and svgElementFromText (:303-312) then throws 'could not find \<svg>' as an error notification. The registry XSS tests (icon-registry.spec.ts:203-244) show the same: SVG markup through this sanitizer leaves nothing, and registration throws. The path is dead and undocumented. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because it is P3.
  Fix: Remove the markup-name branch, or accept only SafeHtml through a dedicated API.
- **[icon] kbq-icon-flip-h applies scaleY(-1) and kbq-icon-flip-v applies scaleX(-1); the names look swapped and are undocumented** — confirmed in the source.
  icon.scss:22-24 `.kbq-icon-flip-h { transform: scaleY(-1) }` and :26-28 `.kbq-icon-flip-v { transform: scaleX(-1) }`. A horizontal flip conventionally mirrors left-right (scaleX(-1)), so the pair is swapped against that convention. No mdx page mentions these classes. A repo-wide grep finds them only in icon.scss and the icon-adopted-stylesheets dev app. They date from the init commit (bebf3bbef), so swapping them now would be a breaking change. This is CSS, so jsdom cannot show it.
  Fix: Document the current meaning, or swap the transforms with a migration/deprecation note.
- **[inline-edit] A focusable control in the label, or one in the view that interactiveSelectors does not match (a plain button by default), opens the editor on click, and Enter/Space on it are default-prevented, which cancels the control's own activation** — confirmed in the source.
  The host listens for (click), (keydown.enter) and (keydown.space) and sends all three to onClick (inline-edit.ts:187-189). onClick (528-539) returns early only if isInteractiveElement(target) matches interactiveSelectors, which default to \['a','kbq-tag'\] (299). isInteractiveElement (1057-1067) checks the whole host, label included. Any other control gets preventDefault(), stopPropagation() and toggleMode(). Clicking a plain \<button> in the view runs its handler and also opens the editor. Clicking the focusable kbq-icon-button info icon in the label opens the editor too; that icon appears in the docs example inline-edit-customized-design-example.ts:45-53, and icon-button.component.ts:50 makes it focusable. In a real browser, a prevented Enter/Space keydown stops the native button activation; jsdom does not synthesize that click, so this part is shown from source. detectInteractiveContent (1053-1055) scans only viewContent (460). So a button in the view that the list does not match keeps the view at role=button around a focusable child (nested-interactive), and controls in the label are never considered. The JSDoc example (294-297) and spec:510-521 document the workaround of adding 'button' to the list, which also covers the label because the click filter spans the host. That is why this is P3.
  Fix: Add button/\[role=button\]/input to the default selectors, or skip events whose target is a focusable element other than the view content or anchor, and leave the label slot alone.
- **[input] A step at a bound re-emits the unchanged value and marks the control dirty** — confirmed in the source.
  stepUp/stepDown (input-number.ts:502-524) clamp res to min/max, then call valueState.set(res), cvaOnChange(res) and valueChange.next(res) with no comparison to the current value, unlike viewToModelUpdate (:612). Angular's registerOnChange pipeline sets \_pendingDirty and runs updateControl, which calls markAsDirty() and setValue(), so valueChanges emits too. kbq-stepper calls stepUp/stepDown on every press and every long-press repeat (form-field/stepper.ts:157,162). Classed P3, so no repro was run.
  Fix: Return early from stepUp/stepDown when the clamped result equals this.value.
- **[input] integer mode does not stop fractional values from a form write or a fractional step** — confirmed in the source.
  integer() is read only by the keydown filter (input-number.ts:405), inputmode (:166) and onPaste (:492). writeValue -> setValue (:361-363, :581-591) -> formatNumber (:642-648) renders a fraction as is, and stepUp/stepDown (:505, :517) add a fractional step with no rounding. The JSDoc (:204-206) promises integers for input and pasting only, so this is a contract gap rather than a typing bug. Classed P3, so no repro was run.
  Fix: Round (or reject) in setValue and in the step methods when integer() is true, or document that integer only filters typing and pasting.
- **[input] \[value\] / value.set() on kbqInput writes the element without the forms API** — confirmed in the source.
  input.ts:236-242 copies a bound \[value\] into the value signal, and the effect at :246-261 writes it straight to inputValueAccessor.value, the element. KbqInput provides no NG\_VALUE\_ACCESSOR (:48-53), so a \[formControl\]/ngModel on the same element (DefaultValueAccessor, which listens only to input events) keeps its old value. Angular's native \[value\] binding next to a form directive behaves the same way. The one internal writer, inline-edit, uses the AbstractControl whenever there is one and calls value.set only without it (inline-edit/inline-edit.ts:1181-1186), so no library path hits this. It only affects consumers who mix \[value\] or value.set with a form control. Classed P3, so no repro was run.
  Fix: When ngControl is present, route value writes through ngControl.control.setValue (or warn in dev mode on \[value\] combined with a form directive).
- **[link] An external link is marked only by an icon that KbqIcon hides with aria-hidden="true"; the accessible name does not say the link leads outside the product** — confirmed in the source.
  link.ts:112-114 renders `\<i class="kbq-link\_\_external-icon" kbq-icon="kbq-north-east\_16">` with no aria-hidden binding, so KbqIcon resolves aria-hidden to 'true' (icon.component.ts:44, 70-78; defaultAriaHidden = true). Nothing else in the template, the host bindings (link.ts:121-136) or the a11y locale (core/locales/\*.ts has no 'external link' string) adds visually hidden text or aria-describedby. Screen-reader users therefore get no equivalent of the visual cue the docs describe (link.en.mdx:51: 'The distinction is indicated by an icon'). axe has no rule for this, and the existing 'no violations on an external link' test (link.spec.ts:573) passes. The defect is the missing information for assistive technology, not a rule violation, so it is a11y polish.
  Fix: Add visually hidden text (or an aria-describedby) sourced from kbqInjectA11yLocaleConfiguration() when `external` is set, e.g. 'external link'.
- **[link] An \<a kbqLink> without a href and without pseudo gets tabindex="0" but no role and no keyboard activation** — reproduced.
  isPseudo (link.ts:237) is false for an anchor without `pseudo`, whether or not it has a href. actsAsButton (link.ts:249) is false, so role() is the declared role (null, link.ts:252) and activate() returns early (link.ts:322). focusable is true for an \<a> (link.ts:170), so hostTabIndex gives tabIndex(), whose default is 0 (link.ts:195, 265-269). A native \<a> without a href is not focusable and does not activate on Enter, so the component adds a tab stop that the keyboard cannot use. The repro on `\<a kbqLink (click)>` shows: no href, tabindex="0", no role, no kbq-link\_pseudo, Enter and Space not prevented and 0 clicks, while a mouse click() fires the handler. The existing test 'should keep an anchor a regular link unless it is marked pseudo' (link.spec.ts:151) checks only the class and the role, not the tabindex. It needs the author to leave out both href and pseudo (or a routerLink bound to null), so P3.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/link/anchor-without-href.spec.ts`](defect-repros/link/anchor-without-href.spec.ts)
  Fix: Either leave the tabindex off an anchor that has no href and is not pseudo, or treat such an anchor as a pseudo link (button role plus Enter/Space).
- **[link] kbqLinkOptionsProvider({ size: undefined }) overrides the built-in 'normal' with undefined and leaves links without any size class** — reproduced.
  kbqLinkOptionsProvider (link.ts:71-76) takes Partial\<KbqLinkOptions> and spreads it over the defaults, so an explicit `size: undefined` replaces 'normal'. The root tsconfig does not enable exactOptionalPropertyTypes, so this type-checks. The size input defaults to options.size (link.ts:224), and none of the three size host-class bindings (link.ts:123-125) matches undefined. The repro, providing { size: undefined }, finds no kbq-link\_size\_\* class on the link, so it loses the size-driven typography and icon gaps. `underline: undefined` and `useVisited: undefined` are harmless (the ?? chain at link.ts:241 and booleanAttribute), so only size is affected. It needs an explicit or variable-driven undefined, so P3.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/link/undefined-size-option.spec.ts`](defect-repros/link/undefined-size-option.spec.ts)
  Fix: Drop undefined entries before the spread in kbqLinkOptionsProvider, or default the input with `this.options.size ?? 'normal'`.
- **[list] In single selection Ctrl/Cmd + A goes to typeahead as the letter A, and the default is not prevented; in multiple modes Ctrl + any letter except C also feeds typeahead** — confirmed in the source.
  onKeyDown calls preventDefault only for SPACE/ENTER/LEFT/RIGHT and vertical keys (list-selection.component.ts:1172-1174). The select-all branch requires this.multiple (1176) and the copy branch only matches C (1180). Every other key goes to keyManager.onKeydown (1204-1208). That method's default branch (core/a11y/key-manager/list-key-manager.ts:302-312) feeds event.key into the typeahead stream with no modifier check, unlike CDK, which ignores modified keys. It then returns without preventDefault. Typeahead is on (list-selection.component.ts:855), so about 200 ms later the active option and focus jump to an option starting with that letter. No selection changes, because the letter is not a navigation key (1213-1216). jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced: the run budget was kept for P0-P2.
  Fix: Do not feed typeahead when ctrl/meta/alt is held (in the list or in ListKeyManager's default branch); in single mode treat Ctrl/Cmd+A as a no-op.
- **[list] PageUp/PageDown page size is the list's own height divided by the first option's height; in a list scrolled by an outer container one page is every option** — confirmed in the source.
  updateScrollSize (list-selection.component.ts:969-983) sets withScrollSize(floor(list host height / options.first height)). The host is not a scroll container: list.scss has no overflow or max-height on it. In list-select-all-example.ts:14-26 and 43, the 272px height is on the kbq-scrollbar wrapper, so the host is as tall as all its rows and the page size covers at least every option. setNextPageItemActive (list-key-manager.ts:348-352) then jumps to the last item, and PageUp jumps to the first. The size is measured only at ngAfterContentInit and on window resize (883-896). This is layout-dependent, so jsdom cannot show it.
  Fix: Measure the page from the nearest scrolling ancestor's client height (or the visible viewport), not from the list host.
- **[list] In a horizontal list with autoSelect on, Up and Down do not move the active option but still re-select it and emit selectionChange** — confirmed in the source.
  In a horizontal list the key manager is built with vertical orientation off (list-selection.component.ts:856). For Up/Down, onKeyDown calls preventDefault (1172), the horizontal branches do not match (1192-1195), and keyManager.onKeydown returns without moving (list-key-manager.ts:248-260). isNavigationKey (1228-1234) still returns isVerticalMovement(event) = true. So 1213-1223 calls setSelectedOptionsByKey, whose autoSelect branch (1012-1019) deselects everything, selects the active option and emits selectionChange plus a value report. An unselected active option (for example, after Tab into an empty selection) gets selected by Down. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced: P3 is outside the repro scope.
  Fix: Make isNavigationKey orientation-aware (horizontal: Left/Right/Home/End/PageUp/PageDown only) or select only when the active index actually changed.
- **[list] Focus entering a list whose first selected option is disabled, or not rendered under virtual scrolling, stays on the list host and reaches no option** — confirmed in the source.
  KbqListSelection.focus (list-selection.component.ts:919-931) only calls options.find(o => o.selected)?.focus() when the model has a selection. KbqListOption.focus returns early for a disabled option (2184). A selected option whose view sits in CDK's virtual-for cache stays in selectionModel but is not in the options query, so find() returns undefined. There is no fallback to setFirstItemActive in either case. Focus stays on the listbox host and the key manager has no active item. Space/Enter then do nothing (1107-1109), and an arrow key starts from the first item. jsdom could show the disabled case, but it was not reproduced: P3 (a11y focus polish) is outside the repro scope.
  Fix: Focus the first enabled rendered selected option, falling back to keyManager.setFirstItemActive() when none can take focus.
- **[loader-overlay] Marker attribute without its imported directive is silently dropped; a marker nested in a wrapper hides the default part but is not projected** — reproduced.
  loader-overlay.component.ts:74-76: whether a part is custom is decided by contentChild(\<directive>) (descendants by default). loader-overlay.component.html:2-26 renders `\<ng-content select="\[kbq-...\]">` only inside the @if branch for that flag, and ng-content matches only top-level projected nodes. Repro, test 1: a standalone host that imports only KbqLoaderOverlay renders the default spinner and adds kbq-loader-overlay\_empty, and the marked indicator, text and caption are absent from the DOM. Angular reports nothing, because a static attribute is not checked. Repro, test 2: with KbqLoaderOverlayModule imported and the markers inside a wrapper div, the queries match. The default spinner and the bound text and caption are hidden, but the wrapper and the markers are not projected, so the overlay renders empty. Every docs example imports KbqLoaderOverlayModule, so this is a developer trap that shows up during development, not a runtime regression.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/loader-overlay/marker-dropped.spec.ts`](defect-repros/loader-overlay/marker-dropped.spec.ts)
  Fix: Decide on projection with the same mechanism in both places, for example by always rendering the ng-content slots and falling back with CSS (:empty) or by documenting that the markers must be direct children with their directives imported; at least warn in dev mode.
- **[loader-overlay] KbqLoaderOverlayModule imports A11yModule and PlatformModule, which the overlay does not use; they appear in the public typings** — confirmed in the source.
  loader-overlay.module.ts:1-2 and 13-16 import A11yModule and PlatformModule. loader-overlay.component.ts imports neither CDK a11y nor platform: its only imports are @angular/core, @koobiq/components/core and progress-spinner (lines 1-16), and its template uses only kbq-progress-spinner. tools/public\_api\_guard/components/loader-overlay.api.md lists `typeof i1.A11yModule, typeof i2.PlatformModule` in the ɵmod declaration, so these unused dependencies are part of the published typings.
  Fix: Drop A11yModule and PlatformModule from the module imports and run approve-api for components/loader-overlay.
- **[markdown] Header cells of aligned table columns get no kbq-markdown\_\_th class** — reproduced.
  markdown.values.ts:30-33 puts `th` in the whole-tag list. markdown.service.ts:35-42 matches only `\<th\s\*>`, so marked's `\<th align="left">` stays unaliased. The `td` of the same column is aliased because the prefix rule at markdown.service.ts:27-33 matches it. The repro confirms this (aligned th has no class, aligned td has `kbq-markdown\_\_td`), and the existing snapshot shows it too (\_\_snapshots\_\_/markdown.component.spec.ts.snap:342-360). No style reads `.kbq-markdown\_\_th`: markdown.scss:160-178 uses bare `th` selectors, so nothing changes visually. Consumer CSS that targets the class misses these cells, and the docs-pages compiler aliases `th align` correctly (tools/docs-pages/compile-page.spec.ts:113), so the two disagree.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/markdown/tag-aliasing.spec.ts`](defect-repros/markdown/tag-aliasing.spec.ts)
  Fix: Match `th` and `p` as whole tag names followed by whitespace or `>` (e.g. `\<th(?=\[\s>\])`), the same way the prefix list does, rather than only attribute-less tags.
- **[markdown] The component uses the global marked instance, so marked.use()/setOptions() anywhere changes every kbq-markdown** — reproduced.
  markdown.service.ts:2 imports the `marked` singleton, and line 21 calls `marked.parse(markdown, options)`, which merges in `marked.defaults`. In the repro, `marked.setOptions({ breaks: true })` turns `first\nsecond` from no `\<br>` into a `\<br>` in the output of `KbqMarkdownService.parseToHtml` with no options passed. Any other code in the app that configures marked, including a third-party library, silently changes every kbq-markdown, and none of this is documented.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/markdown/marked-parse-options.spec.ts`](defect-repros/markdown/marked-parse-options.spec.ts)
  Fix: Create a private `new Marked()` instance in the service, configured from KBQ\_MARKDOWN\_MARKED\_OPTIONS, or document that the global instance is shared.
- **[markdown] The --kbq-markdown-\*-max-width tokens (incl. --kbq-markdown-size-max-width: 650px) are declared but no style rule reads them** — confirmed in the source.
  markdown-tokens.scss:8,11,14,17,20,23,26,29,36,49 declare `--kbq-markdown-{h1..h6,p,list,blockquote,image}-size-max-width`, and line 59 declares `--kbq-markdown-size-max-width: 650px`. A repo-wide search (excluding node\_modules, dist and openspec) finds no `var(--kbq-markdown-\*max-width)` in markdown.scss, \_markdown-theme.scss or anywhere else. A consumer who overrides these tokens gets no effect. @koobiq/design-tokens (web/components/markdown.json5) marks these component max-width tokens deprecated.
  Fix: Either apply the tokens as `max-width` on the matching rules or remove the dead declarations, noting the change in the changelog.
- **[modal] The dim-layer hand-over runs only when a dialog registers while it is already shown, so a declarative dialog opened over another shown dialog after its own init keeps both dim layers** — reproduced.
  handleMultipleMasks (modal-control.service.ts:188-237) is called only from registerModal (:82), which the declarative dialog runs in ngOnInit (modal.component.ts:503) while it is still hidden, so modals.filter(kbqVisible).length > 1 is false there. Nothing re-runs it when kbqVisible later becomes true (handleVisibleStateChange at :792-819 only calls setVisible). The service path works because ModalBuilderForService calls open() (modal.service.ts:37) before the first change detection, so ngOnInit already sees the dialog visible. Repro: two \<kbq-modal \[(kbqVisible)\]> start hidden and are opened one after the other: 2 masks without kbq-modal-mask-hidden, against modal.en.mdx:141 ("only one dim overlay should remain"). The effect is visual only (double dimming) and occurs only with stacked dialogs, which the docs call incorrect usage.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/modal/declarative-double-mask.spec.ts`](defect-repros/modal/declarative-double-mask.spec.ts)
  Fix: Run the mask hand-over from setVisible(modalRef, true) (or key it on topVisibleModal() in the dialog) instead of from registration.
- **[modal] confirm() and open() write kbqModalType and kbqClassName into the options object the caller passed; reusing one object repeats the kbq-confirm classes** — confirmed in the source.
  modal.service.ts:146-147 assign options.kbqModalType = 'confirm' and options.kbqClassName = `kbq-confirm kbq-confirm-${confirmType} ${options.kbqClassName || ''}` on the caller's object, and open() assigns options.kbqModalType = 'custom' (:153). A second confirm() with the same object prefixes the already prefixed class name again (e.g. 'kbq-confirm kbq-confirm-confirm kbq-confirm kbq-confirm-confirm '). A later create() with the same object also inherits the confirm or custom layout. Not run in jsdom: rated P3, and the repro budget is for P0–P2. The mutation is deterministic from the source.
  Fix: Build a new object ({ ...options, kbqModalType, kbqClassName }) instead of writing into the caller's options.
- **[modal] A composed title, caption or footer registers itself for good; removed conditionally, aria-labelledby/aria-describedby point at a missing id and the dialog still counts as having a footer** — confirmed in the source.
  KbqModalTitle, KbqModalCaption and KbqModalFooter call modal.registerTitle/registerCaption/registerFooter in their constructors (modal.directive.ts:65-68, 91-95, 132-136) and have no destroy hook. registerX only sets composedTitle/composedCaption/composedFooter = true (modal.component.ts:602-614), and nothing ever resets them (grep: only :438-444 init, :603/:608/:613 set, :353/:783/:788 read). So after an @if removes the composed title or caption, ariaLabelledBy/ariaDescribedBy (:357-369) keep the ids of elements that no longer exist, and hasFooter (:352-354) keeps kbq-modal\_no-footer off. Not run in jsdom: rated P3 (a11y polish, needs conditional composition), and the repro budget is for P0–P2.
  Fix: Unregister on destroy (DestroyRef in each directive) and keep the flags as signals or counters so the bindings follow them.
- **[navbar] The control of a projected kbq-form-field keeps its own tab stop** — confirmed in the source.
  navbar-item.component.ts:286-292: ngAfterContentInit sets tabIndex=-1 only on a projected KbqButton. Nothing changes the form field control, and KbqInput (input.ts:54-66) has no tabindex binding, so the native input keeps tabIndex 0. On Tab the navbar only emits tabOut (navbar.component.ts:391-394), which takes the host out of the order (leave-tab-order.ts:28-31) and does not prevent the default. The browser then moves to the next tabbable element in DOM order, which is the field's input when it follows the focused item. jsdom does not do sequential Tab navigation, so this was not run. Items stay reachable by arrows, so the result is an extra tab stop inside the composite widget.
  Fix: Also take the form field control out of the tab order (as done for kbq-button), or document that fields keep their own tab stop.
- **[navbar] Suffix and dropdown chevron are rendered only while the item has a title** — confirmed in the source.
  navbar-item.component.html:3-16: the \<ng-content select="\[kbqNavbarItemSuffix\]"> slot and both chevron icons are inside @if (title()). Content matching a slot that sits in a false @if is assigned to that slot and never attached, and it does not fall back to the default slot, so a suffix on an item without a title is silently dropped. The missing chevron on an icon-only dropdown item looks like the intended design: it matches the collapsed presentation (navbar-item.component.ts:587-594), and the overview example's icon-only bento and profile dropdown items (navbar-overview-example.html:27-37, 93-102) have no chevron. The real issue is only the dropped suffix in an edge case, so no repro was written.
  Fix: Render the suffix slot outside the @if (title()) block, or document that the suffix requires a title.
- **[navbar] Collapsed items are expanded starting from the last one** — confirmed in the source.
  navbar.component.ts:305-309: collapsableItems is reversed (last DOM item first). collapseItems (420-430) and expandItems (432-441) both walk that same reversed list, so the first item collapsed (the last in the DOM) is also the first one expanded. expandItems has no break, so a smaller earlier item can also expand while a wider later one stays collapsed. A partial widening therefore leaves e.g. item 4 collapsed and item 5 expanded. The layout still fits and the state is stable for a given width, so this is an ordering inconsistency only. No repro written because it is P3.
  Fix: Expand in the reverse of the collapse order (iterate the non-reversed list) and stop at the first item that does not fit.
- **[navbar] Horizontal arrow navigation and the vertical dropdown key ignore RTL** — confirmed in the source.
  navbar.component.ts:342 fixes withHorizontalOrientation('ltr'), and no Directionality is injected anywhere in navbar/. navbar-item.component.ts:775-778 opens a vertical item's dropdown on RIGHT\_ARROW regardless of direction, and the kbqInput edge handling (navbar.component.ts:395-398) also assumes LTR. With dir=rtl the flex row reverses, so Right (setNextItemActive = next in DOM) moves to the visually left item. Other kbq components (accordion, breadcrumbs, button-toggle, dropdown) respect Directionality, but the library ships no RTL locale, so this is P3. Visual order needs a browser, so no repro.
  Fix: Inject Directionality and pass its value (and changes) to withHorizontalOrientation, and swap RIGHT/LEFT for the vertical dropdown and the field-edge handling in RTL.
- **[navbar] openOver and expanded have no boolean transform** — confirmed in the source.
  vertical-navbar.component.ts:133 openOver = input\<boolean>(false) has no booleanAttribute transform, unlike e.g. KbqNavbarItem.collapsable (navbar-item.component.ts:543). A bare `openOver` attribute passes '' and the host binding (87) evaluates it falsy, so the open-over mode stays off. Under strictTemplates (strictAttributeTypes) the static attribute fails type checking. expanded (136) is a model(), which cannot take a transform, so only openOver is fixable without an API change. API ergonomics inconsistency, no repro.
  Fix: Use input(false, { transform: booleanAttribute }) for openOver.
- **[navbar] In a vertical navbar every tooltip refresh forces placement right** — confirmed in the source.
  navbar-item.component.ts:705-708 sets tooltipPlacement and tooltipPlacementPriority to Right whenever the element is vertical, and navbar-brand.component.ts:223-225 does the same for placement. updateTooltip runs from the constructor effect (navbar-item.component.ts:664-674), afterNextRender, the content observer (677-682), every rectangle state change (647-651) and ngAfterContentInit (686-688). The tooltip applies a bound kbqPlacement only in ngOnChanges when the input changes (tooltip.component.ts:645-651), so the next refresh overwrites it. The re-exposed kbqPlacement/kbqPlacementPriority inputs therefore have no lasting effect in a vertical navbar. Minor inconsistency, no repro.
  Fix: Apply the Right default only while the consumer has not bound kbqPlacement/kbqPlacementPriority.
- **[navbar] The host tab index counts disabled and decorative items** — confirmed in the source.
  navbar.component.ts:86-88: renderedTabIndex is -1 only when focusableItemsQuery() is empty, and disabled/decorative items count. On focus, focus() (165-177) calls keyManager.setFirstItemActive(). setActiveItemByIndex (list-key-manager.ts:443-462) skips every disabled item and returns without activating anything, so focus stays on the host, an empty tab stop. Realistic case: a navbar whose only element is a decorative \<div kbq-navbar-brand>. A11y polish, no repro.
  Fix: Derive renderedTabIndex from the presence of at least one item that is not disabled (signal-backed disabled/decorative state).
- **[notification-center] backdropClass has no effect because the overlay never has a backdrop** — confirmed in the source.
  notification-center.ts:589-590 declares the backdropClass input ('Class that will be used in the background'). overlayConfig at 704-708 hard-codes `hasBackdrop: false`. CDK attaches a backdrop only when hasBackdrop is set (\_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:796-798), and backdropClass is applied only to that backdrop element (~line 993), so the value is never used. It is a dead input and misleading JSDoc, with no user-facing breakage. jsdom could show it, but the repro budget is reserved for P0-P2.
  Fix: Deprecate and remove the input, or document it as a no-op.
- **[notification-center] Silent-mode switch is named after the current mode as an action; menu options carry no checked state** — confirmed in the source.
  notification-center.html:15-24: aria-label and tooltip are `silentMode() ? doNotDisturb : showPopUpNotifications`. en-US.ts:255-256 has doNotDisturb 'Turn off notifications' and showPopUpNotifications 'Turn on notifications'. ru-RU.ts:269-270 has the same wording ('Отключить уведомления' / 'Включить уведомления'). So while pop-ups are on (silentMode false) the button reads 'Turn on notifications', even though activating it only opens a dropdown. The options (html:169-192) are plain kbq-dropdown-item buttons. dropdown-item.component.ts:53 says items set no ARIA role. The active option is shown only through the visual class `kbq-notification-center-switcher\_\_mark\_hidden` (html:177,189), with no aria-checked or aria-current.
  Fix: Give the switch a stable name (e.g. 'Notification settings') and expose the active option as a checked item (menuitemradio with aria-checked).
- **[notification-center] Delete buttons of every row and every day group share one accessible name** — confirmed in the source.
  notification-center.html:98: the group delete button has \[attr.aria-label\]="localeConfiguration().remove". notification-item.html:315: the row delete button uses the same panel.localeConfiguration().remove. Neither has aria-describedby or aria-labelledby pointing at the group title or the item title. en-US remove is 'Delete' (en-US.ts:253), so every delete control in the panel is announced as 'Delete'.
  Fix: Compose the name with the item title or group date (or add aria-describedby pointing at them).
- **[notification-center] \[disabled\] on a plain \<button> trigger leaves the button enabled and without aria-disabled** — confirmed in the source.
  notification-center.ts:734-737 declares an input with alias 'disabled'. Angular routes a `\[disabled\]` property binding to a matching directive input and then does not set the native property, so a plain \<button kbqNotificationCenterTrigger \[disabled\]> keeps button.disabled false. The disabled setter (659-665) only hides the panel. The host bindings (552-557) set neither `disabled` nor `aria-disabled`. The button stays focusable and is announced as enabled, but does nothing. With kbq-button, KbqButton's own disabled input also receives the binding, so only plain elements are affected. The existing spec host (SimpleNotificationCenter, a plain button with \[disabled\]) only asserts that the panel does not open. jsdom could show it, but the repro budget is reserved for P0-P2.
  Fix: Add host bindings `\[attr.aria-disabled\]` (and `\[attr.disabled\]` for native buttons) driven by the trigger's disabled state.
- **[notification-center] scrolledToBottomOffset and popoverMode reach the panel only when it opens** — confirmed in the source.
  notification-center.ts:788-805 updateData() is the only place that copies popoverMode (795) and scrolledToBottomOffset (797) to the instance. It runs only from attachAndShow (pop-up-trigger.ts:432). ngOnChanges (739-769) never calls it. The popoverMode setter (606-627) changes placement and priority but neither touches the instance nor repositions. scrolledToBottomOffset is a signal input (599) with no effect. By contrast, the popoverHeight setter (642-650) forwards to an open instance. A change while the panel is open is therefore ignored until the next open, including the host class kbq-notification-center\_popover (161) and the position.
  Fix: Forward both to an open instance (call updateData() from ngOnChanges when isOpen, or use an effect on the signal input).
- **[notification-center] \<kbq-notification-center> rendered in place steals focus to its silent-mode switch on render** — confirmed in the source.
  notification-center.ts:323-331: ngAfterViewInit calls focusMonitor.focusVia(switcher, ...) whenever the switch is not disabled. There is no check that the panel was opened from a trigger (`this.trigger` is unset when rendered in place; compare the `this.trigger?.stickToWindow` guard at 309). The e2e fixtures render it in place (e2e.ts:73, 97, 117, 137, 157, 180), so focus jumps into the panel as soon as it renders. The component is @docs-private (126), so in-place use is not a documented consumer path, hence P3. jsdom could show it, but the repro budget is reserved for P0-P2.
  Fix: Focus the switch only when the panel is attached by a trigger (e.g. guard on `this.trigger`, or move the focus call into the trigger's show path).
- **[overflow-items] Everything is shown before the first recalculation and on the server** — confirmed in the source.
  overflow-items.ts:42: hiddenState starts false, so items and the result have no hiding styles and aria-hidden="false". result.hide() runs only inside getHiddenItems (:316), and only the subscription in setupObservers calls it. That subscription returns early on the server (:257) and in the browser runs after debounce(timer(debounceTime)) (:278) following the first items() emission. So the SSR HTML, and the client before that timer, contain every item plus the result ("and 0 more" in the docs examples). The row does not spill out, though: setStyles in the constructor (:337-340) sets overflow:hidden on the server too, so the excess is clipped mid-item. Whether a browser actually paints a frame before the first timer can only be seen in a real browser.
  Fix: Start the result hidden (or render it hidden until the first measurement) and consider a visibility:hidden container until the first recalculation on the client.
- **[overflow-items] Duplicate kbqOverflowItem values collapse in hiddenItemIDs** — confirmed in the source.
  overflow-items.ts:289 `new Set(hiddenItems.map(({ id }) => id()))`: equal IDs merge, so a counter built on hiddenItemIDs().size reports too few. The JSDoc at :101-104 asks for unique values, but no dev-mode check warns. A bare `kbqOverflowItem` attribute gives '' to every item, so all of them collapse into one. In-repo consumers use unique IDs: breadcrumbs uses 0, $index+1 and allItems.length-1, and the top-bar and actions-panel examples use action ids.
  Fix: Warn in dev mode when two items share an ID, or document that the set is keyed by ID and expose a count of hidden items.
