- **[popover] Custom kbqPopoverOffset margin stays on the old side after a placement flip** — confirmed in the source.
  popover.component.ts:178-189 calls applyPopupMargins only inside visibleChange(true). applyPopupMargins (core/pop-up/pop-up-styles.ts:3-37) writes a single side and never clears the others. A flip goes through onPositionChange (pop-up-trigger.ts:617-650), which only swaps the placement class, so the inline margin stays on the old side and the new side gets 8px from popup-margins (core/pop-up/\_pop-up.scss:1-25). The described trigger needs one correction. A scroll does not flip the panel: the strategy is withLockedPosition (pop-up-trigger.ts:560), so CDK apply() reapplies the last position (@angular/cdk \_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:1150-1152). A flip does happen on window resize, which sets \_isInitialRender=true and calls apply() (1140-1143), and on a reposition after an input change or an origin change: updatePosition passes new position objects, withPositions clears \_lastPosition (1268-1272), and reapplyLastPosition falls back to apply(). jsdom has no layout, so it cannot flip for real.
  Fix: Re-apply (and reset the other sides of) the custom margin from onPositionChange/updateClassMap, or express the offset as a CSS custom property read by the placement rules.
- **[popover] kbq-hide-nested-popup rule decided once; later closeOnScroll writes do not add or remove it** — confirmed in the source.
  popover.component.ts:973-980: ngAfterContentInit subscribes hideIfNestedPopupScrolledAway only if closeOnScroll === null at that moment. The handler (1168-1179) never re-reads closeOnScroll, and nothing unsubscribes it. The setter (564-566) coerces to boolean, so the value can never return to null. Only one path shows a difference: the input is left unbound and code later writes closeOnScroll = false. The rule keeps closing the popover when it scrolls out of a .kbq-hide-nested-popup container, against the JSDoc (550-559): 'false — no scroll closes the popover, not even a .kbq-hide-nested-popup one'. Writing true later is covered by ancestorScrolled in closingActions (1089). An initial binding is applied in ngOnChanges before the decision, so it is unaffected. This is a P3 edge case of writing the property at runtime, so it was not reproduced. Showing it would need stubbed getBoundingClientRect.
  Fix: Always subscribe and check `this.closeOnScroll === null` inside hideIfNestedPopupScrolledAway.
- **[popover] kbqPopoverPlacementPriority of only unknown names leaves the strategy without positions** — confirmed in the source.
  updatePlacementPriority (pop-up-trigger.ts:346-352) accepts any non-empty value. getPriorityPlacementStrategy (777-792) drops names missing from POSITION\_MAP, which includes CSS-style names such as 'bottom-left' and any comma-separated string, and returns \[\]. The popover's updatePosition (popover.component.ts:1043-1048) then calls withPositions(\[\]). In dev mode CDK withPositions runs \_validatePositions and throws 'At least one position is required' (@angular/cdk \_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:1268-1273, 1716-1719). That throw happens inside attachAndShow after the overlay is attached (pop-up-trigger.ts:420) and before instance.show (444), so show() throws from the native click listener. The invisible panel stays attached, and later clicks return early (403-413) until an outside click detaches it. In production, apply() dereferences an undefined fallback (\_overlay-module-chunk.mjs:1211/1214). By contrast, an unknown kbqPopoverPlacement warns and falls back (pop-up-trigger.ts:327-342). jsdom could show the throw, but the trigger is invalid consumer input that fails loudly in development, so it is rated P3 and was not reproduced.
  Fix: Fall back to the default strategy (with a console.warn, like updatePlacement) when the filtered priority list is empty.
- **[progress-bar] Binding undefined to the projected text id removes the id, leaving the bar with neither aria-label nor aria-labelledby** — reproduced.
  progress-bar.component.ts:40 `KbqProgressBarText.id = input\<string>(generated)`. A signal input bound to undefined holds undefined and does not fall back to the default; the existing spec says the same for `mode` at progress-bar.component.spec.ts:74-77. :35 `\[attr.id\]: id()` therefore removes the attribute. :124 `text()?.id() ?? null` gives a null aria-labelledby, and :127 `text() ? null : ...` skips the locale name because a text is projected. Repro run: the text has no id attribute, the host has no aria-label and no aria-labelledby, and axe reports `aria-progressbar-name`. Rated P3 rather than P2: `undefined` is not assignable to `input\<string>`, so strict templates reject the binding. It needs non-strict templates or an `any` value. The caption (:56, :82) has the same pattern, but it only loses aria-describedby.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/progress-bar/undefined-text-id.spec.ts`](defect-repros/progress-bar/undefined-text-id.spec.ts)
  Fix: Resolve the id through a computed that falls back to the generated id when the input is undefined or empty (or give the input a transform), for the text and the caption alike.
- **[progress-spinner] color accepts every KbqComponentColors value but only contrast and contrast-fade change the stroke** — confirmed in the source.
  KbqColorDirective (core/common-behaviors/color.ts:33-36,63-66) writes kbq-\<color> for any string; KbqProgressSpinner extends it (progress-spinner.component.ts:59) with default Theme (:103). The only stroke rules are \_progress-spinner-theme.scss:5 (base --stroke = theme), :11-12 (.kbq-contrast) and :15-16 (.kbq-contrast-fade); the tokens file declares only these three circle colors (progress-spinner-tokens.scss:11-13). No other stylesheet in packages/components targets the spinner by color (grep of '--stroke' and 'progress-spinner' in \*.scss). So error, warning, success and theme-fade fall through to the theme stroke. loader-overlay.component.spec.ts:437 binds colors.Error to a spinner. jsdom loads no component styles, so the cascade itself cannot be shown in a spec; rated hygiene because the design tokens define no error/warning/success spinner colors, so it is an API/behaviour mismatch rather than a lost feature.
  Fix: Narrow the color input type to theme | contrast | contrast-fade (or add stroke rules and tokens for the remaining colors if the design calls for them), and fix the loader-overlay spec that binds Error.
- **[radio] A \[checked\] binding on a button of a group bound to formControl/ngModel changes what the group shows without updating the control** — reproduced.
  FormControlDirective writes the control value synchronously before the buttons bind. The button's ngOnChanges (radio.component.ts:586-590) then applies checkedInput through the checked setter, which at 390-391 sets radioGroup.selected and with it group.value. That code path never calls controlValueAccessorChangeFn, which only onInputChange calls (696). The repro passes: with control('fire') and \[checked\]="true" on water, the control stays 'fire' while group.value is 'water' and the buttons show \[false,true\]. With ngModel the disagreement lasts only for the first render: NgModel writes the model in a microtask (writeValue, 304-306), which overrides the \[checked\] state, so the view converges on the model. This part was traced from source only. The severity is P3 because it takes two conflicting sources of truth on one group, and Decision 4 of the design states that code writes are silent.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/radio/checked-binding-desync.spec.ts`](defect-repros/radio/checked-binding-desync.spec.ts)
  Fix: Document that \[checked\] must not be combined with a form-bound group (or warn in dev mode), or make the group ignore button \[checked\] bindings while a ControlValueAccessor is registered.
- **[radio] The group's focus() targets the selected button even when it is disabled, so focus does not move** — confirmed in the source.
  radio.component.ts:263-268 picks `this.\_selected() ?? this.radios?.find((radio) => !radio.disabled)`, so the disabled filter applies only to the fallback. KbqRadioButton.focus (657-659) calls focusMonitor.focusVia on the native input, which is bound \[disabled\]="disabled" (radio.component.html:16), and browsers ignore focus() on a disabled control, so nothing gets focus. jsdom's focus rules differ from a browser's (the disabled input carries tabindex=-1), so this was not reproduced. For context: in Chromium a group whose checked radio is disabled is skipped by Tab altogether, which matches the JSDoc's 'same rule the browser follows'. The mismatch is with radio.en/ru.mdx, which promise that focus goes to the checked option, and with a programmatic focus() that silently does nothing. The dev app's 'Disabled selected buttons' groups (components-dev/radio/template.html:42-45) set up this state.
  Fix: Use the selected button only when it is enabled, otherwise fall back to the first enabled one (or document the no-op).
- **[resizer] Direction change during a drag makes the size jump; \[0, 0\] does not end the drag** — reproduced.
  updateSize reads `this.direction()` on every move (resizer.ts:312-314) and applies it to the starting size and the whole travel. In the repro, \[1,0\] dragged by 30px gives 130px; after flipping to \[-1,0\], a 1px move writes 69px. The \[0,0\] check runs only at pointerdown (resizer.ts:187-190), so switching to \[0,0\] mid-drag does not end the drag. The effect at resizer.ts:153-157 sets the body cursor to resolvedCursor, which is 'default !important' (resizer.ts:144) when no `cursor` input is set. Both axis writes are then skipped (resizer.ts:318-319), but `sizeChange` keeps emitting the starting size (resizer.ts:322) while the element keeps its last written width. The repro confirms all of this, and the body cursor is restored on pointerup. The splitter maps `disabled` to \[0,0\] (splitter.ts:640-641), and its handleResizeTo (splitter.ts:859-868) does not check `disabled`. Reading that code, disabling a splitter mid-drag would snap the panel back to its starting size (not run). The effects are transient and need an input change during an active drag.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/resizer/direction-change.spec.ts`](defect-repros/resizer/direction-change.spec.ts)
  Fix: Capture the direction at pointerdown and use it for the rest of the drag, or end the drag when the direction becomes \[0, 0\].
- **[resizer] preventDefault on the starting pointerdown suppresses compatibility mousedown/mouseup** — confirmed in the source.
  handleElementPointerDown calls `event.preventDefault()` unconditionally once a drag starts (resizer.ts:194). Under the Pointer Events spec, cancelling pointerdown suppresses the compatibility mouse events for that pointer (mousedown, mousemove, mouseup) until it is released. click, dblclick and contextmenu are not compatibility events and still fire. So a consumer's `(mousedown)` on a handle, and any document-level mousedown or mouseup listener (outside-close logic, modality detection), never sees a drag press. The content panel (content-panel.ts:264), the description list (dl.component.ts:100) and the splitter (splitter.ts:358) bind only `dblclick`, so they are unaffected. The description list and the splitter also set their focus origin explicitly in resizeStart (dl.component.ts:322-326, splitter.ts:498-501). jsdom does not generate compatibility mouse events, so this cannot be reproduced there. The side effect is undocumented and probably intentional (it prevents text selection and native drag).
  Fix: Document the side effect on the resizer page and the resizeStart JSDoc, or prevent selection with `user-select`/`selectstart` instead of cancelling pointerdown.
- **[scrollbar] kbqScrollbarMode bound to undefined or null creates a track without the hover class (behaves like always)** — reproduced.
  scrollbar.ts:320 and :1008 declare `input\<KbqScrollbarMode>(this.options.mode, { alias: 'kbqScrollbarMode' })` with no transform, so a bound undefined/null replaces the default. The effect at :344-361 computes `showTrack = mode !== 'native' && mode !== 'hidden'`, which is true for undefined/null, creates the track and calls `setInput('mode', undefined)`. The track host binding at :845 (`mode() === 'hover'`) is then false, so the hover class is missing. The viewport binding at :296 still hides the native scrollbar. In the repro, an explicit 'hover' control gets the class, while undefined and null do not, even though KBQ\_SCROLLBAR\_OPTIONS.mode is 'hover'. With strict template type checking a consumer cannot bind undefined/null, because the input type excludes them. The library's own bindings are typed KbqScrollbarMode (select.component.ts:945, tabs/paginated-tab-header.ts:320). So only consumers without strict templates, or with a cast, hit this.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/scrollbar/undefined-mode.spec.ts`](defect-repros/scrollbar/undefined-mode.spec.ts)
  Fix: Give the mode input a transform that falls back to options.mode when the value is null or undefined.
- **[scrollbar] A selector passed to scrollToElement is matched inside the injected track; a generic selector such as div resolves to a bar** — reproduced.
  scrollbar.ts:416 resolves a string with `this.getNativeElement().querySelector(target)` on the scrolling element. createTrack inserts the track as that element's first child (:454), and the bars and thumbs are `div`s (:805, :813, :822, :830), so they come first in document order. In the repro, with a vertical bar drawn, `scrollToElement('div')` called CdkScrollable.scrollTo with the bar's offset (11), not the consumer div's (200). Without bars, kbq-scrollbar's own `.kbq-scrollbar\_\_content` wrapper div (:992) is matched before consumer content. Only generic selectors (tag names, `\*`, `:first-child`, role selectors) are affected.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/scrollbar/selector-matches-track.spec.ts`](defect-repros/scrollbar/selector-matches-track.spec.ts)
  Fix: Skip matches inside the track: take querySelectorAll and the first match without closest('kbq-scrollbar-track'), and possibly search inside .kbq-scrollbar\_\_content for kbq-scrollbar.
- **[scrollbar] A bar is drawn only when the visible part is at most 99 % of the content; an overflow up to 1 % scrolls without any bar** — reproduced.
  scrollbar.ts:945-946 draws a bar only when `Math.ceil((clientHeight / scrollHeight) \* 100) \< 100`, and the same for width. Any ratio above 0.99 rounds up to 100. The viewport still hides the native scrollbar in hover/always (:296, scrollbar-viewport.scss:10-18). In the repro, clientHeight 1000 with scrollHeight 1010 draws no vertical bar while the native-scrollbar-hidden class is set, and the 1011 control draws one. The threshold grows with the viewport (about 10 px at 1000 px, 20 px at 2000 px). It may be intended as a rounding tolerance, but it scales with size.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/scrollbar/one-percent-threshold.spec.ts`](defect-repros/scrollbar/one-percent-threshold.spec.ts)
  Fix: Use a fixed pixel tolerance (e.g. scrollHeight - clientHeight > 1) instead of a percentage.
- **[scrollbar] Any mouse button starts a thumb drag, the right button included** — confirmed in the source.
  Neither the bar mousedown stream (scrollbar.ts:655-658) nor the thumb mousedown stream (:659-673) checks `event.button`. A right or middle press on the thumb switches to document mousemove until mouseup, the same as a left press, and a press on the bar jumps the scroll position. The existing spec 'scrolls on a track click made with the %s button' (scrollbar.spec.ts:1045-1062) already passes for button 2 on the bar and records this as current behaviour. The thumb path has the same code without a filter. It is not confirmed whether a right press on the thumb leaves a drag active after the context menu closes, on platforms that open the menu on mousedown and may withhold the mouseup. That needs a real browser.
  Fix: Ignore mousedown with event.button !== 0 in both streams, and update the spec that records the current behaviour.
- **[scrollbar] Thumbs have role="scrollbar" but are not focusable or keyboard-operable, and the scrolling element gets no tabindex** — confirmed in the source.
  The KbqScrollbarThumb host sets role/aria-\* only (scrollbar.ts:633-641), with no tabindex and no keydown handling. The KbqScrollbarViewport host (:293-298) sets no tabindex, and scrollbar.ts contains no tabindex or keydown anywhere. Neither scrollbar.spec.ts nor e2e.playwright-spec.ts runs axe. A scrollable region whose content has no focusable element therefore depends on the browser making scroll containers focusable. Chrome 130+ and Firefox do; Safari does not. This is the same as a plain overflow:auto element, but the region exposes a scrollbar widget that cannot be operated. How assistive technology announces it needs a real screen reader.
  Fix: Add an axe test with non-focusable overflowing content, and either make the viewport focusable when its content has no focusable element or keep the thumbs out of the accessibility tree (role none or aria-hidden), since native scrolling does the work.
- **[search-expandable] A bound emitValueTimeout of null or a non-numeric string becomes NaN** — confirmed in the source.
  search-expandable.ts:198 declares emitValueTimeout with transform numberAttribute. Angular's numberAttribute (node\_modules/@angular/core/fesm2022/core.mjs:2546-2548) returns its NaN fallback for null, undefined and unparsable strings. timer(NaN) at line 349 is scheduled as is: rxjs timer clamps only due \< 0, which is false for NaN. The delay then reaches setInterval, where browsers and the fake timers treat NaN as 0, so every keystroke is emitted almost at once instead of after 200 ms. tabIndex (279-282) uses kbqOptionalNumberAttribute and falls back to the default; emitValueTimeout does not. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because it was rated P3: it needs the consumer to bind null or a non-number.
  Fix: Use a transform that falls back to defaultEmitValueTimeout for null, undefined or NaN (e.g. kbqOptionalNumberAttribute ?? defaultEmitValueTimeout).
- **[search-expandable] Escape is bound only on the input, not on the close button** — confirmed in the source.
  search-expandable.html:31 binds (keydown.escape) only on the input. The close button (35-42) has only (click). kbq-form-field and the icon button have no Escape handler (grep of form-field/\*.ts and icon/icon-button.component.ts). Inside a modal, modal.component.ts:675-686 cancels the dialog on an Escape whose defaultPrevented is false. So Escape with focus on the close button leaves the search expanded, and inside a modal it closes the modal. The baseline spec records 'Escape ... on the close button MUST NOT be handled' as current behaviour. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because it was rated P3.
  Fix: Move the Escape handler from the input to the kbq-form-field wrapper (or add it to the close button) so that any Escape inside the expanded field collapses it and is consumed.
- **[search-expandable] tabIndex is not applied to the close button** — confirmed in the source.
  search-expandable.html:26 binds \[tabIndex\] on the input, and line 11 binds it on the collapsed button. The close button (35-42) gets no tabindex binding. KbqIconButton binds \[attr.tabindex\] to its own tabindex, which defaults to 0 (icon/icon-button.component.ts:51,71-79). With tabIndex=-1 the input leaves the tab order and the close button stays in it. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because it was rated P3.
  Fix: Bind \[tabindex\]="tabIndex" on the close button as well.
- **[search-expandable] An empty-string tooltipText or placeholder leaves the button or input without an accessible name** — confirmed in the source.
  The getters at search-expandable.ts:201-203 and 212-214 fall back to the locale only on null or undefined (??), so '' is used as given. The template then renders aria-label="" on the collapsed button (html:6) and aria-label="" plus placeholder="" on the input (html:25,27). The button's only content is the \<i kbq-icon>, which has no text, and the input has no other label, so both lose their accessible name. A consumer passing '' to hide the tooltip hits this. The baseline spec records 'the empty string included, is used as given', and the axe tests (spec.ts accessibility block) never bind ''. jsdom/axe could show this, but it was not reproduced because it was rated P3: the consumer has to pass '' explicitly.
  Fix: Fall back to the locale string for an empty or blank tooltipText/placeholder when it is used as the aria-label (e.g. `||` instead of `??` for the name).
- **[select] With #kbqSelectTagContent the remove control falls back to tabindex=-1 and the bare name "Remove"; docs do not show how to restore them** — confirmed in the source.
  The KbqTagRemove defaults are tabIndex input -1 (tag.component.ts:891) and the name a11y `remove` without the value (tag.component.ts:882). The docs snippet (select.en.mdx:40-47, same in select.ru.mdx:44) and the live example (select-custom-tag-content-example.ts:30-35) bind neither \[tabIndex\] nor \[aria-label\]. select.en.mdx:264 only says to 'reproduce the control'. A consumer template also cannot reuse the select's own logic through `select`, because `removeItemAriaLabel` (select.component.ts:1805) and `isTriggerValueVisible` (:1080) are protected. Docs and a11y gap only, no runtime fault, so it was not reproduced.
  Fix: Show `\[tabIndex\]="0"` and a value-specific `\[aria-label\]` in the docs snippet and the example, or expose a public helper for the name and visibility.
- **[select] Under virtual scrolling one lookup calls compareWith without the runCompareWith guard** — confirmed in the source.
  createVirtualOptionForValue calls `source.find((item) => this.compareWith()(item, value))` directly (select.component.ts:2389-2392). The other lookups, getCorrespondOption (:2353) and resolveSelectedOption (:2437), go through runCompareWith (core/select/compare-with.ts:12-24), which catches, warns in dev mode and returns false. The path runs synchronously from writeValue (:1619-1623) through applyValue (:2291-2297), setSelectionByValue (:2304-2340, try/finally with no catch) and selectValue (:2361-2379) whenever withVirtualScroll is on and no rendered option matches. A comparator such as `(a, b) => a.id === b.id` meeting an undefined placeholder item in the source therefore throws out of `control.setValue()` instead of reading as 'no match'. Not reproduced because it is P3: it needs virtual scroll plus a throwing comparator, and it is an inconsistent guard.
  Fix: Use runCompareWith(this.compareWith(), item, value) in the virtual-source lookup.
- **[select] A custom id is not written to the host element, and the panel id ignores it** — confirmed in the source.
  The host object (select.component.ts:250-273) has no `\[attr.id\]`. id() is computed at :880, and panelId is built from the uid only (:1156). The siblings bind it: tree-select (tree-select.component.ts:242) and KbqInput (input.ts:58). A static `id="x"` still reaches the DOM as a plain attribute, so the claim only holds for a bound `\[id\]`, as in the spec host SelectWithCustomId (select.component.spec.ts:437-447). The id tests (:9066-9106) only read select.id(). The panel id ignoring the custom id matches tree-select (:883) and is harmless, since aria-controls points at it either way. The form-field label id is derived from control.id() (form-field.ts:273), so the label wiring is unaffected. Not reproduced because it is P3.
  Fix: Add `'\[attr.id\]': 'id()'` to the host bindings, as tree-select and input do.
- **[select] useDefaultHandlers of kbq-select-matcher has no effect on kbq-select** — confirmed in the source.
  KbqSelectMatcher.useDefaultHandlers is declared at core/select/common.ts:45-47. Only tree-select reads it (tree-select.component.ts:1215, 1430, 1444, 1458). KbqSelect binds (click) toggle, (keydown), (focus) and (blur) on its host (select.component.ts:269-272), and toggle/handleKeydown/onFocus/onBlur (:1486, :1671, :1682, :1692) never consult customMatcher(). For an input inside the matcher, every click toggles the panel, and handleClosedKeydown preventDefaults Space and Enter (:2129-2131). The select docs never mention the input (only examples.tree-select.en.mdx:15 does). Not reproduced because it is P3, an inconsistency with tree-select.
  Fix: Mirror tree-select: return early from the host handlers when customMatcher()?.useDefaultHandlers() is false, or document that the input is tree-select only.
- **[sidebar] The shortcut ignores only the Meta key (Shift + \[ and macOS Option + \[ still toggle)** — reproduced.
  sidebar.component.ts:377 listens to `keypress`. handleKeydown (390-401) bails out only on isControl/isInput. isControl (core/keycodes/keycodes.ts:133-144) compares event.keyCode with SHIFT/CONTROL/ALT and otherwise returns metaKey. On a keypress, keyCode is the character code (123 for '{'), so only metaKey is ever filtered. isLeftBracket (keycodes.ts:183-185) checks only `code === 'BracketLeft'`. Repro: a keypress with shiftKey and key '{', and one with altKey and key '“' (macOS Option), both close the left sidebar. Browsers fire no keypress for Ctrl or Windows Alt combos, so in practice Shift, macOS Option and AltGr layouts get through. These keys are pressed outside text fields only, hence P3.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/sidebar/shortcut-filters.spec.ts`](defect-repros/sidebar/shortcut-filters.spec.ts)
  Fix: Listen to keydown and ignore the event when any of shiftKey/ctrlKey/altKey/metaKey is set, instead of comparing keyCode with the modifier codes.
- **[sidepanel] kbqSidepanelBody / kbqSidepanelFooter / kbqSidepanelActions selectors lack brackets and match elements, not attributes** — confirmed in the source.
  sidepanel-directives.ts:129, 154 and 169 end their selectors with bare 'kbqSidepanelBody', 'kbqSidepanelFooter' and 'kbqSidepanelActions'. Angular parses a bare selector as an element name, so only \<kbqSidepanelBody> matches and \<div kbqSidepanelBody> silently gets no class, overflow shadow or scrollbar. KbqSidepanelClose (line 29) shows the intended camelCase attribute form: button\[kbqSidepanelClose\]. The selectors are in the golden file (tools/public\_api\_guard/components/sidepanel.api.md:45,54,128). Nothing in packages, apps or docs uses them. jsdom could show this, but it is P3 and was not run under the repro budget rules.
  Fix: Change them to \[kbqSidepanelBody\], \[kbqSidepanelFooter\] and \[kbqSidepanelActions\] (and approve the API golden), or drop them.
- **[sidepanel] A close button without a value closes with '' instead of undefined** — confirmed in the source.
  sidepanel-directives.ts:33 closes with kbqSidepanelClose() ?? sidepanelResult(). sidepanelResult is input(undefined, { alias: 'kbq-sidepanel-close' }) (line 45). A bare static attribute sets that input to '', so close('') runs. The built-in header button (line 79-88) is a bare kbq-sidepanel-close too. Escape, backdrop and indent clicks close with undefined (sidepanel-ref.ts:123,133), so the header X and Escape give different results. The baseline spec documents '' as SHALL (specs/sidepanel/spec.md:209-216), and Angular Material's mat-dialog-close behaves the same way, so this is an inconsistency rather than a break. jsdom could show it, but it is P3 and was not run.
  Fix: Map an empty-string attribute value to undefined (e.g., a transform on the hyphenated input), at least for the header's built-in button.
- **[sidepanel] aria-hidden is applied only to page siblings present when the first modal opens; later-appended elements stay reachable** — confirmed in the source.
  hideContentFromAssistiveTechnology (sidepanel.service.ts:518-545) marks overlayContainer.parentElement.children only when modalSidepanels was empty (firstModal, 519/523). Nothing re-scans for later modals and no MutationObserver exists, so body children appended while a modal panel is open never get aria-hidden. This is the same approach CDK Dialog takes. aria-modal="true" on the container (sidepanel-container.component.ts:53) limits the impact in screen readers that honour it.
  Fix: Observe body children (MutationObserver) while a modal sidepanel is open, or re-scan on each modal open.
- **[sidepanel] Backdrops become transparent only within a same-position stack, so a modal at another edge over a modal dims the page twice** — confirmed in the source.
  A backdrop's opacity goes to 0 only on the start of a Lower or BottomPanel transition, or of Hidden (sidepanel-ref.ts:49-57, 93-97). attachContainer demotes only panels returned by getOpenedSidepanelsWithSamePosition (sidepanel.service.ts:301-307, 441-443). So with a right modal open, a left modal leaves both backdrops with kbq-overlay-dark-backdrop and its semi-transparent --kbq-background-overlay (\_sidepanel-theme.scss:19-21, sidepanel-tokens.scss:28-31) at full opacity, stacked. The visual result follows from the CSS. Whether single dimming across edges is required is a design decision: the existing test only covers a same-position stack.
  Fix: Hide every backdrop but the topmost across all open sidepanels, not per position.
- **[skeleton] Element presets apply KbqSkeleton as a host directive without exposing its input; \[kbqSkeleton\] on them fails to compile or matches the directive twice** — reproduced.
  skeleton-presets.ts:81-133: the five element presets declare `hostDirectives: \[KbqSkeleton\]` with no `inputs`, so `kbqSkeleton` (skeleton.ts:165, alias of `enabled`) is not bindable on them. With KbqSkeleton imported next to a preset, the selector `\[kbqSkeleton\]` (skeleton.ts:150) also matches the element and Angular's dev-mode check (node\_modules/@angular/core/fesm2022/\_debug\_node-chunk.mjs:7838/8069-8079, `ngDevMode && assertNoDuplicateDirectives`) throws NG0309 'Directive KbqSkeleton matches multiple times on the same element'. Reproduced: `\<kbq-skeleton-button \[kbqSkeleton\]="loading()" />` with imports \[KbqSkeleton, KbqSkeletonButton\] throws NG0309 on createComponent/detectChanges (test passes). Without the import it is an unknown-property binding (NG8002 in AOT). The baseline spec states this as intended ('Element presets cannot be turned off', swap with @if), and the failure is loud at development time, hence P3 rather than P2.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/skeleton/element-preset-input.spec.ts`](defect-repros/skeleton/element-preset-input.spec.ts)
  Fix: Either document that element presets are swapped with @if and never take \[kbqSkeleton\], or forward the input with `hostDirectives: \[{ directive: KbqSkeleton, inputs: \['kbqSkeleton'\] }\]` (consumers must then not import KbqSkeleton for that element).
- **[skeleton] Providing KBQ\_SKELETON\_PRESETS\_CONFIGURATION directly with a partial object leaves missing keys undefined for presets right below it, which then render a single item** — reproduced.
  skeleton-presets.ts:56-59: the token's factory default is replaced wholesale by a direct provider; only kbqSkeletonPresetsConfigurationProvider (65-74) spreads the defaults back in. The presets read the value straight into input defaults (e.g. 187, 252, 311), which are not passed through the transform, so `undefined` reaches range() (77-78) and gives one item; equalColumns(undefined) gives one column. Reproduced with `{ provide: KBQ\_SKELETON\_PRESETS\_CONFIGURATION, useValue: { tableRows: 4 } }` (no cast needed: useValue is `any`): dl renders 1 row, table 1 header cell and 4 body cells, group 1 item instead of 3/3/3 (test passes). The existing spec 'should take the keys a parent configuration lacks from the defaults' (skeleton-presets.spec.ts:305-322) only covers a partial value with the helper provider below it. The docs show only the helper, so this is a misuse that fails silently: P3.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/skeleton/partial-token.spec.ts`](defect-repros/skeleton/partial-token.spec.ts)
  Fix: Merge the defaults where the presets inject the token (e.g. a small inject helper spreading KBQ\_SKELETON\_PRESETS\_DEFAULT\_CONFIGURATION under the injected value), or document that a direct provider must supply every key.
- **[split-button] panelAutoWidth has no boolean transform, so a bare attribute passes '' and is ignored** — reproduced.
  split-button.ts:48 declares `input\<boolean>(false)` with no transform. disabled (split-button.ts:122-125) uses booleanAttribute. split-button.ts:204 tests the value for truthiness, so '' leaves widthOrigin undefined. Repro with `\<kbq-split-button panelAutoWidth>`, a 600px host and a 50px chevron: panelAutoWidth() is '', trigger.widthOrigin is undefined, and the pane gets min-width 200px, not 600px. Specs compile without type checking. Under strictTemplates the same markup is a compile error instead (string is not assignable to boolean), so only projects without strict templates hit the silent failure.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/split-button/panel-auto-width-attribute.spec.ts`](defect-repros/split-button/panel-auto-width-attribute.spec.ts)
  Fix: Declare `input(false, { transform: booleanAttribute })`, as disabled does, and approve the API golden.
- **[split-button] The host color class does not follow the style (kbq-contrast-fade host vs kbq-contrast buttons under transparent)** — confirmed in the source.
  The host class comes from color.ts:65, `kbq-${color() || defaultColor()}`, and split-button.ts:145 sets the default to contrast-fade whatever the style. With no color bound, nothing is propagated (split-button.ts:74-78), so the buttons take their style's default: transparent maps to contrast (button.component.ts:89-93, 630). The existing spec split-button.spec.ts:214-222 shows the buttons reading Contrast under unbound transparent. So the host is kbq-contrast-fade kbq-button\_transparent and the buttons are kbq-contrast kbq-button\_transparent. The host-level rules in \_split-button-theme.scss:46-113 key on host pairs: contrast-fade/contrast with filled, theme-fade with outline, theme with transparent. None of them targets contrast or contrast-fade with transparent, so nothing renders differently today. The defect is latent coupling, not a visible bug.
  Fix: Derive the host's default color from the current style, the same way KbqButton does with getDefaultColorForStyle, or key the host rules on the nested buttons' classes.
- **[split-button] contrast-fade set in code through color.set() is not propagated, unlike the same value bound through \[color\]** — confirmed in the source.
  split-button.ts:74-78 computes `this.colorInput() || color !== this.defaultColor() ? color : undefined`. After color.set('contrast-fade') with no input, colorInput() is undefined and color() equals defaultColor() (contrast-fade, split-button.ts:145), so the effect at split-button.ts:147-151 calls setColorFromGroup(undefined). Under transparent the buttons then fall back to contrast (button.component.ts:630). A bound `\[color\]="'contrast-fade'"` makes colorInput() truthy, so contrast-fade is propagated. The path is deterministic and was not run because the defect is P3. The JSDoc at split-button.ts:69-73 describes this rule, and KbqButtonGroupRoot shares it (button-group.ts:51-55).
  Fix: Track whether a color was set in code (colorSetInCode is private to KbqColorDirective) and propagate it even when it equals the default.
- **[split-button] A kbq-button wrapped in another element is dropped without warning; as the only button it triggers the 'at least one button' error** — reproduced.
  split-button.ts:23 projects only `\<ng-content select="\[kbq-button\]" />`, and split-button.ts:43 collects `contentChildren(KbqButton)` without descendants. Repro: (1) `\<span>\<button kbq-button>Only\</button>\</span>` makes detectChanges throw 'kbq-split-button must contain at least one button' (split-button.ts:166-167), although the template has a button. (2) With a direct first button and a wrapped second one, the host renders one button, the wrapper is in neither the host nor the fixture DOM, `kbq-split-button\_styles-for-nested` is absent, and nothing warns (fail-on-console would have failed the test). The overview pages do not say that only direct children are rendered.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/split-button/wrapped-button.spec.ts`](defect-repros/split-button/wrapped-button.spec.ts)
  Fix: Document the direct-child requirement and add a dev-mode warning for kbq-button descendants that are not projected, or reword the error to say 'direct kbq-button children'.
- **[splitter] kbqSplitterOptionsProvider spreads the given keys over the built-in defaults, so a nested provider resets the outer options** — confirmed in the source.
  splitter.ts:271-276 returns `useValue: { ...KBQ\_SPLITTER\_DEFAULT\_OPTIONS, ...options }`. Its value is built from the module constant and never injects the parent KBQ\_SPLITTER\_OPTIONS. An inner `{ snapTolerance: 64 }` therefore yields `appearance: 'divider'` whatever an outer provider set. The existing spec 'should leave the options it was not given at their defaults' (splitter.spec.ts:1215-1223) shows the same merge with a single provider. No repro was run because the policy reserves repros for P0-P2. The behaviour does what the JSDoc says ('Overrides the default splitter options'), and kbqLinkOptionsProvider (link.ts:71-76) and kbqScrollbarOptionsProvider (scrollbar.ts:174-179) use the same pattern. That makes it a library-wide design choice with a surprising result, so I rated it hygiene rather than P2.
  Fix: Use a factory that injects the parent KBQ\_SPLITTER\_OPTIONS ({ optional: true, skipSelf: true }) and spreads over it, ideally for the link and scrollbar providers too; at least document the replace semantics.
- **[splitter] Only separators handle F6/Shift+F6; from a panel F6 does nothing** — confirmed in the source.
  F6 is handled only by handleSeparatorKeydown (splitter.ts:920-925), which is bound only on the separator in the panel template (splitter.ts:359). The panel host (368-373) has tabindex=-1 and no keydown binding. The splitter host (581-590) has none, the KbqResizable host directive has none (resizer.ts:56-67), and the separator is a child of the panel, so a keydown on the panel never reaches it. After F6 moves focus to a panel (focusPanel, 1010-1012), F6 and Shift+F6 do nothing; Tab still works, so focus is not trapped. The existing spec 'should move focus between panels on F6' (splitter.spec.ts:979-990) dispatches Shift+F6 on the separator after focus is already on panel\[1\], which hides this. splitter.en.mdx:82 documents F6 as moving between panels. No repro was run because the policy reserves repros for P0-P2.
  Fix: Handle F6/Shift+F6 on the panel host (or the splitter host) as well, moving to the next/previous panel or separator, and fix the existing test to dispatch on the focused element.
- **[splitter] Nothing relates collapsedSize to minSize (no-minSize collapsible panel collapses only past its far edge; collapsedSize > minSize makes collapsing grow the panel)** — confirmed in the source.
  (a) expandedMinSizes defaults to 0 (splitter.ts:649-653), and pinsFor flips a panel only when `aimed \< min/2` (1137-1145), which here is aimed \< 0. KbqResizer reports an unclamped `this.width + direction\*(x - this.x)` (resizer.ts:308-313), so aimed falls below 0 only once the pointer passes the panel's far edge. Until then the panel can sit at 0px with collapsed=false and no collapsedChange. (b) collapsedSizes are resolved on their own (669-673), and a collapsed panel's min and max are pinned to that strip (698-709). With minSize=100 and collapsedSize=150, a drag past 50px flips collapsed and fitSplitterSizes or resizeSplitterSizesAt pin the panel at 150, so it grows from 100. Home at the minimum calls `panel.collapsed.set(true)` (957) with the same effect. No dev-mode warning covers either case: reportUnsupportedSizes (524-546) checks units only. These are misconfigurations the component neither validates nor documents, so I rated them hygiene and ran no repro.
  Fix: Clamp the effective collapsedSize to at most minSize (or warn in dev mode), and document or require minSize for collapsible panels.
- **[splitter] resizeSplitterSizesAt and KbqSplitterSnapPoint are public API without @internal/@docs-private** — confirmed in the source.
  splitter.ts:157-166: the JSDoc of resizeSplitterSizesAt has no @internal. splitter.ts:206-207: KbqSplitterSnapPoint has none either. Both are reached through public-api.ts `export \* from './splitter'` and appear in tools/public\_api\_guard/components/splitter.api.md:166 and :172. The sibling helpers resolveSplitterSize (69), fitSplitterSizes (91) and snapSplitterSize (216) are @internal. Outside splitter.ts, resizeSplitterSizesAt is used only by splitter.spec.ts, and KbqSplitterSnapPoint is used only by the @internal snapSplitterSize, so both could be stripped. No release tag contains the rewrite commit 45123c321, so stripping them now is not yet a breaking change; after the 21.x release it would be.
  Fix: Mark resizeSplitterSizesAt and KbqSplitterSnapPoint @internal (an inline type for the snapSplitterSize parameter works too) and run approve-api for components/splitter before the first release.
- **[table] The compact padding matches a kbq-button anywhere in a cell, so wrapped text next to a button loses its full block padding** — confirmed in the source.
  table.scss:34-36 uses `& > :is(tbody, tfoot) > tr > :is(td, th):has(.kbq-button, .kbq-button-icon) { padding-block: var(--kbq-size-xxs) }`. `:has()` with no combinator matches any descendant, and button.component.ts:130-131 binds `kbq-button`/`kbq-button-icon` on every button. So a cell with several lines of text plus a button, or a button nested deeper (in a wrapper or a dropdown trigger), gets 4px block padding instead of the 10px row padding (table-tokens.scss:3) around all its content. The comment at table.scss:31-32 states the intent: keep a row with only a button at the button's height. The only button-cell case the e2e covers is a cell holding a single button (e2e.ts, 'button in a cell'). This is a visual spacing inconsistency under specific content, and arguably a design choice, so it is P3.
  Fix: Limit the compact padding to cells whose only content is the button, for example `:has(> .kbq-button:only-child)`, or offer an opt-in cell class.
- **[tabs] Unbinding tabNavPanel at runtime leaves role="tablist" on the nav bar and role="tab", aria-selected and aria-controls on the links** — confirmed in the source.
  tab-nav-bar.ts:127-129: without a panel, KbqTabNavBar.role returns elementRef.getAttribute('role'), which is the 'tablist' that the host binding at line 67 wrote in panel mode, so the value never changes and the attribute stays. aria-orientation (lines 135-137) follows that role. KbqTabLink reads role (line 325), aria-selected (line 319) and aria-controls (line 332) back from its own element in the same way, so the values written in panel mode (host bindings at lines 236-238) freeze. Meanwhile ariaCurrent (line 337) starts emitting 'page', so a link ends up with role=tab, aria-selected and aria-current together. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because the rule limits repros to P0-P2. Rated P3: rare runtime toggle, and the effect is a11y attributes only.
  Fix: Track author-provided attribute values separately (read once, or via inputs) instead of reading back attributes the host bindings themselves write.
- **[tabs] Holding a pagination arrow and releasing the button anywhere but on the arrow, while the pointer stays over the strip, keeps the strip scrolling to its end** — confirmed in the source.
  tab-header.html and tab-nav-bar.html bind three handlers on each paginator: (mousedown) handlePaginatorPress, (click) handlePaginatorClick (which calls stopInterval, paginated-tab-header.ts:670-673) and (touchend) stopInterval. The only other stop is the host mouseleave listener (lines 329-331). There is no mouseup or pointerup listener. When the button is released off the arrow, the browser sends the click to the common ancestor, not the arrow, so nothing stops the timer(650, 100) started at lines 732-750. It ends only when the far bound is reached (lines 743-746) or the pointer leaves the host. The same code exists in Angular Material. Not reproduced: rated P3 (unexpected scroll after a rare gesture, recoverable by leaving the strip), and the click retargeting is a browser behaviour.
  Fix: Stop the interval on a document-level mouseup/pointerup (attached for the press only), not just on click/touchend/mouseleave.
- **[tags] A tag input whose kbqTagInputFor is unbound or undefined throws a TypeError on keydown in its empty input, on focus, on blur and on a distinct check** — confirmed in the source.
  tag-input.ts:184 types the tagList input as `KbqTagList | undefined`, and :240-249 sets `\_tagList` only for a truthy binding. Several calls use it without a guard: :266 `this.\_tagList.keydown(event)` (any key in an empty input), :365 `this.\_tagList.unselectAll()` (focus), :300 `this.\_tagList.focused()` (blur), and :418 `this.\_tagList.tags` in tagValues() (distinct check in emitTagEnd/onPaste, and hasDuplicates). Only addTag (:406 `?.`) and the disabled getter (:205) guard it. The selector `input\[kbqTagInputFor\]` (:111) accepts a valueless attribute, and the JSDoc (:106-109) says the input "may be placed inside or outside" a list, which invites `\<input kbqTagInputFor>` inside a list. I rated it P3 because every usage in the repo binds the input, and the misuse shows up at once in development. jsdom could show it, but a P3 does not qualify for the repro budget.
  Fix: Fall back to an injected parent KbqTagList and guard the calls, or throw a descriptive error when no list is registered.
- **[tags] Examples use \<i kbq-icon kbqTagRemove>; KbqIcon's default aria-hidden="true" hides the role="button" remove control and its name** — confirmed in the source.
  icon.component.ts:44 binds `\[attr.aria-hidden\]` to resolvedAriaHidden(), which is 'true' when unbound (:71-78, defaultAriaHidden = true). KbqTagRemove (tag.component.ts:857-871) sets role="button", aria-label and tabindex but never aria-hidden. On the same element, the button and its name are therefore removed from the accessibility tree. With the default tabindex -1 (:891), axe's aria-hidden-focus rule passes (focusable but not tabbable). In docs-examples/components/tags, 16 remove controls use kbq-icon and 16 use kbq-icon-button; the latter sets defaultAriaHidden = false (icon-button.component.ts:61) and is fine. Keyboard removal still works through Delete on the tag.
  Fix: Have KbqTagRemove bind aria-hidden="false" (or null), or switch the examples to kbq-icon-button.
- **[tags] distinct compares the typed text with the tags' value, so with object values it never detects a duplicate** — confirmed in the source.
  tag-input.ts:417-419 builds a Set from `this.\_tagList.tags.map(({ value }) => value)`. That Set is checked against the trimmed input string at :325 (hasDuplicates) and :398 (addTag). With `\[value\]` bound to objects, as tag-input-overview binds `{ value: 'Tag 0' }`, Set.has(string) is never true, so duplicates are requested. No example combines distinct with object values: tag-autocomplete-onpaste-off and tag-autocomplete-option-operations bind strings. It only bites consumers who combine the two, and the spec text literally says "equals the value". jsdom could show it, but a P3 does not qualify for the repro budget.
  Fix: Compare against the tags' text (or accept a comparator / value-to-text function) when values are not strings.
- **[textarea] KbqFormField.focus(options) drops preventScroll/focusVisible for a textarea** — confirmed in the source.
  form-field.ts:396-397 calls `this.control().focus(options)`, and the control interface at form-field-control.ts:78 declares `focus(options?: FocusOptions)`. KbqTextarea.focus() at textarea.component.ts:358-360 takes no parameter and calls `nativeElement.focus()` without options, so the options are lost. KbqInput.focus() at input.ts:293 has the same pattern. No internal caller passes options (core/select/common.ts:113, dropdown/dropdown-search.ts:62, form-field/cleaner.ts:163), so only consumers calling formField.focus({...}) are affected. jsdom could show this; it was not reproduced because it is P3.
  Fix: Accept and forward the options: `focus(options?: FocusOptions) { this.elementRef.nativeElement.focus(options); }` (same for KbqInput).
- **[textarea] line-height: normal makes the measurement NaN, so the textarea stops growing and maxRows never triggers** — confirmed in the source.
  textarea.component.ts:287 runs `parseInt('normal', 10)`, which is NaN. That NaN goes into lineHeight and minHeight at :289-290. At :343 `Math.max(NaN, …)` gives NaN. At :347 rowsCount is pinned at 0 because `NaN > 0` is false, so maxRowLimitReached never turns true. At :351-353 coerceCssPixelValue(NaN) gives 'NaNpx', which the CSSOM rejects, so min-height stays frozen. With the field not resizable, textarea.scss:24-27 sets `overflow-y: hidden`, so the extra lines are clipped without a scrollbar. The existing spec helper (textarea.component.spec.ts:53-56) documents that jsdom is in this state. In the usual setup, \_koobiq-theme.scss:38 applies kbq-textarea-typography globally, which gives a px line-height. So this only happens without Koobiq typography or with an explicit `line-height: normal`. Not reproduced because it is P3.
  Fix: Fall back to a measured pixel line height (e.g. a probe or font-size based estimate) when parseInt yields NaN, and guard the Math.max against NaN.
- **[textarea] The textarea stylesheet is carried by kbq-form-field, so a kbqTextarea outside a form field is unstyled** — confirmed in the source.
  The directive (textarea.component.ts:42-60) cannot carry styles. textarea.scss ships only through kbq-form-field's styleUrls (form-field.ts:92-107, '../textarea/textarea.scss', with a comment saying the form field is the only component it is always rendered inside of). Without a rendered form field there are no `.kbq-textarea` rules, such as `resize: none` (textarea.scss:12) and the overflow rules. The native textarea then keeps its UA `resize` handle and border while it grows; this is CSS that jsdom cannot show. Angular also removes component styles once the last form field is destroyed, so the same probably applies after all form fields go away (not checked). Every example and internal use wraps it in kbq-form-field (for example filter-bar pipe-text.html:28-29), so this is an undocumented setup.
  Fix: Either ship the textarea rules globally (theme or a standalone stylesheet) or document that kbqTextarea requires kbq-form-field.
- **[textarea] Each measurement inserts a shallow clone with the same id next to the textarea** — confirmed in the source.
  textarea.component.ts:333 `cloneNode(false)` copies every attribute, including the id bound at :49, plus name, required and aria-\*. :335 appends the clone to the same parent, :340 writes its style, :345 removes it and :351 writes the textarea's min-height. All of this is synchronous, so no other script sees the duplicate id. A MutationObserver on an ancestor still receives the childList add/remove records (plus attribute records) on every grow, which is once per keystroke as the existing 'should grow once per input change' test shows. For example, KbqScrollbar's content observer (scrollbar.ts:552) re-measures per keystroke when the field sits in a kbq-scrollbar. jsdom could show the records; not reproduced because it is P3.
  Fix: Remove id/name (and other identifying attributes) from the clone, or measure with a persistent detached probe instead of inserting a clone per keystroke.
- **[time-range] aria-expanded / aria-haspopup="dialog" / aria-controls sit on the wrapper instead of the focusable control, and the popover has neither role="dialog" nor a name** — confirmed in the source.
  popover.component.ts:284-286 binds aria-expanded, aria-haspopup="dialog" and aria-controls on the kbqPopover host, which is the \<kbq-time-range-title> wrapper (time-range.ts:73-89). The focusable \<a kbqLink pseudo> (role=button via link.ts:132) gets none of them. time-range.ts:73-89 passes no kbqPopoverHeader and no kbqPopoverAriaLabel, so popover.component.html renders \[attr.role\]="labelledByHeader || ariaLabel ? 'dialog' : null" as null and leaves the panel unnamed, although aria-haspopup promises a dialog. The focus repro also asserts aria-haspopup="dialog" on the wrapper, which has no tabindex. The impact on assistive technology is clear from the markup.
  Fix: Bind the expanded/haspopup/controls state on the focusable trigger element and give the popover a localized kbqPopoverAriaLabel so the panel keeps role=dialog.
- **[time-range] kbq-time-range-title-as-control is focusable and named via aria-labelledby but has no role, and exposes no aria-invalid / aria-required** — confirmed in the source.
  time-range-title-as-form-field.ts:32-39: the host binds tabindex 0, id and aria-labelledby (the form-field label id, line 59) but no role. Under ARIA 1.2 a generic element must not be named. The class computes errorState and required (lines 63-86), but no aria-invalid or aria-required reaches the DOM, and there is no aria-expanded or aria-haspopup either. The only axe test (time-range.spec.ts:1371-1389) runs on the editor element, not the trigger.
  Fix: Give the host a role (button or combobox) and bind aria-invalid, aria-required and aria-expanded from the existing signals; add an axe test on the trigger.
- **[time-range] Both fields of one range end are named only by the end's prefix ("с"/"по"), so time and date fields are indistinguishable to a screen reader** — confirmed in the source.
  time-range-editor.html:53-60 and 64-70 give both the time and the date input \[attr.aria-labelledby\]="fromLabelId"; lines 88-105 do the same with toLabelId. aria-labelledby overrides every other naming source, so both fields of an end are named "с" (or "по"). The placeholders from timepicker.directive.ts:142 and datepicker-input.directive.ts:313 are at most a description, and the fields are prefilled anyway. The existing test time-range.spec.ts:1336-1358 asserts exactly this shared naming.
  Fix: Reference a second, localized id per field (e.g. aria-labelledby="fromLabelId fromTimeLabelId") so each name says time or date.
- **[time-range] A type unknown to both the built-in set and KBQ\_CUSTOM\_TIME\_RANGE\_TYPES fails with a bare TypeError instead of a named error** — confirmed in the source.
  time-range.service.ts:109-115 indexes timeRangeConfig\[type\].units and .translationType with no guard. Callers: calculateTimeRange (service.ts:177, 195), checkAndCorrectTimeRangeValue (service.ts:227-230), the title context and label (time-range-title.ts:58, 86), the editor's timeRangeTypesWithoutRange (time-range-editor.ts:142-143), and the constructor default (time-range.ts:203-206). An unknown type such as \['lastHuor'\] or a cast custom type that was not provided therefore throws "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'translationType'/'units')". No validation exists anywhere on the path.
  Fix: Look the type up through one helper that throws a named, descriptive error (like createMissingDateImplError) for an unregistered type.
- **[time-range] ru-RU and en-US durationTemplate.title.YEARS singular reads "# год"/"# year" instead of "последний год"/"Last year"** — confirmed in the source.
  core/locales/ru-RU.ts:184-188 has YEARS one {# год}, while every other title unit reads 'последний/последняя …' and option.YEARS one is 'последний год'. en-US.ts:184-187 has one {# year} against 'Last month', 'Last hour' and option 'Last year'. es-LA.ts:186-187 ('# año') and pt-BR.ts:186-187 ('# ano') have the same pattern; tk-TM is correct ('soňky # ýyl'). The trigger uses durationTemplate.title (time-range-title.ts:118), so a custom {units:{years:-1}, translationType:'years'} preset reads '1 год' / '1 year'. No built-in preset uses years: last12Months is in months.
  Fix: Change the singular YEARS title strings in ru-RU, en-US, es-LA and pt-BR to the 'last year' wording used by the option templates.
- **[timepicker] Arrow keys act on the last parsed value, not on the text: Up over typed '12:3' shows 12:19** — reproduced.
  Incomplete text such as '12:3' does not parse (getDateFromTimeString :926-977 returns null), so onInput :516-522 leaves value() at the old time while the view keeps '12:3'. verticalArrowKeyHandler :720-744 reads only this.value(), increments the part under the caret via getTimeEditMetrics on the current text, then setValue re-renders. Repro: value 12:18, type '12:3', caret at 4, press Up: the input becomes '12:19' and the control 12:19, and the typed '3' is lost. Rated P3 because the result is visible and easy to correct.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/timepicker/arrow-stale-value.spec.ts`](defect-repros/timepicker/arrow-stale-value.spec.ts)
  Fix: Parse the current text (formatUserInput + getDateFromTimeString) before stepping, and fall back to value() only when the text is complete or empty.
- **[timepicker] Gaining focus marks the control touched, so a required empty timepicker shows its error as soon as it is focused** — reproduced.
  host '(focus)': 'focusChanged(true)' at :148. focusChanged :455-460 calls onTouched() on any focus change, gaining focus included, and the forms blur pipeline then calls markAsTouched. ngDoCheck :423-429 → updateErrorState. The default ErrorStateMatcher (core/error/error-state-matcher.ts:60-61) is invalid && touched. Repro with a required null FormControl in a FormGroup: a focus event alone, without input, gives touched=true, errorState() true and kbq-form-field\_invalid on the form field. KbqDatepickerInput does the same (datepicker-input.directive.ts:721-725), while KbqInput does not touch on focus (input.ts:302-304). inline-edit.spec.ts:2470 relies on focus touching the control. Rated P3 because the behaviour is shared with the datepicker and looks deliberate, but it shows the error early in normal use.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/timepicker/focus-touches-error.spec.ts`](defect-repros/timepicker/focus-touches-error.spec.ts)
  Fix: Call onTouched only on blur (focusChanged(false)), together with the datepicker, and update the inline-edit spec that relies on it.
- **[timepicker] A time zone change while the field has no value renders empty text over what the user has half typed** — reproduced.
  The timezone subscription :416-420 calls `this.setValue(this.valueState() ?? null)` without a guard. setValue(null) → updateView :1025-1029 writes '' and also resets lastValueValid, which clears a pending parse error. The locale/format effect :354-366 guards `if (value)` for exactly this case. Repro: type '1' into an empty field; a format change keeps '1', but KbqDateTimezoneService.setTimezone('UTC') leaves the input ''. Rated P3: the zone has to change while unparsed text sits in an empty field, which is rare.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/timepicker/timezone-wipes-typed.spec.ts`](defect-repros/timepicker/timezone-wipes-typed.spec.ts)
  Fix: Apply the same `if (value)` guard as the locale/format effect in the timezone subscription.
- **[timezone] `multiline` turned on after the first change detection is not rejected; the select then behaves as multi-selection over a single selection model** — confirmed in the source.
  timezone-select.component.ts:152-158 checks `multiple || multiline()` only in ngOnInit. The guard that runs after init, the effect at select.component.ts:1225-1232, watches only `multipleInput`, so nothing re-checks `multiline`. SelectionModel is built once at :1294 with multiSelection=false. Afterwards `multiSelection` (:1118-1120) reads multiline() live, and every claimed effect follows from that. Arrows on a closed select open it (:2129). A user click does not close the panel (:2473). propagateChanges emits `selected` as an array (:1042, :2553-2554). Options become `multiple` and toggle (core/option/option.ts:239-241, :408). In addition, a string value written afterwards (writeValue or \[value\]) throws getKbqSelectNonArrayValueError (:2313-2316). This is jsdom-visible, but the trigger is an unsupported configuration toggled at runtime (a guard that only partly rejects it), so it is rated P3 and was not reproduced under the P0–P2-only repro policy.
  Fix: Also throw getKbqTimezoneSelectMultipleError from an effect (or the existing multiple guard) when multiline() changes after init.
- **[timezone] A kbq-timezone-option without \[timezone\] throws while rendering; the value getter falls back to \[value\] as if a missing zone were supported** — confirmed in the source.
  timezone-option.component.ts:56 declares `timezone` as a non-required input with `undefined!`. The template reads `resolvedOffset` at timezone-option.component.html:1, which calls resolveZoneOffset(undefined), and `zone.offset` at timezone.utils.ts:38 throws a TypeError. It also reads `timezone.city` at html:18 and `timezone.cities` at html:24. viewValue (:74-76) and getLabel (:92-94) dereference `this.timezone` unguarded as well. Meanwhile the `value` getter (:64-66, `this.timezone?.id ?? super.value`) and its JSDoc ('unless no time zone is bound') imply that a missing zone is supported. A missing \[timezone\] is consumer misuse, but it gives a cryptic TypeError instead of a required-input error. Rated P3 and not reproduced (P0–P2-only repro policy), although jsdom would show it.
  Fix: Make the input `input.required` (or guard every read) and drop the misleading `?.`/super.value fallback, or document and support the zone-less case.
- **[timezone] Offsets west of UTC are rendered with U+2212; a query typed with a hyphen-minus (-03) marks nothing, although the search example normalizes the dash to find the zone** — confirmed in the source.
  offset() writes U+2212 for a non-digit first character (timezone.utils.ts:142-144), so '-03:00:00' renders as '−03:00' in the offset column (timezone-option.component.html:1-11). The highlight is either literal (highlight-base.ts:32-43, `/-03/gi`) or folded (search-base.ts:12-18 NFKD; U+2212 has no decomposition to '-'), so in both modes '-03' never matches '−03'. filterCitiesBySearchString drops '-03' as an offset pattern (timezone.utils.ts:218, 228), and no city contains it, so nothing in the option is marked. The example still finds the zone because it compares against normalizeOffsetDash(offsetFormatter(...)) (timezone-search-overview-example.ts:24-26, 120). Only the highlight is missing; the result list is right, so this is P3 and was not reproduced (P0–P2-only repro policy).
  Fix: Normalize the dash family (U+2212, em dash and the hyphen) on both sides before matching in the offset highlight, or highlight the offset columns with a dash-insensitive matcher.
- **[timezone] Only the \<kbq-timezone-select-trigger> element is a trigger slot; a kbq-select-trigger matches no slot and is projected into the default slot inside the option list** — confirmed in the source.
  timezone-select.component.html:26 has `\<ng-content select="kbq-timezone-select-trigger" />` (element only, and the directive selector at timezone-select.component.ts:29-32 is element-only too). kbq-select's slot is 'kbq-select-trigger, \[kbq-select-trigger\]' (select/select.html:21, core/select/common.ts:38). KbqTimezoneSelect overrides customTrigger with contentChild(KbqTimezoneSelectTrigger) (:106), so a kbq-select-trigger is not recognized. It falls through every named slot into the default `\<ng-content />` at html:97, which sits inside the role="listbox" container (html:86-98) of the overlay panel. The trigger keeps showing triggerValue, and the stray content appears as a non-option child of the listbox while the panel is open. The timezone docs only document kbq-timezone-select-trigger, so this is an API inconsistency rather than broken documented usage. Not reproduced: P3.
  Fix: Also accept `kbq-select-trigger, \[kbq-select-trigger\]` (and the attribute form of the timezone trigger) in the trigger slot and in the customTrigger query, or document the difference explicitly.
- **[timezone] An explicit offset written with a leading '+' is labelled as negative but sorted as positive** — confirmed in the source.
  Label: offset('+03:00:00') splits hours '+03', `/^\d$/.test('+')` is false (timezone.utils.ts:142), so U+2212 is prepended to '03' giving '−03:00' (:143-144). Sort: sortZonesByOffset/timezonesSortComparator resolve through resolveZoneOffsetMinutes, i.e. kbqResolveTimezoneOffset(zone.offset) (timezone.utils.ts:25-27, 188-211). Its FIXED\_OFFSET\_PATTERN accepts `\[+-\]` (core/datetime/timezone.ts:40), and sign '+' yields +180 (:57-61), so the zone sorts among UTC+3 zones while being labelled UTC−03:00. KbqTimezoneZone.offset documents only an optional leading '-' (timezone.models.ts:6), so '+' is outside the documented format. That makes this a P3 inconsistency, not reproduced (pure functions; the outcome is deterministic from the code).
  Fix: Treat a leading '+' as positive in offset() (check for '-' rather than for a digit), or reject/normalize '+' consistently in both the label and the sort.
- **[title] Resize and mutation observers are bound once, in ngAfterViewInit, to the container resolved then** — confirmed in the source.
  The resize and content observers are bound once to this.parent (title.directive.ts:217-224), while the parent getter (:131-137) re-resolves on every call, so hover and focus measure the live container. Partly refuted: when #kbqTitleContainer appears later inside @if, the host is the observed element. ContentObserver observes it with subtree: true (node\_modules/@angular/cdk/fesm2022/observers.mjs:86-90), so mutations inside the new container ARE followed. What is lost: a resize of the container that leaves the host size unchanged. When a container is destroyed and re-created (@if toggled off and on), both observers stay on the detached old element and neither resizes nor mutations are followed. Impact is limited to updating an already open tooltip or verdict without a new hover. No in-repo consumer renders the container conditionally (button-toggle.component.ts:542, tree-option.html:15).
  Fix: Re-bind the observers when the parentContainer() query changes, e.g. in an effect keyed on the resolved parent.
- **[title] The temporary measuring element is a content mutation the directive observes itself, triggering an extra overflow evaluation** — confirmed in the source.
  For a plain-text host (hasOnlyText, :141-145) whose offsetWidth equals scrollWidth (:285-287), which covers fitting text unless the host has a border, isTextWiderThanContainer appends a span to this.parent and removes it (:318-333). this.parent is the element the ContentObserver watches with childList + subtree (:222-224). CDK ignores only comment-node records (observers.mjs:7-24), so the add/remove batch is emitted. The throttled handler (:225-228) then re-runs isOverflown, which probes and mutates again (that mutation is dropped by the throttle), and resets content (updateData). The result is one extra evaluation with forced layout reads per hover and per resize evaluation. The self-mutation also opens a 100 ms throttle window that swallows a genuine content change right after a hover, which compounds the missing trailing emission. jsdom could show it; not reproduced because of the low severity.
  Fix: Measure without mutating the observed subtree (e.g. a Range over the text node, or a probe outside the observed container), or ignore the directive's own probe records.
- **[toast] Style outside KbqToastStyle with default icon: true renders an empty icon container that still takes width and margin** — confirmed in the source.
  toast.component.html:1-15 renders div.kbq-toast\_\_icon-container whenever icon is truthy, and icon defaults to true (toast.component.ts:116). Its inner '@else if (defaultIcon)' renders nothing for a style outside defaultIcons (toast.component.ts:40-45, 117). toast.component.scss:49-70 gives the container a fixed width/max-width/height, margin-right and flex-shrink: 0, with no :empty rule. The result is a blank gutter. The space is layout, which jsdom cannot show (it only shows the empty div).
  Fix: Render the icon container only when iconTemplate or defaultIcon exists, or treat icon as false for unknown styles.
- **[toast] Focus hand-off goes to the first toast in stack order, not the toast next to the closed one** — confirmed in the source.
  For a keyboard or program origin, removeToast calls restoreFocus (toast.service.ts:297-298, 372-381). getNextFocusTarget (383-400) sorts the surviving hosts in DOM order and returns the close button of the first one. Without onTop, a toast is appended last (toast-container.component.ts:296). So closing the bottom toast sends focus to the top one, past every toast in between: the jump the comment at 384-386 says the stack order avoids. The existing 'adjacent' test (toast.spec.ts:490-508) closes the top toast with onTop set, where the first toast is also the adjacent one. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because it is P3 a11y polish.
  Fix: Choose the next sibling toast of the closed host in DOM order (or the previous one when it was last).
- **[toast] Focus moved into a toast by a mouse press pauses the whole stack until blur; pointer leaving does not end it** — confirmed in the source.
  toast.component.ts:211-218 forwards every FocusMonitor origin, 'mouse' included, to stack.setFocused, and toast.service.ts:342-354 records it. isPaused (184-186) stays true while focusedToasts is non-empty, and mouseleave (toast.component.ts:75, 193-196) clears only hoveredToasts. So after a click on an action that does not close the toast, the stack stays paused until focus leaves the toast. The service calls this exact outcome harmful at toast.service.ts:294-296, and the timer JSDoc (156-158) names only pointer and keyboard focus. The docs page (toast.en.mdx:41) does say focus pauses, without naming an origin, so whether this is a defect is partly a design call. jsdom could show it; not reproduced because it is P3.
  Fix: Do not count a mouse-origin focus toward the pause, or end it on mouseleave.
- **[toast] toast-hide-overview example changes a plain progress field from an rxjs timer in an OnPush component; stale in the zoneless docs app** — confirmed in the source.
  toast-hide-overview-example.ts:48-53 sets the plain field progress = false inside an rxjs timer callback. The example is OnPush (:21), the toast is OnPush (toast.component.ts:60), and the docs app is zoneless (apps/docs/src/app/config.ts:32). Nothing schedules change detection or marks the toast view dirty, so the content template (toast-hide-overview-example.html:17-26, rendered in the toast view through ngTemplateOutlet) keeps showing the progress bar. When close() runs 550 ms later, the only notification is the leavingHeight signal (toast.component.ts:95, 166). It is read only in host bindings (71-72), which run in the host LView, and Angular's detectChangesInView skips a non-dirty OnPush child view. So the 'import complete' state probably never renders unless the user hovers, focuses or clicks the toast: the host listeners and the read-state markForCheck (read-state.ts:82) mark the view dirty. That is stronger than the finding's 'renders only when the toast starts to leave'. Not reproduced, because it is a docs example (P3); the exact timing was not checked in the docs app.
  Fix: Make progress a signal (or call markForCheck after setting it).
- **[toggle] `big` has no boolean transform, so a bare `big` attribute is an empty string and strict templates reject it** — confirmed in the source.
  toggle.component.ts:75 declares `readonly big = input\<boolean>(false);` with no transform. The other boolean inputs use booleanAttribute: disabled 175-178, checked 187-190, indeterminate 193-196, loading 151. KbqCheckbox declares `big = input(false, { transform: booleanAttribute })` at checkbox.ts:73. A bare `\<kbq-toggle big>` passes '' to the input. The host binding '\[class.kbq-toggle\_big\]': 'big()' (toggle.component.ts:60) is then falsy, so the class is never added. Under strictTemplates (strictAttributeTypes) the static '' is not assignable to boolean, so it is a compile error, not a silent bug. Without strict templates it does nothing silently. Every in-repo usage binds \[big\]="true" or \[big\]="big()" (components-dev/toggle/template.html, toggle/e2e.ts), so none is affected. Not reproduced: it looks P3, an inconsistency with checkbox.
  Fix: Use `input(false, { transform: booleanAttribute })` for `big`, matching KbqCheckbox, and approve the API golden.
- **[toggle] With the `check` click action, a click on an indeterminate toggle clears the native checkbox's `indeterminate` property while the component stays indeterminate** — confirmed in the source.
  With clickAction 'check', KbqCheckable.resolveClick returns shouldToggle true and shouldClearIndeterminate false (checkable.ts:109-118). onInputClick (toggle.component.ts:293-314) toggles checked and does not touch indeterminate. Only the noop branch calls resetNativeInput (toggle.component.ts:315-319, checkable.ts:121-124). The native click's legacy pre-activation behavior (HTML spec) has already set input.indeterminate = false. The \[indeterminate\]="indeterminate" binding (toggle.component.html:18) keeps its value true, so Angular does not write the property again. The component, its kbq-indeterminate host class and aria-checked then disagree with the native property until indeterminate changes. aria-checked is always written (toggle.component.html:7), and while indeterminate the role falls back to the native checkbox (line 13). AT that honours aria-checked is unaffected. A browser that prefers the native checkbox state (Firefox ignores aria-checked on input type=checkbox) could announce checked/unchecked instead of mixed after the second click, so the real AT impact needs a browser to settle. KbqCheckbox has the identical code path (checkbox.ts:274-298). Not reproduced: the finding itself rates the impact low (P3).
  Fix: In the shouldToggle branch, when indeterminate is kept, re-sync the native input, for example with checkable.resetNativeInput(inputElement), after toggling.
- **[toggle] No unit test runs axe on the toggle (toHaveNoViolations is not used in its spec)** — confirmed in the source.
  No grep match for toHaveNoViolations or axe in packages/components/toggle/ (toggle.component.spec.ts, e2e.playwright-spec.ts). About 40 other component specs use the matcher (button, checkbox-like radio, button-toggle, select, ...). This is a test-coverage gap, not a runtime defect. The causal claim is only partly right. The repro above shows axe in jsdom reports the host aria-label of defect 1 as 'incomplete', not as a violation, so adding toHaveNoViolations() would not catch that part. The unnamed toggle-indeterminate and toggle-loading docs examples are not mounted by any unit spec, so an axe check in toggle.component.spec.ts would not cover them either.
  Fix: Add awaited toHaveNoViolations() checks to the toggle spec for labelled, aria-label-only, indeterminate, loading and hint states.
- **[tooltip] Escape on the trigger element of a pinned (manual) tooltip, or bubbling from inside it, closes the tooltip through the trigger's own keydown handler and marks the key as handled** — reproduced.
  tooltip.component.ts:271 binds host (keydown) to KbqPopUpTrigger.keydownHandler (pop-up-trigger.ts:372-380), which checks only ESCAPE / defaultPrevented / isOpen / canHide() and then calls preventDefault() + hide(). It has no hasInteractiveTrigger check, unlike the document-level capture listener (tooltip.component.ts:1206-1210), which deliberately returns for manual/none tooltips. Validation hints sit on wrappers: the datepicker/timepicker examples put #tooltip="kbqTooltip" on kbq-form-field and the directives set trigger='manual' (datepicker-input.directive.ts:513, timepicker.directive.ts:388). inline-edit.html:71-80 puts it on the control container, so onPanelEscape (inline-edit.ts:845-852) sees defaultPrevented and the edit is cancelled only by a second Escape. The repro passes. Escape on body leaves the pinned tooltip open and not defaultPrevented. Escape in the input inside the host closes it with defaultPrevented=true. Inside a KbqModal, the first Escape closes only the hint (beforeClose not called) and the second closes the modal. Rated P3 because only the order of dismissal is inconsistent with the 'pinned survives an unrelated Escape' intent, and the next Escape still works.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tooltip/escape-pinned.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tooltip/escape-pinned.spec.ts)
  Fix: Make the host keydown path skip tooltips without an interactive trigger, as the document listener does, or document that Escape in the host dismisses a pinned hint first.
- **[tooltip] With kbqTooltipOffset set, the margin stays on the old side when a fallback flips the placement** — confirmed in the source.
  applyOffset (tooltip.component.ts:184-189) goes through applyPopupMargins (pop-up-styles.ts:3-37). That function writes only the margin that matches the placement class present at that moment and never clears the other three sides. It runs from KbqTooltipComponent.show (tooltip.component.ts:172) and updateData (1118). Both run before CDK's first position apply, which OverlayRef schedules after the next render. When the strategy then picks a fallback, onPositionChange (pop-up-trigger.ts:617-650) changes the placement class through updateClassMap + detectChanges but never re-applies the offset. After a flip from top to bottom, the inline margin-bottom:Npx stays and the stylesheet margin-top (8px, or 4px arrowless, from popup-margins in tooltip.scss) applies. jsdom gives the overlay a 0x0 rect, so CDK never flips there. This needs a real layout.
  Fix: Re-apply the offset after a placement change (e.g. in the tooltip's updateClassMap/onPositionChange) and have applyPopupMargins clear the margins of the other sides.
- **[tooltip] Changing kbqTooltipColor while open does not recolor it; clearing kbqTooltipClass while open does not remove the class** — reproduced.
  The color setter (tooltip.component.ts:505-507) only stores the value. The customClass setter (477-485) calls updateClassMap() only for a truthy value. ngOnChanges refreshes the class map of an open tooltip only for modifierInput (657-661), and colorInput/customClassInput (622-628) reach no refresh. The class map is rebuilt only on the next onPositionChange or on reopen. The repro passes: after color='error' the open tooltip still has kbq-contrast and no kbq-error, and after reopening it gets kbq-error. After customClass is cleared, the open tooltip keeps 'custom-hint'. Rated P3: the stale state lasts until the next reposition or reopen, and changing these inputs while the tooltip is open is uncommon.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tooltip/stale-color-class.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tooltip/stale-color-class.spec.ts)
  Fix: Call updateClassMap() from both setters unconditionally (it is a no-op without an instance).
- **[tooltip] showForElement() writes aria-describedby on the directive's own host, not on the element the tooltip is anchored to** — confirmed in the source.
  showForElement (tooltip.component.ts:1083-1089) calls show() and strategy.setOrigin(element) but never setExternalNativeElement. describeTrigger (1249-1262), which runs on visibleChange(true) (844-846), describes getNativeElement(), that is externalNativeElement || the host (pop-up-trigger.ts:735-737).
