The anchored element therefore gets no description and the host gets it. showForMouseEvent, by contrast, calls undescribeTrigger + setExternalNativeElement (1053-1056). The library itself never calls showForElement. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because it is P3 a11y polish.
  Fix: Re-anchor the description the way showForMouseEvent does (undescribe, then set the external element or record the anchored element for describeTrigger).
- **[tooltip] Pane classes (ignoreTooltipPointerEvents, click-through for pinned kbqTrigger) and the scroll strategy are read only when the overlay is first created** — confirmed in the source.
  KbqTooltipTrigger.createOverlay returns the cached overlayRef (tooltip.component.ts:1155-1157) before panel classes are recomputed (1161, getPanelClasses 1227-1240, which reads ignoreTooltipPointerEvents() and hasInteractiveTrigger). The base createOverlay reads this.scrollStrategy() once (pop-up-trigger.ts:565-570). detach() (pop-up-trigger.ts:521-544) only detaches, and the overlay is disposed and nulled only in ngOnDestroy (313-316). So changing ignoreTooltipPointerEvents, kbqTrigger between manual and interactive, setOverlayPanelClass or kbqTooltipScrollStrategy after the first show has no effect. The existing ignoreTooltipPointerEvents tests check only the first show. In the library, navbar-item calls setOverlayPanelClass from its constructor and inline-edit binds a static strategy, so neither is affected. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because it is P3.
  Fix: Sync the pane classes on every attach (overlayRef.addPanelClass/removePanelClass) and call updateScrollStrategy (or recreate the overlay) when the strategy input changes.
- **[tooltip] A kbqPlacementPriority made only of unknown names leaves the position strategy without positions; CDK throws on open** — reproduced.
  updatePlacementPriority (pop-up-trigger.ts:346-352) accepts any non-empty value. getPriorityPlacementStrategy (777-792) silently drops unknown names and returns \[\]. updatePosition (706-711) passes that to withPositions, which in dev mode throws 'At least one position is required' (CDK \_validatePositions). In production, apply() would dereference an undefined fallback instead. The throw happens inside attachAndShow after this.instance is assigned (pop-up-trigger.ts:420, 432 -> tooltip updateData 1119), so the overlay stays attached but never shown, and every later show() returns early on the existing instance (403-413). The repro passes: show(0) throws /At least one position is required/, isAttached is true and isOpen is false. Rated P3: this is a misconfiguration that throws on every open in development, unlike kbqPlacement, which warns and falls back.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tooltip/unknown-priority.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tooltip/unknown-priority.spec.ts)
  Fix: Fall back to POSITION\_PRIORITY\_STRATEGY\[placement\] (with a console warning, as updatePlacement does) when the filtered list is empty.
- **[tooltip] kbqTooltipColor accepts every KbqComponentColors value, but success, theme-fade and empty render an unstyled tooltip** — confirmed in the source.
  colorInput is typed KbqComponentColors | string (tooltip.component.ts:774), and colorClass is `kbq-${color}` (510-512). \_tooltip-theme.scss styles only .kbq-tooltip.kbq-theme, .kbq-contrast, .kbq-contrast-fade, .kbq-warning and .kbq-error. tooltip-tokens.scss defines no success or theme-fade tokens, and no library stylesheet targets .kbq-success, .kbq-theme-fade or .kbq-empty on a tooltip. .kbq-tooltip\_\_inner has no background outside the color mixin (tooltip.scss), so these values give a transparent tooltip with inherited text color and no shadow. In-repo callers use only contrast, contrast-fade, theme, warning and error. This is CSS rendering, which jsdom cannot show.
  Fix: Either add styles and tokens for the remaining colors or narrow the input type and docs to the styled ones.
- **[top-bar] aria-label rendered on a bar without a role (prohibited on a generic element, nothing warns, axe not run on that case)** — confirmed in the source.
  top-bar.ts:66 binds `\[attr.aria-label\]: ariaLabel() || null` whatever the role is, and the host has no default role (JSDoc top-bar.ts:44-47), so `\<kbq-top-bar aria-label="x">` names a generic custom element. ARIA 1.2 prohibits that and AT ignores the name. No dev-mode warning exists. The only axe test (top-bar.spec.ts:113-121) uses PopulatedTestApp, which has role="banner" (spec:196). Running axe would not catch it either: in axe-core 4.12.1, ariaProhibitedAttrEvaluate (axe.js:27478-27511) returns `undefined` (incomplete, not a violation) when the element has text content, so a populated role-less bar passes `toHaveNoViolations`. The docs always pair the label with role="banner" (top-bar.en.mdx:107, ru.mdx:110), and the JSDoc asks for a landmark role (top-bar.ts:78-80). It only happens on misuse, so this is a11y polish. No repro: P3.
  Fix: Warn in dev mode, or drop the label, when aria-label is set and the host has no role attribute; optionally add an axe/attribute test for the role-less case.
- **[tree] "Select all" emits selectionChange only for rendered options, so a run that changes only collapsed nodes emits none** — reproduced.
  tree-selection.component.ts:1261-1276: selection is applied to every data node through selectAllAdapter (targets include collapsed nodes, 506-508), but changedOptions keeps only rendered selectable options. When it is empty, the `changedOptions.length > 0` guard skips selectionChange, although the model changed and reportValue already ran. selectAllChange/onSelectAll still emit. Repro (passing): multiple="keyboard" with the 5 collapsed roots selected, Ctrl+A raises the value to all 11 nodes and emits selectAllChange once, but selectionChange is never called. Rated P3 because the form value and selectAllChange still report; only the per-option event is missing.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree/select-all-collapsed-no-event.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree/select-all-collapsed-no-event.spec.ts)
  Fix: Emit selectionChange whenever toggleSelectAll changed anything, falling back to an empty options list or the select-all row when no rendered option changed.
- **[tree] Saved expansion values that never match a node stay pending forever and are written back on every save** — confirmed in the source.
  tree-base.ts:225 fills pendingValues from storage. restorePending 496-503 removes a value only when hasValue finds its node, and expandedValues 541 writes expanded ∪ pendingValues on every saveState(). Nothing else prunes the set: clearSavedState (285-287) clears only the store, so the next expansion writes the stale values back as well. The test 'keeps the values it has not seen yet when something else is persisted' (tree-selection.component.spec.ts:5071) pins the union, and 'drops a saved value that names no node' (4869) checks only the expansion. The trade-off is deliberate for lazy data: the tree cannot tell a node that has not loaded yet from a deleted one. Storage hygiene, so no repro was run.
  Fix: Drop pending values once the data is known to be complete (or on clearSavedState), or bound how long an unmatched value is kept.
- **[tree] navigationChange does not fire for a pointer click** — confirmed in the source.
  A real mousedown focuses the option first: it has tabindex=-1 and no mousedown suppression (tree-option.html, tree-option.component.ts:129). focusin then runs focus() (135, 509-525), which emits onFocus, and listenToOptionsFocus (tree-selection.component.ts:1600-1609) moves the active index with keyManager.updateActiveItem, which emits nothing. The click then reaches setSelectedOptionsByClick 1108-1110, and setActiveItem (list-key-manager.ts:217-227) compares against the index already set, so `change` does not fire. navigationChange is emitted only from that `change` (836-843). Non-selectable or disabled options never reach setActiveItem (1104). jsdom's click() does not move focus, so a bare jsdom click shows the opposite. It needs the browser's focus-before-click order, as simulated in the shift-click repro.
  Fix: Emit navigationChange from the option-focus path when updateActiveItem changes the index, or compare against the index before the focus update.
- **[tree-select] Remove controls named only by a11y.remove without the node label; clipped tags behind "+N" keep tabindex=0** — reproduced.
  The name part is reproduced: tree-select.html:45-50 binds no aria-label, so KbqTagRemove.accessibleName falls back to the locale's a11y remove string (tags/tag.component.ts:879-882). The repro shows that the 'Apple' and 'Banana' buttons have the same aria-label and that it does not contain the node label. The clipped part is confirmed from source only, because it needs layout: \[tabIndex\]="0" is unconditional (tree-select.html:48), while KbqSelect binds \[aria-label\]="removeItemAriaLabel(option)" and \[tabIndex\]="isTriggerValueVisible($index) ? 0 : -1" (select/select.html:54-59). The name only matters once defect 1 is fixed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree-select/tag-remove-hidden.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree-select/tag-remove-hidden.spec.ts)
  Fix: Mirror KbqSelect: bind a per-node aria-label and drop clipped tags' remove buttons out of the tab order using hiddenItems().
- **[tree-select] Removing a tag and using the cleaner report the value to the form twice** — reproduced.
  onRemoveSelectedOption deselects (tree-select.component.ts:1527), which triggers reportValue through the changed subscription (1036-1042), and then calls reportValue() again explicitly (1537). clear() does the same (1184 then 1188). Repro: removing a tag gives valueChanges \[\['Banana','Cherry'\],\['Banana','Cherry'\]\], and the cleaner gives \[\[\],\[\]\]. The value is correct but emitted twice.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/tree-select/remove-reports-twice.spec.ts`](defect-repros/tree-select/remove-reports-twice.spec.ts)
  Fix: Remove the explicit reportValue() calls, or report only when the model did not emit.
- **[tree-select] typeAhead and autoSelect copied to the tree once at content init; later @if search keeps typeahead on; later \[autoSelect\] change desyncs tree and tree-select** — confirmed in the source.
  tree.typeAhead = !this.search() is set only in initKeyManager (tree-select.component.ts:1888), and tree.autoSelect = this.autoSelect only in ngAfterContentInit (1080). The search effect (955-968) re-applies only optionShouldHoldFocusOnBlur and the search subscription. tree.autoSelect is a linkedSignal written imperatively (tree-selection.component.ts:321-336), so a later \[autoSelect\] change leaves it stale. The tree's setSelectedOptionsByClick/ByKey (tree-selection 1094, 1121) read the stale tree value, while the tree-select's panelKeydownHandler reads its live getter (1674, 1698). A search added later keeps typeahead (withTypeAhead(200, 0)), so letters typed in it move the active option, and with it DOM focus, after 200 ms. Not reproduced: jsdom could show it, but it is P3 and outside the repro budget.
  Fix: Mirror typeAhead and autoSelect to the tree from effects on search() and autoSelectInput(), like selectAll.
- **[username] null/undefined fullNameFormat or pipe/formatter format throws TypeError; undefined mode/type yields kbq-username\_undefined** — reproduced.
  `kbqFormatUsername` calls `format.split('')` (username.pipe.ts:74) and `kbqFormatUsernameCustom` calls `format.split('')` (:112-113) without a guard. The default parameters of the pipes (:149, :171-172) and of the injected formatter (:188) apply only to `undefined`, so `null` throws. The component input (username.ts:109) keeps a bound `undefined`, so `formattedName` (username.ts:131-133) and `expandFormat` (`replaceAll`, username.ts:31) throw. Change detection rethrows a TypeError for both `null` and `undefined`. The class computed (username.ts:169-171) maps an `undefined` mode or type to `kbq-username\_undefined`. The repro passes for all six cases: functions, both pipes, the injected formatter, the component with null and with undefined, and mode/type undefined. Severity is P3 because every one of these values breaks the declared types (`input\<string>`, `format: string`), so strict template checking rejects them. It hits only consumers without strictTemplates, or values typed `any`.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/username/null-format.spec.ts`](defect-repros/username/null-format.spec.ts)
  Fix: Treat null and undefined as the default inside the formatters (e.g. `format ?? kbqDefaultFullNameFormat`), or give the inputs a transform that falls back to the default for nullish values.
- **[username] Visually hidden site label has no whitespace before the (site) hint** — reproduced.
  In username.html:21-23, 30-32, 46-48 and 59-61, the hidden label span and the hint span are separated only by a whitespace-only text node, which Angular's default whitespace removal drops. The repro confirms that the hidden label's `nextSibling` is the hint element. The secondary text is `mroot площадка(corp)`, and the host's `textContent` contains the label. In compact mode the name span and the label are also adjacent, giving `Root M.площадка (corp)`; the NBSP only separates the label from the hint. A screen reader may still insert a pause, because the absolutely positioned visually hidden span is blockified. That part needs a real browser; the textContent defect is confirmed.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/username/site-label-whitespace.spec.ts`](defect-repros/username/site-label-whitespace.spec.ts)
  Fix: Put an explicit space (`{{ ' ' }}` or `&#32;`) or `ngPreserveWhitespaces` around the hidden label so that it is a separate word in textContent and in the accessible text.
- **[username] \[kbq-username-custom-view\] projected without importing KbqUsernameCustomView renders alongside the default view** — reproduced.
  The `ng-content select="kbq-username-custom-view,\[kbq-username-custom-view\]"` (username.html:2) matches the attribute statically in the consumer template. `customView = contentChild(KbqUsernameCustomView)` (username.ts:124) matches only directive instances, so it is undefined when the directive is not imported. `@if (!customView() && profile)` (username.html:4) then renders the default view as well. The repro host imports only `KbqUsername` and renders `CustomRoot mroot`: the projected content plus the default primary and secondary parts, with no console error. The projected element also lacks the `kbq-username\_\_custom-view` class, so the layout wrapper of username.scss:6-11 applies to the host rather than to the custom view. This happens only when `userInfo` is bound. The element form would instead fail with an unknown-element error.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/username/custom-view-without-directive.spec.ts`](defect-repros/username/custom-view-without-directive.spec.ts)
  Fix: Detect projected custom content independently of the directive (e.g. a content check on the ng-content slot) or warn in dev mode; at minimum document that KbqUsernameCustomView must be imported.

## P4

- **[badge] A badgeColor string with whitespace is split into several classes** — confirmed in the source.
  badge.component.ts:115 binds `'\[class\]': 'badgeColorClass()'` and :136 builds `kbq-badge\_${badgeColor()}`. The transform at :130-133 does not trim or validate the value. Angular's `\[class\]` string binding (classMap) splits on whitespace, so `badgeColor="success foo"` adds `kbq-badge\_success` and `foo`. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because it is a consumer-misuse nit below P2. The input type is `string | KbqBadgeColors`, so arbitrary strings are allowed by design.
  Fix: Optionally trim or reject whitespace in the transform, or narrow the input type to KbqBadgeColors; otherwise leave as is.
- **[checkbox] Setting `checked` in code on a form-bound checkbox does not update the control, while toggle() does** — confirmed in the source.
  The `checked` setter (checkbox.ts:172-174) only does `checkable.checked.set(value)`. toggle() goes through KbqCheckable.toggle (checkable.ts:95-102), which calls notifyFormValueChange. ngOnChanges (checkbox.ts:142-146) uses the same setter, so a `\[checked\]` binding does not reach the control either. So the claim is true. It is, however, the usual ControlValueAccessor split, the same as Angular Material's MatCheckbox: the property is a model-to-view write and only user interaction or toggle() reports to the form. Mixing the property API with a form control is the consumer's choice. It is jsdom-showable but was not reproduced because of its low severity.
  Fix: Document that `checked` set in code or bound does not update a bound form control (use the control's setValue). Changing the setter would also push `\[checked\]` bindings into forms.
- **[content-panel] clearSavedState() does nothing while useStateSaving is false** — confirmed in the source.
  content-panel.ts:467-469 delegates to stateSaving.clear(), and clearState returns at once when `!this.useStateSaving()` (state-saving.ts:231-232). This is deliberate: core test state-saving.spec.ts:630-636 ('does not clear the persisted state while disabled') pins it, with the rationale that the key may be shared with a component that still persists. The entry can still be removed through KbqStateSavingService.remove(key) or clearOrphans() (state-saving-service.ts:146, 167). The only gap is that the clearSavedState JSDoc does not mention it.
  Fix: Document in the clearSavedState JSDoc that it is a no-op while useStateSaving is off and point to KbqStateSavingService.remove(); no behaviour change needed.
- **[empty-state] empty-state-text-only example declares unused styles and colors fields** — confirmed in the source.
  packages/docs-examples/components/empty-state/empty-state-text-only/empty-state-text-only-example.ts:22-23 declare `styles = KbqButtonStyles` and `colors = KbqComponentColors`. The template (lines 14-18) contains only a kbq-empty-state with a text slot and uses neither. Their imports from @koobiq/components/button and core (lines 2-3) exist only for these fields. The example is shown verbatim on the docs site, so the dead code is copied by readers.
  Fix: Remove the two fields and the KbqButtonStyles/KbqComponentColors imports.
- **[file-upload] Without DataTransferItem.webkitGetAsEntry a drop logs the warning and then calls the missing method** — confirmed in the source.
  primitives/file-drop.ts:27-30 only console.warns when `'webkitGetAsEntry' in DataTransferItem.prototype` is false (:8). Execution continues to :38 `.map(item => item.webkitGetAsEntry()!)`, which throws a TypeError inside the fromEvent subscriber (rxjs reports it as an unhandled error). There is no plain-files fallback: filesDropped never emits, and the cleanup after super.onDrop never runs (`dragover.set(false)` at file-drop.ts:99, `this.close()` of the full-screen service at dropzone.ts:197). Every browser Angular 21 supports (current Chrome, Edge, Firefox, Safari, iOS) implements webkitGetAsEntry, so this path is unreachable in practice; the warning and the missing fallback are dead code.
  Fix: Either fall back to `dataTransfer.files` when webkitGetAsEntry is missing or drop the check and the warning.
- **[filter-bar] The tree-select pipe projects a kbq-cleaner into kbq-tree-select, but the custom matcher replaces the slot that would render it** — confirmed in the source.
  pipe-tree-select.html:37 projects a top-level `\<kbq-cleaner />` and :39-50 a `kbq-select-matcher` button. tree-select.html:11-12 renders only the matcher slot when `customMatcher()` is set, and the cleaner slot (`@if (canShowCleaner)` with `\<ng-content select="kbq-cleaner">`, :80-84) lives in the `@else` branch, so it is never rendered. tree-select.component.ts:399 still resolves `cleaner` as a content child, but its only use is canShowCleaner (:794-796) inside that dead branch. Clearing works anyway through kbq-pipe-button when `cleanable` is set, so this is dead markup (an instantiated but detached KbqCleaner) with no user-visible effect. The `kbq-cleaner` at :27 inside the search form field is unaffected.
  Fix: Remove the top-level \<kbq-cleaner /> from pipe-tree-select.html.
- **[form-field] Fieldset examples and fixtures use \<legend kbqLegend> inside kbq-fieldset, which is not a native fieldset** — confirmed in the source.
  The HTML content model allows `legend` only as the first child of a `fieldset`. It is used inside the custom `kbq-fieldset` element in form-fieldset-overview-example.ts:20, form-fieldset-with-button-example.ts:18, fieldset.spec.ts:30 and 77, and form-field/e2e.ts:72. KbqLegend's selector is `\[kbqLegend\]` (fieldset.ts:7), so any element works. The group name still comes from `aria-labelledby` (fieldset.ts:58), and axe does not flag it, so the impact is validator conformance only.
  Fix: Use a neutral element such as `\<div kbqLegend>` or `\<span kbqLegend>` in the examples and fixtures.
- **[inline-edit] Selecting the text on open is guarded by if (initialValue), always true for the default array snapshot; with getValueHandler a value of 0 or '' skips the selection** — confirmed in the source.
  inline-edit.ts:597 runs 'if (this.initialValue) input?.select();'. initialValue comes from getValue() (581). Without getValueHandler that is an array with one entry per form field (1159-1163), so the guard is always true and does nothing. With getValueHandler returning 0, '', null or false, the guard skips input.select(). For '' there is nothing to select. For 0, the '0' in the input stays unselected, unlike every other value.
  Fix: Drop the guard, or check the input's own value instead of the snapshot.
- **[sidebar] Toggling twice before the first change is reported emits stateChanged once, with the unchanged state** — confirmed in the source.
  Each changing setOpened calls waitForStateAnimation (sidebar.component.ts:348). That function first destroys the pending wait (362), and kbqAfterAnimations' destroy cancels its callback (core/animation/animations-state.ts:98-117). Only the last wait fires, and it emits openedState() (369). Two toggles inside one transition (or before the first render) therefore produce a single stateChanged carrying the state the sidebar started with. The event reports the settled state, so a consumer tracking the state stays correct and only gets a redundant event. jsdom could show this, but it was not reproduced because it is below the P0-P2 threshold for a repro.
  Fix: Skip the emission when the settled state equals the last emitted one, or document that stateChanged reports the settled state.
- **[sidepanel] aria-labelledby keeps pointing at a removed header title; a later header cannot rename the panel** — confirmed in the source.
  The header only calls containerInstance.setAriaLabelledBy(titleId) in its constructor (sidepanel-directives.ts:120-122). It has no ngOnDestroy to clear it, so the id dangles after the header is removed. The 'cannot rename' half is effectively refuted. titleId is `${sidepanelRef.id}-title` (line 118), the same for every header in a panel, and setAriaLabelledBy refuses only once a value is set (sidepanel-container.component.ts:264-268). So a header rendered later carries the same id, and aria-labelledby resolves to it again. Two headers at once would only produce duplicate ids. With no header left, the accessible name algorithm skips an aria-labelledby whose IDREFs are all missing and falls back to aria-label, so the practical impact is minimal.
  Fix: Clear ariaLabelledBy in the header's destroy hook when it still equals its titleId.
- **[skeleton] Once off, a kbq-skeleton element loses its block display and minimum height and becomes an empty inline element, so consumer width and height no longer apply** — confirmed in the source.
  skeleton.scss:28-31: `display: flex` and `min-height` are set only by `:where(kbq-skeleton:not(.kbq-skeleton\_disabled))`; once off, skeleton.ts:153 adds `kbq-skeleton\_disabled`, nothing else sets display, and an unknown element defaults to `display: inline`, where width/height from a consumer class are ignored. The exclusion is deliberate (comment at skeleton.scss:27, baseline spec 'While off, the element MUST lose this display and minimum height'), and for an empty element the visual result matches removing it with @if. The only real inconsistency: inside a flex/grid parent the off element is blockified and keeps the consumer size as an empty box (as in e2e.ts:44-48, whose rows are flex), while in block flow it collapses. Layout cannot be observed in jsdom.
  Fix: Document that a kbq-skeleton element is meant to be swapped with @if, or keep `display: block` on the element when off if it should keep its box.
- **[split-button] Nothing limits the split button to two buttons; middle buttons of three get only kbq-split-button\_item** — confirmed in the source.
  split-button.ts:171-181 adds \_first to buttons.at(0), \_second to buttons.at(-1) and \_item to all, so a middle button gets only \_item. secondDisabled reads only buttons.at(-1) (split-button.ts:112-116). The joined styles key on \_first/\_second and on their adjacency: split-button.scss:127-174 (radius removal, divider, `\_first + \_second`) and \_split-button-theme.scss. A middle button therefore keeps its own radii and borders. The only content check is the zero-button error at split-button.ts:166, so three or more buttons pass silently. Three or more buttons is outside the component's two-part contract, so this is a missing guard rather than a user-facing bug. The CSS effect needs a browser; the classes come straight from the code.
  Fix: Add a dev-mode warning when more than two kbq-button children are projected.
- **[tags] kbq-tag-with-icon is bound to the array of projected icons, which is always truthy, so every tag carries the class; no style uses it** — confirmed in the source.
  tag.component.ts:238 binds '\[class.kbq-tag-with-icon\]' to 'contentChildren()', and tag.component.ts:343 makes that a contentChildren(KbqIcon) array signal. For an object value, Angular's class binding runs normalizeSuffix (node\_modules/@angular/core/fesm2022/\_debug\_node-chunk.mjs:16319-16326), which turns \[\] into the string "\[\]". applyStyling (:5072-5078) then adds the class for any truthy value, so every tag gets the class whether or not it has an icon. A grep of packages/apps/tools for scss/ts/html/css finds the class only at tag.component.ts:238, so nothing styles it. jsdom could show this, but I did not reproduce it because a P4 does not qualify for the repro budget.
  Fix: Bind it to `contentChildren().length > 0`, or drop the class since nothing styles it.
- **[title] disabled reads false until the first hover, focus, resize or mutation, even for text that fits** — confirmed in the source.
  ngAfterViewInit (title.directive.ts:202-256) sets the content but never calls setDerivedDisabled. foldDisabled with derivedDisabledWins returns explicitlyDisabled === true || (derivedDisabled ?? false), which is false (tooltip.component.ts:912-917). KbqOptionTooltip does seed the verdict (select-option.directive.ts:82-83). The existing spec already shows it: title.directive.spec.ts:1097 asserts disabled is not true right after creation of a fitting jsdom host. Hover and keyboard focus re-evaluate before showing. The directive's (mouseenter) host listener is registered at creation, before the base adds its own in ngOnInit (pop-up-trigger.ts:303-304, 654-687). The focus path calls handleElementEnter() before show() (title.directive.ts:234-239). The gap is therefore visible only to a programmatic show(), to \[kbqVisible\], or to reading disabled through exportAs before the first evaluation. In a browser the first ResizeObserver entry closes it after the 100 ms debounce. Not reproduced (low severity).
  Fix: Call this.setDerivedDisabled(!this.isOverflown) once in ngAfterViewInit, as KbqOptionTooltip does.
- **[toast] Toast ids and template ids come from two counters both starting at 0, so hide(templateId) removes the toast with the same number** — confirmed in the source.
  Toast ids come from 'let id = 0' (toast.component.ts:37, 106) and template ids from 'let templateId = 0' (toast.service.ts:49, 263). hide() looks only in toastsDict (toast.service.ts:276-281), so a template id passed to hide() removes a live toast with the same number, if one exists. This happens only when a template id goes to the wrong method (hideTemplate exists for template ids); show() and showTemplate() both return { ref, id: number }, so the types do not catch it. The library's only caller, the notification center, pairs show/hide correctly (notification-center.service.ts:325, 337). Not reproduced: a nit reachable only through misuse.
  Fix: Draw both kinds of ids from one counter, or keep the id spaces distinct, so a wrong-method call is a no-op.
- **[top-bar] placement outside 'start' | 'end' compiles without strictTemplates and leaves the container without layout** — confirmed in the source.
  top-bar.ts:37 types the input as `input.required\<'start' | 'end'>()` and has no runtime check. The host bindings at top-bar.ts:31-32 add a modifier only for exact 'start'/'end', so `placement="center"` gives neither class. Without a modifier the container gets none of the rules at top-bar.scss:66-78 (display:flex, gap, flex, justify-content, min-width) and lays out as a plain block flex item. With strictTemplates off, Angular does not type-check input bindings or static attributes (strictInputTypes/strictAttributeTypes off), so the template compiles. The repo itself has strictTemplates on (tsconfig.json:118). Only consumers who turn off type checking can reach this, and the typed contract is the usual Angular practice, so it is a nit. No repro: P4.
  Fix: Optionally fall back to 'start' (or warn in dev mode) for an unknown placement value.
- **[top-bar] kbq-animations-disabled reads prefers-reduced-motion once, so the class goes stale when the setting changes** — confirmed in the source.
  top-bar.ts:87 stores `kbqAnimationsDisabled()` as a plain boolean field when the bar is constructed. animations-state.ts:40 reads `matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches` once and registers no change listener, and its JSDoc says it reads the setting once (animations-state.ts:28-29). The host binding at top-bar.ts:62 therefore keeps the initial value. The bar's own visual is unaffected: the `kbq-animations-off()` mixin (\_animation.scss:75-78, used at top-bar.scss:43) also emits `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { transition: none }`, which follows the live setting. Only external CSS keyed on `.kbq-animations-disabled` sees the stale value, and that class is @docs-private (top-bar.ts:83-86). This is library-wide, documented behaviour. No repro: P4.
  Fix: If the class should track the setting, make kbqAnimationsDisabled a signal backed by a matchMedia change listener; otherwise document that the class reflects the setting at creation time.
- **[tree] A bound tabIndex of 0 is treated as unbound, so an empty tree drops to tabindex=-1** — confirmed in the source.
  tree-selection.component.ts:1537-1539 `tabIndexState.set(this.userTabIndex || (renderedOptions.length === 0 ? -1 : 0))`: a bound 0 is falsy, so an empty tree gets -1. This is the documented intent of that method: 'leaves the tab order, unless a tab index other than 0 is bound'. It is harmless, because focus() returns at once on an empty tree (925-928). The only gap is that the public tabIndex docs do not say so.
  Fix: Document it on the tabIndex input, or use `userTabIndex ?? …` if a bound 0 should be honoured.
- **[tree-select] A projected kbq-select-no-options is rendered whenever projected, also above a non-empty tree** — confirmed in the source.
  tree-select.html:130 projects `kbq-select-no-options, \[kbq-select-no-options\]` with no condition. This is the same contract as KbqSelect, which projects it through its default `\<ng-content />` (select/select.html:168) and expects the consumer to guard it, as in docs-examples select-no-variants-example.ts:31 (`@if (noOptions)`), the spec fixture TreeSelectWithoutOptions and filter-bar pipe-tree-select.html. The behaviour is as described but consistent with KbqSelect; it is undocumented rather than broken.
  Fix: Either document that the consumer renders it conditionally, or render the slot only when the tree has no nodes.
- **[tree-select] When searchMinOptionsThreshold hides the search, opening still tries to focus the detached search field** — confirmed in the source.
  The open handler calls search.focus() whenever search() exists (tree-select.component.ts:1000-1004), without the shouldShowSearch() guard used at 1124, 1650 and 1716. When the @if at tree-select.html:119-123 is false the projected input is never inserted into the DOM, so KbqSelectSearch.focus → KbqFormField.focus → KbqInput.focus → nativeElement.focus() (core/select/common.ts; form-field.ts:396-398; input.ts:293-295) is a no-op on a disconnected element, and the highlighted option keeps focus. The call is dead but has no visible effect.
  Fix: Guard the open-time search.focus() with shouldShowSearch().
- **[tree-select] The panel id is built from the generated id and ignores a custom `id`** — confirmed in the source.
  panelId = `${this.uid}-panel` (tree-select.component.ts:880-883), while id() is idInput() || uid (628). The host's aria-controls (244) and the panel's id (tree-select.html:112) both use panelId, so the reference stays valid and unique. KbqSelect builds its panel id the same way (select.component.ts:1156). This is a naming inconsistency only.
  Fix: Derive panelId from id() if a predictable `${id}-panel` is wanted (for both selects).
- **[username] Unmapped-key warning names KbqUsernameCustomPipe and is reported once per key for the whole application** — confirmed in the source.
  The warning text hard-codes `KbqUsernameCustomPipe:` (username.pipe.ts:39). `warnAboutUnmappedKey` is also called by the exported `kbqFormatUsernameCustom` (username.pipe.ts:117-118) when it is used directly. `reportedUnmappedKeys` is a module-level Set (username.pipe.ts:26), and the early return at :33 skips any key already reported, whatever the mapping or call site. A second wrong mapping that leaves the same key unmapped is therefore silent. The existing spec username.spec.ts:171-182 depends on this shared state. jsdom could show it; it was not reproduced because it is dev-mode diagnostics only (P4).
  Fix: Name the formatter generically in the message (or pass the caller name in), and key the dedup on the mapping as well as the letter (e.g. a WeakMap from mapping to reported keys).

## Needs a browser

- **[search-expandable] The collapsed button's aria-label equals its tooltip, which is also wired as aria-describedby** — needs a browser.
  search-expandable.html:6-7 bind the same tooltipText to \[attr.aria-label\] and \[kbqTooltip\]. While the tooltip is open, tooltip.component.ts:1249-1261 (describeTrigger) adds the tooltip id to aria-describedby. It skips this only when describesHostText (1293-1295) finds the content equal to the host's textContent, and the icon-only button's textContent is empty, so in the DOM both the name and the description are 'Search'. Whether this is announced twice depends on the browser's accessibility tree and on the screen reader: some drop a description identical to the name. Only a real browser with AT can settle it.
  Fix: Skip aria-describedby when the tooltip text equals the trigger's aria-label (in the tooltip), or do not describe the collapsed button.

## Refuted

- **[form-field] Forwarded ng-\* classes are read outside the reactive graph; markAllAsTouched() from another component may not reach the host classes** — refuted.
  `shouldForward` is untracked (form-field.ts:434-438), but this does not matter. Angular forms 21 NgControlStatus host bindings on the control element read the internal `\_touched()`, `\_pristine()` and `\_status()` signals (node\_modules/@angular/forms/fesm2022/forms.mjs:602-616, 1053-1098). They run in the same component view that runs KbqFormField's host bindings, so a forms state change refreshes that view and re-evaluates `shouldForward`. Repro specs with an OnPush host and `markAllAsTouched()` called from test code (no event, no value change): the form field gets ng-touched both with the control in the same template and with the control inside an `@if` block. Embedded views have no reactive consumer of their own, so their signal reads refresh the enclosing component view. The exhaustive checkNoChanges of the test setup raised no NG0100. Content queries cannot see a control declared in another component template, so no supported layout escapes this. The only untested gap is a control whose NgControl comes without NgControlStatus.
  Repro: [`docs/review/defect-repros/form-field/forwarded-ng-classes.spec.ts`](defect-repros/form-field/forwarded-ng-classes.spec.ts)
- **[select] When searchMinOptionsThreshold hides the search, opening may focus the detached search field instead of the active option** — refuted.
  The search really is focused without a shouldShowSearch() check, at panelDoneAnimatingStream (select.component.ts:1308-1312) and in keyManager.change (:2449-2453), while the field is projected only inside `@if (search() && shouldShowSearch())` (select.html:125-129) and therefore stays detached. Focus is not lost, though. attachPanel registers its afterNextRender first (:1560-1573), and that callback focuses the active option (scrollActiveOptionIntoView, :2581-2583) before reportPanelRendered's callback (:1575, :1602-1611) runs search.focus(). focus() on a disconnected element is a no-op under the HTML focusing steps, and jsdom's isFocusableAreaElement checks isConnected the same way. A throwaway spec (threshold 4, 3 options, open by clicking the trigger) confirmed the setup (panel open, search input disconnected, active option in the overlay), then failed its DEFECT assertion because document.activeElement was the active option. The unguarded calls are only dead code. The spec was not kept because it fails by design.
- **[toast] Views destroyed from outside are purged only on heartbeat steps, which are skipped while paused, keeping the overlay attached** — refuted.
  The mechanism is real: purgeDestroyed runs only in processToasts (toast.service.ts:472-473), and the timer filters out steps while paused (164). The consequence does not follow. A pause exists only while a live toast is hovered or focused, and every KbqToastComponent releases its hover and focus in ngOnDestroy (toast.component.ts:145-150). Destroying the hovered toast therefore ends the pause at once, and the next step purges it. If a different toast is hovered, that toast is still on screen, so the overlay must stay attached and the heartbeat must run anyway. The toasts/templates getters already filter destroyed views (toast.service.ts:126-136), so a stale record has no observable effect. Template toasts never report hover or focus. The only way an otherwise empty root overlay stays attached is a hovered toast from a consumer-hosted container holding the root pause, which is defect 2.
